- **THE PANE READ PAIR IS TOTAL, AND ITS ROW REDS WITHOUT THE GUARD** (`D-94`'s item *(e)*: *"`startSizeOf`/`boundsOf` are not total over a shape whose `paneSizeOf`/`paneBoundsOf` throw … `readPaneSize`/`readPaneBounds` have no guard; the other six turns do"*). Each now catches its own shape call and answers **what an ABSENT shape answers** — the measured default `100`/`{min:0,max:200}` from the ONE shared declaration — which is the pattern COPIED from those six and not a widening of them (`candidatesFor` answers `[]`, the render turn answers nothing: each its own declared degradation). **PROVED BY A ROW THAT CAN FAIL**: `pane-drag-compliance`'s `F-8/throwing-shape-read` — the row `D-94` recorded the gap in, whose own note said the pair was *"NOT in this row"* — now drives the throwing shape through the drag's START, asserts the default rather than a propagated throw, and keeps the control that a shape which ANSWERS is still believed (`250`/`{10,20}`, figures no default carries). **RED ON REVERT, MEASURED VERBATIM**: with the two guards removed the row answers `AssertionError: the SIZE read absorbs the throw — the seventh turn: expected [Function] to not throw an error but 'Error: boom' was thrown`; restored, the file is `129` passed and the collected count does NOT move, because the pair was brought INSIDE a row instead of given one of its own.

**THE PARITY BAR.** The four legs are **`0/0/0/0`** on the committed tree and `npm test` reads **`2,877` passed / `2` skipped of `2,879` over `124` files — the same figure `D-93` and `D-94` left, because this pass adds no test file and no collected row**; `strata/marks.json` is therefore unmoved (`gating` `175` spec sites in `24` files, `on notice` `2,479`, carve-outs `7`) and `strata-marks.mjs check` is green. The FIVE Electron legs on `DISPLAY=:0` hold every standing figure — `battery` **`184/0`**, `divergence` **`9/0 + 4/0`**, `ui` **`11/11`**, `panes` **`7/7`**, `crossings` **`28/28`** — with **ZERO survivors after every one**, reaped by process tree, and a `ps` census taken before the first leg and after each: **`0`** processes of this repository's throughout, against the SAME four foreign `chrome_crashpad_handler` processes at the SAME PIDs (Discord `4245`, the two snap-chromium `4348`/`4358`, OpenCode `4593`), untouched. **`brief.md` §6's `crossings` row moves in its OWN commit and its line count does not**: the figure is re-written IN PLACE on the line that already carried it, so the file stays at `149` of `150` and NO line is freed — and `pending.md` (`39`), `next-steps.md` (`29`), `strata/records/store-tabs-record-unit.md` (exactly `40`), `docs/L2/view-record/interface.md` (`118`) and `docs/L3/view-record/design.md` (`125`) are untouched, because no declared shape and no tracker row moves.

**WHAT REMAINS OPEN, NAMED RATHER THAN CLOSED.** *(a)* **the `overlay`/`theme` ruling** — the corpus requires both modules and no `src/` importer exists, so their disposal is the user's and not a census's. *(b)* **`security.setExclusion`'s own answer is unread by any instrument** (`D-94`'s item *(d)*, unchanged: the tier the write closes is the tier that would carry the observation). *(c)* **the demo's disposition is now a KEEP on the corpus's naming**, so a future deletion owes the `demo-panes` map row and L1's coverage sentence rather than only a `git rm`. *(d)* **the view record's push has no authored-terminal reading**, so no rendered-surface claim is made for it — the limit is the app's no-op subscriber, not this leg's reach.

## D-96 — THE `overlay`/`theme` RULING IS TAKEN: `theme` IS WIRED AT LAST — its consumer is the app renderer's wiring module, the declaration reaches the AUTHORED node as provident data through ONE `state-slice`, and the driving row REDS on both reverts — while `overlay` is **STOPPED AND NAMED**, because this app authors no overlay to consume it

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-03 · **Source:** the user's ruling, VERBATIM: **"overlay/theme should be consumed by the module renderers (Assuming these are related to the light/dark mode)"** — read against `D-95`'s open item *(a)*, which left exactly this open: *"the corpus requires both modules and no `src/` importer exists, so their disposal is the user's and not a census's."* Measured on this head over the installed `provident-storeroom@1.9.1`.

**Decision.** **The two mechanisms are NOT legacy and NOT dead — they were OWED a consumer, and one of them now has it.** `theme` is consumed by `src/renderer/renderer.ts`, the app's renderer-wiring module; `overlay` has **no lawful consumer in this tree** and is left standing, with the reason measured rather than assumed (the `D-90` refusal, applied to a module instead of a store).

- **WHAT THEY ARE, ANSWERED FROM THEIR OWN BYTES AND THEIR OWN FILED CONTRACTS — AND THE LIGHT/DARK GUESS IS HALF RIGHT, WITH THE HALF MATTERING.** `src/shared/theme.ts` is a pure total stateless PAIR: `resolveTheme(setting, env)` resolves **a caller-supplied OPAQUE appearance token** against **a caller CLAIM about its own environment** (`env = { prefersDark: boolean }`) into the three-member `ThemeResolution` — `{setting, prefersDark, source: 'env' | 'degraded-env'}` — and `applyThemeDeclaration(attributeName, resolved)` returns `{name, value, removal}`, *"the attribute write it would perform"*, **the name caller-supplied, echoed BY IDENTITY, and the removal case represented as DATA**. **So `theme` IS the appearance resolver behind a light/dark setting — and the module OWNS NO SPECK OF THAT VOCABULARY**: `§1` item 2 is *"the module contains NO `'light'`, no `'dark'`, no `'system'`, no token name, no token value"*, and `prefersDark` is a *"CALLER CLAIM ABOUT AN ENVIRONMENT, never an OS behaviour this repo can observe"* (`§2.3` item 2). The light/dark vocabulary is the CONSUMER's, and the consumer authored here is the demo envelope's own card (`THEME_INITIAL_TOKEN = 'dark'`, the two `theme-set` buttons, the `theme-setting` state node). `src/shared/overlay.ts` is the SIBLING form of the same discipline and is a different mechanism: a **four-state overlay state machine** (`closed`/`open`/`held`/`closing`) over five verb words, `overlayTransition(state, verb, callback?)` → `{state, changed}` — *not* a backdrop and *not* a rendered transition — plus `overlayInertDeclaration(target, attributeName, inert)` → `{name, value, removal, target}`, **the INERT-BACKGROUND declaration** a consumer puts on its background while an overlay is up (`value: 'true'` on the strict-boolean set arm, `false` + `removal: true` on every other input, `target` echoed by identity and read by NOTHING).
- **THE DECLARATION IS DATA FOR SOMEONE ELSE, AND THE CORPUS NAMES WHO.** Both records are `{name, value, removal}` (+ overlay's echoed `target`): the modules take no element, write nothing and own no attribute name, so the applied half is the CONSUMER's by contract (`overlay.md` §2.5 item 2: *"the applied write is the CONSUMER's"*). **And the corpus already names the ROUTE, in the overlay guide's own words**: *"**(a) `props: { inert: 'true' }` on an authored node** — the project-wide UI-constraint route: the attribute rides the authored envelope as pure data"* (`archive/gate-corpus-2026-10-29/guide/overlay.md` § "Making the background inert"). That is the route taken here, and it is the `UI-RENDERED-WITH-PROVIDENT` constraint's own route.
- **THE CONSUMER, IDENTIFIED, AND THE CORPUS NAMED ITS *KIND* BUT NEVER THE FILE.** Two modules in this tree render UI at all: `src/renderer/runtime.ts` (the app graph's producing process, `renderProducingProcess` at `:192`/`:197`) and `src/renderer/secure-panels.ts` (the isolated Security/Debug panes' own Runtime at `:684`). `src/renderer/renderer.ts` is the app renderer's **wiring** module — `demo-envelope.ts:6` calls it *"the renderer module … core-only plumbing"* — and it ALREADY carries both of the appearance control's holders: the authored card lives in the envelope it bootstraps, and `themeWiringRole` (`:1043`) holds the caller-supplied attribute NAME and derives the authored state node's id from the envelope (`§5.1` row 12's ONE bounded wiring role). **`D-94` measured the corpus as it stood — *"No consumer is planned by anything in the corpus"* — and that reading is TRUE OF THAT HEAD AND ITS OWN DATED FINDING, not a prohibition**: what the corpus DOES grant, and what this pass acts on, is the LICENCE, in the mechanism's own contract: *"a later `F1` pass LAWFULLY importing this module must NOT be read as falsifying `U-THEME`. The denial binds this unit's own diff; it does not declare the module permanently importer-less"* (`theme.md` §2.5 item 3). `F1` is `U-THEME-CONTROL`, whose surfaces are in THIS file, so the file is the lawful consumer and no consumer was invented.
- **THE WIRING, AND THE DECLARATION REACHES THE AUTHORED NODE AS PROVIDENT DATA.** `startThemeDeclaration(runtime)` (`renderer.ts:1076`) is held in `main()` at `:821` and driven from the app's OWN change push at `:862` — `themeDeclaration.drive()` sits in `handleRequest`'s notify callback, the one `panels?.refreshDebug(runtime)` already rides (`RH-3` half (b)), BEFORE the notify so a reader of the push sees the graph the declaration left. The drive READS the authored setting (`Runtime.nodeState`, `get_node_state`'s own surface), calls **`resolveTheme`** and **`applyThemeDeclaration`** (the mechanism stays pure: it computes, the wiring applies), and applies the answer as **ONE `state-slice`** — `{kind:'state-slice', node: <derived authored id>, mutation: [{targetProp: 'props.' + declaration.name, mode: 'replace', value: removal ? undefined : declaration.value}]}`. `props.<name>` renders as the bare attribute (`props.resizable` → `resizable`, `demo-envelope.ts`) and an `undefined` value is the engine's declared attribute REMOVAL, so the removal arm is the mechanism's `removal` member and never an empty string that happens to be falsy. **NO hand-written DOM**: no attribute stamp, no class flip, no style write, no created element, no injected CSS — measured green by `gutter-ui`'s `R-13(i)` wiring-token scan, which reds on any of them.
- **IT DECLARES ON A CHANGE AND NOT ON A BOOT, AND THE REASON IS MEASURED.** `props.<name>` reaches **`renderedHtml` AND `ssrHtml`** (measured on a scratch probe: `<div … id="theme-setting" … theme="dark">` in both), and `npm run divergence`'s `SSR fragment matches (structural)` row compares that `ssrHtml` between the REAL app and the headless battery host — which holds no renderer wiring at all (`src/main/battery-host.ts` boots a `Runtime` and its own `invoke` switch). A declaration at boot, or on a reply that moved nothing, would therefore make the two realms render differently and red a leg that exists to prove they do not. So the drive records the authored setting it FOUND (a read, never a write) and declares only when that setting MOVES — which is the ruling's own condition, *a change of state produces the declaration*. **Its one stated limit**: an MCP-originated mutation drives it and a DOM-originated one does not, exactly as the Debug pane's refresh does not; the runtime exposes no post-render hook and this pass claims no new one.
- **PROVED BY A ROW THAT CAN FAIL, AND BOTH REVERTS MEASURED VERBATIM** (`tests/theme-declaration.test.ts`, `6` rows, no `strata:marked` marker so it GATES): `D-96/1` drives the authored `theme-light` handler (`provident.dispatch theme-light click light`), sees the setting move `dark → light`, drives, and finds `props.theme="light"` on the authored node in `renderedHtml` — with the runtime's own `'applied'` beside it; `D-96/2` the removal arm (`''` → `removal: true` → attribute GONE); `D-96/3` a no-op drive moves not one byte of `renderedHtml` or `ssrHtml`; `D-96/4` the three resolved members, and the caller's `env` claim given no precedence over its own token; `D-96/5` the wiring's own presence with two controls; `D-96/6` totality over an unreadable carrier and a throwing write. **REVERT A — the `themeDeclaration.drive()` line removed from the reply notify**: `D-96/5` answers `AssertionError: the declaration is driven by the reply notify: expected false to be true`. **REVERT B — the `state-slice` write removed so the declaration is COMPUTED and never sent**: `D-96/1`, `/2`, `/3`, `/5`, `/6` red, the first being `AssertionError: the runtime's OWN answer to the state-slice write: expected 'no-answer' to be 'applied'`. Restored, the file reads `6 passed`.
- **THE 157 ROWS, DISPOSITIONED ROW BY ROW: FOUR RE-GRAINED, AND NOT ONE CONTRADICTED.** **Unchanged: all `69` of `tests/overlay.test.ts`** (whole subject `overlay.ts`, which this pass does not touch — its `X-5` "no overlay surface under `src/**`" and `X-6` "ZERO importers" are both still TRUE) **and all `58` of `tests/theme.test.ts` but ONE**: `R-6`'s no-importer half asserted *"zero importers, not one"* (`D-93`'s rollback form) and is now FALSE, so it is **re-grained** to a CLOSED SET WITH ONE NAMED MEMBER — `src/renderer/renderer.ts`, exact-membership, with its bounded-extension control kept intact and the `D-93` form kept visible beside. The same form is taken by **`tests/store-modules-seams.test.ts`'s `S-TH-1`**, **its `E-TH-1`**, and **its register row `P-SMS-TH-IM-1`'s `tree-census` attempt**, each re-grained to one admitted carrier by name with the planted-consumer control unchanged; and by **`tests/theme-control.test.ts`'s `P-TC-TP-3` attempt 12**, whose allowed set gains the one path and whose bounded-extension control still proves a SECOND carrier is a FINDING — plus one new assertion that the admitted member is MEASURED rather than merely allowed. **The register's count does not move** (still `62` collected rows in `store-modules-seams`, `58` in `theme`, `61` in `theme-control`, `69` in `overlay`): every re-grain is an in-place term, and `theme-control`'s `P-TC-TP-3` attempt 11 — *"the wiring role holds NO mechanism reference"* — stays TRUE and untouched, because the new role is a SEPARATE declaration and `themeWiringRole` remains the deliberately inert name-holder `§2.4` item 2 requires.

## D-97 — NO BOOT-TIME DECLARATION: THE APPEARANCE FAMILY BECOMES A CONSTRAINT REPAIR'S PRODUCT — `['theme']` at `mem`, a pair with a SECOND ARM over a DERIVATION, a route that carries the ACT — and the renderer APPLIES the record, computing nothing

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-03 · **Source:** the user's instruction this pass carries, VERBATIM: **"No boot time declarations / Use constraint repairs"**. Measured on this head over the installed `provident-storeroom@1.9.1`; the probe that decided the family's mechanics is recorded at §3's `TH-1`…`TH-11`.

**THE GROUND, MEASURED BEFORE ANYTHING WAS WRITTEN.** `D-96`'s `startThemeDeclaration` (`src/renderer/renderer.ts`) held the mechanism's only consumer in this tree: it BOOT-READ the authored setting through `runtime.nodeState`, called `resolveTheme(setting, env)` and `applyThemeDeclaration(attributeName, resolved.setting)`, and applied the answer as ONE `state-slice` of `props.<name>` on the authored state node — driven from the app's reply notify, and keyed on a `changed` record precisely because a boot declaration would move `ssrHtml` and red `npm run divergence`. Its three recorded limits were: **no boot declaration** (deliberate, that row says so in as many words), **an MCP-push-only drive**, and **`env` undefined, so `source: 'degraded-env'`**. The mechanism is a pure total stateless pair: `resolveTheme(setting: unknown, env: unknown) → {setting: string|null, prefersDark: boolean, source: 'env'|'degraded-env'}` and `applyThemeDeclaration(attributeName: unknown, resolved: unknown) → {name: string|null, value: string, removal: boolean}`; it owns no light/dark vocabulary, no attribute name and no write, and `applyThemeDeclaration` reads ONLY the setting — so `prefersDark` has no effect on any declaration, which is why nothing in this tree can honestly supply it (`matchMedia` is banned corpus-wide and the crossing holds no window) and the claim stays OMITTED rather than fabricated. **THE TOKEN'S HOME WAS THE AUTHORED GRAPH**: `demo-envelope.ts`'s `theme-setting` node carries `THEME_INITIAL_TOKEN`, the two `theme-set` handlers rewrite its `content`, and `themeWiringRole` holds the caller-supplied attribute NAME beside the node id it derives from the envelope — so a user action reached the setting as provident data and reached the mechanism only through that file.

- **THE FAMILY IS A FOURTH ONE ON THE NORMAL CROSSING — `['theme']` AT `mem`, BESIDE `['panes']`/`['focus']`/`['zones']`** (D-87, D-91). UI state with no security character, so the crossing that already holds the tab record's and the view record's three; **and the tier is the MEASUREMENT and not a preference**: the app persists no appearance setting today — it is the authored envelope's `content`, re-authored at every boot, and the address the deleted `theme-store.ts` projected is gone with the frozen store — while a DURABLE appearance preference is the ambient store `U-THEME-CONTROL`'s prohibition row R-2 fails and its F-8 falsifier names. So a `file` address would be a behaviour change nothing measured; `mem` re-mints the family at every boot, which is what the app does today, and the profile's bytes never carry it (`measured`: boot two answers the placeholder and the file is byte-identical). **THE RECORD IS `{ token, attribute, declaration: { name, value, removal } }`** — the setting as `resolveTheme` normalises it, the caller-held name the act carried, and **the declaration the mechanism computed for that pair, STORED and never recomputed at a read**: clause 1 is a constraint over a VALUE, so a member derived on read could never be stale and the pair below would have nothing to judge, and clause 4 forbids a re-derivation on a read. `token`, `attribute` and a null declaration `name` are written as ABSENCE, because the crossing refuses `null` (measured, D-87/D-91).
- **THE PAIR IS `theme-must-stand` — THE TENTH ID OF THE BOOT'S ONE NAMESPACE AND THE FOURTH OF THIS MODULE'S — and it has TWO ARMS.** The first is existence, `tabs-must-stand`'s and `focus-must-stand`'s: not a plain record, repaired to the PLACEHOLDER, which is `applyThemeDeclaration(null, null)` — the mechanism's own `{name: null, value: '', removal: true}`, minted by the same one derivation so the repair and the read cannot disagree. **THE SECOND IS STALENESS, AND IT IS WHAT MAKES THE RESOLUTION A REPAIR'S PRODUCT**: a record that STANDS but whose carried declaration is not the one the mechanism would produce for the setting and the attribute it carries is INTERNALLY CONTRADICTORY — a resolution for a token it no longer carries — so the pair answers `stale-theme` and its repair re-derives from the value's OWN two members and writes ONE whole legal value through `writeWholeValue`, the module's one repair-write path, answering the credited arm through `repairAnswer` (D-72, D-73). The comparison is STRUCTURAL (`sameThemeValue`) and never a serialisation: **the crossing normalises a value's key order (measured: a literal `{value, removal}` reads back `{removal, value}`)**, so a byte comparison would report every legal record stale. **AND THIS MODULE IS NOW THE TREE'S ONLY CALLER OF `resolveTheme`/`applyThemeDeclaration`** — the consumer `D-96` named moved from the renderer to the crossing, which is the whole of what "use constraint repairs" buys.
- **THE ROUTE CARRIES THE ACT AND NEVER A RESOLUTION**: `commitTheme(token, attributeName)` writes the caller's PAIR — a string setting, the empty one admitted as the mechanism's declared absence, and a non-empty attribute name — and the engine re-runs the pair inside that very commit, so the repair resolves it and ONE delivery follows (measured: one, not two). Its shape arms answer `malformed-theme` before any store verb. **THE ATTRIBUTE NAME IS A REQUIRED ARGUMENT AND NOT A CONSTANT OF THIS MODULE**, which is `theme-control`'s rule and not a preference: the name is CALLER-SUPPLIED and held by the consuming wiring (`themeWiringRole`), so `src/main/` spells no attribute name anywhere and the mechanism echoes the caller's by identity into the record. **A user action reaches it in three moves**: the authored control writes the setting, the renderer's `commit()` turn reads it (`runtime.nodeState`, the surface `get_node_state` answers on) and sends `commitTheme(setting, attributeName)` — **an EXTERNAL EVENT, clause 4's own class**, with no timer, poll or write-on-read anywhere in the module, its surface or the wiring; the crossing's pair resolves it; and the delivery applies it.
- **THE RENDERER APPLIES, AND `startThemeDeclaration` IS DELETED WHOLE** — the role, its `drive`, its boot read and its `changed` record. `startThemeApplication(runtime)` replaces it with two turns: `commit()` (the user action's — it reads the authored setting and sends it only when it MOVED, so a reply that moved nothing sends nothing) and `apply()` (the delivery's — it reads `getViewClient().themeRecord()` and writes `props.<declaration.name>` with the declaration's own `value`/`removal` as ONE `state-slice`, the surface D-96 established). `startViewShape(themeApplication)` hands the delivery to it, and the boot's ONE stated `fill()` applies nothing: **the record a boot carries is the placeholder, whose declaration names no attribute, so no `props.<name>` exists to write and boot silence is a property of the DATA rather than a guard on a drive**. **ONE READ OF THE AUTHORED SETTING SURVIVES AT BOOT AND IT DECLARES NOTHING** — `D-96`'s seed, which records the setting this realm FOUND (`themeWiringRole`'s node, `Runtime.nodeState`) so that the first act is a CHANGE a user made and not the token an envelope authored. **THAT LINE WAS ADDED BACK AFTER `npm run divergence` REDDED ON THE FIRST DRAFT**, and the reading is kept: the draft carried the found setting on the boot's first reply, the delivery wrote `props.theme` from it, and the leg answered `dirtied ids match` `electron=[5 …]` against `shim=[3 …]` with `SSR fragment matches (structural)` failing — the leg doing exactly what this instruction relies on it for. `resolveTheme` and `applyThemeDeclaration` are no longer in the renderer's scope at all, so "computes nothing" is a compile-time fact and not a promise; the `themeWiringRole` holder survives byte-unmoved, its name now carried to the route instead of into a computed declaration. **`env` is answered by finding**: nothing in this tree can supply `prefersDark` honestly — the media-query surface is banned corpus-wide and the crossing holds no window — so the claim is OMITTED, `resolveTheme` answers `degraded-env`, and neither the record nor the declaration carries a fabricated one.
- **PROVED BY ROWS THAT CAN FAIL, AND THE REVERTS ARE NAMED.** `tests/integration/store-view-record/theme-family.test.ts` (11 rows, gated, unmarked): **`TH-1`** the creation instant — with the batch the store's OWN read answers the placeholder before any route runs, and a store with NO batch answers `AddressNotFound` at the same address; **`TH-4` THE STALE ARM** — the pair refuses a record whose declaration is a resolution for another token, its sentence and token are asserted by name, and what stands after the write is the CURRENT resolution with the token it carried kept; **`TH-3`** the route's one write and the ONE delivery it causes; **`TH-5`** a legal record passes, and the serialisation comparison it does NOT use is measured unequal; **`TH-8`** two boots re-mint and the profile never carries the family; **`TH-10`** the write census (two sites, the one repair path) with no timer and no poll; **`TH-2`** the placeholder IS the mechanism's own answer. `tests/theme-declaration.test.ts` is RE-GRAINED WHOLE onto the application (8 rows): **`T-4` is the boot-silence row, measured THREE ways** — the record's half, the surface's (the rendered HTML and the SSR fragment both byte-identical across a boot), and the wiring's OWN boot site, whose fill continuation carries no application at all — and it states the leg that would catch a boot application, `npm run divergence`, whose `SSR fragment matches (structural)` row compares the app's `ssrHtml` against the headless battery host's; **`T-7` is the BASELINE row, and it is the one this pass's own first draft failed**, a boot carrying the token an envelope authored rather than a change a user made. **FOUR REVERTS WERE DRIVEN, EACH RED THEN RESTORED**: `theme-must-stand` out of the batch reds **14 of the two batteries' 19** rows (`TH-1`…`TH-11`, `T-1`…`T-4`, and `T-6`'s cold arm) — the address then answers `AddressNotFound` and the route's write stands at the caller's pair with no declaration; the stale arm disabled, the pair answering `true` for any plain record, reds **11 of 19** (`TH-3`…`TH-10`, `T-1`…`T-4`), the first reading `expected {token: null, attribute: null, …} to deeply equal {token: 'light', …}`; a boot application added to the fill's continuation reds **`T-4`**, whose boot-site clause says so in as many words; and the boot baseline removed — this pass's own first draft — reds **`T-7`** (`expected ['dark', true, true] to deeply equal ['dark', false, false]`) and redded `npm run divergence` while it stood (`dirtied ids match`: `electron=[5 …]` against `shim=[3 …]`, `SSR fragment matches (structural)` failed). On the committed tree that leg reads `9 checks, 0 failures` plus `4` extension checks.
- **WHAT IT COSTS, MEASURED.** `npm test` **`2,883` passed / `2` skipped of `2,885` over `125` files → `2,896` / `2` of `2,898` over `126`** — this pass's own THIRTEEN rows and ONE file, and nobody else's; `srcTypeScriptFiles` stays `50` and `mapRows` stays `21` (no new module — the family lives in `view-record`'s own files); `strata/modules.json`'s `store-view-record` row gains the battery's path and its `endpoints` moves **`5 → 6`** (cap `100 → 110`); `strata/marks.json` REGENERATED by `strata-marks.mjs index` — `gating` `181 → 194` sites in `25 → 26` files, `on notice` `2,479` and the carve-outs `7` unmoved — and `strata-marks.mjs check` is green; the four legs are `0/0/0/0` on the committed tree. **THE TEST SURFACE MOVED IN SIX PLACES AND EACH IS A RE-GRAIN, NOT A WEAKENING**: `tests/theme.test.ts`'s `R-6` and `X-5` (the mechanism's importer and name censuses, to a closed set with named members — `X-5` bought with a TIGHTENING, its ambient-appearance vocabulary now scanned over the WHOLE `src/**` tree with no exemption), `tests/store-modules-seams.test.ts`'s `S-TH-1`/`E-TH-1`/`P-SMS-TH-IM-1`, `tests/theme-control.test.ts`'s `P-TC-TP-3` attempt 12, and the crossing doubles of `store-focus`, `pane-drag-compliance`, `store-modules-seams` and `client.test.ts`, which now speak the fourth family; **one instrument was CORRECTED and the correction is stated in its own bytes** (`P-TC-TP-3`'s import form ended at the next `;`, and this tree is semicolon-free, so a forty-line span was read as an import); and `tests/gesture-session.test.ts`'s sibling-artifact list gains `src/main/view-surface/index.ts`. **The prose that re-spelled the authored control's node ids outside the envelope and the wiring role was rewritten** — `theme-control`'s `R-4` caught it, which is the row working.
- **ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-96`**, as prose and never as a bare number: the change push returned to the record root; a repair's credit was made explicit and its write made to name its commit; the boot battery's teardown was made to prove zero survivors; the landing entry's id was decided and its seat derived and then the id moved; the renderer's tab client appeared read-only; the two-crossing ruling named the purge; the surplus instance was collapsed onto the normal crossing and the `data.*` tools pinned to the central store; the module registry's legacy file was dropped rather than migrated; the data-flow convention was audited clause by clause; the imperative boot write was deleted for a sixth declaration; the `mem` pair's existence was declared at the store's creation; the absence-keyed import went inside the repair; `1.9.1`'s landing-arm save was measured; the purge's two successor addresses were declared; the renderer swap was refused on measurement and then landed; the enumeration that stopped on the zone family; the zone family's own address; the frozen store made unused and then DELETED; the two stale imports taken with the crossings given a live leg a leg runs; the view record's crossing given a live leg with the pane read pair made total; and the appearance mechanism's consumer named at last. **The package-facing rows among them stay the package's** — `provident-storeroom` is read and never modified, its own corpus's decision ids are never spelled here, and a defect or a requirement gap of it belongs to the unit that surfaced it, with symptom, reproduction, suspected root cause and proposed fix shape (D-5, D-52).

**IT SUPERSEDES THE FRAMING OF THE ROWS ABOVE IT, NEVER THEIR DATED TEXT** (D-46). `D-96`'s boot-drive statement is SUPERSEDED BY POINTER and not reworded: the role it describes is deleted, its `changed` keying is answered at the root instead of at the drive, and its three recorded limits now read — **no boot declaration** stands, and it is now a property of the data rather than a deliberate omission; **the MCP-push-only drive** stands unchanged, since a DOM-originated click still reaches the crossing only through the reply the dispatch causes; and **`env` undefined** stands, now with the finding that nothing in this tree can honestly supply it. `D-95`'s open item is untouched, `overlay` is untouched and still unwired for the reason `D-96` measured, and `strata/pending.md` is unchanged at `39`.

**⟶ APPENDED 2026-11-03 — `D-97`'s TIER SENTENCE IS SUPERSEDED BY POINTER, AND NOT ONE WORD OF IT IS REWORDED (`D-46`).** The user's ruling *"Appearance preferences should be durable"* overturns the paragraph above that set `['theme']` at `mem`: the family's tier is `file` from `D-98`, its boot datum is the FILE, and the prohibition clause that made `mem` the only lawful tier is FALSE of durability and superseded there. **WHAT STANDS FROM THIS ROW**: the family, its address, its record's three members, its pair with the staleness arm, its route carrying the ACT, the renderer applying the record and computing nothing, and the four reverts — all unchanged and re-measured at `D-98`. **WHAT DOES NOT**: the sentence *"a `file` address would be a behaviour change nothing measured"*, the sentence *"the profile's bytes never carry it"*, and the reading of the placeholder as what keeps a boot silent.

## D-98 — APPEARANCE PREFERENCES ARE DURABLE, ON THE USER'S RULING: `['theme']` MOVES FROM `mem` TO `file` BESIDE `['panes']` — the prohibition row that made `mem` the only lawful tier is FALSE of durability and is SUPERSEDED BY POINTER, the boot's datum is the FILE, the stale arm still refuses and repairs over a RESTARTED store, and the four rows that pinned the `mem` shape are INVERTED with their controls kept

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-03 · **Source:** the user's ruling, VERBATIM: **"Appearance preferences should be durable"** — read against `D-97`'s tier judgement, which this pass OVERTURNS. Measured on this head over the installed `provident-storeroom@1.9.1`.

**THE GROUND, MEASURED BEFORE ANYTHING WAS WRITTEN, AND THE CLAUSE IT LANDED ON.** `D-97` set the tier by two grounds and said which: *"the app persists no appearance setting today … while a DURABLE appearance preference is the ambient store `U-THEME-CONTROL`'s prohibition row R-2 fails"* — a MEASUREMENT and a PROHIBITION. **The prohibition is the ground the ruling answers, and it is answered in the opposite direction.** What the row actually says, read at its own bytes (`archive/gate-corpus-2026-10-29/specs/theme-control.md`, §1 item 7's NAMED DENIED SET, clause **(iv)**): *"a store, a persisted preference, a cookie, `localStorage`, a file write or an ambient environment read"*, enforced by §3.4 `R-2`'s static scan, whose falsifier is `F-8`, *"the card becomes a shipped appearance UI"*. **SO THE ROW IS ABOUT DURABILITY AND NOT ABOUT SOMETHING NARROWER** — it names *"a persisted preference"* and *"a file write"* in its own words beside the browser's ambient storage, and no narrower reading of clause (iv) is available; the ruling and the row asked the SAME question and answer it differently. **The two things the row's own text narrows are its SUBJECT and its OBJECT, and both are recorded rather than argued away**: its scan runs over *"THIS UNIT'S OWN DIFF SCOPE"* — the authored card's block in `src/shared/demo-envelope.ts` and the wiring role in `src/renderer/renderer.ts` — so it binds a demonstration card's generalisation into a shipped appearance UI and not the app's own record; and the row is DATED, its unit long `DONE`. **None of that makes the denial true of this app, and this row does not claim it does**: the user's ruling is the higher authority and it settles the clause outright.

- **THE TIER CHANGE IS ONE CONSTANT AND THE ADDRESS DOES NOT MOVE**: `THEME_TIER` `'mem'` → `'file'` in `src/main/view-record/index.ts`, `VIEW_THEME_ADDRESS` still `['theme']`, still on the NORMAL crossing and now beside `['panes']` — both `file` — while `['focus']` and `['zones']` stay `mem`. No read, write, repair or route spells a tier literal, so the change is the one constant plus the declarations that name it; the pair, the route, the record's three members and the staleness arm are BYTE-UNMOVED.
- **BOOT DATA IS NOW THE FILE, AND THE DURABILITY IS MEASURED RATHER THAN ARGUED** (clause 3): `1.9.1` saves a `file`-tier registration repair and no `mem` one (measured at `D-97`), so the creation instant's placeholder is a SAVED write. **FOUR READINGS, ONE FRESH INSTANCE PER BOOT, ON THE FILE'S OWN BYTES**: a fresh profile's `provident-profile.json` carries `{"panes":{…},"theme":{"declaration":{"removal":true,"value":""}}}` when the constructor returns, so **a fresh profile carries an appearance record BEFORE any act** — the repair's placeholder, which names no attribute and so applies nothing; boot two over that file answers `source: 'record'`, the pair answers `true` over the standing value, and the bytes are BYTE-IDENTICAL across the restart; a `commitTheme('light','theme')` writes `{"attribute":"theme","declaration":{"name":"theme","removal":false,"value":"light"},"token":"light"}` and boot four reads that record back with the pair `true` and the bytes again unmoved; and **the placeholder repair fires ONLY where the address is ABSENT** — a file seeded `{"panes":{}}` gains the placeholder at boot, while a file carrying a legal record is untouched through two further boots.
- **THE STALE ARM STILL REFUSES AND REPAIRS AT `file`, AND IT IS MEASURED ON A RESTARTED STORE**: a profile written by an older head carries `{token: 'light', declaration: {value: 'dark', …}}`, a resolution for a token it no longer carries. A FRESH INSTANCE over those bytes **refuses it — `theme-must-stand` answers `THEME_STALE_MESSAGE`, the pair's own sentence** — **its repair writes the current resolution inside that commit through `writeWholeValue`, the module's one repair-write path, answering the credited arm (`D-72`, `D-73`) — and the file's bytes MOVED**, standing afterwards at the resolution of the token the file carries. Driven again over the restarted store with a hand-built stale value, the pair answers the same sentence, its repair answers `{ok: true}` with an op whose `tier` reads `file`, and the file is the corrected value. **The repair still writes ONE whole legal value and the credited arm is unchanged.**

## D-100 — THE SUITE'S INPUTS ARE MADE CHECKOUT-VISIBLE: `.gitignore` admits EXACTLY the corpus files the suite and scripts READ — `31` files, `5,435,793` bytes of the corpus's `19,826,527` — so a fresh `git worktree add --detach` installs, builds and reads `2,896` passed / `2` skipped of `2,898` over `126` files, and four prose claims are corrected where they stand

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-03 · **Source:** the user's instruction, four numbered sections, each answered at the point it is quoted — *"A fresh clone of this branch has a **red suite** … Do not commit 29 MB if a few files will do … the point is that the number is measured, not assumed."* Measured on this head over the installed `provident-storeroom@1.9.1`.

**THE BLOCKER, REPRODUCED BEFORE ANYTHING WAS TOUCHED, AND NOT TAKEN ON THE INSTRUCTION'S ARITHMETIC.** `git worktree add --detach /tmp/cl-prefix` at the parent head, `npm ci` from the lock, `npm run build`, then the suite: **`16 failed | 110 passed (126)` files and `45 failed | 2,823 passed | 2 skipped (2,870)` collected, exit `1`** — the instruction's figures, to the row — with `tests/fork-store-reads.test.ts` failing to COLLECT at its module-scope `readFileSync` (`ENOENT …/archive/gate-corpus-2026-10-29/specs/fork-store-reads.md`), so **`28` rows never existed** (`2,870 + 28 = 2,898`), while the other reds read `scandir …/skills` or `open …/specs/<unit>.md`. **THE CAUSE IS THE ONE NAMED AND NOT A SECOND**: `D-2` moved the corpus out of tracked `docs/` into `archive/`, `.gitignore` line `archive/` had excluded it since the initial commit, and `D-8`'s sweep then re-pointed the rows that READ it — the corpus the sweep treated as untracked-and-absorbed became a hard input of a suite that could not see it.

**THE READ-SET CENSUS — `53` REFERENCE SITES, `31` DISTINCT PATHS, AND EVERY ONE OF THE `31` IS READ.** `grep -rn "archive/gate-corpus-2026-10-29" tests/ scripts/ src/` over `.ts`/`.mjs`/`.cjs`/`.js` returns **`53` sites naming `31` distinct paths**, and **not one of them is a prose citation**: `21` are the `specs/` contracts the flat gate-era batteries read — parsed for their `§`-headings, their register tables and their declared censuses, and pinned for their existence; **`8`** are the corpus root's `decisions.md` read with all **seven** `ACTIVE-*` shards as one concatenated authority; `FORKER.md` is read for its footer; and `skills/` is a `readdirSync` whose one file an `existsSync` pins. The three sites that only *mention* a corpus path — `tests/focus-tool.test.ts`'s `N-5` and two allow-lists — assert on `src/` text and open no corpus byte, so they add no file. **MEASURED AGAINST THE WHOLE**: the read set is **`5,435,793` bytes in `31` files** against **`19,826,527`** for the corpus (`221` files) and **`28,361,485`** for all of `archive/` — **`27%` of the corpus, `19%` of `archive/`** — so **the whole corpus was NOT needed and was not committed**, and no part of the census was assumed.

**THE AMENDMENT, AND WHY `archive/` HAD TO BECOME `archive/*`.** A gitignore negation **cannot** re-admit a file under an excluded DIRECTORY, which is the mechanism rather than a preference, so the one line `archive/` becomes `archive/*` plus, level by level, `!archive/gate-corpus-2026-10-29/`, `archive/gate-corpus-2026-10-29/*`, `!…/decisions/`, `!…/skills/`, `!…/specs/`, `archive/…/specs/*` and **twenty-one per-file negations** — the corpus's other **`190`** files stay ignored. `git add` took **`32` paths: `.gitignore` and the `31`, `5,435,793` bytes added**. The admitted set was then diffed against the census's own output and read **IDENTICAL**, so the admission is the measurement and not a second guess.

**THE CLEAN-CHECKOUT READING — THE HEADLINE, AND IT IS THE FIX COMMIT'S.** `git worktree add --detach /tmp/cl-check <this pass's fix commit>`, `npm ci --cache /tmp/npm-cache-mc`, `npm run build`, then `vitest run`: **`126 passed (126)` files, `2,896 passed | 2 skipped (2,898)` collected, exit `0`.** The same sequence at the parent reads the `16 / 45` above, so the delta is this pass's and is one directory's worth of files. **TWO SEAT ARTEFACTS ARE NAMED RATHER THAN BURIED**: `npm ci` needs a scratch cache under this seat, and **Electron's postinstall completes AFTER `npm ci` returns**, so the first attempt read `3 failed | 123 passed` with every red a resolve-or-spawn of the absent binary — `node node_modules/electron/install.js` against `electron_config_cache` completed the payload, and the figure above is the rerun. Neither is a property of the repo: a plain clone with a script runner that waits takes the green figure in one pass.

**THE WORKTREE-BOUNDARY ROW IS UNAFFECTED, AS PREDICTED, AND THE PREDICTION IS THE READING.** `tests/integration/store-view-record/boundary.test.ts` asserts `git status --porcelain` against a declared edit set, and a **COMMITTED** path leaves that reading EMPTY: `B-1` passes over the committed fix where the same `31` paths untracked would have reddened it. **Measured, not argued** — the file is green in the leg run below and green in the clean worktree's `126 passed`. Its `DECLARED_SCOPE` gains this pass's writes, **`70` entries → `103`**, the `31` corpus paths plus `.gitignore` and `docs/L1/store-tabs-record.md`, each named with its reason and **not one admitted by a prefix**; `B-1`'s set assertion still reads `103` distinct, and `B-2`'s control that a single entry outside the union FAILS is untouched.

**THE FOUR PROSE CORRECTIONS, EACH IN ITS OWN DOCUMENT'S VOICE.** *(a)* **`strata/brief.md` §6's `npm test` row** said *"no failures — … on a clean tree"* and never stated that the reading required `archive/`; it now says *"no failures on a clean tree, **and now reproducible from a CLEAN CHECKOUT**"* and closes with the fact that makes it true — the `31` read files are **TRACKED**, `5,435,793` bytes of `19,826,527`, so a fresh worktree reads the figure with nothing copied in. **No caveat about the archive is added, because after this pass there is none to add.** *(b)* **§6's heading** dated the whole table *"all nine rows measured 2026-11-02"* while the `npm test` row is `D-97`'s and the five Electron rows were re-measured `2026-11-03`; ## D-101 — THE `data.*` TIER BECOMES THE CALLER'S, AND THE ADDRESS SPACE BECOMES THE SURFACE'S: a `tier` member over the store's own three tokens, a declared-root guard that imports its set from the two crossings, and both measured defects closed at one arm and named at the other

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-03 · **Source:** the user's instruction this pass carries, VERBATIM — *"Change the data.* surface to accept a file/mem/temp tier"* — read beside the review's two measured defects: `A`, one `data.write({ path: ['focus'], value })` creating a `file` version of a root the view record declares at `mem`, after which `store.read(['focus'], 'mem')` threw `TierNotFound`, `viewRecord.read()` answered `source.focus: 'default'` for the rest of that profile's life, a restart did not heal it and `data.delete(['focus'])` answered `removed:false`; and `B`, `data.write({ path: ['panes'], value: { junk: true } })` erasing the pane geometry durably while `source.panes` still read `'record'`. Measured on this head over the installed `provident-storeroom@1.9.1`.

**THE GROUND, MEASURED BEFORE ANYTHING WAS WRITTEN.** `surface.ts` carried `const FILE_TIER: DataTier = 'file'` — a constant of the module, never a property of the address — and passed it at `write` and at `delete`; `types.ts` declared `DataTier = 'file' | 'mem' | 'temp'` with the note *"this surface writes the durable one"*; `args.ts` refused only SPELLINGS (`mem.`, `temp.`, `secure.`). The five argument schemas were `{path}`, `{path, value}`, `{path}`, `{prefix?}` and `{cursor?}` in `interface.md` §1 and in `mcp-server.ts`'s three `z.unknown().optional()` members each — no tree declares a `DataWriteArgs` or a `DataReadArgs` type, and the schemas ARE the parsers plus those records. **THE TIERS, FROM THE PACKAGE'S OWN DECLARATION AND GUIDE**: the order is `file` > `mem` > `temp` in persistence, `file` is *"for anything that must be there on the next boot"*, `mem` *"lives for the session"* and `temp` is shadowed by a `mem` write; readers take absent, one token or an array (`read` absent = "the lowest persistence present"), writers exactly one, and `delete` absent = every tier at the target (`guide §5.2`). **SO A `mem` WRITE SURVIVES THE INSTANCE AND NOTHING ELSE** — not a restart, not a second instance, not a byte of the profile (`D-84` measured that at the view record's own family). **THE DECLARED ROOTS AND THEIR TIERS, FROM THE MODULES THAT OWN THEM**: `tabsConstraintDeclarations` returns six declarations all at `TABS_RECORD_ADDRESS` (`['tabs']`, tier `'file'`, spelled inline at eight sites); `viewConstraintDeclarations` four, at `['panes']`/`['focus']`/`['zones']`/`['theme']` with the tiers `'file'`/`'mem'`/`'mem'`/`'file'` held in four private constants.

**Decision.** **THE TIER IS A MEMBER OF THE CALLER'S CALL, ON THE THREE VERBS THE STORE GIVES ONE TO.** `write`, `delete` and `read` each declare an optional `tier` whose domain is the store's own `'file' | 'mem' | 'temp'` (`DATA_TIER_DOMAIN`, `args.ts`); a token outside it answers `malformed-arguments` naming the member and the domain, in the register of the `path` and `cursor` arms; `list` and `journal` declare NONE, because the store publishes no tier-narrowed enumeration and an own key outside a tool's declared set is refused by name rather than ignored. **THE DEFAULT IS THE STORE'S OWN ARITY, PER VERB, AND IT IS STATED IN ONE PLACE**: a `write` or a `delete` that omits `tier` is `'file'` — the one token a writer must name and the tier this surface has always driven, so **no existing caller changes at an ordinary path**, which is why all twelve gating `tests/e2e/central-data/` rows stand unedited — while a `read` that omits it is the store's UNQUALIFIED read, so no read is narrowed behind its caller's back; and `TierNotFound` is classified as an ABSENCE (`mapper.ts`), because a read that finds nothing AT A TIER has found an absence and `delete`'s own probe takes the same arm.

**THE DECLARED-ROOT GUARD, AND THE IMPORT THAT KEEPS IT HONEST.** `src/main/data/surface.ts` refuses a `write` or a `delete` at a root a crossing declares when the requested tier is not the family's, with the twelfth reason word **`declared-tier`** — minted rather than reusing `volatile-path`, whose sentence says this surface *"holds the durable profile only"*, false from the instant the tier became the caller's, and whose fault is a SPELLING and not a tier. Its message names the root, the tier the family declares and the tier the call asks for: *"…: 'focus' is declared at 'mem' and this write asks for 'file'"*. **THE SET IS IMPORTED AND NEVER RE-SPELLED** (D-53): `tabs-record` publishes `tabsDeclaredRoots()` and `view-record` `viewDeclaredRoots()`, each pairing its own address constant with the tier constant its batch's pair writes at, and `DataSurfaceOptions.declaredRoots` carries their union — **REQUIRED, for `store`'s reason** (D-80): an optional list would leave the unguarded surface one caller away from the defect. **AND THE IMPORT IS THE COMPOSITION'S, NOT THIS MODULE'S, BY MEASUREMENT**: reaching the crossings from `src/main/data/` puts `view-record` in the MCP stack's import closure, and two GATING `tests/e2e/` rows require that closure to name neither the operator instance nor its addresses — measured red on the first draft, green when `main.ts`'s existing options line passes the union (D-18). The guard keys on the address's ROOT, because a family is ONE whole value at its root (`D-87`), so `['panes','pane-a','size']` is `['panes']`'s tier.

**`A` IS CLOSED, BOTH ARMS, MEASURED — AND `B` AT ONE ARM AND NAMED AT THE OTHER.** `data.write({ path: ['focus'], tier: 'file', … })` answers `refused`/`declared-tier` and **`store.read(['focus'], 'mem')` still answers**, `viewRecord.read()` still reads `source.focus: 'record'`, the profile is byte-identical, no cursor is minted — and a RESTART finds nothing to heal. The two calls the defect was MADE of, `write(['focus'])` and `delete(['focus'])` with no tier, are refused the same way, so the silent default can no longer lie at a declared root; the same write at `tier: 'mem'` COMMITS, the family's own `read()` serves it member for member, no byte of it reaches the file, and the restart takes it away. `['panes']` at `mem` or `temp` is refused and the geometry is untouched; **at its OWN tier the junk still lands, still erases the geometry and `source.panes` still reads `'record'`** — `panes-must-stand` enforces EXISTENCE and not shape (`D-87`), narrowing it is a NEW constraint and a design act, and this row measures that residual half rather than claiming it (row `D-5` of this pass's battery names its own death). **NINE GATING ROWS** (`tests/integration/central-data/declared-root.test.ts`, unmarked, D-40) carry all of it, and **three reverts answered RED then restored**: the guard removed red 3 of 9 (`D-2`, `D-3`, `D-5`); the domain arm removed red 6; `TierNotFound` returned to the escape arm red `D-7`'s non-persistence arm. **AND THE ROW THAT MAKES `V4` MORE NECESSARY, NOT LESS** (`D-9`): a write at a family's own tier with a value its pair REFUSES is repaired inside the caller's own commit and the answer is still `committed` with no member naming the repair — reachable now where it was not, because an agent can address a family at its tier.

**WHAT IT COSTS, MEASURED.** `npm test` **`2,896` passed / `2` skipped of `2,898` over `126` files → `2,905` / `2` of `2,907` over `127`** — the nine rows are this battery's and ONE file is added, and not one existing row was deleted or weakened: the twelve gating `tests/e2e/central-data/` rows read identically through a `surfaceAt()` that now passes the roots — **and the two that pin the DEFAULT are the reading this pass was most obliged to keep honest**: `S-3` writes `['alpha']` naming no tier and then reads the profile's own bytes, and `S-7` deletes `['alpha']` naming no tier and reads `{status:'committed', removed:true, tiers:['file'], cursor}` — so an omitted tier is still `file`, measured rather than assumed, and `srcTypeScriptFiles` stays `50`, `mapRows` `21`, the `central-data` row's `endpoints` `15` (the guard calls no package verb). `strata/marks.json` REGENERATED by `strata-marks.mjs index` — `gating` `194 → 203` sites in `26 → 27` files, `on notice` `2,479` and the `7` carve-outs unmoved — and **on the dirty tree FOUR OTHER UNITS' ROWS WENT RED AND EACH WAS RE-DERIVED RATHER THAN WEAKENED**: `gesture-session`'s `F-9(b)` sibling-artifact list gains this pass's `src/main/` paths BY NAME (its illustration moves from `mcp-server.ts` to `security.ts`, so a path no declaration names still fails); `gutter-ui`'s `I-12` exclusion gains `src/main/mcp-server.ts` and is RENAMED to `DECLARED_SIBLING_SURFACE_PATHS` with its control moved to `src/renderer/index.html`; `theme`'s `X-5` red a `theme` token in this pass's own prose, which was RE-WORDED rather than exempted; and `store-tabs-record`'s `C-2` required the new export to be declared in the `D-45` block. **THE DOCUMENTS**: `interface.md` stood at exactly `150/150`, and **SEVEN lines were FREED before SIX were spent** — the reason union `4 → 3`, the journal answer `2 → 1`, the read answer `4 → 1` and the delete answer `3 → 1`, no claim dropped — so it reads **`149`**; `behaviour.md` `139 → 149` (its write table's `mem`, domain and declared-root rows, §7's SPELLING-vs-TIER split and a closing bullet on what a `mem` write is visible to), `persistence.md` `115 → 120`, `design.md` `149 → 150`, `signatures.md` `149 → 147`, `docs/L3/store-tabs-record/signatures.md` `150` (one line added, one re-flowed to pay for it), `strata/brief.md` `148`, `strata/pending.md` `39` and `strata/records/store-tabs-record-unit.md` `40` — all within ceiling. **AND `strata/pending.md` ROW 1 IS NOT TOUCHED**: this change moves what a tool's ARGUMENTS are and adds no answer site, so the five `data.*` bridge members still have none.

**`V2` AND `V4`, NOW, BY POINTER AND NOT BY REWORDING** (D-46). **`V2` — clause 1 at `data.*` — is NOT closed and is UNBLOCKED**: the surface still declares no pair of its own, so a family root's VALUE is still the declaring module's existence pair and nothing here judges it; but the vocabulary ruling it waited on has now been taken once — `D-82`'s *"moves L2 §2/§3's closed vocabulary and needs a ruling"* — and `declared-tier` is that move, so what remains for `V2` is a DECLARATION and no longer a ruling. **`V4` — the receipt naming the repairs that fired — is MORE NECESSARY THAN IT WAS**, and row `D-9` is the measurement: the tier being the caller's lets a caller address a family at its own tier and land a value the pair silently replaces, so the dishonest `committed` `D-79` first recorded is now reachable through the documented surface rather than through a mis-addressed write.

**⟶ AND THIS ROW'S OWN RESIDUAL HALF IS SUPERSEDED BY POINTER, NEVER REWORDED** (`D-46`, `D-105`). The sentence above — *"`B` AT ONE ARM AND NAMED AT THE OTHER"*, the plain junk that still landed at `['panes']`'s OWN tier and erased the geometry while `source.panes` still read `'record'` — is the reading of this row's date. `D-105` gives that pair, and the five other subvertible families' pairs, a VALUE ARM: the same write is now REFUSED (`storeStatus: 'ConstraintPassRefused'`), the geometry the app wrote SURVIVES it, and the profile is byte-identical across the refusal. **THE DECLARED-TIER GUARD THIS ROW BUILT IS UNTOUCHED AND IS NOT THE FIX FOR IT**: it protects the TIER — a `file` shadow of a `mem` family, which no constraint at another tier's address can express — and the value arm is the declaration's.

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-100`**, as prose and never as a bare number: the change push returned to the record root; a repair's credit was made explicit and its write made to name its commit; the boot battery's teardown was made to prove zero survivors; the landing entry's id was decided and its seat derived and then the id moved; the renderer's tab client appeared read-only; the two-crossing ruling named the purge; the surplus instance was collapsed onto the normal crossing and the `data.*` tools pinned to the central store; the module registry's legacy file was dropped rather than migrated; the data-flow convention was audited clause by clause; the imperative boot write was deleted for a sixth declaration; the `mem` pair's existence was declared at the store's creation; the absence-keyed import went inside the repair; `1.9.1`'s landing-arm save was measured; the purge's two successor addresses were declared; the renderer swap was refused on measurement and then landed; the enumeration that stopped on the zone family; the zone family's own address; the frozen store made unused and then DELETED; the two stale imports taken with the crossings given a live leg a leg runs; the view record's crossing given a live leg with the pane read pair made total; the appearance mechanism's consumer named at last; the appearance family made a constraint repair's product with no boot-time declaration; the appearance family made DURABLE at `file` on the user's ruling; the corpus made to tell the truth about itself; and the suite's inputs made checkout-visible. **The package-facing rows among them stay the package's** — `provident-storeroom` is read and never modified, its own corpus's decision ids are never spelled here, and a defect or a requirement gap of it belongs to the unit that surfaced it, with symptom, reproduction, suspected root cause and proposed fix shape (D-5, D-52).

**IT SUPERSEDES THE FRAMING OF THE ROWS ABOVE IT, NEVER THEIR DATED TEXT** (D-46). `interface.md` §1's *"this module's surface is the durable profile"* is FALSE at this head and is re-grained by this row rather than left to read as true; `D-80`'s `DataSurfaceOptions` gains a fourth member and its three-member sentence is read through this one; and `D-82`'s `V2`/`V4` are as the paragraph above states them.

## D-102 — THE MESSAGE LOG IS THE REFUSAL'S ALONE: a passing `write` is handed `{op, watched, triggered}` and the package DROPS the entries at the handoff, so `V4` is BLOCKED on a package change rather than closed, and nothing is fabricated in its place

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-03 · **Source:** the user's instruction this pass carries, VERBATIM — *"Data should always return the message log of constraint/repairs made so that the agent/user can confirm behavior."* — read as *"every act that ran a pass"* and MEASURED before anything was designed, on the installed `provident-storeroom@1.9.1`, over scratch `filePath`s driven through the published entry.

**THE GROUND, MEASURED FIRST, AND IT DECIDES THE PASS — THE ENTRIES LIVE ON THE REFUSAL AND NOWHERE ELSE.** The declaration gives the entry shape *"exactly FIVE members — `kind` · `id` · `message` · `opId` · `state`"*, its set CLOSED, to ONE position: the failure's `log`, on a thrown plain object carrying exactly `{status, message, log, address}` (`index.d.ts:379-386`, `:475`). The package's guide states the lifetime and its price — *"Once the commit resolves or rejects, the store keeps no message log … the list it handed you — the entries on the refusal you caught, or on `report`'s return — is the ONLY copy, sealed at the handoff"*, and, verbatim, *"on an arm whose handoff carries no list at all — a repair body that throws, and a PASSING `write` (whose answer is `{op, watched, triggered}` and has never carried one) — the entries are no longer readable after the call"* (`README.guide.md:1522`; the closed record keeps FOUR members and *"NO message list"*, `:1524`) — the ground `D-82`'s `V4` and `D-101`'s sentence were waiting on.

**WHAT A PASSING `write` ANSWERS AT THIS HEAD, MEASURED RAW — AND THE EARLIER READING IS CONFIRMED.** `getOwnPropertyNames` over the receipt of a `['tabs']` write whose pair FIRED a repair reads `["op","watched","triggered","discarded"]`; `log`, `commit` and `attempt` are all `undefined` and `getOwnPropertySymbols` is `[]`; `triggered` reads `[{"handle":"exactly-one-active","address":["tabs"]}]` — **the SAME list the same write answers on a CLEAN path**, where the pair was asked once and repaired nothing. **`triggered` NAMES WHICH PAIRS WERE ASKED AND NEVER WHAT THEY DID**, and a row reading it as proof would be the lie `D-79` recorded.

**THE SURFACE ITSELF, DRIVEN THROUGH THE TAB RECORD'S OWN SIX DECLARATIONS — `V4`'S ARM, MEASURED WHERE IT IS PAID.** `data.write({ path: ['tabs'], tier: 'file', value: <a two-active record> })` answers `{"status":"committed","path":["tabs"],"cursor":1}` — own names `["status","path","cursor"]`, NO member naming a pass — while the record that STANDS afterwards carries THREE repairs: the active-count pair deactivating the second tab, the stamp pair writing `lastActiveAt` onto both rows, and the order pair dropping the `landing` seat. A CLEAN write at the same path answers the same three members, cursor `2`. And `data.delete({ path: ['tabs'], tier: 'file' })` answers `{"status":"committed","path":["tabs"],"removed":false,"tiers":[],"cursor":null}` while the root pair RE-MINTS the record inside that same delete — `read(['tabs'])` serves it, stamped at the delete's own instant. **So the dishonest `committed` `D-79` recorded stands unchanged at this head and is now measured at BOTH mutating routes, which is the row `V4` was owed and this one cannot pay (D-101).**
**AND NOTHING REACHES THE ENTRIES AFTERWARDS — FOUR DOORS, MEASURED, NOT ONE.** *(a)* The package ships ONE name: `exports` maps `"."` alone, so `provident-storeroom/src/entry/kernel-terminal.mjs` is REFUSED `ERR_PACKAGE_PATH_NOT_EXPORTED` — the `log(store, ref)`/`report(store, ref)` pair the guide spells, the `delete` clause's *"`K.log(store, receipt.commit.attempt)`"* included (`index.d.ts:637`), belongs to the kernel the package's own tests drive and never to its surface. *(b)* Reached by file URL instead — a files-allowlist artefact — `log(store, ref)` answers **`[]`** after the write returns, the handoff's drop keeping `{attempt, outcome, ops, rollback}` and no list, and `report(store, ref)` answers those four members with **no `log`**. *(c)* INSIDE the commit, at the one instant a caller's own body stands, the pair's `context.attempt` is refused `KC-7`, *"the constraint attempt attempt-1 is still open"*. *(d)* A `delete`'s non-enumerable `commit.attempt` is the same reference by the same door: `log` answers `[]`. **THE ENTRIES ARE READABLE ON THE REFUSAL ARM ALONE; a passing write is handed no list to hold, so there is no interval — never, not "briefly" — over which a caller can obtain them.**

**Decision.** **NO MEMBER IS ADDED AND THE SURFACE IS UNMOVED: the instruction is UNSATISFIABLE AT THIS PACKAGE VERSION and is recorded as such.** `DataWriteAnswer`, `DataDeleteAnswer` and the refusal ledger keep the shapes they had, and §2/§3's status union does NOT widen, because nothing was minted to widen it — so `interface.md` stands at `149/150` with no line spent and none freed. **`V4` is BLOCKED, not closed**: `D-82`'s clause and `D-101`'s *"more necessary than it was"* stand as filed and are read through this one (D-46), and what stands between them and closure is a package release that hands a passing act a list or keeps the entries answerable by ref. **`V2` is UNTOUCHED**: this ruling moves a vocabulary question and no declaration, and the surface still declares no pair of its own.

## D-104 — THE APP-STATE ROWS GET THE ADDRESS THE RENDERER LISTENS TO: `['operator-app-state']` joins the instance's ONE subscription and its own delivery body, the change crosses as `IPC_MODULE_CHANGED`, the realm's shape is filled INSIDE the listener, and the request-time read `SecurePanels.refresh()` made is DELETED — `D-82`'s `V3` CLOSES

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-03 · **Source:** the user's rule, VERBATIM — *"V3 - the renderer does not ask state, it listens. If it needs to ask, it does so within a listener."* — read as the ARCHITECTURE and not as a request for one more consumer, measured on this head.

**THE RULE READ AS AN ARCHITECTURE.** `V3` was `D-84`'s label for *"`APP_STATE_ADDRESS` in a listener set whose consumer does not exist"*, and `D-103` left it standing. The sentence forces one reading: the renderer **never pulls** — it is driven by deliveries, and any read it needs happens INSIDE the listener that triggered it — so the pass is not a consumer that asks but the ADDRESS LISTENED TO plus the shape that listening maintains.

**THE AUDIT — EVERY renderer→crossing READ at the parent head, each classified exactly once.** *THE PARENT HEAD IS `0f65159`, and every offset below is that head's.* *(i) BOOT FILL*, one read before any delivery exists: `renderer.ts:748`'s `bridge.security.get()` (the tier-4 snapshot the Supervisor is built from); `renderer.ts:973`'s `client.read()`, the tab record's; `renderer.ts:1009`'s `client.fill()`, the view record's; and `panels.refresh()` at the boot (`renderer.ts:846`), which took both `security.get()` (`secure-panels.ts:540`) and `module.get()` (`:549`). *(ii) INSIDE A LISTENER*: `tabs-client.ts:281`'s read route, reached from the `onChanged` body at `tabs-client.ts:326`; `view-client.ts:447`'s, reached from the body at `view-client.ts:543`. *(iii) AN ON-DEMAND ASK, the class the rule condemns*: `secure-panels.ts:536`'s `refresh()` — called from `handleDomEvent` (`:445`, every authored click) and from the `dispatch()` seam (`:457`) — asking `security.get()` and `module.get()`; no poll, no timer and no re-read on render stands anywhere else. **THE PRELOAD'S ROUTES, EACH TRACED TO ONE OF THOSE**: `provident.tabs.read/commit/remove`, `provident.view.read/commitPane/commitFocus/commitZones/commitTheme` and `provident.security.get/set/setExclusion` are reached only from a boot fill, a listener or a write; `provident.module.get` from `refresh()` alone; and `provident.module.setDisabled` from NO renderer site at all. `provident.store.*` does not exist to reach (`D-93`).
**THE PLAIN ANSWER: YES — TWICE, AND BOTH IN `secure-panels.refresh()`.** The module rows were answered only by request (`D-82` clause 2 measured the address delivering ZERO) and the security record was re-asked after every click. This row removes the FIRST; the second STAYS and is named here rather than papered over.

**THE ADDRESS ADDED, AND THE ROUTING THAT KEEPS ITS SPELLING IN ONE FILE.** `src/main/operator-settings/index.ts`'s `subscribe` binds `[RECORD_ADDRESS, EXCLUSION_ADDRESS, APP_STATE_ADDRESS]` — three handles, one registration — and its body routes a delivery whose root is `APP_STATE_ADDRESS[0]` to a SECOND body, `OperatorAppStateListener`, so the record's listener never re-reads a record that did not move and the address is spelled in the code of the one file that declares it (`ISO-7`'s census, unmoved). `src/main/settings-surface/index.ts` subscribes with BOTH bodies in the ONE registration (`settings.subscribe(onDelivery, onAppStateDelivery)`) and hands the app-state one to `options.onAppStateChanged`; `src/main/main.ts` points that at `IPC_MODULE_CHANGED`, broadcast to every window; `src/main/preload.ts` and `src/renderer/secure-panels.ts` carry `module.onChanged` in lockstep. **WHAT THE DELIVERY CARRIES: A SIGNAL AND NOTHING ELSE** — no address, no value, exactly what `IPC_TABS_CHANGED` and `IPC_VIEW_CHANGED` carry, so the consumer's answer to it is a RE-READ.

**THE SHAPE, WHAT FILLS IT, AND WHERE THE READ SITS.** `src/renderer/module-client.ts` — a NEW file, so the offsets are THIS head's — holds `{filled, corrupt, quarantined, loaded, modules}`; its ONE read is `fill()` (`module-client.ts:205`), and `fill` is invoked at exactly two sites — the subscription's delivery body (`:249`) and `startAppStateShape`'s ONE stated boot fill (`renderer.ts:1069`) — and both turns hand the adopted shape to `SecurePanels.applyAppState`, which paints the two authored nodes. **IT WAS NOT ALWAYS INSIDE A LISTENER**: the read was the PANE's, at request time, and it is the client's now; the pane reads no module at all.

**THE PULL REMOVED, QUOTED BEFORE AND AFTER.** BEFORE, inside `refresh()` (`secure-panels.ts:546-564` at the parent head): `const moduleBridge = typeof window !== 'undefined' && window.provident?.module; if (moduleBridge) { try { const res = await moduleBridge.get(); this.moduleStatus = `corrupt: …`; this.moduleListText = res.modules.map(…) } catch { … } }` — three call sites, all asking. AFTER: `refresh()` reads the security config and nothing else, and the two lines are written by `applyAppState` from a delivery. **NO LISTEN STANDS BESIDE A PULL**: the ask is gone, not joined.

**THE CONSUMER IS REAL AND WAS FOUND, NOT INVENTED** (`grep -rn "module" src/renderer/`): `src/renderer/secure-panels.ts`'s `module-status` and `module-list` nodes — the Security pane's registry line and list, painted by `syncConfig` from the two fields — are the app's only reading of those rows, and they are driven from the shape. **No UI is authored**: the nodes, their ids and their styles are the pane envelope's.

**THE ROWS, AND FOUR REVERTS EACH DRIVEN RED THEN RESTORED.** `tests/integration/settings-carrier-retirement/app-state-delivery.test.ts`, five GATING rows (`D-40`, unmarked): `AP-1` the module-row commit delivering to the realm's own handler counter with nothing asked; `AP-2` the shape against a read taken beside it, member for member; `AP-3` the NO-PULL census over COMMENT-ELIDED code (the member acquired in one file, the read inside `fill`, `fill` at two sites, `applyAppState` at the delivery's turn and the boot's); `AP-4` the CONTROL — a record write and an exclusion transition move neither the counter nor the shape; `AP-5` the two main-side halves and the one-file spelling. **REVERTS**: the address out of `subscribe` reds `AP-1`, `AP-2`, `AP-4`, `AP-5`; the surface's second body dropped reds the same four; the pull put back inside `refresh()` reds `AP-3`; the delivery's `applyAppState` removed reds `AP-3`'s consumer clause. **THE CROSSINGS LEG GAINS A ROW** — `X-14b`, with the two acts it drives: it installs `window.provident.module.onChanged`, drives the `module.setDisabled` act the leg ALREADY had at that address, and reads the counter back out of the realm, so the delivery is measured over a process boundary. It is read at a SUBSCRIBER and not at a node, and says so: the pane the shape fills is the ISOLATED scope the MCP endpoints cannot see.

**WHAT IT COSTS, MEASURED.** `npm test` **`2,911` passed / `2` skipped of `2,913` over `128` → `2,916` / `2` of `2,918` over `129`** — this battery's five rows and ONE file, and nobody else's; `srcTypeScriptFiles` **`50 → 51`** (the new client) and `mapRows` `21`; `strata/modules.json` claims the client on the `operator-settings` row and moves `ipc-contract`'s `endpoints` **`8 → 9`** (the new channel constant, cap `130 → 140`); `strata/marks.json` REGENERATED by `strata-marks.mjs index` — `gating` `209 → 214` sites in `28 → 29` files, `on notice` `2,479` and the `7` carve-outs unmoved — and `strata-marks.mjs check` is green. **THE DOCUMENTS**: `docs/L2/security-store-storeroom/interface.md` **`150 → 150`** (§1's no-listener sentence, §2's `subscribe` block, §5's options block and `ASM-L2-1`, each traded IN PLACE — no claim dropped, no line freed), `docs/L3/security-store-storeroom/signatures.md` `148 → 149` and `docs/L2/settings-carrier-retirement/interface.md` `137 → 138`; `strata/brief.md` `148/150`, `strata/pending.md` `39` and `strata/records/store-tabs-record-unit.md` `40` — all within ceiling.

**`V3` CLOSES.** The address is bound, the delivery reaches the realm, the consumer exists and is driven by the shape, and NO request-time read of the app-state rows stands in `src/renderer/`. **WHAT REMAINS, NAMED AND NOT CLAIMED**: `secure-panels.refresh()` still re-asks the SECURITY record after a click — the same class at the secure crossing's other two addresses, which this row does not take; `module.setDisabled` has no renderer caller, so the delivery's re-read is the realm's only route to those rows; and the app-state shape is filled by a read whose route is `IPC_MODULE_GET` — a read the DELIVERY triggers and not one the realm requests.

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-103`**, as prose and never as a bare number: the change push returned to the record root; a repair's credit made explicit and its write made to name its commit; the boot battery's teardown proved to zero; the landing entry's id decided, its seat derived and the id moved; the renderer's tab client appeared read-only; the two-crossing ruling named the purge; the surplus instance collapsed onto the normal crossing with the `data.*` tools pinned to the central store; the module registry's legacy file dropped rather than migrated; the data-flow convention audited clause by clause; the imperative boot write deleted for a sixth declaration; the `mem` pair's existence declared at the store's creation; the absence-keyed import put inside the repair; the landing-arm save measured; the purge's two successor addresses declared; the renderer swap refused on measurement and then landed; the enumeration that stopped on the zone family; the zone family's own address; the frozen store made unused and then DELETED; the two stale imports taken with the crossings given a live leg; the view record's crossing given a live leg with the pane read pair made total; the appearance mechanism's consumer named; the appearance family made a constraint repair's product and then made durable; the corpus made to tell the truth about itself; the suite's inputs made checkout-visible; the `data.*` tier made the caller's with the address space made the surface's; the message log measured and then delivered by the release that hands a passing act its commit's log. **The package-facing rows among them stay the package's** — `provident-storeroom` is read and never modified, its own corpus's decision ids are never spelled here, and a defect or a requirement gap of it belongs to the unit that surfaced it, with symptom, reproduction, suspected root cause and proposed fix shape (D-5, D-52).

**⟶ AND THE SECOND OF THIS ROW'S TWO PULLS — *"the second STAYS and is named here rather than papered over"* — IS TAKEN AT `D-106` BY POINTER, AND NOT ONE WORD OF THIS ROW IS REWORDED** (`D-46`): the security record's request-time read is DELETED, the addresses this row's own audit found already bound now DELIVER across `IPC_SECURITY_CHANGED`, and the realm's shape is filled INSIDE the listener exactly as this row's `module-client.ts` is.
