## D-105 — THE VALUE ARMS, AND `V2`'S MISATTRIBUTION: `data.*` CONFORMS BY CONSTRUCTION — it IS the package surface and the package executes the constraints — so a family a legal-tier write can subvert is a failure of its DECLARATION, and SIX of the eight bodies gain a refusal with no repair

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-03 · **Source:** the user's ruling, VERBATIM: **"Data.\* should be package-surface tools. Package surface tools execute constraints on write. If a family invariant can be subverted then this is a failure to enforce invariants with constraints"** — read as three claims and measured here, this seat, on this head over the installed `provident-storeroom@1.9.2`.

**THE THREE CLAIMS, EACH VERIFIED RATHER THAN GRANTED.** *(1) `data.*` IS A PACKAGE-SURFACE TOOL.* Its whole store-verb census over COMMENT-ELIDED code is `store.write(path, tier, value)`, `store.delete(path, tier)`, `store.read` and `store.readLiteral` — `src/main/data/surface.ts`, and its port constructs nothing (`D-80`): `addConstraint`, `constraintOn`, `constraintOff`, `rollback` and `storeCreate` occur NOWHERE in either file's code, and the one of them that stands in prose is the control that proves the elision did the work. *(2) A PACKAGE-SURFACE WRITE EXECUTES THE CONSTRAINTS, WHATEVER THE CALLER.* Measured on the `data.*` path: a `provident.data.write` at each of the five declared roots answers the COMMIT'S LOG (`D-103`), and that log names the family's own pair — `constraint-check: tabs-must-stand` · `panes-must-stand` · `focus-must-stand` · `zones-must-stand` · `theme-must-stand` — while a write at a root no crossing declares answers ONE entry, `commit`, because no pair stands there. *(3) THEREFORE THE SURFACE CAN ONLY DO WHAT THE PACKAGE PERMITS*, and a family a legal-tier write can break is its declaration's fault.

**THE AUDIT OF ALL EIGHT DECLARATIONS, EACH DRIVEN BY A LEGAL-TIER WRITE AT ITS OWN STORE.** The five normal-crossing roots at `data.*`: **`['panes']` was SUBVERTIBLE** — `{junk:true}` committed, the geometry was erased, `source.panes` still read `'record'` and the junk was DURABLE in the profile's bytes; `{'pane-a':{size:'big'}}` and `{'pane-a':{size:5}}` landed ONE LEVEL IN and the pane was silently dropped by the read; **`['focus']` was SUBVERTIBLE** — a DANGLING `activeId` landed and `mirrorFrom` nulled it at the read, `{junk:true}` landed and the family read `'default'`, and a repeated entry id landed; **`['zones']` was SUBVERTIBLE** — `{junk:true}` and `{'zone-1':{size:'big'}}` both committed and both emptied the family while `source` said `'record'`; **`['theme']` was NOT** — its second arm already re-derives any standing value to the mechanism's own record (`repaired:true`, `D-97`); **`['tabs']` was NOT** — `{junk:true}`, `42` and a row with `active:'yes'` are all REPLACED inside the caller's own commit by `order-names-its-tabs` and `exactly-one-active`, `repaired:true`, and the record that stands always seats exactly one tab whose `order` names a row it holds. The three secure-crossing addresses, which `data.*` CANNOT reach (`D-80`, measured: a write at that name lands a root in the PROFILE while the instance's own file is byte-identical): **`['operator-settings']` was SUBVERTIBLE** — `{junk:true}` committed durably over a standing `token:'tok'` and `enabled:['read']` and `read()` served a FIRST RUN as though one had happened; **`['operator-exclusion']` was SUBVERTIBLE** — `{junk:true}`, `{}` and `{exclusion:'bogus'}` all committed and `read()` answered `SAFE_PAIR.exclusion`, which is what OPENS the gate the operator had closed; **`['operator-app-state']` was SUBVERTIBLE AT THE ROW** — `{rows:[{name:7,value:'x'}]}` committed and `readAppState()` answered `rows: []`, the row list gone with no member of the receipt saying so (a junk value there was refused by accident, as the failed arm of a repair that needs a registration route). **SO SIX OF THE EIGHT WERE SUBVERTIBLE AND TWO WERE NOT** — and the two that were not are `['tabs']`, whose six declarations judge its value, and `['theme']`, whose second arm repairs one. **AND `D-101`'s DECLARED-TIER GUARD IS A DIFFERENT THING**: it protects the TIER (a `file` shadow of a `mem` family, which no constraint at another tier's address can express), it stays exactly as it stands, and it is not the fix for this.

**THE VALUE ARMS, ONE PER SUBVERTIBLE FAMILY, AND THEY ARE REFUSALS WITH NO REPAIR.** `panes-must-stand`, `focus-must-stand`, `zones-must-stand`, `operator-record-must-stand`, `operator-exclusion-must-stand` and `operator-app-state-must-stand` each gain a second arm: a body distinguishes `context['value']` ABSENT (the existence arm and its placeholder, unmoved) from PRESENT AND UNUSABLE (the value arm), and the latter answers `{message}` alone — the kernel's unrepairable arm, so the store's own ROLLBACK leaves the record that stood. **The convention's repair arm is for data that is MISSING**; a placeholder over a corrupt value would destroy what the caller had. Each sentence names the FAMILY and the FAULT, in the register of the absent ones — `'the pane geometry carries a member that is no geometry — no finite non-negative size, or no bounds pair of two finite numbers whose min does not exceed its max'` · `'the focus mirror carries an entry the read cannot state, two entries under one id, or an active id that is no entry of its own list, so the mirror names a tab it does not hold'` · `'the zone family holds a member that is no record, or a zone carrying a size that is not finite and non-negative, a distance that is not finite, a display that is not a string, or a slot the crossing refuses'` · and the three secure ones beside the module's absent sentences. **THE LEGAL SHAPE OF EACH FAMILY IS THE READER'S AND NOT AN INVENTION**: `paneOf`'s size and bounds, `mirrorFrom`/`focusEntryOf`'s entries and active id, `zoneOf`'s four optional members, `fromStored`'s four durable members, the pair's one word, `fromAppState`'s three row tests. **NO NEW REPAIR PATH, NO NEW WRITE, NO SECOND AUTHORITY**: the three older bodies are UNWRAPPED (no handle) and judge the node by the SAME admission the read uses, `nodeOf` subtracting the family's root from `context.address` — measured: a pass is asked at the WRITE's own address, so `['panes','pane-a']` is judged as that member — and `writeInstant` reads the instant off the same context (`tier` is `null` at a registration, set at a write). **`['theme']` KEEPS ITS SECOND ARM UNTOUCHED**: it is the same KIND of judgement and a different CLASS of answer — a REPAIR, and legitimately so, because its declaration is derived from the value's own two members and its repair cannot destroy anything a corrupt value carried.

**WHAT A `data.*` CALLER SEES, QUOTED** (`D-103`'s vocabulary, and nothing added to it): the refusal is a VALUE of the closed ledger — `{status:'refused', reason:'store-refused', message:<the pair's own sentence>, storeStatus:'ConstraintPassRefused', path:['panes']}` — the store's status verbatim under the escape arm `D-101` left in place, no new reason word minted, no `log` and no `repaired` member on the refusal arm (`D-103`'s named residue, unchanged). **AND THE ONE ARM THE ARMS DO NOT TAKE IS STATED AND MEASURED**: a value a hand-edited FILE carries is NOT refused, because a no-repair refusal at a REGISTRATION instant refuses the CONSTRUCTION — `provident-storeroom@1.9.2` throws `ConstraintRefusedAtRegistration` before the instance exists (measured, at both crossings' batches) — so the registration instant answers `true`, the boot over a corrupt profile stands, the bytes are unmoved, and the FIRST LEGAL WRITE at the root replaces it.

**THE INVARIANTS NOW HOLDING, MEASURED ON THE BYTES AND AGAIN AFTER A RESTART.** Every corrupt write above is REFUSED, and the PREVIOUS LEGAL VALUE SURVIVES it — the profile byte-identical across each refusal, `['panes']`'s geometry still the family the app wrote, `['focus']`'s mirror still the one that stood (no dangling id behind it), `['zones']`'s family intact, the operator's token and grants still read by a fresh instance, the pair still the state the operator set, the row list still the rows it had — and each family's own route still lands (`commitPane`, `commitFocus`, `commitZones`, `write`, `writeAppState`). The EXISTENCE ARMS still work on every family: a cold profile carries all five placeholders at the creation instant and the secure instance its three, `{}` is still a legal operator record (`D-84`'s own measurement) and a zone carrying nothing is still a zone (`D-91`'s). **THE ROWS**: `tests/integration/central-data/value-arms.test.ts`, THIRTEEN rows, unmarked and GATING (`D-40`) — claim one's verb census, claim two's own log, the seven families, the appearance family's repair arm, the existence arms, the restarts, and the registration instant — and **FOUR ROWS RE-DERIVED RATHER THAN WEAKENED**, each keeping its control: `declared-root`'s `D-5` INVERTED (its own text said its death would be this refusal), `creation-declarations`' `CD-4` (the creation instant kept as the control for the write instant it now refuses), `settings-carrier-retirement`'s `OS-5` (the skip rule proved at the only door that can carry such a row, the refused write its control) and `profile-file`'s roots census unmoved. **TWO REVERTS, EACH DRIVEN RED THEN RESTORED, AND EACH RED ON THE SURVIVAL CLAUSE** (2 of the 13 rows red, `VA-3` and `VA-12`, both back green restored): the pane value arm DROPPED — the corrupt write commits again and the geometry the file carried reads `[]`; the same arm made a REPAIR instead of a refusal — the write answers `committed`/`repaired:true` and the geometry reads the PLACEHOLDER (`size:100`) where the app's `size:61` stood, which is exactly the destruction the refusal exists to prevent.

**`V2`'S DISPOSITION, IN THE RULING'S OWN READING.** **`data.*` CONFORMS BY CONSTRUCTION** — it IS the package surface, and the package executes the constraints on every write — and **`V2` AS FILED WAS A MISATTRIBUTION**: it recorded `data.*` as deviating at clause 1, and the deviation was never in the surface, which can only do what the package permits. What replaced it is the obligation on **every family's DECLARATION** to enforce its own invariant against **any** caller, and the families that failed are named: `panes-must-stand`, `focus-must-stand`, `zones-must-stand`, `operator-record-must-stand`, `operator-exclusion-must-stand` and `operator-app-state-must-stand`. **WHAT REMAINS, NAMED AND NOT CLAIMED**: the registration instant's arm above (a corrupt FILE value is the first legal write's to replace, not a refusal's); `['theme']`'s second arm keeps its REPAIR semantics on purpose; a value written BELOW a member (`['panes','pane-a','size']`, three segments is the store's depth domain) is refused only where its node is one this family states — a scalar leaf that is not a plain value is the existence arm's and is repaired, as it always was.

**WHAT IT COSTS, MEASURED.** `npm test` **`2,916` passed / `2` skipped of `2,918` over `129` files → `2,929` / `2` of `2,931` over `130`** — the thirteen rows are this battery's and ONE file is added, and not one existing row was deleted or weakened; `srcTypeScriptFiles` stays `51` (no `src/` file is added), `mapRows` `21`, and the `central-data` row's `endpoints` `15`. `strata/marks.json` REGENERATED by `strata-marks.mjs index` — `gating` `214 → 227` sites in `29 → 30` files, `on notice` `2,479` and the `7` carve-outs unmoved, `check` green — because a test file is added. **THE DOCUMENTS**: `docs/L2/view-record/interface.md` **`120 → 120`**, its four paragraphs re-packed and its rev block grown (no claim dropped, no line freed — the §1 pair and the §3 note paid for by the two re-packed paragraphs, and §4/§5 traded in place); `docs/L3/view-record/design.md` `124 → 130` (its last free lines spent on §4's arm machinery and §7's discharged debt); `docs/L2/security-store-storeroom/interface.md` **`150 → 150`** (§2's `SAFE_PAIR` clause re-grained IN PLACE, because the non-token arm is now reached by a REFUSED READ alone); `docs/L3/security-store-storeroom/design.md` `149 → 149` and `signatures.md` `149 → 149` (the two private-name censuses, each edited in place, the arms' sixteen declared); `strata/brief.md` `148/150` (the `npm test` figure and its own arithmetic), `strata/pending.md` `39`, `strata/records/store-tabs-record-unit.md` exactly `40` and `strata/platform.md` `60/60` — all touched nowhere.

`D-87`'s and this unit's L3 §7 *"owed rather than invented"* clause is DISCHARGED here. **ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-104`**, as prose and never as a bare number: the change push returned to the record root; ## D-106 — THE LAST DEVIATION CELL D-104 NAMED IS TAKEN: the secure crossing's record and its `mem` pair get the DELIVERY they never had, `IPC_SECURITY_CHANGED` carries it as a signal, the realm's shape `src/renderer/security-client.ts` is filled INSIDE the listener, and the request-time read `SecurePanels.refresh()` made — the boot's, one per authored click and one per `dispatch()` seam — is DELETED

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-03 · **Source:** the user's rule, VERBATIM — *"the renderer does not ask state, it listens. If it needs to ask, it does so within a listener."* — read as it was at `D-104`, as the ARCHITECTURE, plus this pass' instruction that the crossing already carries the change and only the renderer's half of clause 2 is missing.

**THE GROUND, MEASURED BEFORE ANYTHING WAS WRITTEN.** `src/main/operator-settings/index.ts`'s `subscribe` binds `[RECORD_ADDRESS, EXCLUSION_ADDRESS, APP_STATE_ADDRESS]` and `settings-surface`'s `onDelivery` has always acted on the first two — but **no delivery crossed to the renderer**: `grep "SECURITY_CHANGED|security:changed" src/ tests/` answered **ZERO**, where `IPC_TABS_CHANGED`, `IPC_VIEW_CHANGED` and `IPC_MODULE_CHANGED` each stand. So the crossing already carried the change, and what was missing was the renderer's half. `SecurePanels.refresh()` opened with ONE IPC read — `this.cfg = await security.get()` — at **two request-time sites** (`handleDomEvent` after every authored click, and the `dispatch()` seam) plus the boot's call, which `D-104`'s audit had already classified as class (iii), the class the rule condemns, and named as what remained.

**THE DELIVERY, IN THE SAME THREE HALVES `D-104` LANDED.** *(1) THE BROADCAST*: `createSettingsSurface` takes an optional `onSecurityChanged` and calls it INSIDE `onDelivery` — once per delivery, before the sink's arms, contained like the app-state body — and `src/main/main.ts` points it at `IPC_SECURITY_CHANGED`, broadcast to every window. One callback for BOTH addresses because it carries NEITHER: which of the two moved is the main side's business and the realm's answer to either is the whole record's read. *(2) THE CHANNEL AND THE LOCKSTEP PAIR*: `IPC_SECURITY_CHANGED` is declared in `src/shared/types.ts` beside its security siblings and names no address, and a `security.onChanged` member is declared in **lockstep** in `src/main/preload.ts` and `src/renderer/secure-panels.ts` — the pair a census reads back and compares. *(3) THE SHAPE*: `src/renderer/security-client.ts` — a NEW file, so the offsets are THIS head's — holds `{filled, token, enabled, maxJournalLength?, exclusion, read}`; its ONE read is `fill()`, and `fill` is invoked at exactly two sites — the subscription's delivery body and `startSecurityShape`'s ONE stated boot fill — both handing the adopted shape to `SecurePanels.applySecurity`, which writes `cfg` and paints.

**ONE DELIVERY, ONE SIGNAL, NO PAYLOAD** — no address and no value, exactly what the three pushes beside it carry, so the consumer's only correct answer is a re-read. **THE PULL REMOVED, QUOTED BEFORE AND AFTER.** BEFORE, `refresh()` (`src/renderer/secure-panels.ts`, this head's parent): `const security = typeof window !== 'undefined' && window.provident?.security; if (security) { try { this.cfg = await security.get() } catch { /* keep the last-known config on a bridge error */ } }`. AFTER: `refresh(): void { this.syncConfig(); this.render() }` — **the read is DELETED, not guarded**, and the fields it wrote are written by `applySecurity` from a delivery. **THE TWO REQUEST-TIME SITES ARE GONE AND THE ONE BOOT FILL REMAINS**: `handleDomEvent` and the `dispatch()` seam now call a paint; the boot's `panels.refresh()` is still the first paint (the constructor renders nothing) and the read it used to make is `startSecurityShape`'s stated boot fill, started from `main()` beside the three others and never awaited. **AND THE ONE READ THIS REALM MAKES OUTSIDE THE CLIENT STAYS AND IS NAMED**: `main()`'s `bridge.security.get()` is the TIER-4 SNAPSHOT the app Runtime's Supervisor is constructed from — a boot fill whose value a construction consumes and which paints nothing (`D-104`'s class (i)).

**THE ROWS — FIVE, GATING, AND THE CENSUS IS THE STRUCTURAL ONE.** `tests/integration/settings-carrier-retirement/security-delivery.test.ts`, a SIBLING of `D-104`'s battery and not four more rows in it (its subject is the FIRST delivery body, the record and the pair; that file's is the SECOND): `SD-1` the delivery by the realm's own handler counter, EXACT, zero before the change; `SD-2` the shape against a read taken beside it, member for member, and the pair's and the refusal's arms moving with it; `SD-3` **`AP-3`'s NO-PULL CENSUS EXTENDED TO THE SECURITY MEMBER over COMMENT-ELIDED code** — the pane performs no `security.get` and acquires no bridge binding to read one through, the route is ONE expression inside `fill`, `fill` is invoked from the subscription's body and by the ONE stated boot fill, `refresh()`'s whole body is `syncConfig()` then `render()`, and exactly ONE security read stands in the wiring module and is the tier-4 snapshot; `SD-4` the CONTROL — an app-state commit moves neither the counter nor the shape; `SD-5` the two main-side halves, the one constant and the lockstep pair. **TWO REVERTS WERE DRIVEN RED THEN RESTORED, and each is the one this pass' instruction names**: the surface's broadcast call removed reds `SD-1` (`0` deliveries where the exact count is asserted) and `SD-5`; the read put back into `refresh()` reds `SD-3` on the FIRST clause (`the pane performs NO security read`) with `SD-1`, `SD-2` unreddened — the census is the row that makes the rule structural.

**THE LIVE LEG GAINS `X-13b`, AND IT IS MEASURED AT A SUBSCRIBER AND NOT AT A NODE, AND SAYS SO** (`tests/e2e/store-crossings/crossings-live.mjs`): the subscriber is installed on `window.provident.security.onChanged` BEFORE the `security.set` act `X-13` already drove — the `X-14b` discipline, because a delivery is observable only from a handler that stands when the crossing delivers — and the row reads the counter back out of the realm beside a re-read carrying the driven journal cap. The app's own subscriber fills the ISOLATED pane graph, which the MCP endpoints cannot read, so no authored node of the app graph moves when the record does; the row states that rather than claiming a rendered surface. It is a THIRTIETH row on an instrument this unit does not own, so no `scripts` key and no key-set census moves.

## D-107 — THE REFUSAL LEDGER'S `log` IS FORWARDED, AND THE LAST DEVIATION CELL IS RE-READ AS `N/A`: the package's refusal arm has always handed `{status, message, log, address}`, so the ledger carries that list on the passing arm's own member name and through the SAME one accessor — ENUMERABLE there where the passing arm's is not, present where a store answered and absent where this module composed the sentence — while `data.*`'s clause-2 cell is measured against the three facts it rests on and recorded as no deviation at all; the two prose corrections, `ST-F1`'s re-read at the installed `1.9.2`, and the residual list as it now stands

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-03 · **Source:** six residuals taken in the order this pass's instruction names, measured on this head over the installed `provident-storeroom@1.9.2`, one fresh instance per arm.

**THE FOUR FACTS ABOUT THE REFUSAL'S MEMBER, EACH MEASURED, AND THE THIRD IS THE ONE THAT DECIDES THE CODE.** *(1) THE PACKAGE HAS ALWAYS HANDED IT*: a `data.*` write refused by a family's value arm throws a value whose own names are exactly `['status','message','log','address']`, and the log names the pair — `constraint-check: panes-must-stand` · `outcome` — so the list `D-103` recorded as discarded is on every refusal MEASURED — `ConstraintPassRefused` from a write and from a delete, `ParentAnchorMonotonicityViolation`, `AddressMalformed` — and not on some. *(2) IT IS ENUMERABLE, WHICH IS THE ONE THING THAT DIFFERS FROM THE PASSING ARM*: `Object.getOwnPropertyDescriptor(caught,'log')` reads `{enumerable:true, writable:true, configurable:true}` against the passing record's `enumerable:false`, so `Object.keys`, `{...caught}` and `JSON.stringify(caught)` all CARRY it — and the surface still copies it, because the answer must carry nothing that aliases the store's release (G10) and because a refusal that reached no package arm at all carries no list. *(3) `[]` IS AN ANSWER HERE AND IT MEANS "NO PASS RAN"*: a refusal composed before any pass — `ParentAnchorMonotonicityViolation`, `AddressMalformed` — carries `log: []`, which is exactly the `D-103` warning read the other way (there `[]` was not the clean arm's answer; here it is the pre-pass arm's), so the two arms are told apart by the presence of a `storeStatus` and never by a length. *(4) ONE ACCESSOR SERVES BOTH, AND NO SECOND WAS MINTED*: `commitLog` is a PROPERTY READ with an `Array.isArray` guard and a per-entry copy, and a property read finds a non-enumerable own member exactly as it finds an enumerable one — so `refusalLedger` calls it and nothing in `src/main/data/` learns the member's name (`D-103`'s `L-6` still reads `0` for `.log` in all seven of the module's files).

**Decision.** **THE MEMBER IS `log`, ON THE LEDGER, OPTIONAL, AND FORWARDED WHERE THE PACKAGE HANDED ONE.** `DataLedger` gains `log?: readonly DataLogEntry[]` — the SAME name, the SAME plain enumerable array and the SAME `DataLogEntry` shape the two mutating verbs already answer with, so a caller reads ONE vocabulary off both arms and no word is minted. **IT IS OPTIONAL AND THE OPTIONALITY IS THE MEASUREMENT AND NOT A HEDGE**: the six refusals this module composes itself — `malformed-arguments`, `declared-tier`, `volatile-path`, `root-not-writable`, `cursor-unknown`, `store-unreachable` — are answered before any store is consulted, so an `[]` there would be this surface's claim about a pass that never began; `refusalLedger` therefore spreads the member only where `commitLog` answered an array, the same rule `storeStatus` follows one member below it. `read`, `list` and `journal` are untouched — a read consults no constraint, which is `D-103`'s reading and stands.

**THE ROWS AND THE REVERT, DRIVEN.** `tests/integration/central-data/message-log.test.ts` gains **two, unmarked and GATING** (`D-40`, and the file `D-103` made is where the same member's second arm belongs): `L-7` drives a `data.write` at `['panes']` carrying `{junk:true}`, asserts the refusal's `log` is an ARRAY, an ENUMERABLE own member of the ledger, TWO entries in order (`constraint-check` naming `panes-must-stand`, then the refusal's own `outcome` at `state:'KC-3'`), each entry the package's five keys and no sixth, the log crossing `JSON.stringify` intact — the form the MCP caller receives, since `mcp-server.ts` answers `JSON.stringify(value, null, 2)` — and, as its control the other way, the SAME store's passing write still ending at `commit` while the refused one reaches none; `L-8` drives the five module-composed arms and asserts each carries NO `log`, with the store-answered refusal on the same surface carrying one as the control. **THE REVERT: DROP THE MEMBER FROM `refusalLedger` AND 2 of 8 RED** (`L-7` whole, and `L-8`'s control), restored and green.

**THE `data.*` CLAUSE-2 CELL: THREE FACTS MEASURED, AND IT IS `N/A` AND NOT A DEVIATION.** **ONE ACT, NOT TWO.** The survey that preceded `D-106` concluded the cell is `N/A`; that row recorded it as the one cell standing; the two readings were never reconciled, and they are reconciled here by the pointer clause at `D-106`'s own row rather than by rewording it (`D-46`). ***(i) NO SUBSCRIBER***: `listen(` is called at THREE sites in `src/` and by the three modules that declare the addresses — `src/main/tabs-record/index.ts:1264` binds `[TABS_RECORD_ADDRESS]`, `src/main/view-record/index.ts:1368` binds `['panes']`, `['focus']`, `['zones']`, `['theme']`, `src/main/operator-settings/index.ts:1041` binds `['operator-settings']`, `['operator-exclusion']`, `['operator-app-state']` — eight addresses over three subscriptions, with `register(` the same count beside each; while `src/main/data/` calls `listen` **0** times and `register` **0** times, so a `data.*` write at a caller-chosen path fires no delivery and one at a declared root fires THAT FAMILY's listener, which re-reads the family it already owns. ***(ii) NO RENDERER ANSWER SITE***: `data.` occurs **0** times in `src/renderer/renderer.ts` and **0** times in `src/main/preload.ts`, and its ONE occurrence in `src/main/main.ts` is a comment (`:27`). ***(iii) NO MCP RESOURCE AND NO NOTIFICATION***: `ALL_RESOURCES` is THREE and names `data` nowhere, and the tree's only `sendResourceUpdated` targets `mcp://provident/app` with its only caller `src/main/main.ts:561` on the graph push. **THE READING, AND IT IS THE SURVEY'S: `data.*` is a tool surface over caller-chosen paths and carries no family of its own, so there is nothing for it to carry** — at every address it may lawfully write that a crossing declares the family's own listener already delivers, and at every other address the caller that just wrote is the only consumer there is, answered synchronously by the receipt it holds. **THE DEVIATIONS COLUMN IS THEREFORE EMPTY AT ALL SIX OF `D-82`'S FAMILIES ON ALL FOUR CLAUSES**, and `strata/pending.md` row 1 — which IS the revival condition — now says so: the cell becomes a deviation again the day a consumer exists, and the fix then is a **NEW ADDRESS IN A FAMILY'S LISTENER SET**, never a listener hung off `data.*`.

**`ST-F1` RE-READ AT THE INSTALLED `1.9.2`: IT STANDS, AND IT IS THE KERNEL'S OWN TWO READERS THAT DISAGREE.** The surviving clause was filed at `1.6.1`; measured one fresh instance per arm on `1.9.2`, a value whose `message` and `repair` are INHERITED (`Object.create({message, repair})`, `Object.hasOwn` false for both) **REGISTERS** — the registration path reads `'repair' in outcome`, a `[[HasProperty]]` that walks the prototype chain (`kernel-registration.mjs:399-401`), invokes the inherited repair ONCE and lets the batch stand — while the SAME value at the WRITE instant is read by `Object.hasOwn(outcome,'message')` and `ownMember(outcome,'repair')`, a `[[GetOwnProperty]]` that finds neither (`kernel-attempt.mjs:810-818`), so the repair does not run and the write answers `ConstraintPassRefused` with *"returned a value outside the three declared outcome arms"*; the controls are an OWN `{message, repair}` that repairs and lands at the write instant, and the inherited arm that registers. **So the version line moves and no claim does**: the finding is re-filed against `1.9.2`, upstream-owned and not patched here (`D-5`, `D-52`), with the suspected root cause — `1.9.0`'s narrowing of the write-time arm to own members was never applied to the registration-time arm — and the proposed fix shape — that arm's predicate changed to `Object.hasOwn` — recorded in `strata/records/store-tabs-record-unit.md` §1, which stays at exactly `40` because the `1.6.1` "not documented" bullet is REFLOWED from two lines to one and no clause, quotation or dated reading is removed.

## D-108 — `ST-F1` CANNOT BITE THIS APP, AND THAT IS NOW A DRIVE RATHER THAN A READING: all THIRTEEN declarations the two crossings register build their refusals as OBJECT LITERALS whose `message` and `repair` are OWN at both instants, `VA-14` drives every one of them on the very objects the store adopted, `VA-15` reddens on a planted `Object.create({message, repair})` pair — and the population the corpus has never carried is recorded: ELEVEN `src/shared/` modules, `3,579` lines, unreachable from all five entries, every one of them DECLARED and not one of them rot

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-03 · **Source:** the instruction that `ST-F1` be made structural here — "the strongest form is a drive" — and that the modules nothing imports be censused and classified; measured on this head over the installed `provident-storeroom@1.9.2`, one fresh instance per arm.

**THE GROUND, QUOTED, AND IT IS ONE SHAPE THIRTEEN TIMES.** A declaration's `constraint` is ONE function object: the batch is the array `src/main/main.ts` hands `createProfileStore` and the write path invokes the SAME stored function, so "own at both instants" is "is the value this function RETURNS built with own members". **THE TAB RECORD'S SIX**: `tabs-must-stand` returns `{message: 'the tab record does not stand', repair: mintRecord(getStore)}` (`src/main/tabs-record/index.ts:960-963`); `order-names-its-tabs` binds `const refuse: TabsConstraintAnswer = {message: ORDER_SEAT_MESSAGE, repair: repairOrderSeat(getStore)}` once per invocation and returns it (`:1009`); the three field pairs return `{message, repair: healRecord(store, liveHints(store))}` (`:875`, `:884`, `:894`); and `exactly-one-active` returns `{message: <one of its three sentences>, repair: healRecord(store, {removals, written, removed, preOrder})}` (`:813-821`). **THE VIEW RECORD'S FOUR**: the existence arms `{message: 'the pane geometry does not stand', repair: repairPanes(getStore)}` (`src/main/view-record/index.ts:940-943`), `'the focus mirror does not stand'` (`:979-982`), `'the zone family does not stand'` (`:1020-1023`) and `'the appearance record does not stand'` (`:1066`) beside its staleness arm `{message: THEME_STALE_MESSAGE, repair: repairTheme(getStore)}` (`:1069`) — while the `D-105` value arms return `{message: PANE_UNUSABLE_MESSAGE}` and its two siblings (`:955`, `:991`, `:1032`), a BARE arm that must carry no `repair` at all. **THE SECURE CROSSING'S THREE**: `{message: EXCLUSION_ABSENT_MESSAGE, repair: mintExclusion}` (`src/main/operator-settings/index.ts:805`), `{message: RECORD_ABSENT_MESSAGE, repair: (c) => mintRecord(c, userDataDir)}` (`:823`) and `{message: APP_STATE_ABSENT_MESSAGE, repair: (c) => mintAppState(c, userDataDir)}` (`:842`), each beside a bare value arm (`:809`, `:827`, `:846`). **THIRTEEN OBJECT LITERALS, NOT ONE SPREAD OF A SHARED BASE AND NOT ONE BASE OBJECT CARRYING THE MEMBERS** — and the only `Object.create` in the three modules is `Object.create(null)`, the caller-data copy map, which has no chain to inherit through (`operator-settings/index.ts:173-176`).

**THE ROW MAKES IT STRUCTURAL, AND IT IS A DRIVE** (`tests/integration/central-data/value-arms.test.ts`, two rows, unmarked and GATING — `D-40`). `VA-14` constructs the central store with the app's REAL batch and KEEPS the arrays, so the declarations it drives are the objects the store registered and not look-alikes rebuilt beside it; it asserts SIX + FOUR + THREE = THIRTEEN, the ten ids as one namespace, and then EXERCISES EACH PAIR'S REFUSAL ARM and asserts the ANSWER: `Object.getPrototypeOf(answer) === Object.prototype` FIRST and separately (a base would pass an own-`message`-only probe and still fail the write instant — the divergence is exactly that gap), `Object.getOwnPropertyNames` exactly the arm's declared members, `Object.hasOwn` for each, `typeof repair === 'function'` where the arm is repairable, and `'repair' in answer` FALSE on the six bare arms, because an INHERITED `repair` is what a `'repair' in outcome` probe finds and what a `{message}` arm must not have. **FOUR OF THE THIRTEEN ARE DRIVEN THROUGH THE BATCH'S OWN HOLDER AND THE ROW SAYS SO**: `exactly-one-active` and the three field pairs read the STANDING RECORD, and a violation they judge can only stand inside a commit — every write that would leave one is repaired in that same commit, and the package consumes the answer and never hands it back — so the getter the SAME declaration objects close over is pointed at the record such a commit would see. Nothing is rebuilt and nothing is faked except the handle's answer, which is the one thing no caller can observe otherwise. **AND THE WRITE INSTANT IS MEASURED WHERE THE KERNEL IS THE ONLY READER**: an own `repair` is INVOKED inside the caller's own commit, so the six tab pairs' repair is made to run through a corrupt whole-record write (`repaired:true`), and each value arm's refusal is read for ITS OWN family sentence with `ST-F1`'s divergence sentence asserted absent from all of them.

**IT CAN FAIL, AND THE PLANT IS IN THE SOURCE RATHER THAN IN A FIXTURE.** `VA-15(a)` registers a planted declaration whose refusal is `Object.create({message, repair})` and measures `ST-F1` at this head rather than quoting it: the **registration instant ACCEPTS it** — the kernel reads `'repair' in outcome`, invokes the INHERITED repair once and lets the batch stand — while the **write instant refuses it `ConstraintPassRefused` with *"returned a value outside the three declared outcome arms"* and NO `repair-run` entry in the log**. ## D-109 — THE BUMP TO `provident-storeroom@1.9.3`, `ST-F1` DISCHARGED UPSTREAM, AND ITS INSTRUMENT INVERTED RATHER THAN DELETED: the kernel's two instants now ask ONE own-member question, so the divergence `D-107` re-read and `D-108` made structural has no second instant left — `VA-15` becomes the registration refusal it now is, with its two reverse probes and a class instance, `VA-14`'s stale clauses are corrected around an untouched drive, and every figure a bump can move was re-measured and none did

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-04 · **Source:** the user's instruction this pass carries — the published `1.9.3` taken, every live pin moved in place, `VA-15` inverted rather than deleted, `ST-F1` discharged in its own record, the two tracker sentences it would falsify corrected, and the census re-measured rather than carried — measured on this head over the installed `provident-storeroom@1.9.3`, one fresh instance per arm.

**THE BUMP, AND THE LOCK IS WHAT PINNED IT.** `package.json` read `"provident-storeroom": "^1.9.2"` — a CARET RANGE that already admitted `1.9.3`, so the range decided nothing and the lock was the whole of the pin; the install was `npm install provident-storeroom@1.9.3` with a scratch cache, never a name resolved off the range. **ELEVEN CELLS MOVED TOGETHER AND ALL SIX LOCK NODES CARRY `1.9.3`**: the SIX `version` cells (the main package and its five platform packages) and the FIVE exact `optionalDependencies` entries, with the SIX `resolved` tarball URLs and the root `dependencies` spec moving beside them — read as `1.9.3` at `package.json:28` and `package-lock.json` lines `14`, `25`/`26`, `41`/`42`, `57`/`58`, `73`/`74`, `89`/`90`, `2353`/`2354` and `2361`–`2365`, with NOT ONE `1.9.2` left in the file. **THE PINS MOVED IN PLACE, THE PIN FOLLOWING THE REVIEWED INSTALL AND NEVER THE OTHER WAY** — the corpus's own habit: `tests/storeroom-surface.test.ts`'s literal and the comment above it, re-derived to `1.9.3`'s own subject (the two instants agreeing) with its closing claim KEPT TRUE; `strata/brief.md` §2's pinned row and its reason sentence; and `src/main/storeroom.ts`'s census header, where the VERSION moved and no figure did.

**THE CENSUS IS UNMOVED, RE-MEASURED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** One fresh instance over the installed bytes reads `Object.getOwnPropertyNames(store)` → **14** own string members in the order the header pins, `getOwnPropertySymbols` → **13** at construction with `Symbol(provident-storeroom.registration-save)` fifth, **14** after one `write` (`Symbol(provident-storeroom.kernel)`), the closed status set **20**, and the tiers `mem` · `file` · `temp` — every figure `1.9.2` read, and `RELEASE-NOTES.md` §1.9.3's "what does not move" paragraph names the same set.

**`ST-F1` IS DISCHARGED, AND THE ONE-WORD-HALF CORRECTION IS THE PART THAT MATTERS: UPSTREAM NARROWED THREE READS WHERE THE FINDING PROPOSED ONE.** `RELEASE-NOTES.md` §1.9.3 item 2, quoted: *"a constraint whose `message` or `repair` the returned shape carries on a prototype no longer registers a repair the write then refuses to read, so the failure surfaces where the constraint is authored rather than at the first write."* **THE CODE IS TWO MEMBERS PLUS A RETURN**: `kernel-registration.mjs:399-400` reads `Object.hasOwn(outcome,'message')` and only then `ownMember(outcome,'repair')` — the finding proposed the `repair` probe alone, and the `'message' in outcome` guard beside it would still have admitted an own `repair` paired with an inherited `message` — and `:676` reads the repair's RETURN's `ok` by `ownMember` too. `kernel-attempt.mjs` is byte-identical and `index.d.ts`'s whole diff is one docblock, which is why no other module in this tree moves.

**`VA-15` IS INVERTED, NOT DELETED, AND IT KEEPS EVERYTHING THAT MADE IT REAL** (`tests/integration/central-data/value-arms.test.ts`; unmarked and GATING, `D-40`). **(a)** the SAME plant — the same `Object.create({message, repair})` value, the same real declaration answering it, the same scratch profile — now measures the registration instant's FIXED behaviour: the construction THROWS `ConstraintRefusedAtRegistration`, the refusal's own names are exactly `["status","message","log","address"]`, its `log` is `[]`, and the base's `repair` — counted, which `1.9.2` could NOT assert because it was invoked once — is invoked **ZERO** times. **THE TWO REVERSE PROBES ARE ADDED**, because they are what distinguishes a whole fix from the one-word half: own `message` + inherited `repair`, and own `repair` + inherited `message`, each refused at registration with the inherited member reached zero times (and the second's OWN repair zero too, the kernel refusing before it runs a member that shape really carries). **AND THE CLASS INSTANCE** — `get message()` on the prototype with an own `repair`, `1.9.2`'s quietest acceptance — is refused with them; a fifth arm pins the third narrowed read, an own pair whose RETURN carries an inherited `ok`, refused with a log of `constraint-check` · `repair-run` and its repair run once. **THE DIVERGENCE'S SECOND INSTANT IS NOW UNREACHABLE AND IS NOT ASSERTED** — no store exists, so there is nothing to write to and a `null` store would prove nothing. **(b)** the static half over comment-elided code is **byte-for-byte** as filed.

**ITS BITE WAS PROVED AND THEN REVERTED, BECAUSE THE PACKAGE MAY NOT BE TOUCHED.** With `1.9.3`'s two narrowed members unchangeable from here, the row's subject was removed instead: the plant's DRIVEN refusal was made own at BOTH members — the minimum that removes it, since narrowing either member alone is still refused, measured — and the construction then REGISTERED with the repair invoked once, reddening the refusal clause (*"expected false to be true"*) and, with the count hoisted above it, the zero-invocation clause (*"expected 1 to be +0"*). Both temporary edits were reverted and the row re-greened: `15 passed` of `15`.

**`VA-14`'S DRIVE IS UNTOUCHED; FOUR PROSE CLAUSES ARE STALE AND ONE OF THEM READ BACKWARDS.** (i) the header paragraph that described the registration path as reading `'repair' in outcome`; (ii) the assertion message that would have printed a FALSE diagnostic — *"an `Object.create({message, repair})` answer REGISTERS and then answers every write with…"* — a sentence no longer true of any instant; (iii) the clause calling registration *"the instant ST-F1 says is the permissive reader"*, which reads backwards: from `1.9.3` it is the STRICTER arm, since it demands an own `message` before it reads a repair at all and refuses the CONSTRUCTION where no own repair answers; (iv) the absence assertion's rationale, which credited a `'repair' in answer` probe the chain could win, and — corrected with it because it was the same defect one paragraph down — the block preamble that said an inherited `repair` on a `{message}` arm *"is invoked at the registration instant and IGNORED at the write instant"*, true at `1.9.2` and false at `1.9.3`, where NO instant reads one. Not one assertion, context or drive changed.

**THE TWO TRACKER SENTENCES THAT WOULD HAVE BECOME FALSE, CORRECTED IN PLACE AND WITHOUT A LINE.** `strata/pending.md:23` — *"What it did not discharge — the two instants still disagree on an inherited `repair` — stays live"* — now records the `1.9.3` discharge with its measurement, the file holding at **40**; `strata/next-steps.md:27` — *"`ST-F1` stays live in `strata/records/store-tabs-record-unit.md` §1"* — now says it was DISCHARGED and that the record carries it, the file holding at **29**.

**`ST-F1` IS DISCHARGED IN ITS OWN RECORD, AND THE CEILING IS HONOURED BY FREEING BEFORE SPENDING.** `strata/records/store-tabs-record-unit.md` gains ONE bullet in `CD-F3`'s own shape — *"⟶ DISCHARGED 2026-11-04 by the installed `1.9.3`, `RELEASE-NOTES.md` §1.9.3 item 2: …"* — carrying the both-members-and-the-return correction, the four measured arms and the unreachability of the second instant; the line it spends was FREED FIRST by merging §1's free-standing continuation into the paragraph it continued (`D-108`'s own clause), so not one clause is dropped and only a line break goes: **`40 → 40`**. ## D-111 — THE RULING ON THE ELEVEN UNIMPORTED `src/shared/` MODULES IS THE USER'S AND IT IS **CARRIED AS DESIGNS**: the population `D-108` measured stands as declared surfaces awaiting a caller — ratified rather than left as a measurement nothing gates — with what carrying leaves unsaid stated in the same row, the three follow-ups it was conditioned on discharged, and `strata/pending.md` row six gone because its work landed

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-04 · **Source:** the user's ruling, given against the two dispositions `D-110` put in that row's own `Waits on` cell — *"carried as designs"* and *"retired with their contracts and batteries"* — with the three follow-ups named in the same breath as the condition of the first.

**THE RULING, QUOTED, AND WHOSE IT IS.** The word taken is **"carried as designs"**, against the alternative **"retired with their contracts and batteries"**, and **it is the USER'S and not a layer's**: `D-108` measured the population, `D-110` made the row ready to take one word, and neither took it, because deleting a declared deliverable whose contract asserts it is unimported would falsify that contract and redden its own battery, while giving one a consumer is what `D-90` refused to invent. A ruling is not a measurement and this row does not dress it as one.

**WHAT CARRYING ASSERTS, AND IT IS THREE THINGS.** *(i)* **AN UNIMPORTED MODULE MAY BE A DELIVERABLE** — nothing in this corpus makes reachability from an entry point a condition of a thing being a thing. *(ii)* **A CONTRACT CAN BE COMPLETE, TESTED AND CORRECT WHILE NO ENTRY POINT REACHES IT** — which is what seven of the eleven asserting their own unimportedness in their own bytes means: that is the state those contracts were authored to describe, not one they tolerate. *(iii)* **THE ELEVEN STAND AS SURFACES A CALLER — this app's, or a fork's — MAY ONE DAY ADOPT**: `census` · `container` · `layout-projection` · `menu-template` · `mount-invariant-guard` · `overlay` · `owned-list-host` · `path-fork-cycle` · `relocate` · `slot-host` · `zones`, `3,568` lines by a `wc -l` over the eleven files (`D-108`'s own instrument read `3,579` over the same population — ONE population and two instruments, and neither figure is rewritten). **THE MEASUREMENT THIS RESTS ON IS `D-108`'s OWN SENTENCE**: *"AN UNIMPORTED DELIVERABLE IS A DESIGN, AND NOT ONE OF THESE IS AN ORPHAN"* — reached by resolving the four import forms over comment-elided bytes from all five entry points and finding eleven unreachable, not one of them unclaimed as a row of `strata/modules.json`.

**WHAT CARRYING LEAVES UNSAID, STATED RATHER THAN HIDDEN.** Nothing **EXPIRES**: no clause here sets a date by which a consumer must arrive, and a ratified design has no expiry to miss. Nothing **RE-MEASURES**: the reading above is this pass's, and no instrument re-takes it, so it is a dated reading and never a standing one. And **NO INSTRUMENT WOULD NOTICE one of them drifting from the platform it was written against** — the eighteen files that pin them run under a layout-less DOM shim and every one reports ON NOTICE, so a `provident-storeroom` bump or an envelope change could leave a contract asserting a shape the platform no longer has while every leg stays green. **They are RATIFIED, NOT WATCHED**, and a reader who takes this row as a watch has read it wrong.

**THE TWO CAVEATS, NAMED — NEITHER A DEFECT, BOTH FACTS A CORPUS THAT SAYS "DESIGNS AWAITING A CONSUMER" SHOULD ALSO SAY.** *(i)* `src/shared/owned-list-host.ts:295-296` and `src/shared/slot-host.ts:313` COMPOSE `mem.list.*` and `mem.slots.*` — the address space of the frozen graph store `D-93` DELETED — so their one specified integration target no longer exists, and what remains is the lawful caller-supplied handle both contracts already declare. *(ii)* `src/shared/path-fork-cycle.ts` IS DUPLICATED BY ITS OWN CONSUMER: `tests/e2e-battery.test.mjs:503` defines its own `pathForkCycle` with different CSS — `background-color: hsl(…)` there against the module's per-level `10px`/`20px` — so **the fixture the gating leg runs is not the module**, and the module's own `pathForkCycleLegacyData` has no caller at all.

**THE POPULATION, AS `D-108` MEASURED IT, AND THIS ROW ADDS NO FIGURE TO IT.** ELEVEN modules; SEVEN contracts asserting the unimportedness in their own bytes (quoted at that row); TWO more asserting it in a note beside the contract rather than in it — `listhost.md:1144`'s diff-scope paragraph and `slothost.md:166`'s evidence-status paragraph, the pair `D-108` counted as silent and this row does not; and TWO asserting nothing about importers at all (`mount-invariant-guard`, `path-fork-cycle`). EIGHTEEN batteries pin them and **EVERY ONE carries `strata:marked-file`**, so the whole population reports **ON NOTICE** and nothing gating pins it — bar **ONE gating carve-out**, `tests/menu-template.test.ts:992`'s `R-13`, a no-importer probe that ASSERTS the unimportedness and would have to assert its opposite the day a consumer lands.

**THE THREE FOLLOW-UPS, EACH DISCHARGED, AND EACH IS A CLAUSE ABOVE RATHER THAN A PROMISE.** *(a)* **THE DISPOSITION IS RECORDED** — this row: the ruling quoted and ascribed, what carrying asserts, and what it leaves unsaid. *(b)* **THE ONE FALSE CLAIM IN LIVE CODE IS CORRECTED, AND IT WAS IN `src/`, NOT IN THEM**: `src/renderer/renderer.ts:1793` read *"… the candidate/slot read feeding the PURE `withinProximity` comparator …"* and now says the composition IMPLEMENTS THE SEAM SHAPE `src/shared/relocate.ts` DECLARES WITHOUT IMPORTING IT (`CandidateFor`), which is what `PaneDragSurface`'s own docblock said all along; the comparator has no call site under `src/` and its only importer in the tree is a battery's own drive (`tests/pane-drag-compliance.test.ts:136`). **A comment can move a built artifact and this one did** — `renderer.js` `475,374` → `475,848` bytes, md5 `8d559143…` → `b2c5a850…`, the other four byte-identical — so the five Electron legs were re-run rather than inherited. *(c)* **THE TWO CAVEATS STAND IN THE PARAGRAPH ABOVE**, one clause each.

**WHAT IT COSTS, MEASURED ON THE COMMITTED TREE, THE BOUNDARY ROW DECLARING EVERY PATH AND GAINING NO ENTRY BECAUSE EVERY ONE WAS ALREADY DECLARED ABOVE.** `npm test` `2,938` passed / `2` skipped of `2,940` over `131` files — not one row added and not one file, so `strata/marks.json` is NOT regenerated and `strata-marks.mjs check` is green UNREGENERATED at `236` gating sites in `31` files, `2,479` on notice, the `7` carve-outs unmoved; `typecheck` `0`, `typecheck:tests` `0`, `build` OK at `preload.cjs` `8,354`, `main.mjs` `1,408,722`, `standalone.mjs` `1,282,773`, `battery-host.mjs` `1,622,407` and `renderer.js` `475,848`; `strata-walkthrough.mjs refs` resolves every `D-n` at `111` rows; `strata-lint` reads `47` PASS / `1` FAIL before and after, the register's seven over-length rows being the one FAIL this corpus ACCEPTS at `D-110` and does not chase. **THE FIVE ELECTRON LEGS WERE OWED BY THE ARTIFACT AND ARE REPORTED IN THE PARAGRAPH BELOW, and `brief.md` §6 carries them and carries no artifact byte figure**, so the bundle's move has no cell there to have changed and nothing in that document is rewritten.

**IT SUPERSEDES BY POINTER AND NEVER REWORDS** (`D-46`). `D-108`'s split, its `3,579` and its *"eleven DECLARED deliverables and zero rot"* stand as the readings of their own date and are read THROUGH this row; `D-110`'s *"the eleven unimported modules on the ruling row six now waits for"* is a dated statement of what waited and the wait is over; `D-90`'s refusal to invent a consumer is untouched, since carrying invents none. **ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-110`** — from the change push's return to the record root, down through the repair writes, the boot battery's teardown, the landing entry's id, the renderer's tab client, the two-crossing ruling, the purge of the frozen graph store and its re-homed boundary row, the crossings and view legs, the deliveries and the refusal ledger, the unreachable population itself, the `1.9.3` bump with `ST-F1` discharged, to the pass that made row six ready to take one word — as prose and never as a bare `D-n`.

## D-112 — THE FIVE `data.*` ROUTES ARE ANSWERED: five renderer arms, five `ipcMain.handle` sites feeding `createDataSurface`, one `data` member declared in lockstep in both halves, and NO `data.*` push — so `strata/pending.md` row 1's work lands while its second role, the clause-2 revival condition, moves into this row and the cell stays `N/A`, because a route is not a consumer

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-04 · **Source:** `strata/pending.md` row 1, the user's instruction to answer the five members; `D-11`/`D-14`'s declaration, `D-103`/`D-105`/`D-107`'s answer shapes, `D-106`'s pointer clause; measured on this head over the installed `provident-storeroom@1.9.3`.

**WHAT STOOD UNANSWERED, AND WHAT WAS NEVER MISSING.** `RpcMethod` has carried `data.read`, `data.write`, `data.delete`, `data.list` and `data.journal` since `D-11` — 22 → 27 members, `D-14` counting the declaration — and `docs/L2/central-data/interface.md` §5 stated the consequence in its own bytes: *"each is declared, not answered … the tool call is the only answer site and the route's answer site does not exist"*. **THE MCP TOOLS NEVER LACKED AN ANSWER**: `src/main/mcp-server.ts` enumerates all five in `ALL_TOOLS` and each one drives the `DataSurface` operation of the same name. What did not exist was the BRIDGE ROUTE, and this pass builds it in the shape the corpus already carries — `tabs-channels.ts`/`tabs-surface`/`tabs-client` for the route, and `IPC_*_CHANGED` for a push where a push is owed.

**THE ROUTE, TERM BY TERM.** *(1) THE CHANNELS*: five constants in `src/shared/types.ts` beside `IPC_MODULE_CHANGED` — `IPC_DATA_READ` … `IPC_DATA_JOURNAL`, `provident:data:{read,write,delete,list,journal}` — each a REQUEST with its own `ipcMain.handle` site, the precedent `D-104`/`D-106` set for a new channel. *(2) THE MAIN SIDE*: `main()` names the surface ONCE (`const dataSurface = createDataSurface({…})`) and passes that one object to both the five handlers and `new ProvidentMcpServer({…, dataSurface})`, so a bridge request and a tool call reach one adapter and cannot carry two answers; each handler forwards the caller's argument object UNTOUCHED and inspects nothing, because the surface's own arms refuse a malformed call BY NAME. *(3) THE BRIDGE*: one `data` member, five routes, declared as the SAME TEXT in `src/main/preload.ts` and `src/renderer/secure-panels.ts` (the lockstep rule the `security`, `tabs`, `view` and `module` members already follow), each answering `<ModuleAnswer>` through a type-only import of `src/main/data/index.js`. *(4) THE RENDERER*: five arms in `handleRequest`'s switch, each `value = await dataRoute(<verb>, req.payload)`, handing the payload to the preload member of the same name and composing nothing — no path, no tier and no value is read, defaulted or re-spelled on this side; and a realm with NO `data` member answers the ledger's own `store-unreachable` with a sentence naming the missing member rather than a throw, minting no thirteenth reason word (`§2`'s closed twelve).

**`MUTATING_METHODS` GAINS NOTHING, AND THE REASON IS THE PREDICATE'S OWN MEANING RATHER THAN ITS PIN.** The set's declared subject is *"the MCP methods that mutate the APP GRAPH"* and its one consequence is a `mcp://provident/app` resource-updated push. A `data.*` write moves the PROFILE register and adds no graph node — this module *"renders nothing and changes no rendered surface … adds no graph node and emits no notification"* (`interface.md` §7) — so a `data.write` inside that set would announce a resource that did not change; and where a write lands at a root a crossing declares, THAT FAMILY's listener already delivers it, so a second signal would duplicate the first. The set therefore stays at its seven, name for name, and `BR-8` drives all five arms against a `dispatch` control and reads ZERO pushes for the five against ONE for the control.

**A `data.*` PUSH IS NOT WARRANTED, AND THE ABSENCE IS THE DECISION.** A write at a declared family root changes that family, whose own listener already carries the change into this realm; a write anywhere else changes no family and no rendered surface at all. So a sixth `data` channel would either duplicate `IPC_TABS_CHANGED`/`IPC_VIEW_CHANGED` or tell a realm that nothing it can read moved. **AND THE CLAUSE-2 READING RESTS ON THERE BEING NONE**: `D-107` re-read `D-82`'s `data.*` cell as `N/A` on three measured facts, and this pass adds no listener, no subscriber and no delivery — `src/main/data/` still calls `listen(` 0 times and `register(` 0 times, the vocabulary carries five `data` channels and no `provident:data:changed`, and `ALL_RESOURCES` is still 3 with `data` named nowhere.

**THE HONEST LIMIT OF THIS PASS, STATED WHERE IT CANNOT BE MISSED: A ROUTE IS NOT A CONSUMER.** `D-107`'s second fact — *"no renderer answer site"* — is FALSE as measured from this head, and this row says so rather than leaving a corpus claim standing unamended. **BUT THE CELL DOES NOT MOVE, BECAUSE THE TRIGGER `strata/pending.md` ROW 1 LISTED IS A CONSUMER OF A `data.*` CHANGE AND AN ANSWER SITE IS NOT ONE**: an arm answers a REQUEST; it subscribes to nothing, re-reads nothing on a signal and holds no state a delivery would have to update — the realm that calls it already holds the answer it just asked for. The revival condition therefore survives UNCHANGED — the day anything SUBSCRIBES at a `data.*` path, or serves the register as an MCP resource or a notification, the fix is a NEW ADDRESS IN A FAMILY'S LISTENER SET, never a listener hung off `data.*` — and it now lives HERE, in this row, because the pending row that carried it departs. **THE REVIVAL CONDITION IS NOT DELETED WITH THE ROW, AND THAT IS THE ONE THING THIS PASS OWED THE CORPUS ON ITS EXIT.**

**THE ROWS AND THE REVERT, DRIVEN.** `tests/integration/central-data/bridge-route.test.ts`, ELEVEN rows, unmarked and GATING (`D-40`): `BR-1` the `read` arm (`row` arm, `abs`ence answered), `BR-2` the `write` arm (`committed`, `path`, `cursor`, `repaired:false`, a materialized ENUMERABLE `log` of the package's five-member entries), `BR-3` the `delete` arm (`removed`/`tiers`/`cursor`/`repaired`/`log`, and `removed:false` over an absence), `BR-4` the `list` arm, `BR-5` the `journal` arm plus its `cursor-unknown` ledger, `BR-6` the refusal crossing as a VALUE with no `log` on the six module-composed arms, `BR-7` the payload arriving at the adapter BY IDENTITY, `BR-8` the seven-member set with its `dispatch` control, `BR-9` the no-listener census, `BR-10` the five sites beside the ABSENT frame guard, and `BR-11` the `unknown method` control. **THE REVERT: DELETE `case 'data.list'` AND THREE ROWS RED** (`BR-4` whole, `BR-7` and `BR-8`, which drive all five), restored and green.

**THE FRAME GUARD: THE SIBLINGS HAVE NONE, SO THE NEW SITES HAVE NONE, AND THE RULE IS STATED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** There is no `permitted()` IPC guard in this tree: `ipcMain.handle` appears nine times in `main()` and EVERY one discards its `IpcMainInvokeEvent` (the parameter is spelled `_event`; no handler reads a bare `event`, no handler inspects `sender` or `senderFrame`). A guard on the five new sites alone would make one crossing stricter than the four beside it — the tab record's three, the view record's five and the secure crossing's four all write under the same absence — so `BR-10` asserts the ABSENCE at all five new sites and across the file, and the tier-4 exclusion remains what it always was for the bridge: an MCP invocation-turn guard, not a frame check.

**THE IPC LEG, AND THE ONE ROW THAT STANDS ON THE FAR SIDE OF THE HOP.** `tests/e2e/store-crossings/crossings-live.mjs` gains the act `xg-data-route` and the row **`X-31`**, so the leg reads `31/31` where it read `30/30`: every call is `window.provident.data.*` → `ipcMain.handle` → `createDataSurface` on the boot's one store, driving write → read → list → journal → delete → a second read that answers `absent`, at a caller-chosen root with the probe removed inside the act. **THE NODE BATTERY CANNOT REACH THAT HOP** — `ipcRenderer` is obtainable only in the preload — so it drives the ARM and the ADAPTER with the hop replaced and SAYS SO in its own header; the leg is the complement. **AND `npm run ui`/`npm run panes` ADD NOTHING, WHICH IS STATED RATHER THAN PASSED OVER**: no authored UI calls a `data.*` route — this pass authors no element, no handler body and no binding for one, because a rendered consumer would be the consumer `D-90` refused to invent — so neither leg has a route to exercise and both were run anyway and read unmoved.

**IT SUPERSEDES BY POINTER AND NEVER REWORDS** (`D-46`). `D-107`'s fact *(ii)* and its sentence *"the cell becomes a deviation again the day anything … answers one of the five `RpcMethod` members in the renderer's switch or in `main()`"* are read THROUGH this row: the answer sites now stand and the cell still does not move, because the trigger was always a CONSUMER of a change and a route is not one; `D-107`'s bytes are unmoved, its three measured facts stand as the readings of their own date, and its *(i)* and *(iii)* still hold unmoved. `D-11`'s *"the route's answer site does not exist"* and `D-15`'s seven-member `MUTATING_METHODS` are the filings this row completes and does not reword.

## D-113 — THE OVERLAY GETS THE CONSUMER IT WAS OWED, AND `strata/pending.md` ROW 5 LEAVES BECAUSE ITS WORK LANDED: the app AUTHORS the modal surface, the wiring hands `overlayTransition`'s state to `overlayInertDeclaration` and applies its `props.<name>` as ONE `state-slice` on the AUTHORED app root — and what the inert root measurably blocks is the RENDERED SURFACE, never the MCP dispatch path

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-05 · **Source:** the user's ruling, VERBATIM: **"The overlay is used to contain UI elements that should block general app access while open, ex. security tools and other configuration settings"** — read against `strata/pending.md` row 5, `D-96`'s stopped second half and `D-95`'s open item *(a)*. Measured on this head over the installed `provident-storeroom@1.9.3`.

**WHAT STOOD STOPPED, AND WHY THIS RULING IS THE ACT IT WAITED FOR.** `src/shared/overlay.ts` has carried a pure, total, stateless four-state machine (`closed`/`open`/`held`/`closing` over five verb words) plus an inert-background declaration since the gate corpus, and `D-96` took the `overlay`/`theme` ruling as far as it could go: `theme` got its consumer and `overlay` **STOPPED AND NAMED**, because *"this app authors no overlay, scrim, dialog, modal, popover or `inert` element for a declaration to land on"* and *"authoring one is a DESIGN ACT, and inventing a consumer is exactly what `D-90` refused"*. **THIS RULING IS THAT ACT**, taken by the user, and row `5` — whose blocker was *"admitting a UI unit that authors an overlay"* — clears by it.

**THE NODES AUTHORED, ALL OF THEM PROVIDENT DATA** (`AGENTS.md` § "UI authority"): `src/shared/demo-envelope.ts` gains the overlay layer as envelope nodes and handler-body STRINGS, in the file's own idioms — `div`, `section`, `h2`, `button`, `props` for the attributes the runtime emits, `css` for the declarations, and no hand-written DOM anywhere. Quoted from the authored template: the app root `{ type: 'div', css: { id: APP_ROOT_ID, classes: ['app-surface'] }, props: { id: APP_ROOT_ID }, children: [ …every general-app card…, the opener ] }`; the opener `{ type: 'button', css: { id: OVERLAY_OPEN_ID, classes: ['btn', 'overlay-open'] }, content: 'Security & settings…', handlers: [{ name: 'overlay-open', event: 'click', body: overlayVerbBody(OVERLAY_VERB_TOGGLE) }] }`; the scrim `{ type: 'div', css: { id: OVERLAY_SCRIM_ID, classes: ['overlay-scrim'] }, props: { id: OVERLAY_SCRIM_ID, role: 'presentation' }, handlers: [{ name: 'overlay-scrim-close', event: 'click', body: overlayVerbBody(OVERLAY_VERB_CLOSE) }] }`; and the dialog `{ type: 'section', css: { id: OVERLAY_DIALOG_ID, classes: ['card', 'overlay-dialog'] }, props: { id: OVERLAY_DIALOG_ID, role: 'dialog', 'aria-modal': 'true' }, children: [ h2, the security pane, the settings surface, the state carrier, the close control ] }`. **THE DIALOG IS A SIBLING OF THE APP ROOT AND NEVER A CHILD OF IT, AND THAT IS THE DESIGN**: `inert` on a root that CONTAINED the dialog would block the dialog's own close control, and the tools the overlay is open FOR would be unreachable. **AND NO CAPABILITY UI IS AUTHORED HERE**: the Security pane and the Settings surface are the ISOLATED pane graph's, and `tests/isolation-adversarial-e2e.test.ts` refuses their ids and their words inside the app graph's rendered HTML because an app-graph node is `provident.dispatch`-reachable — so the dialog hosts the FRAME those surfaces belong to and authors no route of its own.

**THE MECHANISM WIRED, AND THE ATTRIBUTE NAME IS THE CALLER'S.** `startOverlayApplication` is the `startThemeApplication` form. `overlayWiringRole()` holds the CALLER-SUPPLIED name **once** (`'inert'`) beside the authored ids the envelope exports; `drive()` reads the verb token the authored body wrote into the authored carrier and hands it to `overlayTransition`; and `apply()` hands the resulting state to `overlayInertDeclaration(target, attributeName, state === 'open' || state === 'held')` and writes the answer as ONE `runtime.applyCommand({ kind: 'state-slice', node: APP_ROOT_ID, mutation: [{ targetProp: `props.${name}`, mode: 'replace', value: removal ? undefined : value }] })`. **`overlay.ts` OWNS NO ATTRIBUTE NAME AND NO VERB WORD** — measured, its comment-elided bytes carry neither — and **the wiring spells no verb either**: `attach()`'s three listeners read the token the authored body has just written, so the tree has ONE spelling of the name and NONE of the verbs outside the envelope. The removal arm is the mechanism's `removal` member reaching the node as `undefined`, which is the engine's declared attribute REMOVAL and never an empty string. The three listeners are the `startGutterAffordance` precedent — DOM listeners on graph-resolved elements, which create nothing.

**WHAT THE OVERLAY MEASURABLY ACHIEVES, AND WHAT IT DOES NOT — BOTH SAID, NEITHER IMPLIED BY THE OTHER.** *It does:* the inert attribute reaches the AUTHORED app root and comes back off it, and `provident.get_rendered_html` shows it — the `ui` leg reads `<div … id="app-surface" … >` closed, `<div … id="app-surface" … inert="">` open, and the closed form again. *It does:* in a browser, `inert` removes the subtree from the tab order and from pointer targeting — the HTML standard's semantics for the attribute the engine emits. *It does NOT:* block `provident.dispatch`. Measured — with the root inert, a dispatch on the `inc` control UNDER it still runs and still takes the counter to `1`, and the word `inert` appears NOWHERE in `src/renderer/runtime.ts` or `src/main/mcp-server.ts`, so no code on that path could consult it. *It does NOT:* stop the graph, the gesture session or the pane drag — the drag's commit route is ONE `state-slice` on an authored node and still answers `applied` under an inert root, and `gesture-session.ts`, `gutter-affordance.ts` and `relocate.ts` name `inert` nowhere. **NO FOCUS TRAP EXISTS AND NONE WAS ADDED**: the app has none, `inert` alone takes the subtree out of the tab order, and this pass adds no keyboard handler beyond the dialog's own authored `keydown` body. **AND THE SPELLING IS THE REALM'S**: the DOM shim writes `inert="true"`, the real `DomAdapter` writes the boolean `inert=""` — both measured, neither mistaken for the other.

**THE ROWS AND THE TWO REVERTS.** `tests/integration/overlay-application.test.ts`, TEN rows, unmarked and GATING (`D-40`, `AGENTS.md`): `OV-1` the machine through all four verbs with the four-state set closed; `OV-2` `escape` invoking the caller's callback once and ABSORBING its throw; `OV-3` the `{name, value, removal, target}` declaration on both arms, the name echoed by identity; `OV-4` the authored surface as a provident node, with a HAND-WRITTEN-DOM POSITIVE CONTROL that `list_targets` cannot see and `dispatch` cannot address; `OV-5` the two panes inside the dialog and outside the inert root; `OV-6` nothing else moves — byte-identical `renderedHtml` and census across construction and `attach()`; `OV-7` the drive through the producing graph; `OV-8` the measured reach of `dispatch` under an inert root; `OV-9` the gesture session's untouched commit route; `OV-10` no hand-written DOM, no stamped attribute, no injected CSS, and ONE spelling of the name. **TWO REDS ON REVERT, DRIVEN AND RESTORED**: dropping the inert application from `apply()` reddens `OV-4` (*"the declaration LANDED: the runtime answered `applied`: expected false to be true"*) and `OV-7` (*"and the inert declaration landed on the authored app root: expected false to be true"*); replacing the transition with a hand-rolled state word reddens `OV-1` (*"an `open` on an already-open overlay moves nothing: expected true to be false"*), `OV-2` (*"expected `open` to be `closed`"*) and `OV-7` (*"and the machine opened: expected `toggle` to be `open`"*).

## D-115 — THE HANDOVER'S CEILING IS `60`, AND THE `40` A READER CARRIED ONTO IT IS THE UNIT WALKTHROUGH RECORD'S: `strata/README.md:27` is the only statement that bounds `records/handover.md`, `D-110`'s two *"of `40`"* sentences are SUPERSEDED BY POINTER, and `git log` decides it — `6` of `29` versions legal at `40`, `27` of `29` legal at `60`, a peak of `66`, and `D-99`'s *"rewritten under its ceiling"* confirmed true as filed

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-05 · **Source:** the user's ruling — **`60`**, taking `strata/README.md:27` — on the verification pass that read the ceiling table against `records/handover.md`'s history and named `D-110`'s two *"holds at `39` of `40`"* sentences as the drifted prose. Measured on this head (`1dc13ce`): `strata-lint` prints `records/handover.md 39/60`, and that file's own first twelve lines state no ceiling at all.

**THE TWO AUTHORITIES DO NOT CONFLICT, AND THE `40` IS A THIRD DOCUMENT'S.** `strata/README.md:27` reads *"| `records/handover.md` | the session handover: where the branch stands and what a fresh session does next | 60 |"* — the only statement in this corpus that bounds that file. `D-1`'s record rule lives in `strata/brief.md` §1 and binds **three kinds**, none of them a handover: *"| State | scripts only, never prose (`strata-state`) | overwritten at every transition | 40 lines |"*, *"| Design | exactly one layer (§3) | replaced on revision; git is the history | 150 lines |"*, *"| Decisions | L0 only (`decisions.md`) | append-only, id'd `D-n`, user- or measurement-sourced (D-47), never reworded | 40 per row |"*. **The `40` a reader carries onto the handover is the UNIT WALKTHROUGH RECORD's**, and that bound has a home of its own: `strata/walkthrough.md` §6 reads *"L0 writes the report to `strata/records/<unit>-walkthrough.md`, ≤ 40 lines, replaced on every run (git holds the history)"*. Three documents, three bounds, no collision.

**THE HISTORY DECIDES IT, READ OFF `git log` RATHER THAN ARGUED.** `strata/records/handover.md` has **`29` versions**. **Six** are legal at `40` — the last six, every one of them `39`, `D-99` onward — and **`27`** are legal at `60`. Its peak is **`66`**, and **two** versions were legal at neither, `61` and `66`. From the seventh version to the twentieth it sat at **exactly `60`**, fourteen consecutive versions: the width the file takes when a session handover carries what a fresh seat needs. **`D-99`'s *"rewritten under its ceiling"*, of the `58`-line version it replaced with `39`, IS CONFIRMED TRUE AT `60` and needs nothing** — that version was legal before that rewrite and after it, under the bound the ceiling table states, and under `40` neither of the two readings was.

**THE FILE DECLARES NOTHING, SO THE BOUND IS THE PATTERN'S `60` AND NO DECLARATION IS NEEDED.** Measured over the parser's twelve-line site window, `records/handover.md` states no ceiling, so its bound resolves from the record pattern table's first row and the instrument prints `39/60` with nothing in the file saying so. **Adding `ceiling 40` would bind through the CLAMP and cost one line of headroom**: a declared value binds as the lower of itself and the pattern's number, so `40` would print `39/40` — a single line of headroom, a trade owed at every future handover edit, which is the drift this row closes installed as the bound it was mistaken for.

**`D-110`'S TWO *"OF `40`"* SENTENCES ARE SUPERSEDED BY POINTER, NEVER REWORDED** (`D-46`): its *"and the file holds at `39` of `40`"* and its *"`strata/records/handover.md` **`39` → `39`** of `40`"* are the readings of their own date, and the clause that supersedes them is **appended at `D-110`'s own row** — not carried here in its place — and read THROUGH this row from today, which is the mechanism `D-114` already applies to `D-110`'s other clauses, applied here to the two that bound a file `strata/README.md:27` bounds at `60`.

**AND THE CONSEQUENCE, STATED PLAINLY.** Under `60` the corpus's register prose has been the drift, **twice, both times in `D-110`** — the ceiling table was never wrong and the file was never over its bound; the two sentences that bounded it at `40` were. **There is no false green to fear once the corpus stops asserting `40`**: the file is at `39`, the instrument prints `39/60`, and a pass that shortened a `39`-line handover to please a bound no document states would be buying a colour with content.

## D-116 — THE GATE-INDEPENDENCE RULE IS ADOPTED, ON THE USER'S RULING: this harness does not adopt the compressed pipeline for its own instruments, its carrier is `strata/modules.md` and neither pointer `AGENTS.md` nor `README.md` may carry it (`D-3`), and no fourth blind pass is owed by the three corrections the ruling obliged

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-05 · **Source:** the user's ruling — **adopt it** — of the sentence the harness agent stated, adopted VERBATIM: *"this harness should not adopt the compressed pipeline for its own instruments — a corpus-internal gate built that way would have no independent seat at all."* It is recorded as a RULING OF THE USER's and **not as `ASM-3`'s consequence**, and that distinction is not pedantry: `ASM-3` stands **open** in `strata/brief.md` §8 and only the user closes an assumption (`D-1`), so hardening an unratified assumption into a rule would be this corpus legislating what only the user closes.

**ITS GROUND IS THE LAYER WALL, WHICH IS OLDER THAN EITHER ROW.** `AGENTS.md`'s non-negotiable reads *"No layer judges its own design: examiners diagnose and never patch, and L4 owns implementation only"*, and `strata/rebuild.md` §3 states the same wall from the examiner's side: *"a layer cannot adjudicate its own design"*. `ASM-3` — *"the four legs of §6 (`test`, `typecheck`, `typecheck:tests`, `build`) stay the floor of verification, and strata's per-layer batteries add to them rather than replace them"* — is that wall one layer down, **outside instruments are the floor and a layer's own instruments add, never replace**; it is cited here as SUPPORT and never as the source, because a gate this corpus built for itself would be the corpus adjudicating its own design at the one site nothing above it can see.

**THIS PASS IS ITS OWN EVIDENCE.** The instrument is external and was measurably not writable from this repository — `Permission denied`, recorded at `D-114` — so the coverage of `strata/records/` landed with its owner, in that owner's own design and its own L0 walkthrough record, and **every correction this pass makes was found at the instrument's own site**: the three forms and the site rule that closed its own `F-1`, `F-2` and `F-5` are readable only in its bytes, the record-path table it flattens is the residue it files as its own `F-4`, and the count it prints carries the standing seven-row register FAIL. **A corpus-owned gate could not have been examined by anyone the corpus did not appoint**, which is the whole content of the sentence above.

**ITS OPERATIVE CARRIER IS `strata/modules.md`, BECAUSE THE TWO POINTERS ARE FORECLOSED** (`D-3`, of `AGENTS.md` and `README.md`: *"Neither carries a workflow rule — a restated rule is a rule that drifts, and it would be loaded ahead of `strata/brief.md` on every session start"*). That procedure owns the harness, and it stood at **exactly `40/40`**, so by `strata/brief.md`'s own class rule — *"at the ceiling split by owner — never append"* — the clause was **PAID FOR, NOT APPENDED**: **§1's second paragraph was re-wrapped from three lines to two with not one word changed**, the one trade this corpus can call safe without taking a reading, and the freed line carries the clause at the end of §5, the section that states the enforcement wall. **WHAT IT DOES NOT SAY**: not that this corpus writes no instruments — its boundary row, its marks instrument and its five legs are its own and stay — but that the gate which judges it is not one it appoints, revises or exempts.

**NO FOURTH BLIND PASS IS OWED, AND THE REASONING IS RECORDED SO NO LATER READER RE-OPENS IT.** `strata/walkthrough.md` §5 names the class those three corrections belong to in its own sentence — *"An instrument error is not a corpus finding … that listing is L0's instrument, never evidence about the tree"* — and **they move no reading in this corpus**, measured over the instrument's whole coverage: every covered file's declared ceiling equals its pattern fallback, so none declares below it; no header carries a fence; and of the six headers carrying pipe rows inside the twelve-line window, the five outside `README.md` name no ceiling at all, `README.md`'s six being the site rule's own `F-2` case. **The precedent is `strata/records/strata-bootstrap-walkthrough.md`'s**: runs 1-19 were `STALE` and *"not one finding was a code or defect claim: every one was a document describing a tree that had moved"* — a re-run over a tree that has not moved measures the re-run. **THE TRIGGER TO WATCH**: the next time a live document in the staged root moves, `strata/walkthrough.md` §1's *"current and sufficient"* claim is what a re-run tests, and the pass is owed then, not before.
