## D-118 — THE PANE-DRAG DEMO IS REBUILT FROM THE APP'S OWN SOURCE, AND THE ROOT CAUSE IS TAKEN WITH IT: a source that imports `src/`, a generator with a provenance header, a suite row that reds when the bundle falls behind, and a live CDP drive — the checked-in artefact and its three screenshots are REPLACED, and the corpus that named the path is corrected where it stood

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-06 · **Source:** the user's instruction this pass carries, VERBATIM — *"Rebuild the demo, and fix the root cause rather than repeating it."* — read against `D-94`'s own measurement (the demo's source could not compile and was deleted, the bundle kept because nothing rebuilt it) and `D-95`'s open item *(c)*, which turned that into a KEEP on the corpus's naming. Measured on this head over the installed `provident-storeroom@1.9.3`.

**WHY THE OLD ARTEFACT COULD NOT BE REGENERATED, MEASURED AND NOT REMEMBERED.** `demo/pane-drag-demo/demo.mjs` was `13,217` lines / `511,559` bytes of self-contained esbuild output with **zero** `import`/`export`/`require` statements, carrying `src/renderer/store-core-graph.ts` and `store-graph-references.ts` INLINED — and it embedded a **frozen copy of `startGutterAffordance` still reading `seams.startSizeOf`/`seams.boundsOf`**, the element-attribute pair `D-117` replaced with the composition's shape reads. Its source (`demo.ts` 747 + `demo-entry.ts` 34) was DELETED at `D-94` because it imported a module `D-93` deleted, `grep -rn "pane-drag-demo" package.json scripts/ tests/` answered zero build hits, and no leg ran it. **A checked-in bundle with no generator is a copy, and a copy cannot track its original** — so the pass that replaces it must supply the generator, not a newer copy.

**THE NEW SOURCE IS THE APP'S CODE, AND WHAT IT SUPPLIES IS A PARAMETER.** `demo/pane-drag-demo/demo.ts` (+ `stub-view.ts`) imports VALUES from `src/shared/gesture-session.ts` (`createGestureSession`, `POINTER_TYPES`), `src/shared/gutter.ts` (`clampToBounds`), `src/shared/gutter-affordance.ts` (`createGutterAffordance`, `domEventSource`), `src/shared/demo-envelope.ts` (`gutterSeamExample` — the four seams `D-117` keeps the example's), `src/shared/view-shape.ts` and **`src/renderer/renderer.ts`'s `createPaneDrag`**, whose `PaneDragSurface` it takes as a TYPE; nothing is transcribed, and `DB-3` asserts that over comment-elided code with a positive control that the same predicate finds each function in the module that owns it. **`['panes']` IS A CROSSING, SO THE DEMO SUPPLIES A STUB SHAPE** (`stub-view.ts`): four members picked off the app's own `ViewClient` — `paneSizeOf`, `paneBoundsOf`, `zoneOf`, `commitPane` — reading through the app's pure `paneGeometryIn` and `zoneIn`, seeded with the tree's MEASURED defaults (`pane-a`, `100`, `{min 0, max 200}`, re-derived, never re-spelled). **The D-117 wiring is visible and literal**: `createPaneDrag({ view: stub.members }, domEventSource())` is constructed once, the affordance is handed its `startSizeOf`, its `boundsOf` and its `release(gestureId, value, write)` as the single sink writer and the route in one ordered turn, and **the sink is the demo's own status node**. The stub's `commitPane` COMPOSES the write into its own family at the call (the crossing's "a supplied member is written over what stands" rule) instead of crossing a bridge, and the README says so rather than implying a boundary.

**THE CONTROL, AND THE DECOY THAT MAKES THE SHAPE'S AUTHORITY OBSERVABLE RATHER THAN ASSERTED.** The panel changes the stub (`size ±40`, `bounds {20,180}`/`{0,200}`, `reset`), and the next drag starts from the new value — the drive reads `100 → 180` across it. The pane's PARENT ELEMENT carries `size="999" min="0" max="999"`, i.e. exactly the attributes the pre-`D-117` wiring read; a drag that started at `999` would mean the defect is back, and the drive asserts it starts at the shape's figure.

**ONE COMMAND REBUILDS IT, AND NO `package.json` KEY MOVED.** `node demo/pane-drag-demo/build.mjs` bundles `demo.ts` with esbuild (already a devDependency) into `demo.mjs` and prepends a header carrying one `sha256` per input plus the digest of the generated body; `--check` compares that header with the sources as they stand and `--verify <path>` runs the same comparison against any file. **THE `scripts` KEY SET IS PINNED BY SIX CENSUSES, so the command is a literal line, recorded in `demo/pane-drag-demo/README.md` and here.** The bundler applies exactly ONE transformation to app code, and refuses the build unless it finds its subject exactly once: `renderer.ts`'s module-scope boot guard is neutralised, because importing the module would otherwise boot the app into `#app`; nothing else in `src/` is transformed.

**THE STALENESS ROW, AND IT CAN FAIL.** `tests/demo-bundle.test.ts` (new, gating, unmarked) carries FOUR rows: `DB-1` runs `--check` and reds when any source moved, any import appeared or the body was hand-edited; `DB-2` drives the same instrument over a doctored copy (one appended byte → refused, its `@body-sha256` named) and a headerless file (refused as such) while admitting the committed bundle; `DB-3` is the import-not-transcription census; `DB-4` asserts the retired mechanism (`createGraphStore`, `store-core-graph`, `store-graph-references`, `temp.drag`) appears NOWHERE in the bundle's code, with the same scanner's positive arm. **RED ON REVERT, DRIVEN VERBATIM**: one line appended to `stub-view.ts` with no rebuild → `--check` exit `1`, `STALE … changed demo/pane-drag-demo/stub-view.ts` and `DB-1` red; the line removed → exit `0`, `CURRENT`, `DB-1` green. **THE GATE IS A CONTENT HASH AND NOT AN MTIME COMPARISON, AND BOTH HALVES ARE MEASURED.** A `touch` on `demo.ts` moves its mtime past the bundle's (`23:14:29` against `23:13:08`) while its BYTES are identical: an mtime gate would red a bundle that is current, and `--check` answers `CURRENT`, exit `0`. And a fresh `git worktree add --detach` checkout of this commit wrote `demo.mjs`, `demo.ts` and `build.mjs` with the SAME mtime to the nanosecond on this filesystem, so the ordering an mtime gate would read is a checkout artefact and a tie — a gate whose answer is a filesystem tie is not a gate. A CONTENT change reds it either way, which is the reading that means something.

**THE LIVE DRIVE, REBUILT AND GREEN.** `run-live.mjs` still drives the page over CDP with REAL `Input.dispatchMouseEvent` and no package: hover writes and clears the cursor declaration, four drags press/move/preview/release, and the panel's buttons sit between them — `29/29` rows, exit `0`, reading **"before the stub's control `100` → after it `180`; clamped commit `180`; back to `100` after the reset"**. Three harness defects it fixed, each measured: the stale-window preflight's pattern required the app path BEFORE `--remote-debugging-port`, which the child's own command line never matches (two survivors, and the driver attached to the older window); the window was killed by pid, leaving renderer children (two survivors), now reaped by process group; and the Chrome profile was written into this directory, now a fresh `mkdtemp` under the OS temp area, removed with the window.

**WHAT IT CANNOT SHOW WITHOUT THE REAL APP — in its README, not implied here.** No crossing, no receipt, no delivery, no second boot; no `Runtime`, no graph-resolved elements, no `applyCommand` sink; no tabs, no zone data, no minimized zone and **no drag ghost** (that was a store mechanism's, and faking one is the simulation this rebuild refuses); and **no real pointer drag against the APP**, because no leg drives one (`npm run panes`'s gesture is a `dispatch` click). What the pass DID find: the session's tracking listeners live on the handle element, so a real pointer that leaves a 14px handle stops targeting it — the demo's own `pointerdown` listener calls `setPointerCapture`, `domEventSource()` advertises none, and whether the app's drag survives that is stated as UNMEASURED.

**THE CORPUS THE REBUILD CORRECTED.** `strata/modules.json`'s `demo-panes` row gains `tests/demo-bundle.test.ts` — without it the L1 coverage sentence (*"every … `tests/**` file and `demo/pane-drag-demo/` claimed once"*) would be false the moment the battery landed — while its `endpoints` stay `0` and `mapRows` stays `21`; `AGENTS.md`'s `demo/` row now names the build command and the staleness row rather than "the live pane-drag demo" alone; **`docs/L1/central-data-management.md`'s coverage sentence is LEFT UNTOUCHED because it is still TRUE** (the map row's directory claim covers the new files), and `strata/platform.md`, `strata/pending.md`, `strata/next-steps.md`, `strata/modules.md`, `strata/records/store-tabs-record-unit.md` and `strata/records/handover.md` are untouched with their ceilings intact. **IT SUPERSEDES `D-95`'s OPEN ITEM *(c)* BY POINTER, NEVER BY REWORDING** (`D-46`): the KEEP it recorded was the corpus's naming, and the corpus now names a generator.

**WHAT IT COSTS, MEASURED ON THE COMMITTED TREE.** Four legs `0/0/0/0`: `npm test` **`2,962` passed / `2` skipped of `2,964` over `134` files** (`84` flat + `50` nested — `D-117`'s `2,958`/`2,960`/`133` plus this pass's FOUR collected rows and ONE file, and nothing else), `typecheck` `0` (**`tsconfig.json` now READS `demo/pane-drag-demo/**/*.ts`**, closing `D-94`'s latent defect — a source no leg compiled), `typecheck:tests` `0`, `build` OK — five artifacts at `preload.cjs` `9,407`, `main.mjs` `1,409,129`, `standalone.mjs` `1,282,773`, `battery-host.mjs` `1,622,407`, `renderer.js` `493,559`, and **no `src/` file moves**, so no artifact byte can move with this pass (they are the parent commit's, its inputs unchanged). **The five Electron legs are NOT OWED and are not run**, and `strata-marks.json` is REGENERATED by `strata-marks.mjs index` (`gating` `259 → 263` sites in `35 → 36` files, `on notice` `2,476`, carve-outs `9`); `refs` resolves `118` rows; `srcTypeScriptFiles` `51` and `mapRows` `21`. **THE LIVE DRIVE'S PROCESS CENSUS READS ZERO** survivors of this repository's after the run, with the profile directory gone and no write into the tree.

## D-119 — BRANCH `strata` IS MERGED INTO `main`, FAST-FORWARD, ON THE MERGE PERMISSION `D-6` RESERVED FOR THE USER: the preconditions are stated with their readings, the two refs agree at one commit, the standing `strata-lint` register FAIL is carried as a known state and not repaired, and NOTHING IS PUSHED

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-06 · **Source (user):** the merge permission `D-6` reserved — the instruction *"merge the branch into `main`"* — relayed through the instruction commissioning this row, which grants **no push** and authorises **no remote operation**. It is the permission `D-6` waited for, and `D-6`'s own row carries the pointer clause that lifts the freeze rather than this row reworking it (`D-46`).

**THE PRECONDITIONS, EACH AS IT READ BEFORE `main` WAS TOUCHED, ON A CLEAN TREE.** `git status --short` **empty** and `git rev-parse HEAD` `12b57e8` (`D-118`'s commit). `git merge-base --is-ancestor main strata` exit **`0`** — `main` IS an ancestor. `git log --oneline strata..main` **empty** and `git rev-list --count strata..main` **`0`**, so there is **no divergence**, while `git rev-list --count main..strata` reads **`364`**. The conflict check — `git merge-tree --write-tree --name-only main strata` — exits **`0`** writing tree `9614097`, **naming no conflicted path**: a merge could not conflict, and `--ff-only` could not refuse.

**THE FOUR LEGS AT THAT HEAD, AND THE INSTRUMENTS BESIDE THEM.** `npm test` **`2,962` passed / `2` skipped of `2,964` over `134` test files; `typecheck` **`0`** errors; `typecheck:tests` **`0`** errors; `build` **OK**, five artefacts. `node scripts/strata-walkthrough.mjs refs` **resolved every `D-n` cite** over `118` rows at that head (this row is the `119`th); `strata-marks.mjs check` **exit `0`** — `263` gating spec sites in `36` files, `2,476` on notice, `9` carve-outs. **`strata-lint` exit `1` with ONE failing check, WHICH IS ITS STANDING, ACCEPTED FAIL AND NOT A CLEAN LINT** — the register's row-length rule, red on `D-68` `41`, `D-70` `43`, `D-71` `43`, `D-73` `53`, `D-74` `43`, `D-75` `42` and `D-76` `43` lines. **This row adds nothing to that set**: it stands under `40` lines, and the merge ships the FAIL as a known state rather than repairing it inside a merge pass.

**WHAT THE MERGE WAS.** `git checkout main` at `28e9ac1`, then **`git merge --ff-only strata`** — a **FAST-FORWARD**, so **no merge commit exists** and no strategy was invented; had it refused, the pass would have stopped rather than inventing one. **`main` now carries the commit that carries this row and `D-6`'s pointer clause**, whose parent is `12b57e8` and whose id `git rev-parse main` returns — a commit cannot carry its own digest, so the commit is named here by construction and by its parent, which is what an audit re-reads — and **`git rev-parse main` equals `git rev-parse strata`** at it, with `git status --short` empty on `main`.

**WHAT THE MERGE MADE FALSE, CORRECTED ON `strata` BEFORE IT, IN PLACE, WITH NO DATED ROW REWORDED (`D-46`).** `AGENTS.md`'s *Current state* paragraph now says `main` carries the work, that the freeze is lifted and that nothing is pushed; `strata/records/handover.md`'s heading names the fast-forward instead of *"`main` still `28e9ac1`, unmerged by D-6"*, and its operating note reads **never push** where it read *never merge or push*; `strata/brief.md`'s header line carries the merge against its `D-6` cite at **ZERO LINE COST**, so no line had to be freed for it; and `D-6` gains the pointer clause. The handover stands at `39/60` and the brief at `148/150`, both under their ceilings (`strata/README.md`).

**THE MERGED STATE IS VERIFIED, NOT MERELY THE REF.** A fresh `git worktree add --detach` worktree of the committed tree — no untracked directory and nothing copied in — installs from the lock with `npm ci`, builds and runs the suite; the worktree is removed after and `git worktree list` reads one entry again. **MEASURED AT `12b57e8`, this pass's parent and the commit whose delta is four documents and no `.ts` file: GREEN — `2,962` passed / `2` skipped of `2,964` over `134` files.** `D-100`'s fix is what buys that: at its own date a clean clone was red, `45` failures and `28` uncollected, until the corpus the suite reads was tracked. **ONE OBSTACLE IS ENVIRONMENTAL AND IS NAMED RATHER THAN HIDDEN**: `npm ci` did not complete Electron's postinstall offline, and the first reading was red — `9` failures over `4` files (`7` boot-contract spawns `ENOENT`, the arrangement's preload row, the UI leg's malformed-config row, and one suite failing to collect) — every one of them traced to the absent binary; completed by hand with `electron_config_cache=/tmp/electron-cache` and re-run, it reads the green above. **The repository's tracked state was sufficient in both arms.**

**NOTHING IS PUSHED**, and that is the whole of the remote's status: no push was granted and no remote operation was taken, so if a remote is configured, `main` now sits ahead of `origin/main` by the merged commits — a fact this row records and no operation it took. **Its register is `D-6` and `D-46`**: the permission `D-6` reserved, exercised once and in the user's words, and `D-46`'s no-rewording rule, obeyed at every place the merge made false by appending a pointer rather than editing a dated sentence.

## D-120 — THE THREE DEFECTS THE RUNNING WINDOW MEASURED, EACH FIXED AT ITS OWN HOME: the shell's page CSS pins the light arm and carries the rules the authored attribute was owed, and the overlay's geometry moves into the envelope's own `css` while its visibility is keyed on the mechanism's `inert` product — so the toggle's two screenshots STOP being byte-identical

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-07 · **Source:** the user's instruction, three defects measured on this window and a home named per fix. Measured on this head (`bb9efea`) over the installed `provident-storeroom@1.9.3`, with a scratch instrument (the DevTools protocol for computed styles and screenshots, `provident.get_rendered_html`/`get_node_state`/`dispatch` for the graph) on a fresh scratch profile per boot.

**1 · WHITE ON WHITE, AND THE HOME IS THE SHELL'S OWN PAGE CSS.** `src/renderer/index.html` declared `:root { color-scheme: light dark }` — the UA chooses an arm — while the page paints `#f4f6f8`/`#fff` and leaves most text colour unset. With the desktop's dark preference EMULATED (not inferred), computed styles read `html`, `body`, `header h1`, `#counter-card h2`, `#counter` and both `#group-toggles label`s at **`rgb(255, 255, 255)`** over `rgb(244, 246, 248)`/`rgb(255, 255, 255)`; the two buttons read only because the UA paints its own dark face (`rgb(255, 255, 255)` on `rgb(107, 107, 107)`) and the panes' hint and status set explicit colours, which is why the report named them as the two that read. **THE FIX IS BOTH ARMS, AND FOR TWO DIFFERENT FAILURES**: `:root` is PINNED to `color-scheme: light` — the design IS light, its backgrounds are literals, and the pin also keeps the UA's controls and scrollbar on that arm — and `body` gains an explicit `color: #1b2430` so every element that sets no colour of its own inherits a legible one even in a realm that ignores the pin. Measured after: `color-scheme` computes `light`, and those same six elements compute `rgb(27, 36, 48)` on the same backgrounds (`#inc` `rgb(0, 0, 0)` on `rgb(239, 239, 239)`). **THE COST, STATED**: a user who prefers dark gets light form controls and a light scrollbar. The app's own dark pair is the AUTHORED control's, because the repo reads no environment for it (`D-97`).
**2 · THE TOGGLE CHANGED DATA AND NOT APPEARANCE, AND THE HOME IS PRESENTATION KEYED ON THE AUTHORED ATTRIBUTE.** The mechanism run directly — `applyThemeDeclaration('theme', resolveTheme(s, undefined).setting)` — answers `{name: 'theme', value: 'dark', removal: false}`, `{name: 'theme', value: 'light', removal: false}` and, for the empty setting, `{name: 'theme', value: '', removal: true}`. **WHERE IT LANDS IS MEASURED AND IS NOT THE APP ROOT**: `get_rendered_html` carries `<div … id="theme-setting" … theme="light">` and then `theme="dark"` (and `ssrHtml` carries both), `get_node_state` reads `props.theme`, while `<div … id="app-surface" …>` carries NO `theme` attribute at any point — the consumer writes `props.<name>` on the AUTHORED STATE NODE (`themeWiringRole` derives it), exactly as `D-96`/`D-97` recorded. So the rules are keyed on **`[theme="dark"]`** and reach the surface above that node through `:has()`; the selector names the ATTRIBUTE and never the node id, because the attribute is the interface the graph publishes. Fourteen declarations in six rules, colours only: the page pair (`#12161c`/`#e8edf4`), the cards (`#1b222b`), the buttons, the input, the counter's value, and the panes' dim colours re-stated (`#9aa7b8`) so nothing drops to illegible. **THE TWO SCREENSHOTS ARE THE PROOF**: before the fix they were **BYTE-IDENTICAL** (`md5 0063049f0275f08410045376b8e745cd` for both), and after it they are three distinct images (`44d94635…` light, `1891dcfc…` dark) with `body` computing `rgb(18, 22, 28)`/`rgb(232, 237, 244)`, cards `rgb(27, 34, 43)`, buttons `rgb(43, 53, 66)`.
**3 · THE OVERLAY MOUNTED IN FLOW, AND THE HOME IS THE ENVELOPE'S OWN `css` — WITH VISIBILITY LEFT TO THE MECHANISM.** Measured: the scrim was `position: static`, full-width, **ZERO HEIGHT** at `y=908`; the dialog was `position: static` at `y=908`, `425x253`, **IN FLOW BELOW the app cards and VISIBLE while the machine said `closed`** — inside a `692`-high viewport, so off the bottom of the window. The authored objects were `{display: 'block'}` for both. **THEY NOW CARRY THE GEOMETRY AS PROVIDENT DATA**: the scrim `{position: 'fixed', top/right/bottom/left: '0', background: 'rgba(10, 14, 20, 0.55)', z-index: '10'}` and the dialog `{position: 'fixed', top: '50%', left: '50%', transform: 'translate(-50%, -50%)', width: 'min(420px, 90vw)', max-height: '80vh', overflow: 'auto', z-index: '11', box-shadow: …}` — rendered as `style="position: fixed; top: 50%; left: 50%; transform: translate(-50%, -50%); …"`. Measured after: open, the scrim is `965x692` over a `980x692` window (the 15px is the scrollbar) and the dialog is centred at `x=256 y=220` `454x253` over the app; closed, BOTH compute `display: none`, `0x0`, and the app root is untouched (`x=33 y=120 425x787`, document height `1209` either way — the layer no longer adds flow height). **HOW VISIBILITY IS DRIVEN, ESTABLISHED AND NOT DUPLICATED**: the authored data carries NO `display` (an inline declaration would outrank any sheet rule and pin a closed overlay open), and the shell's page CSS keys the layer's `display` on the `inert` attribute `startOverlayApplication` applies to the AUTHORED app root — `open`/`held` inert, `closed`/`closing` the removal arm — so the four-state machine still decides and the sheet only reads its declared product, one key and no state word of its own.
**THE ROWS, EACH ABLE TO FAIL, AND EACH REVERT DRIVEN.** `tests/integration/overlay-application.test.ts` gains `OV-11` (the AUTHORED `css` objects carry the backdrop's four insets and the dialog's centring pair, read off the envelope as data, with a control object that omits `position` refused) and `OV-12` (the geometry reaches the RENDERED tag, neither authored style claims a `display`, and the sheet's one key is `#app-surface[inert] ~ #overlay-scrim` — with a key on another attribute refused); `tests/theme-declaration.test.ts` gains `T-8` (the mechanism run over both tokens, the attribute read off the rendered node, and the sheet's key BUILT from `declaration.name`/`value` rather than re-typed, plus the control that a `data-theme` key is not admitted and that the sheet pins no authored node id) and `T-9` (the `:root` arm is exactly `light`, and the `body` foreground clears AA `4.5:1` against the background it paints — `13.1:1` — with the two-arm form and the measured white-on-`#f4f6f8` defect each failing their own control). **REVERTS, DRIVEN AND RESTORED**: `:root` back to `light dark` reds `T-9` (`and it names the LIGHT arm alone: expected 'light dark' to be 'light'`) and dropping `body`'s `color` reds the contrast arm; dropping the `[theme="dark"]` rules reds `T-8` (`the page sheet carries rules keyed on \`[theme="dark"]\`… expected 0 to be greater than 0`); restoring `{display: 'block'}` on both authored styles reds `OV-11` (`expected [ 'position', 'top', 'right', …(4) ] to deeply equal []`) and `OV-12` (`expected '<section …' to contain 'position: fixed'`); and dropping the `inert`-keyed rule reds `OV-12` (`it is SHOWN only while the authored app root carries the inert attribute… expected false to be true`).
**THE PARITY BAR, TAKEN ON THIS PASS'S OWN COMMITTED TREE.** Four legs `0/0/0/0`: `npm test` **`2,966` passed / `2` skipped of `2,968` over `134` files** (`84` flat + `50` nested — this pass's FOUR collected rows and NO file), `typecheck` `0`, `typecheck:tests` `0`, `build` OK. **All five Electron legs green on `DISPLAY=:0`, each with a process census around it reading ZERO survivors of this repository's against the same four strangers' `chrome_crashpad_handler`**: `battery` `184 checks, 0 failures`; `divergence` **`9 checks, 0 failures`** plus `4` extension checks `0` — and its three `inert` extension rows (`P1`/`P2`/`P3`) read set-equal on BOTH realms, which is the leg the envelope change had to leave exactly as it stood; `ui` **`12/12`** at the standing `425x22` (`fontSize=16px`), its overlay row's fragment moving `4,498 → 4,507 → 4,497` bytes (it read `4,261 → 4,270 → 4,260` before, the growth being the authored `style` attributes); `panes` `7/7` over `22` MCP calls and `2` gestures; `crossings` `31/31` over `88` MCP calls (`84` the driver's, `4` envelope-settle polls), `22` acts and `21` observation dumps. `strata/marks.json` is REGENERATED by `strata-marks.mjs index` — the four rows join two files that were already INDEXED, so `gating` moves `263 → 267` sites in the SAME `36` files (`on notice` `2,476` and the `9` carve-outs unmoved), and `strata-marks.mjs check` is green — `strata-walkthrough.mjs refs` resolves every `D-n` over `120` rows, `strata/brief.md`'s §6 figure and its mark census are re-written IN PLACE and the file holds at `148` of `150`, and `strata/modules.json` is UNTOUCHED because the map already claims this pass's one new write path — `renderer-wiring` carried `src/renderer/index.html`, and the `chrome` row claiming it too was a DOUBLE CLAIM `strata-lint` refused (measured: `path "src/renderer/index.html" claimed by "renderer-wiring" and "chrome"`), so `mapRows` stays `21` and no `endpoints` figure moves. `strata-lint` reads `119` PASS / `1` FAIL / `4` INFO at exit `1` BEFORE and AFTER this pass — run on a detached worktree of `bb9efea` and on this tree — the FAIL being the standing over-long register rows (`D-68`, `D-70`, `D-71`, `D-73`, `D-74`, `D-75`, `D-76`) this instrument ACCEPTS and does not chase, and `D-120` is not among them at `11` lines.
**WHAT REMAINS, NAMED RATHER THAN CLOSED.** *(a)* The dark pair is the SHELL's presentation and the app surface's; the operator panes column is the ISOLATED pane graph's surface and is keyed by its own explicit colours (re-stated for the dark arm) rather than by the attribute — a pane graph has no appearance control and this pass invents none. *(b)* The key is `:has()`, which needs Chromium `105`+ (this shell is Electron `44`, far above it); a realm without it would show the light design only, and that limit is stated in the sheet's own comment rather than discovered later. *(c)* `closing` is a state no authored verb produces, and the key hides the layer on it as it hides it on `closed` — if a later pass animates an exit, the wiring's own declaration is the thing to change, never the sheet. *(d)* `npm run ui`'s overlay row still measures only the rendered fragment: browser focus, tab-order and pointer semantics remain unmeasured and no new leg claims them.
**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-119`**, as prose and never as a bare `D-n`: the change push returned to the record root; a repair's credit made explicit and its write made to name its commit; the boot battery's teardown made to prove zero survivors; the landing entry's id decided, its seat derived and that id moved; the renderer's tab client appearing in Stage A; the two-crossing ruling with its purge; the surplus instance collapsed onto the normal crossing with the `data.*` tools pinned to the central store; the module registry's legacy file dropped rather than migrated; the data-flow convention audited clause by clause; the imperative boot write deleted for a sixth declaration; the `mem` pair's existence declared at the store's creation; the absence-keyed import put inside the repair; `1.9.1`'s landing-arm save measured; the purge's two successor addresses declared; the renderer swap refused on measurement and then landed; the enumeration that stopped on the zone family; the zone family's own address; the frozen store made UNUSED and then DELETED; the two stale imports taken with the crossings given a leg a leg runs; the view record's crossing given a live leg with the pane read pair made total; the appearance mechanism given its consumer; the appearance family's tier made durable; the corpus made to tell the truth about itself; the archive made checkout-visible; the `data` tier made the caller's with a declared-root guard; the message log measured at last; the bump to `1.9.2` with the commit's log on the `data.*` answers; the app-state rows given an address; the bump to `1.9.3` with the value arms and the registration refusal discharged; the refusal ledger's `log` with the clause-2 cell re-read as `N/A`; the registration instant made structural; the five `data.*` routes answered; the appearance family made to produce its declaration in a repair; the overlay given the consumer it was owed; the instrument extended over the records; the handover's ceiling decided; the gate-independence rule adopted; the two leftovers taken with the live pane drag wired; the pane-drag demo rebuilt from source; and the merge of `strata` into `main` on the user's permission. **The package-facing rows among them stay the package's** — `provident-storeroom` is read and never modified, its own corpus's decision ids are never spelled here, and a defect or a requirement gap of it belongs to the unit that surfaced it, with symptom, reproduction, suspected root cause and proposed fix shape (`D-5`, `D-52`).

## D-121 — THE USER TAKES **B**: THE WINDOW GOES FRAMELESS AND THE WINDOW CONTROLS ARE AUTHORED INTO THE TAB BAR — the option object measured on Linux, the control channel set and its bridge member, the sender guard a privileged channel owes, and the drag region measured IN THE AUTHORED DATA, with the readings it does not move stated beside the one it does

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-07 · **Source (user, verbatim):** "**B — go frameless and author the window controls into the tab bar.**" It is the FIRST of the four decisions a study left open and the only one this row takes (D-47: user-sourced, and the answer quoted rather than paraphrased).

**WHAT IT SETTLES, AND ONLY THIS.** The OS stops drawing this app's chrome: `src/main/main.ts`'s one `BrowserWindow` construction is created unframed, the window's top edge becomes the authored surface beginning at `y=0`, and minimizing, maximizing and closing become the app's own to draw and to wire. It settles the FRAMING and the controls' ORIGIN. It designs no tab bar, names no node, class, id or layer's write, and **no file under `src/` or `tests/` moves in this pass** — a ruling is recorded and the landing pass that acts on it is a separate unit (`D-6` and `D-119` are the register for who may change the shell: the merge permission that row reserved was exercised by the user's own words, the freeze is lifted, nothing is pushed, and neither clause is reworked here).

**THE OPTION OBJECT, MEASURED ON THIS PLATFORM** (Electron `44.4.5`, this host, a `980x720` construction — the app's own numbers — under `--ozone-platform=x11` as every leg spawns it; `getBounds()` and `getContentBounds()` read from the main process, `innerWidth`/`innerHeight` from the page): default frame — bounds `980x720`, content **`980x692` at `y+28`**, a 28-pixel GTK title bar; `frame: false` — content **`980x720` at `y+0`**; `titleBarStyle: 'hidden'` — content `980x720` at `y+0`, IDENTICAL to `frame: false`; `titleBarStyle: 'hidden'` with `titleBarOverlay: true` — the same content geometry, with the OS painting its own controls over the page. **THE ONE MEMBER B ADDS IS `frame: false`, AND NOTHING ELSE**, and each other member of the family is refused by name: the shipped declaration states it plainly — *"Specify `false` to create a frameless window. Default is `true`."* (`node_modules/electron/electron.d.ts`) — while **`titleBarOverlay`** has the OS draw the very controls B hands to the app and carries `color`/`symbolColor` annotated `@platform win32,linux`; **`trafficLightPosition`** is annotated `@platform darwin` ("Set a custom position for the traffic light buttons in frameless windows"), as are `setWindowButtonVisibility` and `setWindowButtonPosition`, so all three are no-ops on this platform and `main.ts` sets none; **`roundedCorners`** defaults `true` and the same declaration bounds it — *"On Linux, rounded corners are only drawn when the desktop environment supports client-side decorations"* — so it is left at its default rather than asserted; and **`titleBarStyle: 'hidden'`**, which measures the same here, is refused because its own documentation defines the Linux case only *"when combined with `titleBarOverlay: true`"* and says that *"otherwise no window controls will be shown"* — a value whose meaning is a version's, where `frame: false` is the documented frameless switch itself. **THE PREMISE IS CORRECTED RATHER THAN ADOPTED**: the shipped `44.4.5` declaration tags `titleBarStyle` with NO `@platform` at all, so "macOS/Windows-specific" is not what this install says, and Electron's current documentation's `hidden` row names **Windows and Linux** for its overlay branch; what is true on this host is what the four arms measured, and it is what this row commits to. **ONE PLATFORM CAVEAT IS NAMED, NOT SOLVED**: that documentation adds *"On Wayland (Linux), frameless windows have GTK drop shadows and extended resize boundaries by default"*, with `hasShadow: false` as the further member for a fully undecorated window — this host's session is Wayland while the legs spawn X11, so that arm is a measurement the landing pass owes and this row does not claim.

**THE CONTROL CHANNEL SET, AND THE INVENTORY IT JOINS.** At this head the app registers **18 `ipcMain.handle` sites and 3 `ipcMain.on` sites, every one of them in `src/main/main.ts`** — no other file under `src/` registers an IPC handler — over **26 channel-name constants** (`15` in `src/shared/types.ts`, `4` in `src/shared/tabs-channels.ts`, `6` in `src/shared/view-channels.ts`, `1` in `src/main/store-channels.ts`), and `src/main/preload.ts` exposes **9 top-level members carrying 22 routes**. **B ADDS A TENTH MEMBER, `window`, WITH FIVE ROUTES OVER FIVE CHANNELS** in a new `src/shared/window-channels.ts` (`tabs-channels.ts`'s and `view-channels.ts`'s own precedent): `minimize()`, `toggleMaximize()`, `close()` and `onChanged(handler)` over `provident:window:{minimize,toggle-maximize,close,changed}`, and `state()` over `provident:window:state` — four `ipcMain.handle` sites and no `ipcMain.on`. **WHY THESE AND NOT A PAIR PER VERB**: the button is ONE control, so ONE toggle channel with `win.isMaximized()` deciding the direction on the main side and the answer stating the state it left — a `maximize`/`unmaximize` pair would let the renderer compose the very bit it is asking about (`D-112`'s one-site-per-channel rule read on three verbs); the state READ serves the boot's first paint and every change the WM makes without the app, and the change push then carries NOTHING — no verb, no state — exactly as the tab record's, the view record's, the app-state rows' and the security record's four carry nothing (`D-63`, `D-87`, `D-104`, `D-106`). **THE LOCKSTEP THE NEW MEMBER JOINS IS FIVE SITES**: `src/main/preload.ts`'s `ProvidentBridge` and `src/renderer/secure-panels.ts`'s `declare global` (a half-declaration fails), plus the three censuses that assert the member set by EQUALITY — the two `BRIDGE_MEMBERS` constant arrays in the two `security-store-storeroom/isolation` files and the same set spelled inline in `settings-carrier-retirement`'s `ISO-4` — so a tenth member added to the bridge alone reds all three.

**THE PERMISSION MODEL A PRIVILEGED CHANNEL OWES, AND WHERE ITS GUARD MAY LIVE.** No handler in this file checks its sender today: `permitted(` and `senderFrame` occur nowhere under `src/`, 14 of the 18 `handle` sites discard `_event` and the other four take no event at all, and `BR-10` asserts both absences over comment-elided code as a MEASUREMENT of the siblings rather than as an opinion about a new site. **A window-control channel is a different privilege class**: every sibling reaches a store row, while `close()` ends the process and the two others reach the OS window the user is looking at — and the same tree makes every authored control `dispatch`-reachable, so which party may press that button is the chrome-versus-data question below. B therefore commits this group to the ONE guard the rest of the file does not have: a request is refused unless the invoking event's sender is THIS window's own `webContents` and the frame it arrived on is that webContents' main frame, and the refusal is a VALUE in the crossings' own vocabulary, never a throw (`D-101`'s refusal shape read on a channel that owns no store). **AND ITS HOME IS MEASURED, NOT CHOSEN**: `BR-10` reads `src/main/main.ts` with comments elided and reddens on `permitted(`, on `senderFrame` and on a bare `event` token, so the guard belongs in the main-side module that owns the four verbs with `main.ts`'s handler forwarding `_event` in one expression — or that gating row's own sentence is re-grained in the landing pass, and either way it is re-read there rather than assumed.

**THE DRAG REGION, DRIVEN IN THE RUNNING RENDERER.** Three declarations were authored into an envelope and read back through `provident.load` → `provident.dispatch` → `provident.get_rendered_html` on the built bundle under a fresh scratch profile, the observation taken in the renderer realm (an `HTMLDivElement` element and a `CSSStyleDeclaration`, so a real DOM and not the shim): **(a)** `css: {'-webkit-app-region': 'drag'}` — a css MEMBER — renders as the unknown ATTRIBUTE `-webkit-app-region="drag"` with NO `style` attribute and a computed `webkitAppRegion` of `none`, because the engine routes every `css.<key>` through `setAttribute` and only `id`, `classes`, `style` and `cssDef` are special (`node_modules/provident-ssr/dist/core/adapters.js`); **(b)** `css: {style: {'-webkit-app-region': 'drag'}}` — the authored style OBJECT — renders `style="app-region: drag;"`, reads `drag` back from `getPropertyValue('-webkit-app-region')` and COMPUTES `drag`, because `serializeStyle` emits `key: value;` for every pair and Chromium's parser then canonicalises the vendor name to `app-region`; **(c)** the controls — `{color: 'rgb(1, 2, 3)'}` renders verbatim, and `{'-webkit-app-region': 'no-drag'}` and `{'app-region': 'no-drag'}` both render `app-region: no-drag;` and compute `no-drag`. **SO THE RUNTIME FILTERS NOTHING AND THE DRAG REGION IS AUTHORABLE DATA** — through the style OBJECT and never through a bare css key — the bar's `drag` and its interactive children's `no-drag` being declarable in the envelope. **WHAT THAT DECIDES**: "the runtime cannot carry it" is struck as an argument for the shell's page CSS. **WHAT IT DOES NOT**: who authors the bar — and a row that later asserts the RENDERED tag must assert `app-region: drag`, the spelling the attribute actually carries, while the authored object may carry either name.

**WHAT IT DOES NOT SETTLE.** The study's **decisions 2, 3 and 4**, each recorded here as OPEN and untouched by this row, which takes B alone and re-words none of them; and **the chrome-versus-data question** — whether the tab bar and its three controls are authored provident nodes, visible to `provident.dispatch`, `get_rendered_html` and `get_markdown` and therefore pressable by an agent, or the shell's own chrome, the one kind of element the UI-authority rule leaves outside the graph (`AGENTS.md` § "UI authority"). **THE TWO ARE ONE QUESTION FROM OPPOSITE ENDS** and neither is answered here: what this row fixes is that the bar EXISTS and is the window's top edge.

**WHAT IT COSTS, EACH AS MEASURED OR AS READ OFF THE TREE.** *(a) The page begins at `y=0`*: the content area gains the 28 pixels the title bar occupied (`980x692` → `980x720` on this host), so every y-origin moves and no box size does. *(b) The printed readings, checked rather than assumed*: the `ui` leg's `425x22` at `fontSize=16px` and the `panes` leg's `P-3` box are both BOX readings — a line box and a row box — and the same arms measure the probe's `948x22` at `(16,16)` identically framed and unframed, so both stand, as does the `ui` leg's overlay row, whose fragment sizes are the envelope's and not the viewport's; `D-120`'s measured `965x692` scrim over a `980x692` window becomes `965x720` over `980x720`, and its two screenshots are re-taken rather than byte-compared. *(c) A frameless window with no drag region yet cannot be MOVED*: the region and the framing must land in the same pass, or `frame: false` ships a window a user can never move. *(d) The map and the figures beside it*: a new `src/shared/window-channels.ts` joins the `app-shell` row's `paths` and moves that row's `endpoints` `7` → `8`, so the harness cap `strata-lint` prints for it reads `120` → `130`, while `mapRows` stays `21`; `pins`' `srcTypeScriptFiles` moves `50` → `51`, and `strata/brief.md` §2 carries that figure as prose.

**⟶ ONE FIGURE ABOVE IS CORRECTED BY POINTER, ITS SENTENCE LEFT AS WRITTEN (the idiom `D-6` carries and `D-46` names)**: `node scripts/strata-walkthrough.mjs pins` reads **`srcTypeScriptFiles: 51`** at this head, not the `50` this row took from `strata/brief.md` §2 — so `strata/brief.md` §2's figure was ALREADY one behind this tree when this row was written, and the new channels module moves it `51` → `52`; `mapRows` reads `21` as that row says. A reader takes the figure from the instrument, never from either prose (D-26, D-41).

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-120`**, as prose and never as a bare `D-n`: the change push returned to the record root; a repair's credit made explicit and its write made to name its commit; the boot battery's teardown made to prove zero survivors; the landing entry's id decided, its seat derived and that id moved; the renderer's tab client appearing in Stage A; the two-crossing ruling with its purge; the surplus instance collapsed onto the normal crossing with the `data.*` tools pinned to the central store; the module registry's legacy file dropped rather than migrated; the data-flow convention audited clause by clause; the imperative boot write deleted for a sixth declaration; the `mem` pair's existence declared at the store's creation; the absence-keyed import put inside the repair; `1.9.1`'s landing-arm save measured; the purge's two successor addresses declared; the renderer swap refused on measurement and then landed; the enumeration that stopped on the zone family; the zone family's own address; the frozen store made UNUSED and then DELETED; the two stale imports taken with the crossings given a leg a leg runs; the view record's crossing given a live leg with the pane read pair made total; the appearance mechanism given its consumer; the appearance family's tier made durable; the corpus made to tell the truth about itself; the archive made checkout-visible; the `data` tier made the caller's with a declared-root guard; the message log measured at last; the bump to `1.9.2` with the commit's log on the `data.*` answers; the app-state rows given an address; the bump to `1.9.3` with the value arms and the registration refusal discharged; the refusal ledger's `log` with the clause-2 cell re-read as `N/A`; the registration instant made structural; the five `data.*` routes answered; the appearance family made to produce its declaration in a repair; the overlay given the consumer it was owed; the instrument extended over the records; the handover's ceiling decided; the gate-independence rule adopted; the two leftovers taken with the live pane drag wired; the pane-drag demo rebuilt from source; the merge of `strata` into `main` on the user's permission; and the running window's three defects fixed each at its own home (the light arm pinned with a legible inherited foreground, the overlay's geometry moved into the authored `css`, and its visibility keyed on the mechanism's `inert` product). **The package-facing rows among them stay the package's** — `provident-storeroom` is read and never modified, its own corpus's decision ids are never spelled here, and a defect or a requirement gap of it belongs to the unit that surfaced it, with symptom, reproduction, suspected root cause and proposed fix shape (`D-5`, `D-52`).

## D-122 — THE USER SETTLES ALL FOUR: the tab bar is AUTHORED PROVIDENT DATA, the `menu-template` pane's open state BLOCKS THE PORTS with the agent locked out, the ZONE-AS-CONTAINER-FOR-REPOSITIONING use case is ARCHIVED and regenerable, and this template carries the RECORD AND THE REFERENCE while the app data display and its renderer are the APP'S OWN

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-05 (the host's own reading — `git log` dates the commit that carries `D-121` `2026-11-05 03:15 -0600` while that row's `Date:` cell reads `2026-11-07`; the cell stands as filed, `D-46`) · **Source (user, verbatim, four rulings in one answer):** **(1)** "provident" — the tab bar is **authored provident data**. **(2)** "menu-template (note: prevent the dispatch read by blocking the ports while the pane is open, the agent should be locked out while agent settings are configurable)". **(3)** "This is a covered use case already, regenerate from archives if it has been dropped. **Zones are containers for repositioning panes.**" **(4)** "Record that will be referenced by the app data display, which will use app-specific renderer". **This pass RESEARCHES, DESIGNS AND RECORDS and builds none of the four** — no tab bar, no menu, no layout — so no file under `src/` or `tests/` moves and `git status --short` is empty at it: `D-121`'s own shape and its own limit.

**WHAT EACH RULING SETTLES, AND ONLY THIS.** **(1)** THE TAB BAR'S AUTHORITY IS SETTLED: it is envelope data driven through the producing graph, so its nodes answer `provident.dispatch`, `get_rendered_html` and `get_markdown`; the chrome-versus-data question `D-121` recorded OPEN is answered on the DATA side for the bar, while the three window controls stay the shell's own — the one element class `AGENTS.md` § "UI authority" leaves outside the graph — and `D-121`'s measured drag-region recipe (the authored STYLE OBJECT `css: {style: {'-webkit-app-region': 'drag'}}` renders `style="app-region: drag;"` and computes `drag`, while a bare `css.<key>` renders an unknown ATTRIBUTE and computes `none`) is the authored form the bar's own drag region takes. **(2)** THE `menu-template` PANE'S LOCKOUT IS RULED AND ITS DESIGN IS THIS ROW'S OWN (§ the lockout below); the pane is where AGENT SETTINGS are configurable, and while it is open the ports are blocked. **(3)** THE ZONE FAMILY'S SEMANTIC IS NAMED — a zone is a CONTAINER FOR REPOSITIONING PANES, not a width, a track or a resize handle — and the archived use case stands (`D-2`: it is read, never re-pointed) or is regenerated by its own layer (§ regeneration below). **(4)** A SCOPE BOUNDARY IS DRAWN: this template EXPOSES THE RECORD AND THE REFERENCE and the app data display is APP-SPECIFIC, rendered by THAT APP'S OWN renderer; **THE TEMPLATE MUST NOT GROW A RENDERER OF ITS OWN** — `docs/L1/central-data-management.md` §6 `ASM-12` already leaves the rendered data pane owed to a later unit that "must owe provident-authored UI", and this ruling puts that unit in the APP, not here.

**THE ZONE USE CASE IS IN THE ARCHIVE — MEASURED OVER THE WHOLE RETIRED CORPUS, AND IT IS A MECHANISM PLUS A POLICY SHELL PLUS A NAME SET, NEVER ONE PAGE.** `grep -rn "reposition"` answers **ZERO** across `archive/`, `docs/`, `src/` and `strata/`, and so do "between zones", "one zone to another" and "moves the pane" — the corpus never spells the PROPOSITION the ruling states — but the thing the proposition is about is stated, in five places, and these are their operative clauses. *(a) A ZONE **IS** A CONTAINER AND MOVING A PANE BETWEEN ZONES IS THE MECHANISM'S OWN WRITE — `specs/slothost.md` §1: "**a host that maps caller-declared opaque keys to containers holding caller-created nodes, orders and attributes them by caller-supplied policy, owns exactly the nodes it placed, and refuses an undeclared key with a typed refusal instead of creating anything**", and its complete write list (§1 item 4, amended 2026-09-27): "it obtains one container per declared key from the factory; it projects those containers onto the injected `container`; **it places a caller-created node into that key's container; it MOVES A NODE BETWEEN TWO DECLARED CONTAINERS**; it removes a node it placed". The zones are the keys, the panes are the caller-created nodes, and the move is `setNode(newKey, node)` — reference-identical (`§3.1 M-5`/`M-7`, `I-3`), `order`/`setOrder` ordering the CONTAINERS by the caller's `orderOf` policy with **no ordering policy invented** (`M-4`, prohibition 3), and no ordering WITHIN a container existing anywhere in the corpus; `specs/slot-host-envelope-authored-container-source-review.md` §3.3 names the consumer outright — "**the fork's per-zone boxes must be supplied as nodes**, or this is not the right mechanism for that surface (V-5)" — and `FORKER.md` §4 carries the fork-facing form, "for the zone/sidebar case, the `owned-list-host` divergence: one `mount`, no per-key container — supply those boxes as **nodes**". *(b) THE DROP DECISION IS A POLICY SHELL OVER THAT MECHANISM — `specs/relocate.md`/`guide/relocate.md` (`U-RELOCATE`, composed on the frozen gesture session): the caller supplies `candidatesFor` (the candidate set), `resolveTarget`, `onReveal`, `commit` and `threshold`, and "**Two moves across two different zones read as a retarget — one presentation write carrying both the old and the new candidate, and still exactly one reveal at the terminal**". *(c) THE STORED NAMES ARE ENUMERATED AND `slot`/`distance` ARE THE LOCATION'S TWO HALVES — `specs/pane-drag-compliance.md` §2.4 names 3/4: `mem.layout.zone.<id>.slot` "OPAQUE — returned as-is, never interpreted by the store, never interpreted by the family's bytes" and `mem.layout.zone.<id>.distance` "a CALLER-MEASURED scalar — the caller measures it; the store merely stores what the caller wrote; **the family computes no distance**", both "each observed move", while name **7** `mem.layout.pane.<id>.location` is "the pane location member (queued contract clause 1: *"pane locations are STORED values"*) — read by the placement/resolve logic"; and `specs/data-ownership-model-plan.md`'s `ZQ-4` rules the referent: "**A "LOCATION" IS TWO CALLER VALUES — AN OPAQUE SLOT KEY AND A CALLER-MEASURED DISTANCE — NEVER A COORDINATE**". *(d) A MINIMIZED ZONE IS STILL A DROP TARGET — `decisions/ACTIVE-projection-and-store-facility.md`'s `ZONE-SIZE-DOMAIN-IS-CONSUMER-CARRIED-AND-THE-MINIMUM-CLAMP-IS-FAMILY-SIDE`, clause (3) quoting the architect *"The minimized zone should still broadcast a location for the proximity detection to expand back to its configured size and host the pane"*: "A MINIMIZED ZONE KEEPS ITS LOCATION. Minimizing changes the **extent**, never the **position** … **So a minimized member is a drop target that is reachable by proximity, not a member that has been removed**". *(e) THE PANES BELONG TO THE ZONE THEY STAND IN, AND THE GESTURE'S ENTRY AND TERMINAL STATES ARE THE SESSION'S — `guide/zones.md`: "a minimized zone **WITH panes retains a TAB-STRIP list** of its panes (no frames — the tabs are drag sources too); a pane drag **READS THE STORE's EXPANDED size** (`mem.layout.zone.<id>.size`; `canPlace = size >= min`) to decide placement … and **TEMPORARILY EXPANDS** a minimized target to host the ghost, restored on release/abandon"; `specs/live-demo-retrospective.md` D-15: "After committing a pane into the minimized zone, its frame lives in a `display:none` stack … The pane is represented by its TAB", with `T-12`'s row "a `.zone-tab` is a drag source carrying the pane id" driving "**I can move a pane out of its minimized zone by its tab**"; and `pending.md` §Q records "the pane-drag → zone compliance item **ADMITTED AND LANDED**". **AND THE CORPUS OWNS NO ZONE VOCABULARY IN ANY MECHANISM** — `H-r17`: "a dashboard/toolbar use case changes NO zone/track contract … **with no zone vocabulary anywhere**" — which is why `specs/zones.md` and `specs/census.md`, the two pages whose subject is the word "zone", say nothing about repositioning at all.

**WHETHER IT WAS DROPPED: THE READ SIDE SURVIVES, THE TWO HALVES THE RULING NAMES DO NOT.** Alive: `['zones']` at `mem` is a declared family under `zones-must-stand` beside the tab record's six and the module's two in the boot's ONE `createProfileStore` batch (nine declarations, one construction), with a route (`commitZones`), a client read (`zoneOf`) and a composition that READS it — `src/renderer/renderer.ts`'s `candidatesFor` answers "the candidate carries the zone's OPAQUE `slot` and its caller-measured `distance`" off `view.zoneOf(id)`, and its `renderZone` reads `zoneOf('zone-1')` for the layout call. **Dropped, each measured: (i) THE WRITE SIDE HAS NO CALLER** — `grep -rn "commitZones" src/` answers `15` hits and every one is a declaration, an implementation or the `IPC_VIEW_ZONES_COMMIT` handler, never a UI act, so nothing in the running app ever moves a pane and the container relation has no writer; **(i-bis) THE DROP HALF OF THE COMPOSITION IS UNREACHED** — `createPaneDrag` IS live (`const drag = createPaneDrag({ view: getViewClient() }, source)`) and the live gesture takes exactly three members off it, `drag.startSizeOf`, `drag.boundsOf` and `drag.release` (the RESIZE half), while `candidatesFor`, `renderZone`, `move`, `rightClick`, `cancel` and `previewOf` are returned at the surface's own object literal and **called by nothing in the tree** — the zone reads are wired and never invoked; **(ii) `mem.layout.pane.<id>.location` HAS NO SUCCESSOR** — `['panes']` carries `{ size, bounds }` and no location member (`docs/L2/view-record/interface.md` §2's address table), so the REPOSITION half went out with the frozen store; **(iii) THERE IS ONE ZONE ID AND NO MEMBERSHIP** — `renderZone` and `candidatesFor` both fall back to the literal `'zone-1'` and no authored node declares a zone (`src/shared/demo-envelope.ts` authors the pane `gutter-pane` and the word `zone` occurs `0` times in it), so no zone→panes relation exists anywhere in the tree; **(iv) THE DEMO IS A RESIZE DEMO** — `D-117` wired the live gesture onto `['panes']` and `D-118`'s rebuild drives one pane with `zoneOf` answering `null`. **`src/shared/zones.ts` IS NOT THE USE CASE AND NEVER WAS**: it is `U-ZONES`, "a **pure, total two-function mechanism**" (`isEmpty`, `trackFor`, `TrackSpec`), which "carries **no zone/pane/tab vocabulary**" and "ships **no CSS**" — the token-arithmetic half, consumed in-repo only by `census.ts`, itself imported by nothing — and `candidatesFor`'s `[{candidate: slot, distance}]` is `U-RELOCATE`'s `CandidateFor` ("the opaque candidate + its caller-supplied `distance`"), produced by the composition's closure, the pure `withinProximity` having no call site here (`D-111`). **THE LINEAGE, IN ONE LINE EACH WAY:** `SCH-4`/`SCH-7`/`SCH-9`/`SCH-10` → `U-ZONES`/`U-CENSUS`/`U-RELOCATE`/`U-SLOTHOST`/`U-PANE-DRAG-COMPLIANCE` → §Q's queued nine stored names → `D-87`/`D-91`'s `['zones']` → `D-92`'s wired reader → `D-93`'s deletion of the frozen store. **So the CONTAINER relation and the REPOSITION verb were never a landed contract in THIS tree: they were `slot-host`'s mechanism (a fork's per-zone boxes) and the demo's and the fork's own behaviour.**

**WHAT "REGENERATE" MEANS UNDER `D-2`, AND WHAT IT CANNOT MEAN.** `D-2`'s rule is that the archive **"is gitignored and is the retrieval mechanism"** and is cited by one prefix mapping — "`docs/<x>` resolves at `archive/gate-corpus-2026-10-29/<x>`" — and `D-52` refused to archive a unit's record because "archiving a unit's record would put it where `archive.md` §2 says nothing may be treated as authority". **So regeneration is a COPY FORWARD, never a re-adoption**: the archived pages stay exactly where they are and are READ and CITED BY THEIR ARCHIVE PATH; no live file may carry their bytes, and no live cite is re-pointed INTO them as authority. What `D-2`'s three bullets permit is the third alone — "`docs/` stays live and is where the layers write: `docs/L1/`, `docs/L2/<module>/`, `docs/L3/<module>/`" — plus the first, "a superseded document is **replaced**, never annotated". **The re-issued document is therefore a NEW `docs/L1/panes-layout.md`, written by the `panes-layout` row's L1 layer, under the class maximum every L1 scope in this tree declares (`docs/L1/central-data-management.md`'s header: "**Ceiling:** 150 lines (class max)"), stating the use case as THIS tree's own — a zone is a container holding panes, a reposition is one `setNode`-shaped move between two declared containers composed at the crossing, `slot` is the zone's opaque key and `distance` the caller's own measure, and the entry state is the session's `pointerdown` with the terminal a release, reset or right-click — with `docs/L2/panes-layout/interface.md` and `docs/L3/panes-layout/design.md` following at their own declared ceilings (`120` and `130` at the sibling `store-view-record` scope). `panes-layout` is the right row: `strata/modules.json` claims the four mechanisms there (`zones.ts`, `census.ts`, `layout-projection.ts`, `container.ts`, `endpoints: 10`, harness cap `150`), and `strata/modules.md` §3's "L1 files the map as its first act of a unit, and no design is admitted before it (D-7)" is what makes the L1 scope the first re-issued document. **AND NO LAYER HERE MAY WRITE IT**: `D-78`'s and `D-90`'s rule that no layer judges its own design makes the regeneration `panes-layout`'s own spec gate, and this row records the path, the layer and the ceiling rather than the act.

**THE LOCKOUT — WHAT EXISTS, MEASURED FIRST (this pass's own drives, throwaway probes bundled from `src/main/` and deleted, zero surviving processes).** *(i) HOW THE SERVER STARTS AND STOPS.* The transport is chosen once at the composition site — `--mcp-transport=` / `PROVIDENT_MCP_TRANSPORT`, `--mcp-port=` / `PROVIDENT_MCP_PORT`, `default port 3787` (`src/main/main.ts:66-87`) — and `start()` creates the listener: `this.httpServer = createServer((req, res) => { void this.handleHttp(req, res) })` then `await new Promise<void>((resolve) => this.httpServer!.listen(this.port, '127.0.0.1', () => resolve()))`; `close()` already exists and DOES stop it — `await Promise.allSettled([...this.httpServers].map((s) => s.close())); this.httpServers.clear(); … if (this.httpServer) { await new Promise<void>((resolve) => this.httpServer!.close(() => resolve())); this.httpServer = null }`. **DRIVEN: after `close()` a POST to `127.0.0.1:4791/mcp` answers `ECONNREFUSED`, and `start()` called again re-listens on the SAME port and serves — the pair is RE-ENTRANT, and a second `close()` closes again.** On `stdio` there is nothing to stop that way: `start()` builds one long-lived server and `await server.connect(new StdioServerTransport())`, and `close()` closes THAT server — which is the client's own pipe, not a port. *(ii) WHAT THE EXCLUSION ACTUALLY BLOCKS, DRIVEN BOTH WAYS.* The state is not an MCP tool pair: it is the secure crossing's `mem` record, written from the renderer's Settings surface over `IPC_SECURITY_EXCLUSION` ("`provident:security:exclusion`") into `settingsSurface.transition(state)`, delivered by the instance's one subscription and re-patched onto the gate by `applyExclusion`, whose enforcement is `exclusionTurn` at the ONE REGISTRATION BOUNDARY (`const guarded = <H …>(handler: H): H => (async (...a: never[]) => exclusionTurn(liveGate) ?? (await (handler …)(...a)))`) so every `registerTool` and every resource read is wrapped. **DRIVEN on the HTTP transport: with the gate at `'mcp-disabled'` ALL EIGHT probes — `initialize`, `tools/list`, `ping`, `tools/call provident.dispatch`, `tools/call provident.load`, `tools/call provident.data.read`, `resources/list`, `resources/read mcp://provident/app` — answer `http=503` with `{"jsonrpc":"2.0","error":{"code":-32003,"message":"exclusion-closed"},"id":null}`, and the backend's invocation counter reads `0` across the whole pass (it read `3` when enabled). DRIVEN on the STDIO transport: `ping`, `tools/list` and `resources/list` still ANSWER IN FULL — the tool and resource census is still legible — while every `tools/call` and the `resources/read` answer the refusal as an ordinary result, `{"status":"refused","reason":"exclusion-closed","message":"…"}`, and the backend's invocation counter holds at `4` before and after: **no call is refused at the transport and no invocation reaches the renderer.** So the exclusion covers EVERY METHOD's invocation, and on HTTP every METHOD at all; on stdio the protocol's own methods are untouched. *(iii) THE OVERLAY'S OPEN STATE REACHES NOTHING ON THIS PATH.* `D-113` measured it: "**with the root inert, a dispatch on the `inc` control UNDER it still runs and still takes the counter to `1`, and the word `inert` appears NOWHERE in `src/renderer/runtime.ts` or `src/main/mcp-server.ts`, so no code on that path could consult it**" — so `START_OVERLAY_APPLICATION`'s `inert` product is a BROWSER block and the ports (or the exclusion) are the only real wall.

**THE THREE CANDIDATE MECHANISMS, WITH THEIR COSTS, AND THE ONE THE EVIDENCE SUPPORTS.** **(a) CLOSE THE HTTP LISTENER while the pane is open and re-open on close.** Blocks everything at the connection — measured `ECONNREFUSED` — and leaks nothing over the port; an IN-FLIGHT POST is not abandoned, `httpServer.close()` stops accepting and waits for the open connections to drain, so a straddling request completes under whatever state it entered; the STDIO ARM HAS NO PORT AT ALL and forcing this there is measured FATAL — after `server.close()` on stdio the client's next `ping` never returns and never throws (it hung for the whole 25 s window) while `main.ts`'s own stdin handler is `void mcp.close().finally(() => app.exit(0))`, i.e. it exits the app; and the CLIENT SEES a dead socket, which cannot be told from a quit app — the prebuild baseline's own debugging-exposure goal pays for that. **(b) REFUSE AT THE TRANSPORT** with a gate answering every request with a refusal: this is ALREADY the landed shape on HTTP (`503 -32003` before the body is read, measured) and needs no new mechanism; it leaks the STATE, which `specs/secure-exclusion.md` `§0A` item 7(b) DECLARES rather than hides ("an AUTHORIZED caller NOW learns the state FROM THE MESSAGE … and an UNAUTHORIZED caller still cannot"); an in-flight request is refused by `exclusionTurn`; stdio works with no transport change; the client sees a machine-readable refusal, never a throw. **(c) SET THE EXCLUSION AND WIDEN IT TO COVER EVERY METHOD.** Blocks every invocation on both transports (measured, `0` renderer invocations) and on HTTP every method at arrival; it can leak the tool/resource NAMES on stdio (measured); in-flight work is invalidated whole by `backend.abandonPendingForExclusion(EXCLUSION_CLOSED)`, which "rejects EVERY pending entry and returns the count (so a call dispatched under the old state can never deliver a renderer value after the transition)"; stdio is untouched and re-armable; the client sees the declared refusal with its cause and remedy. **WHAT IS REFUSED, BY NAME**: **(b) as a NEW mechanism** — it would duplicate `exclusionTurn`, which `specs/secure-exclusion.md` `§2.3` item 2 already pins at the landed 401-then-503 order; **(a) ON STDIO** — measured fatal (the request hangs, or the app exits), and the corpus already refuses it for the exclusion: "the exclusion does NOT close the stdio transport: closing it would destroy the operator's ability to re-enable (the session is spawn-local) and would make the disabled state observable as a disconnect"; **(a) ALONE** — a socket state machine is not an invariant: it fails open the moment the closing edge is skipped, and it says nothing about a method that never needed the port.

**THE DESIGN CHOSEN, IN PROSE AND NOT IN CODE.** **THE TRIGGER IS ONE AUTHORED STATE AND THERE IS NO SECOND PATH.** `src/shared/overlay.ts`'s machine already answers `closed`/`open`/`held`/`closing`, and `startOverlayApplication`'s `apply()` already computes the one predicate the inert declaration rides — `state === 'open' || state === 'held'` — so the lockout's edge is that same predicate, evaluated at that same site, and it drives the EXISTING `security.exclusion` verb over the EXISTING `IPC_SECURITY_EXCLUSION` channel: one transition site, one channel, no new vocabulary, no second writer (`D-112`'s one-site-per-channel rule). **THE EXCLUSION IS SET FIRST AND THE PORT DROPS SECOND, IN THAT ORDER AND IN THE SAME TURN**, because the exclusion is the invariant that survives a failed close and the port is the belt: the handler's answer already carries `{applied, state}`, so the port arm is a CONSEQUENCE of the state the transition answered, run after it, and its own failure is recorded as a VALUE on that answer and never thrown into the IPC reply. **THE BLOCK IS BOTH, AND NEITHER IS A WIDENING**: every method already refuses under the exclusion (measured), so nothing is added there; the port close adds only the connection-level refusal on `http` — and it is skipped entirely on `stdio`, where the exclusion alone is the whole block and the transport is never closed. **IN-FLIGHT**: the transition's epoch bump runs `abandonPendingForExclusion`, which rejects every pending renderer entry with the one channel token; a POST already accepted by the listener is not killed mid-body (`httpServer.close()` drains it), and its own turn then refuses at the registration boundary if it has not yet dispatched — measured, nothing reaches the renderer either way. **THE STDIO ARM** is therefore the exclusion alone: the transport stays CONNECTED and re-armable, the refusal is a result value, and the census (`tools/list`) stays legible — a stated cost, not an oversight. **THE RE-OPEN PATH** is the mirror edge: the overlay leaving `open`/`held` sets `'mcp-enabled'` through the same channel and re-listens on the SAME port with the SAME `start()`, which is measured re-entrant; the re-open is attempted on EVERY closing edge, not only the first, so a dropped edge heals at the next one, and a boot always calls `start()` so a restart heals it absolutely. **AND THE FAILURE MODE IS NAMED, BOTH WAYS, WITH NO CLAIM BEYOND THE MEASUREMENT.** *The app crashes with the pane open and the port closed*: the kernel releases the socket, so no stale block survives, and the next boot's `start()` binds normally — but the EXCLUSION is not persisted either (`D-19`: "the flag is NOT persisted"), so that boot comes up `mcp-enabled` and re-admits the agent; **the port block inherits exactly that property and no stronger one**, which is the safe-by-availability direction and the unsafe-by-confidentiality one, and it is stated rather than mitigated here. *The re-open throws* (a foreign process took 3787, or a TIME_WAIT collision): the exclusion is still `'mcp-disabled'`, so the agent is still locked out — the failure is in the wrong direction for usability and the right one for the ruling — and it must be answered as a recorded value and retried on the next edge and at the next boot, never swallowed. **NO BINDER AND NO IPC ROUTE IS BUILT BY THIS ROW** (the act is a landing pass's), and no `src/` or `tests/` byte moves for it.

**⟶ ITS LOCKOUT DESIGN IS SUPERSEDED BY POINTER AND NOT ONE WORD OF THIS ROW IS REWORDED (`D-46`).** The user's instruction of 2026-11-05 replaced the sequence with an INVARIANT at `D-123`: the pane's open state is the secure crossing's fourth `mem` address, one declaration at the store's root refuses `paneOpen && portsOpen` whatever route reaches it, its repair settles the ports, and the socket follows the exclusion's delivery (`http` only, `stdio` never). The imperative edges this row designed — the predicate driving the transition, the exclusion-first ordering, and the mirror re-open — are READ THROUGH that row; the one piece of the design that row does NOT build is the auto re-enable, because an invariant is not a toggle.

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-121`**, as prose and never as a bare `D-n`: the change push returned to the record root; a repair's credit made explicit and its write made to name its commit; the boot battery's teardown made to prove zero survivors; the landing entry's id decided, its seat derived and that id moved; the renderer's tab client appearing in Stage A; the two-crossing ruling with its purge; the surplus instance collapsed onto the normal crossing with the `data.*` tools pinned to the central store; the module registry's legacy file dropped rather than migrated; the data-flow convention audited clause by clause; the imperative boot write deleted for a sixth declaration; the `mem` pair's existence declared at the store's creation; the absence-keyed import put inside the repair; `1.9.1`'s landing-arm save measured; the purge's two successor addresses declared; the renderer swap refused on measurement and then landed; the enumeration that stopped on the zone family; the zone family's own address; the frozen store made UNUSED and then DELETED; the two stale imports taken with the crossings given a leg a leg runs; the view record's crossing given a live leg with the pane read pair made total; the appearance mechanism given its consumer; the appearance family's tier made durable; the corpus made to tell the truth about itself; the archive made checkout-visible; the `data` tier made the caller's with a declared-root guard; the message log measured at last; the bump to `1.9.2` with the commit's log on the `data.*` answers; the app-state rows given an address; the bump to `1.9.3` with the value arms and the registration refusal discharged; the refusal ledger's `log` with the clause-2 cell re-read as `N/A`; the registration instant made structural; the five `data.*` routes answered; the appearance family made to produce its declaration in a repair; the overlay given the consumer it was owed; the instrument extended over the records; the handover's ceiling decided; the gate-independence rule adopted; the two leftovers taken with the live pane drag wired; the pane-drag demo rebuilt from source; the merge of `strata` into `main` on the user's permission; the running window's three defects fixed each at its own home; and the window made frameless with its controls authored into the tab bar. **The package-facing rows among them stay the package's** — `provident-storeroom` is read and never modified, its own corpus's decision ids are never spelled here, and a defect or a requirement gap of it belongs to the unit that surfaced it, with symptom, reproduction, suspected root cause and proposed fix shape (`D-5`, `D-52`).
