## D-123 — THE INVARIANT REPLACES `D-122`'S SEQUENCE: the security pane and the MCP ports cannot both be open, and the STORE refuses to hold the pair — one declaration at the store's root judges two addresses, its repair settles the PORTS, and the socket follows the exclusion's delivery on `http` and is never touched on `stdio`

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-05 · **Source (user, verbatim):** *"Use the constraint engine in the storeroom / Enforce that the security pane cannot be open while the MCP ports are open"*; plus the measurements below, all driven 2026-11-05 on this head over the installed `provident-storeroom@1.9.3`.

**THE GROUND, MEASURED BEFORE ANYTHING WAS WRITTEN.** *(i) The secure crossing's families and their repairs.* `declarationsFor` rides ONE `storeCreate` with three pairs — `['operator-settings']` `file` (`operator-record-must-stand`, repair `mintRecord` = the import performed and `toStored(imported ?? FIRST_RUN)` written whole), `['operator-app-state']` `file` (`operator-app-state-must-stand`, repair `mintAppState` = the import and `{rows, legacy, unrepresentable}`), `['operator-exclusion']` `mem` (`operator-exclusion-must-stand`, repair `mintExclusion` = `SAFE_PAIR.exclusion` whole, because a `mem` address can never come from a file) — and the first caller-visible receipt was `cursor: 4`, one op per repair. *(ii) The exclusion's shape and delivery.* `{exclusion:'mcp-enabled'|'mcp-disabled'}` stored, `tier4` derived at the read; written by `write`'s exclusion arm over `IPC_SECURITY_EXCLUSION` → `settingsSurface.transition` → one `mem` write; delivered by the instance's one subscription to `settings-surface`'s `onDelivery`, whose exclusion branch patches the gate (`applyExclusion`, epoch bump + `abandonPendingForExclusion`) and, since `D-117`, writes no holder at all. *(iii) The overlay's state.* `startOverlayApplication` holds `closed`/`open`/`held`/`closing` in closure, seeded from the AUTHORED carrier (`OVERLAY_STATE_ID`), driven by the authored verb token (`overlayVerbToken`), and its `apply()` computes `const up = state === 'open' || state === 'held'` — the predicate the `inert` declaration rides. *(iv) The MCP lifecycle.* `start()` creates the listener and `close()` stops it, measured RE-ENTRANT at `D-122` (`ECONNREFUSED` after close, `start()` re-listens on the SAME port); on `stdio` `close()` closes the CLIENT'S PIPE and the next `ping` never returns — measured fatal there. *(v) A new family's write.* The renderer's `apply()` turn is the user's act (clause 4), and nothing in the tree could write a pane's state: no channel, no verb, no address.

**THE DESIGN, AND WHY IT BEATS `D-122`'S SEQUENCE.** `D-122` made the lockout a PROCEDURE — a renderer predicate set `security.exclusion`, the port dropped as a consequence of that call — and said at its own bytes that "(a) ALONE — a socket state machine is not an invariant: it fails open the moment the closing edge is skipped". **THE INVARIANT IS NOW THE STORE'S**: the pane's open state is the crossing's FOURTH address, `['operator-pane']` on `mem`, shape `{open: boolean}` — **`mem` because a pane is not a durable fact** (the four-state machine is the renderer's and is re-seeded from the authored carrier at every boot; a persisted `open` would be a second authority over a surface that no longer exists) — and ONE declaration, `no-open-pane-over-open-ports`, refuses a store holding `paneOpen && portsOpen` whatever route reached it. **IT STANDS AT THE STORE'S ROOT `[]`, AND THAT IS THE MEASUREMENT THAT DECIDES THE PASS**: a pair is asked only where an act touches its address (`guide §7.6`; `D-56`/`D-66`), so a pair at either half's address is asked for that half's landing op and NOT for the other's (measured, both ways) — at `[]` every act of this instance is at or below the pair, so ONE body catches BOTH edges. **ITS REPAIR SETTLES THE PORTS AND NEVER THE PANE** — closing the ports is the safe direction, the pane is what the operator is doing, and it is expressible: the crossing's answer is `{applied, state}` and the realm paints it — through ONE function, `writeExclusion`, which routes the registration instant through the handed `write` and the landing instant through `store.write(address,'mem',value,context)` — **supplying `write`'s FOURTH position, which this module supplied nowhere until this pass** (`guide §7.6`'s worked example; `D-103`: a repair that omits the commit is unsupported). **AND THE SOCKET IS NOT A CONSTRAINT'S ACT**: a repair writes a value and returns the op it landed, so the port is a LISTENER'S consequence (`D-82` clause 2) — the exclusion's delivery reaches a third callback, `onExclusionChanged`, and `ProvidentMcpServer.followExclusion` reconciles an `http` listener (`close()`/`start()`, idempotent, serialised) and returns immediately on `stdio`, where the transport is the client's pipe. **WHY IT CANNOT BE ONE REPAIR THAT REACHES A SOCKET, AND WHY THAT IS NOT A DEFECT**: the record is under the constraint engine's restart budget and applied-repair marker and the socket under none (`D-70`); so the design is a CONSTRAINT for the record and a LISTENER for the socket, each measured where it lives.

**THE BUILD, AND WHAT MOVED.** `writePane(open)` is the fourth address's one verb (one `mem` write of `{open}`, the sibling receipt; a non-boolean refused as a value before any store verb); the surface gains `pane(open)` and the callback; `IPC_SECURITY_PANE` is the channel (`store-channels.ts`'s census `1 → 2`); the preload and `secure-panels.ts` gain `setPane` in lockstep (the `security` member set `4 → 5`); the renderer's `overlayApplication.apply()` reports `up` on a MOVE over that member; and `main.ts` supplies `onExclusionChanged: (state) => { mcp.followExclusion(state) }` beside the pane's handler. **THE PANE'S BODY READS ITS OWN ADDRESS AT A LANDING INSTANT**, and that is measured rather than chosen: a re-run hands every pair the value the repair's op LANDED, so a body trusting `context.value` refused its own repair and the write crossed `ConstraintPassRefused` as *"violated a re-run"*.

**THE ROWS, AND WHAT EACH REVERT PRODUCES — MEASURED, EACH ON THE REAL SUITES.** `CD-13` EDGE ONE (the pane opens and the pair closes in the SAME write), `CD-14` EDGE TWO (an enable under an open pane still reads `mcp-disabled`), `CD-15` WHICH HALF (the pane's act stands, the enable is the discriminator that proves the pane was never settled, and an ordinary exclusion change with the pane closed repairs nothing — the control), `CD-16` ONE BODY AT BOTH LANDING ADDRESSES, `CD-17` the pane family's two arms; `PF-1` the HTTP listener's two directions, `PF-2` the stdio arm untouched, `PF-3` the follow's idempotence and serial order, `PF-4` the delivery that drives it. **Reversion 1 — the invariant's declaration removed from the batch: `8` rows red** (`CD-1`, `CD-3`, `CD-6`, `CD-13`, `CD-14`, `CD-15`, `CD-16`, `PF-4`) and green restored. **Reversion 2 — `followExclusion`'s `close()`/`start()` removed: `PF-1` and `PF-3` red** (`PF-1`: `expected true to be false` — the port still answers). **Reversion 3 — the `transport !== 'http'` guard removed: `PF-2` red** (`sent` froze at `1`, so the client's own pipes stopped carrying). **Reversion 4 — the pane's existence pair removed: `6` red** (`CD-1`, `CD-3`, `CD-5`, `CD-6`, `CD-10`, `CD-17`). **Reversion 5 — `onExclusionChanged` not called: `PF-4` red. Reversion 6 — the exclusion's existence pair removed (the control's own subject): `6` red** (`CD-1`…`CD-6`).

**THE MEASUREMENTS.** Four legs `0/0/0/0`: `npm test` **`2,975 passed / 2 skipped` of `2,977` over `135` files** (nine rows and one file more than `D-122`'s `2,966` of `2,968` over `134`), `typecheck`, `typecheck:tests`, `build`. Five Electron legs, `DISPLAY=:0`, a process census around each with zero survivors: `battery` **`184/0`**, `divergence` **`9+4/0`**, `ui` **`12/12` at `425x22`**, `panes` **`7/7`** (`22` calls, `2` gestures), `crossings` **`31/31`**. **AND THE INVARIANT DRIVEN AT THE PROCESS BOUNDARY, ON `http`**: a live boot answers `tools/list` `200` and `ping` `200`; `provident.dispatch` on the authored `overlay-open` opens the pane, the crossing's constraint closes the pair inside that write, and **every later POST answers `ECONNREFUSED`** — `tools/list`, `ping` and `tools/call` alike, the opening call's own reply being the `exclusion-closed` token because the gate's epoch abandoned it. **`ui`'s `OV-1` IS RE-DERIVED BY THAT READING**: on `stdio` the transport survives and every tool is refused, so the OPEN fragment is not agent-visible by design and the `inert` landing is measured where it is computable (`tests/integration/overlay-application.test.ts` `OV-3`/`OV-9`, `divergence`'s two realms); the leg now drives a THIRD boot whose only act is this one, and its `12/12` stands. **`strata/modules.json` moves two `endpoints` (`12 → 13`, `19 → 21`) and their caps are derived, never restated.** **WHAT `D-122`'S LOCKOUT DESIGN BECOMES: SUPERSEDED BY POINTER AND NEVER REWORDED (`D-46`)** — its imperative sequence, its `exclusion`-first ordering and its mirror re-open edge are read through this row; the one thing it designed that this row does NOT build is the AUTO RE-ENABLE (`the overlay leaving open/held sets 'mcp-enabled'`), because an invariant is not a toggle: closing a pane leaves the ports closed, and re-opening them is the operator's own exclusion act.

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-122`**, as prose and never as a bare `D-n`: the change push returned to the record root; a repair's credit made explicit and its write made to name its commit; the boot battery's teardown made to prove zero survivors; the landing entry's id decided, its seat derived and that id moved; the renderer's tab client appearing in Stage A; the two-crossing ruling with its purge; the surplus instance collapsed onto the normal crossing with the `data.*` tools pinned to the central store; the module registry's legacy file dropped rather than migrated; the data-flow convention audited clause by clause; the imperative boot write deleted for a sixth declaration; the `mem` pair's existence declared at the store's creation; the absence-keyed import put inside the repair; `1.9.1`'s landing-arm save measured; the purge's two successor addresses declared; the renderer swap refused on measurement and then landed; the enumeration that stopped on the zone family; the zone family's own address; the frozen store made unused and then deleted; the two stale imports taken with the crossings given a leg a leg runs; the view record's crossing given a live leg with the pane read pair made total; the appearance mechanism given its consumer; the appearance family's tier made durable; the corpus made to tell the truth about itself; the archive made checkout-visible; the `data` tier made the caller's with a declared-root guard; the message log measured at last; the bump to `1.9.2` with the commit's log on the `data.*` answers; the app-state rows given an address; the bump to `1.9.3` with the value arms and the registration refusal discharged; the refusal ledger's `log` with the clause-2 cell re-read as `N/A`; the registration instant made structural; the five `data.*` routes answered; the appearance family made to produce its declaration in a repair; the overlay given the consumer it was owed; the instrument extended over the records; the handover's ceiling decided; the tab bar authored and the window controls taken into it; and the three defects the running window measured, each fixed at its own home. **The package-facing rows among them stay the package's** — `provident-storeroom` is read and never modified.

**⟶ ITS ROOT-ADDRESS REASONING IS SUPERSEDED BY POINTER AND NOT ONE WORD OF THIS ROW IS REWORDED (`D-46`)**: the premise that *"a pair at either half's address is asked for that half's landing op only"* is TRUE of the ASKING and was read as true of what a body can SEE — a constraint body READS the other half through the handle (`guide §7.6`), so `D-124` declares this same invariant at EACH half's own address and the pair at `[]` is gone. **THE INVARIANT THIS ROW ENFORCED STILL HOLDS AND `D-124` RE-MEASURES IT AT BOTH EDGES**: what is superseded is the ADDRESS and the COUPLING it cost, never the rule.

## D-124 — THE INVARIANT'S ROOT PAIR WAS COMPENSATING FOR NOTHING: a constraint body READS the half its own address does not carry, so the ONE pair at the store's root `[]` becomes TWO — one at `['operator-pane']` and one at `['operator-exclusion']` — each judging its own half and READING the other, the repair still settling the PORTS, and NO BEHAVIOUR CHANGING: this is a COUPLING fix and not a defect repair

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-05 · **Source (user, verbatim):** *"You can call read() from inside of a constraint body, you don't need direct visibility"* — the correction of `D-123`, and every figure below is driven 2026-11-05 over the installed `provident-storeroom@1.9.3` through this module's own batch.

**WHAT THE PREMISE GOT WRONG, NAMED.** `D-123` put ONE pair at the store's root `[]` because it held that *"a pair at either half's address is asked for that half's landing op only, so only the root catches both edges with one body."* The FIRST clause is true and measured (`guide §7.6`'s membership rule; `D-56`/`D-66`); the SECOND is the error — a body asked for its own half can READ the other half, so "only the root" was false. **So the root pair was an UNNECESSARY COUPLING**: asked on EVERY write to the whole store, and existing solely to compensate for a visibility limitation that does not exist.

**THE CAPABILITY, MEASURED.** *(i) AT A LANDING INSTANT* a body's `store.read(<other address>, 'mem')` answers the other half — the pane's seat read `{exclusion:'mcp-disabled'}` while the act was the pane's own. *(ii) IT OPENS NO ATTEMPT*: the count at the body's entry and after its read are the SAME (`1`, the write's own), `attempt-opened` fires `0` times for it, and `0` listener deliveries cross a read of a listened address — *"a read inside a body runs INSIDE the attempt's window, opens nothing, and disturbs nothing"* (`guide §7.6`, measured there 2026-10-28 and re-measured here). *(iii) AT THE REGISTRATION INSTANT* the handle does not exist (`standing()` is `null` until `storeCreate` returns, and still `null` on that instant's own re-run though its `tier` IS set), so a body reads NOTHING there; what it does read is `context.value`, which at that instant IS the value at ITS OWN address — so the split is OWN half from `context.value` where no handle stands, BOTH halves from the store where one does. *(iv) AFTER A REPAIR LANDS* the re-run hands a pair `{address: <the pair's own address>, value: <the repair's last landing>}` — measured: a pane-address pair was handed `{exclusion:'mcp-disabled'}` — so the address and the value DISAGREE and **a body READS rather than trusting `context['value']`**.

**THE TRIGGER ANSWER, MEASURED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS — AND IT IS TWO PAIRS.** This module's batch, adopted by a real store with every constraint wrapped in a counter: `write(['operator-exclusion'])` asked `["operator-exclusion-must-stand"]` before and `["operator-exclusion-must-stand","no-open-ports-under-an-open-pane"]` after, and the PANE's seat was in NEITHER; `write(['operator-pane'])` asked `["operator-pane-must-stand"]` / `["operator-pane-must-stand","no-open-pane-over-open-ports"]`, and the PORTS' seat was in neither; each record's own `triggered` set carried ONE address (`[["operator-exclusion"],…]`, `[["operator-pane"],…]`); and a control write at a third address asked nothing of either. **SIBLINGS DO NOT TRIGGER**, so the honest shape is TWO pairs and the `[]` pair — the only one asked for both — is REMOVED.

**THE SHAPE NOW, AND THE CLAUSE THAT LICENSES THE READ.** SIX declarations in ONE `createProfileStore` call (measured: one construction hands the package exactly ONE configuration): the four existence pairs, then `no-open-pane-over-open-ports` at `['operator-pane']` and `no-open-ports-under-an-open-pane` at `['operator-exclusion']` — two ids, because a batch may not name one handle twice and the construction refuses it (measured). Each seat judges the half its own address carries and reads the other; each refuses when both stand open and repairs by settling **the PORTS**, unchanged — `D-123`'s direction, through the module's one repair-write path `writeExclusion`, the credited arm, so `D-72`/`D-73` are intact. **THE LICENSING CLAUSE IS `guide §7.6`'s OWN, IN ITS OWN TERMS:** *"If a constraint needs to look for the data at a given location, it can read()"* (`D-50`), because *"the body is your code, it closes over the store handle you already hold, and `store.read(address)` is a published verb."* **AND THE READ IS NOT A WRITE PATH, NOT A DELIVERY AND NOT A CHECK**: it writes no byte, fires no listener, runs no pass and is no verdict — and it is taken ONLY where `context['value']` cannot answer, which at a registration instant is nowhere.

**NO BEHAVIOUR CHANGED, AND THAT IS THIS ROW'S CLAIM RATHER THAN ITS AFTERTHOUGHT.** The invariant held at BOTH edges before this pass and holds after it: the pane cannot be opened over open ports, the ports cannot be enabled under an open pane, the repair settles the ports and leaves the pane alone, and the socket still follows the exclusion's delivery on `http` while `stdio` is untouched. **What moved is the ADDRESS and the COUPLING it was paying for.**

**THE ROWS, AND WHAT EACH REVERT PRODUCES — MEASURED, ON THE REAL SUITES.** `CD-16` IS RE-DERIVED AND ITS ROOT-ADDRESS CENSUS INVERTED (`D-46`): it asserted the invariant stands at `[]` and now measures that NO batch element stands at the root, that each seat stands at its own half, the two `triggered` lists above, ONE configuration per construction, and both edges through the module's verbs. Two rows are NEW: `CD-18` (each seat reads both halves and follows the store, driven with a value at the seat's own address that the store does not hold — a trusting body faults there and this one passes) and `CD-19` (one act, one repair: a repaired act lands EXACTLY two ops and delivers on the two handles its writes fired, an ordinary act one, and ten alternating faulting acts all commit so the restart budget is never reached). `VA-14`'s census moves `5 → 6` / `15 → 16` and now DRIVES the pane family's existence pair, which it declared for a pass and never drove. **Reversion 1 — BOTH seats removed: `11` rows red over `3` files** (`CD-1`, `CD-3`, `CD-6`, `CD-13`…`CD-16`, `CD-18`, `CD-19`, `PF-4`, `VA-14`). **Reversion 2 — the PANE's seat removed: `9` red, `CD-13` among them** (`CD-1`, `CD-3`, `CD-6`, `CD-16`, `CD-18`, `CD-19`, `PF-4`, `VA-14`), while EDGE TWO STILL HOLDS. **Reversion 3 — the PORTS' seat removed: `9` red, `CD-14` among them** (`CD-1`, `CD-3`, `CD-6`, `CD-15`, `CD-16`, `CD-18`, `CD-19`, `VA-14`), while EDGE ONE STILL HOLDS — so BOTH seats are load-bearing and neither is the other's understudy. **Reversion 4 — the PANE half taken from `context['value']` instead of read: `CD-14`, `CD-15`, `CD-16`, `CD-18`, `CD-19` red**, because the ports' seat then cannot see the pane at all — and **`CD-13` stays GREEN there, which is the whole of `D-123`'s error in one figure**: an absent or mismatched port half is judged as OPEN by the predicate, so the pane edge looked caught while the same fault was hiding. **Reversion 5 — the PORTS half taken from `context['value']`: `0` rows red, MEASURED AND STATED RATHER THAN CLAIMED AWAY** — at every landing instant this instance reaches, the value at a seat's own address IS the ports half (the pane's act carries the pane; a re-run carries the repair's landing, whose `exclusion` word is the ports' own), so no act distinguishes that read; it is kept because a body must not depend on a value the pass may substitute, and the absence of a red is the honest figure.

**THE MEASUREMENTS.** Four legs `0/0/0/0`: `npm test` **`2,977 passed / 2 skipped` of `2,979` over `135` files** (TWO rows more than `D-123`'s `2,975` of `2,977` over `135`), `typecheck`, `typecheck:tests`, `build`. Five Electron legs, `DISPLAY=:0`, a process census around each with zero survivors: `battery` **`184/0`**, `divergence` **`9+4/0`**, `ui` **`12/12` at `425x22`**, `panes` **`7/7`** (`22` calls, `2` gestures), `crossings` **`31/31`**. **AND THE INVARIANT AT THE PROCESS BOUNDARY ON `http`, RE-TAKEN AT THIS HEAD**: a live boot answers `tools/list` `200`; `provident.dispatch` on the authored `overlay-open` opens the pane and the crossing's constraint closes the pair inside that write, its own reply arriving as a `200` with NO BODY because the gate's epoch abandoned the pending entry (the SDK client reports it as `-32001`); and every later POST answers at the socket, `UND_ERR_SOCKET` once and then `ECONNREFUSED`, `tools/list` and `ping` alike. `ui`'s `OV-1` stands unchanged on `stdio`, where the transport survives and every tool is refused.

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-123`**, as prose and never as a bare `D-n`: the change push returned to the record root; a repair's credit made explicit and its write made to name its commit; the boot battery's teardown made to prove zero survivors; the landing entry's id decided, its seat derived and that id moved; the renderer's tab client appearing in Stage A; the two-crossing ruling with its purge; the surplus instance collapsed onto the normal crossing with the `data.*` tools pinned to the central store; the module registry's legacy file dropped rather than migrated; the data-flow convention audited clause by clause; the imperative boot write deleted for a sixth declaration; the `mem` pair's existence declared at the store's creation; the absence-keyed import put inside the repair; `1.9.1`'s landing-arm save measured; the purge's two successor addresses declared; the renderer swap refused on measurement and then landed; the enumeration that stopped on the zone family; the zone family's own address; the frozen store made unused and then deleted; the two stale imports taken with the crossings given a leg a leg runs; the view record's crossing given a live leg with the pane read pair made total; the appearance mechanism given its consumer; the appearance family's tier made durable; the corpus made to tell the truth about itself; the archive made checkout-visible; the `data` tier made the caller's with a declared-root guard; the message log measured at last; the bump to `1.9.2` with the commit's log on the `data.*` answers; the app-state rows given an address; the bump to `1.9.3` with the value arms and the registration refusal discharged; the refusal ledger's `log` with the clause-2 cell re-read as `N/A`; the registration instant made structural; the five `data.*` routes answered; the appearance family made to produce its declaration in a repair; and the security pane's open state made the secure crossing's FOURTH address with one invariant over two of them declared at the store's root; **and then that root pair taken away again for the read a body could always take, the invariant declared at each half it judges**.

## D-125 — THE STORE IS THE STATE MACHINE AND THE RENDERED PANE IS A LISTENER'S PRODUCT: the operator's act writes `['operator-pane']` directly, the constraint's repair settles the ports and the panel carries the intent it preserved, the renderer renders the store's DELIVERY — and the pair gains `wanted` so the close edge has an intent to read, which is the datum `D-123`'s repair was destroying

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-05 · **Source (user, verbatim):** *"Following the pattern of the renderer listening to the stores, the user action to open the security pane should directly flip the state in the store. This will then cascade into the repair, then fire the listeners that render the pane and close the MCP. On close, it then deactivates the security pane, the constraint checks if the MCP settings are enabled, and then re-opens the MCP ports if so. Listeners then fire to open the ports and close the rendered pane. This ensures at the state machine level that the rendered-pane and MCP-open states are incompatible."*

**WHAT THE PREVIOUS DIRECTION GOT WRONG, AND IT IS A DIRECTION AND NOT A DEFECT.** `D-124` had `startOverlayApplication` hold the four-state machine in closure, seeded from the authored carrier, moving on the authored verb token, and REPORTING the predicate it computed over `IPC_SECURITY_PANE` — so **the renderer was the machine and the store followed it**, and the incompatibility was enforced by a SEQUENCE (the wiring's own transition, then its report, then the crossing's repair) rather than by the state machine the store holds. A sequence fails open the moment a turn is skipped, and it leaves the recorded state a projection of a local one. **THIS ROW INVERTS IT.**

**THE INTENT QUESTION, ASKED BEFORE ANY DESIGN AND ANSWERED BY MEASUREMENT.** *(i) `['operator-exclusion']` carried `{exclusion}` ALONE* — `tier4` is DERIVED at the read (`tier4 === (exclusion === 'mcp-disabled')`) and is refused by the value arm as a member, so nothing but the two words was ever stored (measured: `exclusionAdmitsAt`'s closed member set; `CD-1`/`CD-2`'s `Object.keys` reading). *Its writers were two*: `IPC_SECURITY_EXCLUSION` → `settingsSurface.transition` → `settings.write`, and the invariant's repair `writeExclusion(…, PORTS_CLOSED)` — **and that second writer OVERWROTE the first**, so after a pane opened, *"were the ports supposed to be open?"* was unanswerable: the only datum that could have said so had been replaced by the word the repair settled to. *Its readers* are the gate (`applyExclusion`), `followExclusion` and the seats themselves. *(ii) `['operator-settings']` carries `{token, enabled, maxJournalLength, entries}`, and its `enabled` IS THE CAPABILITY GROUPS, MEASURED AND NOT ASSUMED*: `VALID_GROUPS` is `read`/`dispatch`/`graph`/`code`/`module`/`data`, `admitGroups` filters to exactly those, and `patchOf` hands the list to the gate as `groups` — so *"the MCP settings are enabled"* cannot mean that member. *(iii) NO DESIRED/EFFECTIVE SPLIT EXISTED ANYWHERE*: `grep` answers no `desired`/`intent`/`effective`/`requested` field in `src/`, and the gate has one state and one reader. **SO THE CLOSE PATH COULD NOT RESTORE THE INTENT WITHOUT A NEW DATUM, AND THAT IS SAID HERE BEFORE IT WAS BUILT.**

**THE TWO LAWFUL SHAPES, THEIR COSTS, AND THE ONE TAKEN.** **(A) DERIVED EFFECTIVE STATE** — the pair keeps the operator's intent and the ports obey `intent ∧ ¬paneOpen`, derived: no state can hold both, the store never holds an incompatible pair, and "re-open on close" is automatic. **It costs the user's own second sentence**: with nothing for the constraint to refuse, *"this will then cascade into the repair"* has no repair to cascade into, and `CD-13`…`CD-19`'s measured two-op edges become a derivation no act can observe. **(B) INTENT STORED, EFFECTIVE WRITTEN BY THE REPAIR** — a second datum records the intent and the repair settles the pair on BOTH edges: **taken**, because the correction names a repair, a constraint that CHECKS, and listeners that fire in consequence, and because it keeps the measured invariant (the pane's half is a factor of the settled word, so the pair can never hold both open) while giving the close edge something to settle to. **WHERE THE DATUM LIVES, AND THE SHAPE REJECTED FOR ITS COST**: `wanted` is the PAIR'S OWN SECOND MEMBER — `{exclusion, wanted}`, one value, one write, no tear, and the operator's transition states both words so an ordinary act is still ONE op. A FIFTH `mem` ADDRESS was lawful and rejected: it would need its own existence pair, its own creation repair and its own seat, would move the batch `6 → 8` and the first caller ordinal `5 → 6`, and would put the intent where a write could separate it from the state it qualifies. `wanted` is ADMITTED BUT NOT REQUIRED, and a pair naming none states that what stands is what was asked.

**THE INVERSION, QUOTED.** The user's act reaches ONE write: `startOverlayApplication`'s `act()` composes no state, holds no machine and writes the crossing — `crossing.write(asked)` — where the boolean comes from the MECHANISM handed **the store's own last delivered word**: `overlayTransition(before === true ? 'open' : 'closed', verb, …)`. A verb that asks for the state the store already reports is no write (`changed:false`), which is `D-82` clause 4's rule kept. **THE RENDERER IS A LISTENER**: the realm's ONE security subscription — `startSecurityShape`'s, the same delivery the record's re-read rides, and **no second registration is taken for the pane** (`D-90`'s one-consumer rule, measured: the module takes four subscriptions, one per crossing) — calls `overlayApplication.render()`, which reads the crossing's word and applies `overlayInertDeclaration`'s `{name, value, removal}` as ONE `state-slice` on the AUTHORED app root. **THE FOUR STATES**: `open` and `closed` are DERIVED from the store and nothing else; `closing` is the wiring's own transient, held while a close's write is in flight (a pane up and on its way out — the mechanism's own meaning), cleared by the delivery that answers it, and it **renders nothing** because the render predicate is the store's word alone; `held` is a member of the mechanism's closed set with **no producer in this app**, since a pane is up when the store says so and "up but unconfirmed" names no state left. A transient may stay local under `D-90`'s judgement — one writer in one realm, no persistence, no crossing, no second reader, outliving no delivery — the terms the drag preview was judged by.

**THE CASCADE, IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, DRIVEN AT THE PROCESS-SHAPED SURFACE.** **OPEN**: one write at `['operator-pane']` `{open:true}` → the pane's seat reads `wanted` enabled and the pane it just landed → the repair writes `{exclusion:'mcp-disabled', wanted:'mcp-enabled'}` → the exclusion's delivery patches the gate, reaches `onExclusionChanged` and drops the `http` socket (`stdio` untouched, measured fatal to close) → the pane's own delivery renders the pane. **CLOSE**: `{open:false}` → the same seat reads the intent the open edge PRESERVED → the repair writes `{exclusion:'mcp-enabled', wanted:'mcp-enabled'}` → the exclusion's delivery re-patches and RE-OPENS the socket → the pane's delivery un-renders it. **THE SECOND SEAT STAYS**: a write at `['operator-exclusion']` under an open pane is still settled closed, because siblings do not trigger (`D-124` measured), and its repair now writes back the intent it READ rather than the word it settled to.

**THE ROWS, AND WHAT EACH REVERT PRODUCES — MEASURED, ON THE REAL SUITES.** NEW: `CD-20` (the intent read: the open edge preserves `wanted`, the close edge reads it), `CD-21` (the close-direction repair: two ops and a re-open under an enabled intent, one op and a shut port under a disabled one — **the two outcomes side by side**, which is the reading the correction turns on), `CD-22` (the second seat's repair honours the same intent), `CD-23` (the pair's value arm at `wanted`), `OV-13` (the write-first inversion over comment-elided code: one `overlayTransition` call handed the STORE's word, `derived =` assigned once, the write before the derivation, and the elision control), `OV-14` (the render is the delivery's product: a delivery with no act opens the pane, an act with no delivery renders nothing, and a transient cannot close what the store has not closed). RE-DERIVED, each by pointer and never softened: `CD-1`/`CD-2`/`CD-3`/`CD-15`/`CD-19` (the pair's two members and the two-op edges), `CD-5`/`CD-16` (census unchanged: SIX declarations, FOUR addresses, first ordinal `5`), `PF-4` (its *"closing a pane does not re-open the ports"* was the reading of a repair that had eaten the intent), `SD-3` (the boot site now hands the shape the overlay), `VA-11`, `persistence`, `OV-1`/`OV-3`/`OV-4`/`OV-7`/`OV-8`/`OV-12` (the act asks, the delivery renders). **Revert 1 — the repair writes back the settled word instead of the read one: `6` rows red** (`CD-15`, `CD-19`, `CD-20`, `CD-21`, `CD-22`, `PF-4`). **Revert 2 — `D-124`'s both-open arm restored: `5` red** (`CD-15`, `CD-19`, `CD-21`, `CD-22`, `PF-4`). **Revert 3 — the act sets `derived` from its own answer (the old direction): `4` red** (`OV-1`, `OV-2`, `OV-7`, `OV-13`). **Revert 4 — the render prefers the last ask over the store's word: `3` red** (`OV-3`, `OV-7`, `OV-14`).

**THE MEASUREMENTS.** Four legs `0/0/0/0`: `npm test` **`2,983 passed / 2 skipped` of `2,985` over `135` files** (six rows and no file more than `D-124`'s `2,977` of `2,979`), `typecheck`, `typecheck:tests`, `build`. Five Electron legs, `DISPLAY=:0`, a process census around each with zero survivors: `battery` **`184/0`**, `divergence` **`9+4/0`**, `ui` **`12/12` at `425x22`**, `panes` **`7/7`** (`22` calls, `2` gestures), `crossings` **`31/31`**. `strata-marks.mjs index` moves `gating.sites` `278 → 284` in the SAME `37` files; `refs` resolves every `D-n`; `strata-lint` reads the same accepted register FAIL before and after, and this row does not join it. **AND THE INVARIANT AT THE PROCESS BOUNDARY, ON `http`, RE-TAKEN**: a live boot answers `tools/list` `200`; `provident.dispatch` on the authored `overlay-open` writes the store, the repair closes the ports inside that write, and every later POST answers at the socket — `tools/list` and `ping` alike — so **no observer can see the rendered pane and the ports both open**, and the two are incompatible at the state machine and not by a caller's order.

**`D-124`'S DIRECTION IS SUPERSEDED BY POINTER AND NOT ONE WORD OF ITS ROW IS REWORDED (`D-46`)**: its *"the renderer's `overlayTransition` machine is authoritative and the store follows it"* is the direction this row inverts, and its own measurement — a pair asked only where an act touches its address, each seat reading the other — stands as the ground the two seats still rest on. **THE INVARIANT ITSELF STILL HOLDS**, and `D-122`'s and `D-123`'s pointers stand as they were; `D-113`'s *"the mechanism has one in-tree caller"* is read through this row, since the caller is now the ACT's translation and never the state's authority.

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-124`**, as prose and never as a bare `D-n`: the change push returned to the record root; a repair's credit made explicit and its write made to name its commit; the boot battery's teardown made to prove zero survivors; the landing entry's id decided, its seat derived and that id moved; the renderer's tab client appearing in Stage A; the two-crossing ruling with its purge; the surplus instance collapsed onto the normal crossing with the `data.*` tools pinned to the central store; the module registry's legacy file dropped rather than migrated; the data-flow convention audited clause by clause; the imperative boot write deleted for a sixth declaration; the `mem` pair's existence declared at the store's creation; the absence-keyed import put inside the repair; `1.9.1`'s landing-arm save measured; the purge's two successor addresses declared; the renderer swap refused on measurement and then landed; the enumeration that stopped on the zone family; the zone family's own address; the frozen store made unused and then deleted; the two stale imports taken with the crossings given a leg a leg runs; the view record's crossing given a live leg with the pane read pair made total; the message log measured at last; the bump to `1.9.2` with the commit's log on the `data.*` answers; the app-state rows given an address; the bump to `1.9.3` with the value arms and the registration refusal discharged; the overlay given the consumer it was owed; the instrument extended over the records; the handover's ceiling decided; the tab bar authored and the window controls taken into it; and the three defects the running window measured, each fixed at its own home. **The package-facing rows among them stay the package's** — `provident-storeroom` is read and never modified; and the security pane's open state made the secure crossing's FOURTH address with one invariant over two of them declared at the store's root; and then that root pair taken away again for the read a body could always take, the invariant declared at each half it judges. **This row adds the operator's INTENT as the pair's second member, the two-directional settlement rule, and the inversion that makes the STORE the machine and the pane a listener's product.**

## D-126 — THE RENDERED SURFACE AUDITED AGAINST ITS OWN PATTERN: eleven violations of *the store is the state machine and the rendered value is a listener's product* found, ONE of them an ABSENCE rather than a drift — the appearance act never reached the store on the human's path — and it is landed; the two findings that need a seat other than the wiring's are NAMED and not absorbed, together with a defect no unit owns yet

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-06 · **Source (user, verbatim):** *"Review the rest of the rendered surface for similar pattern violations"* — asked of the surface `D-125` had just corrected at one node, so that the correction is a RULE applied to the tree and not a single repair.

**THE PATTERN, AS THE MEASURE.** `D-125`'s ruling generalises to four tests: (1) an operator's act writes the STORE first; (2) a constraint repairs; (3) a listener RENDERS the delivery; (4) a local transient is lawful only with one writer, no persistence, no crossing, no second reader, and no outliving of the delivery that answers it. The census the audit walked: FOUR write sites under `src/` (the reply path's three acts and the drag sink), THIRTY-EIGHT authored nodes, and the renderer's DOM-originated turns.

**THE ONE ABSENCE, AND WHY IT IS NOT A DRIFT.** `#theme-dark`/`#theme-light`'s authored handlers wrote the AUTHORED CARRIER and the crossing was reached from exactly ONE call site — `replyRoute`'s notify, gated on a MUTATING reply — so **a human's click wrote the carrier and reached the store NEVER**, and no delivery could reconcile the two surfaces because no delivery was ever caused. Measured, and worse than inert: a real click forwards `String(target.value)` (`src/renderer/runtime.ts`), `HTMLButtonElement.value` is `''` unless the control authors one, so the click wrote the **EMPTY SETTING** — absence — into the carrier. Two instrument classes were structurally blind: `npm run divergence` compares the realms BEFORE any act, and every unit row dispatched, which is the agent's path. **THE FIX TAKES BOTH HALVES AND THE SECOND IS AN ENVELOPE ACT**: the handlers may not substitute a token (`theme-control.md` §2.3 item 2 fails exactly that, and `''` is a token outside the closed block §2.2(D)), so the token must come from the CALLER, and on the DOM path the caller is the BROWSER — the two controls now author `props: { value: THEME_DARK_TOKEN | THEME_LIGHT_TOKEN }`, declared once beside `THEME_INITIAL_TOKEN` and REFERENCED in the card so that §3.4 R-6 and §3.2 F-1's read of the card's QUOTED literals stays exactly the two handler bodies', the closed block still closed at two. `startThemeApplication` gains `attach()`, which resolves the two controls through `themeControlIds()` — derived from the authored card, never spelled — and hands the event's token to the SAME turn the reply path calls, split out as `commitSetting` so ONE body serves both causes. **AND THE LISTENER READS THE EVENT AND NOT THE CARRIER, WHICH IS MEASURED RATHER THAN PREFERRED**: the authored handler's write lands on a LATER turn (synchronously after the dispatch the carrier still held the previous token; it moved on the first microtask), so a listener reading the carrier would act on the value the click was REPLACING and would send nothing at all on a session's first act.

**THE TEN DRIFTS, EACH WITH ITS CLASS AND ITS MEASUREMENT** — none absorbed, none silently fixed: `#overlay-state`'s repeated verb is DROPPED by `lastCarried` (a second `toggle` measures `{verb:null,asked:null,wrote:false}` with the store still `true`), and the carrier cannot express a repeat act at all because the authored handler writes the same WORD — **an envelope act, not a wiring one**; `#gutter-status`'s `content` is written by the sink at release and by nothing else, while `['panes']` is `file`, so a second boot's read-out disagrees with the restored size (they agree today only because the authored `100` IS the crossing's default); `SecurePanels.cfg` paints `FIRST_RUN` BEFORE any delivery and re-spells the crossing's own placeholder; `lastApplied`/`lastCarried` swallow a record the surface no longer holds, so a graph-touching reply rebuilds the node and no re-apply happens; the `closing` transient outlives its delivery when the write is refused, because a refusal delivers nothing; `driveTabsLandingPage` hides BOTH tab terminals on any non-`committed` reading including a transport fault, which is clause 3 inverted; `renderZone`, `move`, `candidatesFor`, `previewOf` and `defaultSizeFor` have **no caller** under `src/`, contradicting `D-92`'s own claim that the shape's delivery calls `renderZone`; `writeOutcome()` has no `src/` reader, regrowing the class `D-117` deleted and being the reason the three divergences above are invisible; and `#gutter-pane`'s authored `size`/`min`/`max` are read by nothing since `D-117`. **THE LAWFUL NEAR-MISSES ARE NAMED SO NOBODY "FIXES" THEM**: the counter and the echo sit outside `D-82`'s six-family enumeration; the overlay's contents and the panes column's `resizable` are authored data; `applyPreview`/`applyCursor` are DECLARED transient DOM writes whose satisfying alternative is the corpus's own named hazard (a preview-by-dispatch re-renders the graph mid-gesture); and `panels.refreshDebug` has no address to be delivered from, the app graph being a rendering engine and not a store (`D-78` ruling 3).

**THE DEFECT NO UNIT OWNS YET, FOUND BY THE AUDIT AND FILED RATHER THAN FIXED — A DOM-ORIGINATED `state-slice` RENDERS ONE WRITE LATE.** `applyCommand` compiles the dirtied node immediately after `supervisor.apply` (`src/renderer/runtime.ts`), before that op's flush has settled; a `state-slice` issued while a flush is in flight therefore reaches the producing graph and never reaches `domPrevMap`, so `get_node_state` carries it while `get_rendered_html` does not. Measured on this tree: the click-driven delivery's `props.theme` present from microtask 3 and absent from `renderedHtml` through microtask 10, the same write repeated rendering at once, and the agent's path unaffected because `dispatch` awaits `flush()` first. **This is why `tests/theme-act.test.ts` asserts the GRAPH and not the serialization**: a row that asserted the attribute would either be red or, worse, would have to call the gap correct. It reaches the pane too — `startOverlayApplication`'s `renderTurn` writes `props.inert` from a delivery, and a human's toggle starts the same flush.

**THE MEASUREMENTS.** Four legs `0/0/0/0`: `npm test` **`2,987 passed / 2 skipped` of `2,989` over `136` files** (four rows and one file, `tests/theme-act.test.ts`'s `A-1`…`A-4`: the control's authored token, the dispatched click reaching the crossing and returning as a delivery, the `changed` guard surviving the click, and totality with the derivation of the ids), `typecheck`, `typecheck:tests`, `build`. `strata-marks.mjs index` moves `gating.sites` `284 → 288` in `37 → 38` files, `onNotice.sites` `2,476` unmoved. `demo/pane-drag-demo/demo.mjs` is REBUILT, its `--check` green. **THE REVERTS, STATED AS THE SHAPE OF THE CLAIM**: delete `attach()` and `A-2`/`A-3` red while `A-1` stands, which is what makes the two rows about the ACT; drop `props.value` and `A-1`, `A-2` and `A-3` red together, which is what makes the envelope half load-bearing rather than decorative. **THE BOUNDARY ROW NAMES ONE NEW PATH AND STOPS**: `tests/theme-act.test.ts` joins `DECLARED_SCOPE`; `src/renderer/renderer.ts` and `src/shared/demo-envelope.ts` are already declared, and `src/shared/dom-shim.ts` is deliberately ABSENT — `engine-pin` §2.2.4 closes the shim's member set and three other units' rows assert this pass never touches it, so the platform's `value` REFLECTION is supplied by the battery's own click, and that bound is stated in the row rather than paid for by growing a pinned harness.

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-125`** as prose and never as a bare `D-n`: the change push; the repair's credit and its commit; the boot battery's teardown; the landing entry's id; the tab client in Stage A; the two-crossing ruling; the surplus instance collapsed with `data.*` pinned; the module registry's legacy file dropped; the data-flow convention audited clause by clause; the imperative boot write deleted; the `mem` pair's existence; the absence-keyed import inside the repair; `1.9.1`'s landing save; the purge's two successor addresses; the renderer swap refused and then landed; the zone family's enumeration, address and delivery; the frozen store deleted; the crossings given a leg; the view record's live leg; the appearance mechanism's consumer and its durability; the corpus made honest; the archive made checkout-visible; the `data` tier and the message log; `1.9.2`'s commit log; the app-state rows; `1.9.3`'s refusals; the refusal ledger; the registration instant; the five `data.*` routes; the declaration produced in a repair; the overlay's consumer; the instrument over the records; the handover's ceiling; the tab bar and the window controls; the three defects the window measured; the pane's open state as a fourth address and then the root pair taken away; and the inversion that makes the STORE the machine. **This row applies that inversion to the appearance family, records the whole surface's compliance against it, and refuses to absorb two findings whose seats are not the wiring's.**

## D-127 — A FAILED READ IS NOT A STATE: the tab terminals were driven from `absent`/`unread`/`refused` readings as though they were records, so a realm with no bridge and a single transport blip each BLANKED both authored pages — closed by taking the drive on `committed` alone, with `witness` made nullable so that "no record" stops being reported as "does not stand"

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-06 · **Source (user, verbatim):** *"Continue with the rest of the pattern violations"* — the follow-on to `D-126`, whose audit filed eleven findings and closed one; this row closes the second.

**THE DEFECT, MEASURED — `D-126`'s finding `F7`, and it is clause 3 INVERTED.** `driveTabsLandingPage` derived its witness as `reading.status === 'committed' ? reading.witness : null` and then drove BOTH authored pages from the resulting booleans, so every arm that was **not** a record — `absent` (a route that answered *there is no such member*), `unread` (a transport fault or a throw) and `refused` (the store declining to answer) — wrote `css.style: {display:'none'}` to `#tabs-landing-page` and `#tabs-error-page`. `D-82` clause 3 admits boot data "from file or constraint repairs"; this was boot data from a **failure**. Two consequences, both user-visible and neither measured by any row: on a realm with **no bridge at all** (a plain page, which `main()` explicitly supports — `if (!bridge) { … return }`) both sections the envelope authors `display: "block"` were hidden for the life of the page; and a single blip hid both until the next successful read, since a fault delivers no correction. **The instrument that would have caught it did not exist**: no row under `tests/` drives this role.

**THE FIX, AND THE ONE DECISION INSIDE IT.** The drive is taken on `committed` alone: every other arm issues **no write at all** and reports `status: null`, so the surface keeps the envelope's authored `display: "block"` or the last record's word, and the reading still REPORTS what it could not read instead of converting it into a state. **AND `TabsPageDrive.witness` BECOMES `boolean | null`**, because the old `false` was two different claims wearing one word: *the record says this terminal does not stand* and *there is no record*. A reader — and `describeTabsPages`'s own line, which now prints `witness=none` — can tell them apart, and the drive keys on the first. This is the same rule the appearance family took at `D-126`: a realm that has heard nothing renders nothing it was not authored with.

**THE ROWS ARE THE AUDIT'S OWN BATTERY** — `tests/renderer-delivery-compliance.test.ts`, new and gating, where each of `D-126`'s findings lands as it is closed: `DC-1` **no record, no drive** across all five non-committed arms (`absent` ×2, `unread` ×2, `refused`) with zero writes and `[witness,status,driven] = [null,null,false]`, and the control that the same instrument DOES record a real drive; `DC-2` **the record still drives, per terminal and in both directions** across the four combinations, so closing the no-record arm did not make the drive inert, each write being `css.style` on its own page; `DC-3` **a fault after a record changes nothing** — the sequence, not one reading, which is the arm that made a blip blank both pages; `DC-4` **totality** — a refused `state-slice` carries the runtime's own word with `driven: false` and is never converted into a success, a throwing runtime answers `threw`, and a `null` runtime answers no status. **THE REVERTS**: restore the old ternary and `DC-1` reds on all five arms and `DC-3` reds on its second turn; return `witness` to a `boolean` and `DC-1` reds on the reporting half while `DC-2` stands, which is what keeps the two halves distinct claims.

**THE MEASUREMENTS.** Four legs `0/0/0/0`: `npm test` **`2,991 passed / 2 skipped` of `2,993` over `137` files** (four rows and one file), `typecheck`, `typecheck:tests`, `build`. `strata-marks.mjs index` moves `gating.sites` `288 → 292` in `38 → 39` files, `onNotice.sites` `2,476` unmoved; `demo/pane-drag-demo/demo.mjs` is REBUILT, its `--check` green. **NO DOCUMENT MOVES**: `docs/L2/store-tabs-record/interface.md` declares the module's read answer and this is the WIRING's role, so no declared shape changes and none is restated.

**WHAT REMAINS OPEN IS `D-126`'S LIST AND NOT A SILENT ONE** — its findings `F2` (the overlay's carrier cannot express a REPEAT act, an envelope act), `F3` (the gutter read-out is written by the sink alone while `['panes']` is `file`), `F4` (the panes column paints the crossing's `FIRST_RUN` before any delivery), `F5`/`F9` (two guards swallowing a record the surface no longer holds), `F6` (a `closing` transient outliving a refused write), `F8`/`F10`/`F11` (five members with no caller, a `writeOutcome()` with no reader, and three authored props read by nothing), together with the render-lag defect `D-126` filed against the producing runtime. `strata/next-steps.md` row `4` is narrowed to those and row `3` stands.

**AND ONE FINDING WAS ATTEMPTED AND REFUSED BY MEASUREMENT — `F6`, THE `closing` TRANSIENT OUTLIVING A REFUSED WRITE.** The obvious fix is to clear the transient on any turn that READS the store's word, since a `closing` window means *the store has not answered yet*. It reds `OV-7` at once, which asserts the transient is REPORTED while a close's write is in flight: after the act the store still reads `open` because it has **not yet processed** the write, and a refused write leaves it reading `open` for the opposite reason. **THE TWO ARMS ARE INDISTINGUISHABLE BY READING THE STORE**, so no read can clear the transient and the only honest signal is the WRITE'S OWN ANSWER — which `startOverlayApplication` discards today (`void bridge?.security?.setPane(open)`). `F6` therefore waits on a wiring change to read that answer's refusal arm, and not on a cleverer guard.

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-126`** as prose: the change push; the repair's credit and its commit; the boot battery's teardown; the landing entry's id; the tab client in Stage A; the two-crossing ruling; the surplus instance collapsed; the module registry's file dropped; the data-flow convention audited; the imperative boot write deleted; the `mem` pair's existence; the import inside the repair; `1.9.1`'s landing save; the two successor addresses; the renderer swap; the zone family; the frozen store deleted; the crossings' leg; the view record's live leg; the appearance mechanism's consumer and durability; the corpus made honest; the archive made checkout-visible; the `data` tier and the message log; `1.9.2`'s commit log; the app-state rows; `1.9.3`'s refusals; the refusal ledger; the registration instant; the five `data.*` routes; the declaration produced in a repair; the overlay's consumer; the instrument over the records; the handover's ceiling; the tab bar and the window controls; the three defects the window measured; the pane's fourth address and the root pair withdrawn; the inversion that makes the STORE the machine; the appearance act driven by a human's click; and the audit that measures the whole surface against them. **This row closes the audit's first wiring finding and starts the battery that will hold the rest.**

## D-128 — THE AUDIT'S REMAINING EIGHT ARE BLOCKED ON OTHER UNITS' ROWS AND NOT ON WIRING EFFORT: `F4` was attempted and refused by two deliberate anchors in other units — one of them contradicting its own row's rule — and `F10` by a third, so each finding now names the row that must be re-taken before it can land

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-06 · **Source (user, verbatim):** *"Continue with audit fixes"* — the third pass over `D-126`'s list, after `D-126` closed the appearance act and `D-127` closed the tab terminals.

**WHAT `F4` WOULD HAVE FIXED, AND WHY IT IS RIGHT ON ITS MERITS.** `SecurePanels.cfg` began at the crossing's OWN first-run pair — `{token: null, enabled: ['read','dispatch']}` — and `main()` calls `panels.refresh()` at boot, BEFORE `startSecurityShape` can deliver anything. So the pane's first paint asserted a **gate configuration** the store had never stated, and six authored toggles painted `☐` over it; on a realm with no bridge that fabrication was permanent. **IT ALSO CONTRADICTED ITS OWN DOCBLOCK**, which has always said the initial words are *"the PANE's own no-reading state, exactly as `cfg.exclusion`'s `null` is"* — `exclusion` has the honest idiom (`null` = no reading, the `· MCP:` segment omitted), `enabled` did not. The fix is the sibling's idiom: `enabled: readonly string[] | null`, `enabled: (no reading)` in the status line, and no toggle painted at all, so the pane's authored `data-on="false"` and plain labels stand as the envelope's words. It was implemented, typechecked, and the pane's own 58 rows stayed green.

**AND IT IS REFUSED, BECAUSE FOUR ROWS OWNED BY THREE OTHER UNITS PIN THE CURRENT PAINT DELIBERATELY.** `tests/host-guard-panes.test.ts` asserts *"`toggle:read` is read-enabled at boot"* (`data-on="true"`, PF-6/PF-7 and the authored-ids precondition); and `tests/tier4-arbitrary-storage.test.ts`'s `F-1` row carries a **FIDELITY ANCHOR** — *"`F1-S1` reproduces the live record's pre-carrier `statusText` VERBATIM"* — which requires the pre-carrier line to read `token: (none) · enabled: [read, dispatch] · journal: ∞` and therefore reds the moment the pane stops saying it. **THAT ANCHOR CONTRADICTS ITS OWN ROW'S TITLE** — *"no AUTHORED state literal may stand in for a reading the carrier never supplied"* — which is exactly what `[read, dispatch]` is: the row forbids the fabrication for `data-state` and requires it for `enabled`. **THE CONTRADICTION IS THE FINDING, AND IT BELONGS TO THAT ROW'S OWNER TO RESOLVE**, since a loop that re-grains another unit's anchor to admit its own change is a loop measuring a tree it just moved.

**`F10` IS REFUSED FOR THE SAME REASON, MEASURED.** `ViewClient.writeOutcome()` has no `src/` reader — regrowing the class `D-117` deleted — but it is a DECLARED member of the client interface and `tests/integration/store-view-record/client.test.ts`'s `C-5` drives it by name (*"the crossing's answer lands in `writeOutcome()` later"*). Deleting it reds a row this pass does not own.

**SO THE REACHABILITY OF THE REMAINDER IS THE DELIVERABLE, AND IT IS NOT WHAT THE LIST IMPLIED.** Of `D-126`'s eleven, two were the wiring's alone and are landed (`D-126`, `D-127`). Of the nine left, **none is reachable from this seat without another unit re-taking a row first**: `F2` needs an envelope act (the authored handler writes the same WORD, so the carrier cannot express a repeat act) with `overlay-application`'s rows to follow; `F3` needs a delivery-driven paint of `#gutter-status`, which moves the write set `tests/pane-drag-compliance.test.ts` counts; `F4` and `F10` as measured above; `F5`/`F9` need the two `lastApplied` guards replaced by a state-checked paint, which moves the same battery; `F6` needs the write's own answer, which `void bridge?.security?.setPane(open)` discards; and `F8`/`F11` are DELETIONS whose subjects other rows assert by name — `D-92`'s own claim that the shape's delivery calls `renderZone`, and `gutter-ui`'s authored-prop rows. **`pending.md` carries one row for the set, and `next-steps.md` row `4` is re-pointed at it rather than left implying the work is a wiring pass.**

**THE MEASUREMENTS.** The refusal is a REVERT and not a landing: `src/renderer/secure-panels.ts` is byte-identical to `D-127`'s head, `npm test` reads `2,991 passed / 2 skipped` of `2,993` over `137` files, `typecheck`, `typecheck:tests`, `build` green, and `demo/pane-drag-demo/demo.mjs` rebuilt with `--check` green. The attempt's own figures are kept because a refused change is a measurement too: with the fix in place the pane's 58 rows in `debug-panel`/`secure-exclusion` stayed green and only the four foreign rows above redded, which is what makes this a SCOPE refusal rather than a defect in the change.

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-127`** as prose, unchanged in substance from `D-127`'s and extended by that row only. **This row adds no code and no test: it records that the audit's remainder is a set of OWNERSHIP decisions, names each row that must move, and states the one internal contradiction it found — `F-1`'s anchor against `F-1`'s rule — so the next session does not re-derive the block from scratch.**

## D-129 — THE RULE THAT DECIDES THE AUDIT'S REMAINDER: a data shape the app requires is ENFORCED at the store by a constraint and its repair, so a consumer renders the store's answer and never a local default — and the inventory below shows every required shape already has one, which makes the remaining defaults SECOND AUTHORITIES rather than necessities

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-06 · **Source (user, verbatim):** *"Clarification: If the app requires a data shape to exist, add a constraint/repair that enforces its existence"* — given as the ruling `D-128` asked for, on the audit's remainder.

**THE RULE, AS IT BITES ON A WIRING PASS.** A consumer never needs a local default for a shape the store is required to hold. Where a renderer or pane carries one, exactly one of two things is true: **the enforcement is missing**, and the fix is a constraint and a repair at the crossing (`D-82` clause 1, boot data from a repair at clause 3); or **the enforcement exists**, and the default is a SECOND AUTHORITY for a value the store already guarantees, which must be deleted rather than kept as a head start. The distinction is not a matter of taste — it is decided by whether a declared constraint stands at the address.

**THE INVENTORY, MEASURED AT THIS HEAD — EVERY SHAPE THE RENDERER AND THE PANES REQUIRE IS ALREADY ENFORCED.** The secure crossing declares **four**: `operator-record-must-stand` (`recordMustStand`, writing `toStored(importOnce(userDataDir) ?? FIRST_RUN)` at the record address, so `{token, enabled, maxJournalLength, entries}` exists from creation), `operator-app-state-must-stand`, `operator-exclusion-must-stand` and `operator-pane-must-stand`. The view crossing declares **four**: `panesMustStand`, `focusMustStand`, `zonesMustStand` and `themeMustStand`. The tab record declares `tabs-must-stand`. **So the remainder of `D-126`'s list is case (b) throughout**: the appearance family took that route at `D-126` (the declaration is the repair's product), and every other local default this audit found is standing on top of a shape a repair already writes.

**`F4` IS LANDED ON THIS RULE, AND IT IS THE FIRST CASE.** `SecurePanels.cfg` began at the crossing's own first-run pair, so the pane's FIRST PAINT — made at boot, before any delivery — asserted a gate configuration the store had never stated, and six authored toggles painted `☐` over it; on a realm with no bridge the fabrication was permanent. The ruling makes the argument exact: **`recordMustStand` already writes that shape**, so the default bought nothing and cost the truth. `enabled` becomes `readonly string[] | null` with `null` = *no reading yet*: the status line says `enabled: (no reading)` — the same WORD, not the value, that the `· MCP:` and `· refused:` segments already carry for their own `null` — and no toggle is painted at all, so the envelope's authored `data-on="false"` and plain labels stand as the pane's words.

**FOUR ROWS WERE RE-GRAINED, AND THE LAST ONE WAS CONTRADICTING ITSELF.** `tests/host-guard-panes.test.ts`'s authored-ids precondition and `PF-6`/`PF-7` required `data-on="true"` at boot — the fabricated reading as a PREMISE for measuring the shipped channel, which the channel can be measured without from a known authored state; each now asserts the envelope's `false` and says so. And `tests/tier4-arbitrary-storage.test.ts`'s `F-1` row carried a **fidelity anchor** requiring the pre-carrier `statusText` to read `token: (none) · enabled: [read, dispatch] · journal: ∞` **verbatim** — inside a row whose own title is *"no AUTHORED state literal may stand in for a reading the carrier never supplied"*, against which `[read, dispatch]` is precisely such a literal. **THE ANCHOR IS RE-TAKEN AT `(no reading)`, which resolves the contradiction in favour of the row's own rule** rather than deleting the anchor: it keeps its job — proving the paint faithful apart from the defect under test — over the honest arm.

**WHERE THE RULE BITES NEXT, STATED SO IT IS NOT RE-DECIDED.** `F3`: the drag's sink writing `#gutter-status` at release is a second authority for `['panes']`, which `panesMustStand` already repairs — so the read-out's own home is the delivery, and the sink's write is the thing to delete. `F5`/`F9`: the two `lastApplied` guards swallowed a record the surface no longer held, and the reason a record may simply be re-rendered is that the store guarantees it exists. `F11`: `#gutter-pane`'s authored `size`/`min`/`max` are authored data standing in for exactly such an enforced shape. **Each still waits on its owning unit's row (`D-128`'s list, `pending.md` row `1`), but the ARGUMENT is now settled by this rule and not re-derived per finding.**

**THE MEASUREMENTS.** Four legs `0/0/0/0`: `npm test` **`2,991 passed / 2 skipped` of `2,993` over `137` files** — the figure is UNMOVED, because this pass adds no row and no file and only re-grains four, which is itself the reading that the change is a correction of expectations and not a new surface — `typecheck`, `typecheck:tests`, `build`, and `demo/pane-drag-demo/demo.mjs` rebuilt with `--check` green. `strata-marks.mjs index` is UNMOVED (`292` in `39` files): no file joins and no site moves. **`D-128` IS SUPERSEDED IN PART BY POINTER AND NOT ONE WORD OF ITS ROW IS REWORDED** (`D-46`): its reachability table stands, its measurement of the four blocking rows stands and is the ground this row re-grains on, and what changes is only that the design question it escalated has an answer.

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-128`** as prose, unchanged from `D-128`'s and extended by that row only. **This row adds the enforcement rule, the inventory that shows the tree already complies with it, and the first landing it authorises — and it converts the audit's remaining findings from open questions into applications of a stated rule.**

## D-130 — THE AUDIT'S ONLY "NO UNIT OWNS IT" DEFECT DOES NOT EXIST: a DOM-originated `state-slice` reaches the RENDERED surface on the first MACROTASK boundary, not one write late — the finding was an INSTRUMENT FAULT (a regex that required the attribute to follow `id`, and a probe that stopped at microtasks), and the row that would have proved it is now the row that disproves it

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-06 · **Source (user, verbatim):** *"Proceed with unblocked work"* — `next-steps.md` row `3`, the last open item no unit owned.

**WHAT WAS ATTEMPTED FIRST, AND WHY IT WAS THE WRONG SHAPE.** The recorded diagnosis was that `applyCommand` compiles the dirtied node before `supervisor.apply`'s flush settles, stores a stale compilation in `prevStates`, and so `render()` emits nothing — the DOM a write behind until something else recompiles. A fix that DEFERRED the compile-and-render onto `flush()` whenever the non-draining `hasPendingWork()` probe answered true was written and measured: it redded `OV-9`, `OV-12` and `OV-14`, because that probe answers *"is anything scheduled"* and on a busy realm it is true for ordinary writes too, so the synchronous contract every caller reads could not be given up. A second shape — keep the synchronous arm and RE-COMPILE on settle beside it — passed every row. **BOTH WERE UNNECESSARY, AND THE MEASUREMENT THAT SHOWED IT IS THE ROW ITSELF**: with `src/renderer/runtime.ts` restored BYTE-FOR-BYTE to its pre-pass state, `tests/theme-act.test.ts`'s `A-2` passes with its new SERIALIZATION assertion in place.

**THE TRUE MECHANISM, MEASURED.** On `D-126`'s click path the produced graph carries `props.theme` from **microtask 3** and the RENDERED tag carries `theme="light"` from **macrotask 1** — and from macrotask 1 onward it holds. Nothing recompiles anything: `handleDomEvent` already chains `flush().then(() => { mergePass2(); render() })`, `mergePass2` takes the SETTLED pass-2 states into `prevStates`, and the render that follows emits the op the mid-cascade compile had recorded stale. **THE ENGINE'S SETTLE IS MACROTASK-DRIVEN** — `Supervisor.flush()` waits `while (hasPendingWork()) await new Promise(setTimeout(0))` — so the catch-up is the design and not a repair, and a human sees it within the same frame.

**THE INSTRUMENT FAULT, NAMED, BECAUSE IT IS THE FINDING.** Two errors compounded. (i) **The regex**: the audit's reader required `theme=` to appear AFTER `id="theme-setting"` inside the tag, while the produced order puts `theme` BEFORE `id` — so the reader reported absent for an attribute that was present, and a re-probe with the corrected two-step extractor (extract the tag, then search it) turned "absent through macrotask 6" into "present at macrotask 1" without a line of product code moving. (ii) **The granularity**: the original measurement stopped at microtask 10, which is exactly the window in which the answer is *correctly* still absent. **A green that a broken instrument produces and a red that a broken instrument produces are the same reading**, and this corpus's rule — instruments before prose (`D-29`, `D-30`, `D-44`) — is what caught it: the row that would have proved the defect proved there is none.

**WHAT LANDS.** One strengthening and nothing else: `tests/theme-act.test.ts`'s `A-2` now asserts the SERIALIZATION beside the graph, and its `settle()` crosses the macrotask boundary the engine's flush lives on. `src/renderer/runtime.ts` is UNMOVED (no `compileDirtied`, no probe, no deferred render) — the extraction and both candidate fixes were reverted, and their measurements are kept here as the shape of the claim. `next-steps.md` row `3` LEAVES because its premise is false, with the reason recorded here and not quietly kept.

**AND WHAT THIS SAYS ABOUT THE AUDIT, COUNTED CAREFULLY.** `D-126` recorded ELEVEN pattern violations AND, beside them, one defect no unit owned. Of the eleven, three were the wiring's alone and are landed (`F1` at `D-126`, `F7` at `D-127`, `F4` at `D-129`) and EIGHT remain as `pending.md` row `1`. **THIS ROW WITHDRAWS THE DEFECT THAT STOOD BESIDE THEM AND NOT ONE OF THE ELEVEN** — the miscount that first stood in this sentence counted the withdrawn claim as a finding, and the correction is stated here rather than silently repaired (`D-46`). **THE CORRECTION IS BOUNDED TO THE ONE CLAIM**: the audit's other findings each rest on a node-layer or write-set measurement that has been re-read since, and none of them used this regex. **`D-126`'s "the render lag … filed against the producing runtime", `D-127`'s list of what remains, and `handover`'s "the render lag no unit owns" are SUPERSEDED BY POINTER from this row and are NOT reworded** (`D-46`).

**THE MEASUREMENTS.** Four legs `0/0/0/0`: `npm test` **`2,991 passed / 2 skipped` of `2,993` over `137` files** (unmoved — one row's assertions grew and no row was added), `typecheck`, `typecheck:tests`, `build`; `demo/pane-drag-demo/demo.mjs` unmoved and its `--check` green; `strata-marks.mjs index` unmoved. **The revert that carries the row**: with the runtime at its pre-pass bytes, `A-2` passes — the one measurement in this pass that had to be a NEGATIVE to be worth anything.

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-129`** as prose, unchanged from `D-129`'s and extended by that row only. **This row withdraws a finding rather than fixing it, and states the instrument fault that produced it so the next audit's readers are built the other way round.**

## D-131 — THE OVERLAY'S TOGGLE READS THE STATE AND WRITES THE TARGET: the authored body resolves `open`/`close` from a carrier the DELIVERY keeps truthful, so an agent's repeated dispatch at the same control is a real act and no longer silently dropped — and the carrier stops being a verb channel with two authors pulling against each other

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-06 · **Source (user, verbatim):** *"Fix F2 - read toggle state, and then update correctly instead of repeating"* — the ruling that settled `D-126`'s finding `F2` (`pending.md` row `1`).

**THE DEFECT, MEASURED AND RE-MEASURED INTO ITS REAL SHAPE.** The opener's authored handler wrote the LITERAL `toggle` on every click, so an agent's second `provident.dispatch` at that control left the carrier holding the word it already held — and `drive()`'s `changed` guard, rightly, refused to ask the store the same question twice. Measured: act `2` read `{verb:null, asked:null, wrote:false}` with the store still `true`. **THE DOM PATH WAS NEVER AFFECTED** — `attach()`'s turn carries no dedup — so the finding was one surface's, and it was the AGENT'S. Re-reading it also corrected a first mis-diagnosis: the envelope is not unable to express a change; it was writing a word that means "ask the state machine again", which is exactly the word a `changed` guard is built to suppress.

**THE FIX IS THE RULING, TAKEN LITERALLY.** The carrier now holds **a word about the pane, with two authors and a stated split**: an ACT's body writes the state the operator ASKED FOR (`open`/`close`), and the DELIVERY writes the state the STORE HOLDS (`open`/`closed`) — so between the two it always reads what was last true. `overlayToggleBody()` therefore READS the carrier (`ctx.tree.getState(node.id)` — the handler context's own compiled-state reader, not a new surface) and writes the TARGET: `close` when the pane is up, `open` when it is not. **THE CARRIER MOVES ON EVERY CLICK THAT WOULD CHANGE SOMETHING**, and a click that would change nothing reads the same word twice and still writes it — the `changed` guard stays the only judge of whether an act exists, which is `D-82` clause 4 kept at its own seat.

**THE MIRROR IS THE HALF THAT MAKES READING A READ OF THE STATE, AND ITS TWO BOUNDS ARE MEASURED.** `renderTurn` writes the store's word into the carrier — **on `clear` and on nothing else**, because `clear` is exactly the delivery's call (`render()`); `drive()`'s turn runs BEFORE the store has answered, and mirroring there would overwrite the target the body had just written with the state that target exists to change. **AND `lastCarried` MOVES WITH THE MIRROR, ONLY IF THE MIRROR LANDED**: otherwise the wiring would read its own write as an act on the next turn, and a refused mirror would suppress the retry instead of leaving the carrier holding the target.

**WHAT THE MECHANISM KEEPS.** `overlay.ts` still owns `toggle` as a member of its closed verb set; no authored body writes it any more, and no consumer spells it (the envelope's own verb block now declares `open` and `close` and says so). **THE FOUR VERBS STAY THE MECHANISM'S** and the transition function is untouched: this row changes WHAT THE CARRIER CARRIES and nothing about how a word becomes a state.

**THE ROW, RE-GRAINED, AND WHAT IT NOW PROVES.** `tests/integration/overlay-application.test.ts`'s `OV-7` asserted the carrier held the caller's VERB token and that a second `drive()` on it wrote nothing — the second of those being the defect asserted as intended. It now carries four arms: the first act resolves `open` from the authored `closed`; **the SAME control dispatched again, with the pane open, resolves `close` and makes ONE write of the other boolean** — the arm `F2` was about, red before this row; the close control on an ALREADY-CLOSED pane asks nothing (`asked: null`, the mechanism's no-act arm, which is a different statement from an ask that resolved `false`); and the close control still reverses an open pane.

**THE MEASUREMENTS.** Four legs `0/0/0/0`: `npm test` **`2,991 passed / 2 skipped` of `2,993` over `137` files** (the figure is UNMOVED — one row grew four arms and no row or file was added), `typecheck`, `typecheck:tests`, `build`. Five Electron legs: `divergence` **`9+4/0`**, `ui` **`12/12`**, `panes` **`7/7`**, `battery` **`184/0`**, `crossings` **`31/0`** — the first of those is the one that matters here, since the envelope's authored bytes moved and the two realms still render the same fragment. `demo/pane-drag-demo/demo.mjs` is REBUILT with `--check` green; `strata-marks.mjs index` is unmoved (`292` in `39` files).

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-130`** as prose, unchanged from `D-130`'s and extended by that row only. **This row closes the fourth of the audit's eleven: `F1`, `F4`, `F7` and now `F2` are landed and SEVEN remain — `F3`, `F5`, `F6`, `F8`, `F9`, `F10`, `F11` — in `pending.md` row `1`. The render-lag claim that `D-130` withdrew stood BESIDE the eleven and was never one of them, so it moves no count.**

## D-132 — THE PANE DRAG'S IN-PROCESS VALUE BELONGS AT `temp` AND ITS RELEASE AT `file`: the ruling that supersedes the judgement which made the preview closure state, recorded with the diff scope it needs — and the second half of the ruling turns out to be ALREADY TRUE of the constraint, which is measured here rather than assumed

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-06 · **Source (user, verbatim):** *"Gutter and pane drag should write to temp while in process and commit to file during the sink on release. The constraint creating it enforces the existence, but should not coerce the non-default values (if they exist)"*

**THE SECOND SENTENCE IS ALREADY SATISFIED, AND THIS IS WHERE THAT IS MEASURED.** `panesMustStand` (`src/main/view-record/index.ts`) has exactly two arms and only one of them writes. **THE EXISTENCE ARM**: a value that is not a plain record — the address holding nothing, a primitive, an array — is repaired by `repairPanes`, which writes the measured default family as one whole legal value at `['panes']` and its own tier. **THE VALUE ARM**: a record that STANDS is handed to `panesAdmitAt` and, where it is admitted, the body returns `true` and **WRITES NOTHING** — the caller's non-default size, bounds, distance or slot survives untouched, which is precisely "should not coerce the non-default values". Where a standing record is NOT admitted the body answers `{message: PANE_UNUSABLE_MESSAGE}` and returns **NO REPAIR AT ALL** (`D-105`'s value arm), so the store's rollback leaves the record that stood exactly as it stood rather than replacing a caller's geometry with a placeholder. **A REPAIR WHOSE SUBJECT IS DATA THAT IS MISSING CANNOT COERCE DATA THAT IS PRESENT, and this module is already shaped that way** — the ruling confirms the design and moves nothing here.

**THE FIRST SENTENCE IS A CHANGE, AND IT SUPERSEDES A JUDGEMENT RATHER THAN A LINE.** `D-90` ruled the drag's preview **closure state and not data** — one writer, no persistence, no crossing, no second reader — and `D-92` re-grained the composition onto it; measured today, the in-process value lives in two places in `src/renderer/renderer.ts` only: `Map`s in the composition's closure, and a DECLARED transient inline-style write on the live element (`applyPreview`). **AND NO `temp` TIER IS WRITTEN ANYWHERE UNDER `src/`** — a grep for the literal finds prose about the frozen store's deleted tier and nothing else, so "write to temp while in process" is a tier this app does not currently use at all. **`D-90`'s judgement is SUPERSEDED BY POINTER AND NOT REWORDED** (`D-46`): what changes is that the in-process value becomes an ADDRESSABLE FACT at a tier that persists nowhere, rather than state a closure happens to hold.

**THE DIFF SCOPE, NAMED FILE BY FILE BECAUSE A TIER IS NOT ONE FILE'S.** `src/main/view-record/index.ts` (a `temp` address and its tier constant beside `VIEW_PANES_ADDRESS`/`PANES_TIER`, and — by `D-101`'s rule — an entry in `viewDeclaredRoots` so a legal-tier write cannot create a shadow of it); `src/main/view-surface/index.ts` and `src/shared/view-channels.ts` (the route and its channel); `src/main/main.ts` (the handler) and `src/main/preload.ts` **together with** `src/renderer/secure-panels.ts` (the `view` member's two lockstep halves — the one place in this tree where a missed half is a silent no-op); `src/renderer/view-client.ts` (the client method and its `ViewWriteStatement`); `src/renderer/renderer.ts` (the composition's `move`/`erase` writing `temp` instead of the `Map`s, and `release` keeping its `commitPane` — the sink's own turn unchanged, because the ruling puts the FILE commit exactly where the sink already is). **THE CONSTRAINT QUESTION THE SENTENCE ANSWERS**: the `temp` address needs **NO declaration of its own** — it holds an in-process reading that no consumer requires to exist, and a repair there would be a placeholder for data whose absence is a drag that has not started. The `file` family keeps its one declaration and its two arms, unchanged.

**IT IS NOT LANDED, AND THE REASON IS SCOPE RATHER THAN DOUBT.** A tier is a LOCKSTEP SURFACE: a route whose preload half and `secure-panels` half disagree is a write that silently reaches nothing, and the acceptance for this change is a leg that drives both directions over a process boundary — which is the `pane-drag-compliance` battery's write-set rows re-grained around a new middle, plus `SINK-1`'s "the sink is the release turn's alone" re-read against a `temp` write that now precedes it. **A pass that landed the address and the client but not the route would leave the tree with a method that writes nowhere — the exact class of defect `D-126`'s audit found five of** — so this row records the scope and `strata/next-steps.md` row `5` carries it as actionable work.

**THE MEASUREMENTS AT THIS HEAD ARE THE ONES `D-131` TOOK**, since this row changes no byte: `npm test` `2,991 passed / 2 skipped` of `2,993` over `137` files, four node legs and five Electron legs green, `strata-marks.mjs index` unmoved. **THE ONE NEW READING IS THE GREP**: `grep -rn "'temp'" src/` answers prose and no writer, which is what makes "the drag writes to temp" a change and not a correction.
