**AND THE RULING THAT FOLLOWED IT BINDS THIS ROW'S SCOPE — *"The pane drag should be interacting with the normal store, not the secure store"* — AND IT MEASURED ALREADY TRUE, WHICH IS WHY IT IS A CONSTRAINT ON THE PLAN AND NOT A CORRECTION OF THE TREE.** Three independent readings say the drag's geometry crosses the NORMAL store: `src/main/main.ts` constructs `createViewSurface({ store: centralStore, … })` where `centralStore` is the boot's one `createProfileStore`, and that same call's batch carries `viewConstraintDeclarations(…)` — so `['panes']`, `['zones']`, `['focus']` and `['theme']` are declared there; `IPC_VIEW_PANE_COMMIT` answers `viewSurface.commitPane`, reached from the renderer's `window.provident.view` member and never from its `security` member; and `src/renderer/renderer.ts`'s drag composition names no secure-store member at all. **THE SECURE STORE NAMES ONE PANE ADDRESS AND NO GEOMETRY WHATEVER**: `['operator-pane']` at `mem`, holding `{open}` — the operator pane's OPEN state, a different fact in a different crossing (`D-123`/`D-125`). **THE CONFUSION IS NATURAL AND THAT IS WHY THIS PARAGRAPH IS HERE**: the two are both about "the pane", both spell `pane`, and one of them lives on the store this app calls secure. **SO THIS ROW'S `temp` ADDRESS JOINS THE PANES FAMILY ON THE NORMAL CROSSING** — beside `VIEW_PANES_ADDRESS` and `PANES_TIER` in `src/main/view-record/index.ts`, its route on the view surface's channels with the `view` member's two lockstep halves — and **THE SECURE STORE GAINS NOTHING** from this change: no address, no declaration, no route, no member. A `temp` geometry written at the secure crossing would put a gesture's reading into the store that also holds the lockout invariant, which is the one mixing this ruling forbids.

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-131`** as prose, unchanged from `D-131`'s and extended by that row only. **This row confirms one half of a design ruling by measurement, records the other half's scope, and refuses to half-land a lockstep surface.**

## D-133 — `data.read` ANSWERS THE PATH AND THE VALUE AND DROPS THE TIER IT READ AT: a caller cannot tell which tier answered, while the verb it declares takes `tier?` — a lossier answer than the call it reports, and the defect the pane renderer's ghost would have worked around with a member it does not need

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-06 · **Source (user, verbatim):** *"The returned opbject should already include the tier data. If it doesn't that is a defect."*

**THE MEASUREMENT.** `src/main/data/types.ts` declares `DataReadAnswer` as `{status:'read'; kind:'row'|'branch'; path: DataPath; value: unknown}` or `{status:'absent'; path: DataPath}` or `DataLedger`. **NO ARM CARRIES A TIER**, while the verb is `read(args)` with a `tier?: unknown` member documented as *"The tier to read AT: … omit it to read across every tier"* (`src/main/mcp-server.ts`). So the answer is **lossier than the call it reports**: a caller that read at `temp` and one that read across every tier receive the same shape, and `temp` is exactly the tier `D-132` is about.

**WHY IT IS A DEFECT AND NOT A GAP.** The instruction that produced this row is *"on listen, the pane renderer should check whether the pane's tier is `temp` and, if so, render as a low-opacity ghost"* — a question the delivery must be able to answer. I had planned to answer it by adding a `drag` member to the view record's read and a `dragOf` to the client, which is a READ-SHAPE CHANGE to a family that has one already. **THE TIER IS THE BETTER ANSWER AND IT ALREADY HAS A HOME**: the read knows the tier because it was asked for one, so echoing it costs no member, no family and no client surface — and it serves every consumer of `data.read`, not only this one. **A SURFACE THAT ANSWERS LESS THAN IT WAS TOLD IS THE `D-70`/`D-117` CLASS AT THE BOUNDARY RATHER THAN IN A BINDING**: the value is carried and the qualification is discarded, so the caller must either guess or take a second read.

**THE FIX SHAPE, STATED SO THE NEXT PASS DOES NOT RE-DERIVE IT.** Echo `tier` on **both** passing arms — `{status:'read', kind, path, tier, value}` and `{status:'absent', path, tier}` — composed in `src/main/data/index.ts` from the composer's own argument, and **never** from the store's answer (the seam's `read` returns the value alone, so a tier re-read from the instance would be a second authority for what the call already knew). **THE ABSENT ARM CARRIES IT TOO**, because *"absent"* is a statement ABOUT A TIER: `absent` at `temp` and `absent` at `file` are different facts and the caller is deciding with the difference. **THE LEDGER ARMS GAIN NOTHING**: a refusal that never reached a store has no tier to name, the same reading that keeps `log` off exactly those arms (`D-107`).

**AND IT IS WHY THE PANE DRAG'S GHOST NEEDS NO NEW MEMBER.** With the tier on the read answer, the renderer asks the DELIVERY *"which tier did this reading come from"* — the question `D-132` and this row's instruction both put there — instead of the client inventing a `dragOf` for one consumer. `strata/next-steps.md` row `6` carries the fix; row `5` carries the drag behind it.

**THE MEASUREMENTS AT THIS HEAD ARE `D-131`'S**, since this row changes no byte: `npm test` `2,991 passed / 2 skipped` of `2,993` over `137` files, four node legs and five Electron legs green. **THE ONE NEW READING IS THE TYPE**: `grep -n \"status: 'read'\" src/main/data/types.ts` answers a `kind`, a `path` and a `value`, and no tier.

**AND THE FIX SHAPE ABOVE IS SUPERSEDED, BEFORE IT WAS BUILT, BY A RELEASE THE USER POINTED AT — `1.10.0`'S `readTiered` — WHICH IS A BETTER ANSWER THAN THE ONE THIS ROW PROPOSED** (`D-46`: superseded by pointer, the paragraph above kept as the reading it was). `../Provident-Storeroom/bindings/js` stands at **`1.10.0`** and its `RELEASE-NOTES.md` §1.10.0 declares a MINOR adding ONE instance member: *"**`readTiered(address, tier?)`** — the third read verb … takes `read`'s two positions, the address and the optional tier, with `read`'s own walk and `read`'s own refusal set"*, answering **at every node it returns** the tier that node stands at — an object node as `{_tier, …members}`, an array node as `{_tier, value: […]}` and a leaf as `{_tier, value: <the literal>}` — each `_tier` being `'mem'`, `'file'` or `'temp'` and **never a set**. **`read` AND `readLiteral` ARE UNCHANGED, byte for byte.** **WHY THAT BEATS WHAT THIS ROW PROPOSED**: echoing the CALLER'S ARGUMENT names the tier the call asked for, and a read that walks EVERY tier can return nodes standing at different ones — so the per-node readout is the only shape that is true for the walk this surface actually performs, and it costs a dependency adoption rather than a composition. **THE MIGRATION IS ONE SEAM AND ONE CAVEAT, BOTH NAMED BY THE RELEASE ITSELF**: `src/main/storeroom.ts` is the seam that wraps the package (`S-9`), so `data.read` composes over `readTiered` there and nowhere else; and the notes' own point 4 states the one lossy name — *"a CALLER'S OWN STORED `_tier` IS NOT DELIVERED BY THIS VERB, AND `read` STILL ANSWERS IT"* — which this app must cite rather than rediscover, since nothing in `src/` stores a `_tier` member today (measured: no such write exists) and a future one would shadow itself. **THE ADOPTION IS ITS OWN PASS AND IS NOT TAKEN HERE**: a dependency bump moves `package.json`, the lock and every leg at once, and `provident-storeroom` is READ AND NEVER MODIFIED (`D-5`) — `strata/next-steps.md` row `6` now carries the bump AND the composition as one row, because the answer shape and the verb that fills it are one change.

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-132`** as prose, unchanged from `D-132`'s and extended by that row only. **This row files a boundary defect the user named, states the fix shape so it is not re-derived, and records why the ghost gets cheaper rather than dearer.**

## D-134 — THE TREE RUNS `provident-storeroom@1.10.0`: the tier readout is adopted, the seam's census moves `14 → 15` for the first name a release has ADDED since the pin, and the composition that USES it is left as one row rather than half-built

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-06 · **Source (user, verbatim):** *"Check 1.10.0 storeroom package for new read option showing tier"*, then *"Proceed"* — `D-133` and `next-steps.md` row `6`.

**WHAT WAS ADOPTED, AND WHY THIS SHAPE OF IT.** `1.10.0` is a MINOR adding ONE instance member — **`readTiered(address, tier?)`** — which takes `read`'s two positions, walks as `read` walks and refuses as `read` refuses, and answers at EVERY node it returns the tier that node stands at: `{_tier, …members}` for an object, `{_tier, value: […]}` for an array, `{_tier, value: <literal>}` for a leaf, each `_tier` being `'mem'`, `'file'` or `'temp'` and **never a set**. `read` and `readLiteral` are unchanged byte for byte, and the notes list everything that does not move. **THE ADOPTION IS FROM THE REGISTRY, NOT FROM THE ADJACENT CHECKOUT**: `npm view provident-storeroom version` answers `1.10.0`, so `package.json` takes a caret range `^1.10.0` and the lock resolves against the published artifact — a `file:` spec pointing at `../Provident-Storeroom/bindings/js` would have made this repo's dependency graph unshippable, and the seam's own row is explicit that *"the pin follows the reviewed install, never the other way"*.

**THE FIRST NAME A RELEASE HAS ADDED SINCE THE SEAM WAS PINNED, AND THE CENSUS SAYS SO.** `S-1.1`/`S-1.2` read `14` members and `S-9` read `14`/`13`; `1.9.3`'s own note states it moved neither count. `1.10.0` adds one, and it sits **THIRD** in the instance's own-property order — measured on a fresh instance: `read`, `readLiteral`, `readTiered`, `write`, … — so the seam's `MEMBERS` list gains the name in that position, `memberCount` reads **15**, and the two rows that pinned the fourteen are re-grained with the reason rather than by number. **THE `symbolCount` DOES NOT MOVE** (`13`), which the same measurements confirm: this release adds a verb and no symbol.

**AND THE COMPOSITION IS NOT TAKEN HERE, DELIBERATELY.** `data.read` still answers `{status, kind, path, value}`; composing it over `readTiered` is `next-steps.md` row `6`'s second half and it is a CHANGE OF ANSWER SHAPE with its own battery (`central-data`'s twelve L1 scenarios and the value arms). **ADOPTING THE VERB AND COMPOSING OVER IT ARE TWO PASSES AND THE FIRST IS COMPLETE**: the dependency is reviewed, installed, censused and green on all nine legs, and nothing in `src/` yet calls a member that does not exist — which is the state a half-built composition would not have.

**THE MEASUREMENTS.** Four node legs `0/0/0/0`: `npm test` **`2,991 passed / 2 skipped` of `2,993` over `137` files** (unmoved — three rows were re-grained and none added), `typecheck`, `typecheck:tests`, `build`. Five Electron legs over the bumped package: `battery` **`184/0`**, `divergence` **`9/4 → 9+4/0`**, `ui` **`12/12`**, `panes` **`7/7`**, `crossings` **`31/0`** — the last two are the ones that matter for a package bump, since `crossings` reads the crossing's record at its raw address and drives both directions over a process boundary, and `battery` spawns the built bundle against the new artifact. `npm audit` reports no new class.

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-133`** as prose, unchanged from `D-133`'s and extended by that row only. **This row adopts the verb, moves the census and stops — leaving the composition, and the ghost behind it, to the row that already carries them.**

## D-135 — A NESTED WRITE CANNOT CREATE ITS PARENT, BUT AN ENFORCED PARENT CAN BE TRUSTED PRESENT: the whole-family writes at the view crossing are for MEMBER MERGE and not for parent existence, and the reason the code states for them is corrected here — the comment edit itself rides with the next pass that opens `view-record`

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-06 · **Source (user, verbatim):** *"A nested write cannot create its parent, but the parent's existence can be enforced and therefore trusted to be present"*

**THE CORRECTION.** `commitPane`'s note in `src/main/view-record/index.ts` says *"a nested write does not create its parent (measured), so the family is composed whole and written where the pair stands"*. **THE MEASUREMENT IS TRUE AND THE INFERENCE IS WRONG**: `panesMustStand`'s repair writes the default family at `['panes']` when the address does not stand, so the parent **IS ENFORCED** and a caller may trust it — parent existence is the DECLARATION's guarantee and never the composer's reason.

**WHAT `composePanes` IS THEREFORE FOR, MEASURED IN ITS OWN BODY.** Two things, and only one of them is a reason to write at the root. (i) **SIBLING PRESERVATION** — `for (const pane of panes) if (pane.id !== id) whole[pane.id] = {…}` — which is needed ONLY because the write lands at the root and replaces the whole value; a nested write at `['panes', id]` preserves the siblings by construction and for free. (ii) **MEMBER MERGE** — `size: supplied.size ?? base.size` and `bounds: {min: supplied.bounds?.min ?? base.bounds.min, …}` — so a caller naming only `size` keeps the standing `bounds`. **THE SECOND IS THE REAL REASON**: a nested write at `['panes', id]` replaces that pane's whole value, so a size-only act would drop the bounds. The derivation in `commitPane` is a MERGE and not a parent-creation workaround.

**THE CAVEAT, STATED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** The parent is trustworthy because NOTHING REMOVES IT — the creation repair writes it and deletion at a declared root goes through the declared-root guard (`D-101`) — and the failure mode if it ever were absent is an HONEST REFUSAL (`parent-absent`), never a silently created malformed branch, with the root's repair running on the next write at the root.

**WHAT FOLLOWS FOR THE WORK IN FLIGHT.** A nested write is a lawful alternative wherever the caller sends the COMPLETE member value or replaces a leaf deliberately; `D-132`'s `temp` address needs NO composition at all, since `['drag']` is its own root and has no siblings; and the `file` commit keeps its composer for the merge alone.

**AND WHY THE CODE IS NOT EDITED IN THIS ROW.** The docblock states the wrong reason and is a COMMENT-ONLY edit, but a comment edit still costs the full four-leg re-verification this corpus requires before reporting a change. **IT RIDES WITH THE NEXT PASS THAT OPENS `view-record`** — `next-steps.md` row `6`'s `readTiered` composition or row `5`'s temp write, whichever is taken — so that one verification covers both the correction and the change.

**THE MEASUREMENTS AT THIS HEAD ARE `D-134`'S**, since this row changes no byte: `npm test` `2,991 passed / 2 skipped` of `2,993` over `137` files and all nine legs green over the `1.10.0` bump.

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-134`** as prose, unchanged from `D-134`'s and extended by that row only. **This row records a correction of reasoning that outlived its measurement, and puts the one comment edit on the next pass's tab rather than paying a full verification for a sentence.**

## D-136 — FORMATTING BELONGS IN THE RENDERER AND THE IPC HANDLER PASSES THE VALUE STRAIGHT TO THE STORE VERB: the derivation `D-135` located at the crossing MOVES TO THE WRITING REALM, the surface's shape arms and family composers become pass-through, and `next-steps.md` row `2` — which filed the locally composed focus mirror as a FINDING — is superseded by this ruling

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-06 · **Source (user, verbatim):** *"Perform the data formatting logic on the renderer side, before pushing data into IPC. The IPC event should be able to push directly to the appropriate store verb without further processing"*

**WHAT MOVES AND WHAT DOES NOT, STATED AS THREE SEPARATE THINGS BECAUSE THE RULING NAMES ONE OF THEM.** **FORMATTING** — shaping a payload into the value the store will hold: the whole family at its root, a pane's complete member pair, the theme's declared record — **moves to the renderer**. **ENFORCEMENT** — the constraint pairs, their admit arms and their repairs — **stays where it is**: a judge is not a formatter, and `panesMustStand`/`theme-must-stand` are what make a formatted value legal or repair what is missing. **TRANSPORT** — the channel, the `ipcMain.handle` site, the preload member — **becomes pass-through**: `store.write(address, tier, value)` and nothing in front of it.

**WHAT THAT DISSOLVES, AND WHY THE DISSOLUTION IS SAFE.** `viewSurface.commitPane`'s shape arms and `record.commitPane`'s `composePanes(standing, id, supplied)` are FORMATTING, so under this ruling they leave the crossing and the handler answers the store verb directly. **THE GUARD THAT REMAINS IS THE STRONGER ONE**: `panesMustStand`'s value arm REFUSES a standing value that is no geometry and writes no repair (`D-105`), so a badly formatted payload is refused with the previous bytes exactly as they stood — never coerced, never dropped member by member. **THE FORMATTER LOSES THE ABILITY TO BE LENIENT AND GAINS THE ABILITY TO BE WRONG IN THE OPEN**, which is the trade this ruling makes deliberately.

**THE ONE COST, NAMED RATHER THAN DISCOVERED: THE RENDERER COMPOSES FROM A ONE-DELIVERY-STALE SHAPE.** The writer holds the shape the last delivery left (`D-117`'s measured wording), so a whole-family composition done there can drop a member another window wrote in between. **THAT IS THE PRICE OF THE RULING AND IT IS CHOSEN WITH EYES OPEN**: the hazard is bounded to the interval between a delivery and the next write, one window writes each family in this app, and the alternative — a crossing that formats — is what the ruling removes. **A PASS THAT IMPLEMENTS THIS MUST SAY WHICH OF THE TWO IT RELIES ON**, and a second window joining a family is the condition that reopens it.

**AND `next-steps.md` ROW `2` IS SUPERSEDED BY POINTER AND NOT REWORDED** (`D-46`). That row filed *"THE FOCUS FAMILY'S MIRROR IS COMPOSED LOCALLY WHERE THE ZONES AND PANE FAMILIES COMPOSE AT THE CROSSING"* as a finding, with `D-117`'s reading of the one-delivery-stale shape as its evidence. **THIS RULING MAKES THE LOCAL COMPOSITION THE PATTERN AND THE CROSSING COMPOSITION THE THING TO REMOVE**, so the row's PREMISE inverts: the focus family is now the example and the other three are the work. The staleness the row named does not vanish — it becomes the cost stated above, and the row is re-pointed at it rather than deleted, because the measurement it carried is still true and now describes a deliberate trade rather than a defect.

**WHAT THIS DOES TO THE WORK IN FLIGHT.** `D-132`'s `temp` write is UNAFFECTED AND SIMPLER: a renderer-formatted value at a declared `temp` root goes through `data.write` — already a pass-through — with no composition anywhere on the main side. `D-133`/row `6`'s `readTiered` composition is UNAFFECTED: it is a READ shape, and this ruling is about writes. The drag's `file` commit keeps its route but loses its composer, so `{ size, bounds }` must be formatted in the renderer from the shape — which is precisely the merge `D-135` identified as the composer's real reason for existing.

**THE MEASUREMENTS AT THIS HEAD ARE `D-134`'S**, since this row changes no byte: `npm test` `2,991 passed / 2 skipped` of `2,993` over `137` files and all nine legs green over the `1.10.0` bump.

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-135`** as prose, unchanged from `D-135`'s and extended by that row only. **This row moves the formatting seat, keeps the enforcement seat, names the staleness cost of the trade, and re-points the one finding whose premise it inverts.**

## D-137 — ONE EVENT PER PLACEMENT CHANGE, AND THE PUSH IS WHAT KEEPS THE RENDERER'S COPY HONEST: a drag that moves one pane updates the location data through ONE event, the read-out is painted from the delivery rather than written beside it, and an external writer is handled by the same push — which is what makes `D-136`'s local formatting safe instead of stale

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-06 · **Source (user, verbatim):** *"When the pane drag is only changing the placement of the one dragged pane, there should only be one event updating the pane location data, and if for some reason there was an external event also manipulating the data, it should be triggering the renderer to update, keeping the renderer's data copy in sync."*

**THE FIRST CLAUSE CLOSES `D-126`'S FINDING `F3` BY DECISION, AND IT IS A DUPLICATE WRITE THAT GOES.** Today one placement change produces **two** updates of the same datum: the release turn's `sink(final)` writes `#gutter-status`'s `content` at the act's own turn, and `view.commitPane(id, {size})` writes `['panes']` at `file` a moment later. **THERE IS ONE DATUM AND THERE MUST BE ONE EVENT** — so the sink's write is removed and the read-out is PAINTED FROM THE DELIVERY, which already fires for the family. The two authorities `F3` named are therefore not to be reconciled; one of them leaves.

**THE SECOND CLAUSE IS THE CONDITION THAT MAKES `D-136`'S TRADE SOUND, AND IT RETIRES THE "COST" `D-136` BOOKED.** That row moved formatting into the renderer and named the price: *the renderer composes from a one-delivery-stale shape*. **THE ANSWER IS NOT TO AVOID LOCAL COMPOSITION — IT IS THAT EXTERNAL WRITES MUST REACH THE RENDERER, AND THEY ALREADY DO**: `createViewRecord`'s `subscribe` registers a listener per address and the surface forwards every one to `webContents.send`, so a write from anywhere — `data.write`, another window, a repair — moves the renderer's copy through the same delivery the drag's own commit uses. **THE COPY IS KEPT IN SYNC BY THE PUSH AND NOT BY THE WRITER'S DISCIPLINE**, which is the only form of the guarantee that survives a writer the renderer does not own.

**AND IT PUTS A HARD REQUIREMENT ON THE `temp` ADDRESS, STATED BEFORE IT IS BUILT.** A `temp` root that the record's subscription array does not cover would be a write the renderer never hears — the one place this clause can silently fail, and exactly the class `D-126`'s audit found five of. **`D-132`'s `temp` root therefore joins that array in the same edit that adds it**, and the acceptance for row `5` names the external-writer arm: a write to the temp root from outside the drag must move the ghost, or the row is not closed.

**WHAT THIS LEAVES `F3` AS.** A DECIDED finding, not an open one: the sink's write to `#gutter-status` is deleted, the read-out is rendered from the delivery the family already produces, and the second authority disappears with it. `pending.md` row `1` records that its work is now one edit rather than a design question, and the finding stays listed there until the edit lands, because a decision is not a landing (`D-3`).

**THE MEASUREMENTS AT THIS HEAD ARE `D-134`'S**, since this row changes no byte: `npm test` `2,991 passed / 2 skipped` of `2,993` over `137` files and all nine legs green over the `1.10.0` bump.

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-136`** as prose, unchanged from `D-136`'s and extended by that row only. **This row removes a duplicate write by decision, converts `D-136`'s booked cost into a guarantee the push provides, and makes the temp root's subscription a stated precondition of row `5` rather than a detail of its implementation.**

## D-138 — THE DRAG CHANGES ONE LOCATION MEMBER, SO IT WRITES THAT LEAF BY ADDRESS: enforcement of the pane's shape AND of its ancestry is what lets the renderer skip composition entirely, `D-137`'s "one event" was about COLLISION and not about collapsing the two-phase drag, and the drag-update/release-commit style STAYS

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-06 · **Source (user, verbatim):** *"Clarification: The pane drag is only changing the location. The data structure of the pane, including location, can be enforced, and with the pane ancestry being enforceable, the address of a given pane's location data can be enforced to exist and be accurate. If the constraints are set up correctly, the renderer can write the data by address directly, without having to compose anything. The drag-update, release-commit style should stay, the instruction was about colliding with another unrelated action also attempting to modify the location data."*

**`D-137`'S FIRST CLAUSE WAS MY MIS-READING, AND IT IS CORRECTED HERE RATHER THAN QUIETLY REPAIRED.** I read *"there should only be one event updating the pane location data"* as an instruction to DELETE the sink's write to `#gutter-status` and close `D-126`'s finding `F3` by decision. **THE INSTRUCTION WAS ABOUT COLLISION** — an unrelated action attempting to modify the location data at the same time — **and the DRAG-UPDATE / RELEASE-COMMIT STYLE STAYS** (`D-132`'s two-phase flow, unamended). The one-event sentence is a statement about the DATUM having one owner, not about removing a turn, so `F3` is **NOT decided by `D-137`** and `pending.md` row `1` is re-pointed to say so.

**WHAT DOES FOLLOW, AND IT IS THE LARGER RESULT: A LEAF WRITE NEEDS NO COMPOSITION AT ALL.** The drag changes ONE member — the pane's location — so the write is `['panes', paneId, 'size']`, a leaf of an enforced structure. **`composePanes`' whole purpose was to avoid dropping `bounds` when a size-only act arrived at the ROOT; a write AT THE LEAF cannot drop it**, because it does not touch the sibling member. So the merge `D-135` identified as the composer's real reason, and the renderer-side formatting `D-136` moved out of the crossing, both reduce to **an address and a scalar** — and `D-136`'s booked staleness cost DISSOLVES for this write: **a leaf write needs no copy of the family, so there is nothing for a stale copy to corrupt.**

**AND THE PRECONDITION IS PER LEVEL, NOT ONE DECLARATION, WHICH IS THE PART THAT HAS TO BE BUILT.** The store will not create a parent (measured), so a nested write lands only where the ancestry already stands: `['panes']` (guaranteed today by `repairPanes`) and `['panes', paneId]` (guaranteed today only for the DEFAULT pane the repair writes). The declaration judges nested nodes already — `panesMustStand`'s `nodeOf` judges *"the node the write is at"*, so `['panes','pane-a']` and `['panes','pane-a','size']` are each judged as themselves — but JUDGING is not CREATING. **WHAT THE RULING REQUIRES IS THAT THE REPAIR GUARANTEE THE RECORD OF EVERY PANE THE APP ADDRESSES**, so a nested location write always finds its parent present and accurate — the `D-85` pattern of an absence-keyed repair that PERFORMS the thing it keys, not a marker that asserts it.

**THE COLLISION CLAUSE, IN ITS OWN TERMS.** If an unrelated action modifies the location data, the renderer's copy must move: the family's push already fires once per address (`createViewRecord`'s subscription loop and the surface's `webContents.send`), and `D-137`'s second clause stands as filed — the delivery is what keeps the renderer honest, and the `temp` root's subscription is a precondition of row `5`. **WHAT CHANGES HERE IS ONLY THE READING OF THE FIRST CLAUSE**: one OWNER for the datum, two phases for the gesture, and a push that carries any other writer's change to the renderer.

**WHAT THE NEXT PASS THEREFORE IS, SAID AS THREE EDITS RATHER THAN ONE REFACTOR.** (i) The pane record's own existence enforced per pane id, so any addressed pane's ancestry stands; (ii) the composition deleted from the write path — `commitPane` answers `store.write(['panes', paneId, 'size'], PANES_TIER, size)` and the renderer names the leaf, per `D-136`'s seat with `D-138`'s simplification; (iii) the read-out rendered from the delivery rather than written by the sink, which is `F3`'s own edit and not this row's decision.

**THE MEASUREMENTS AT THIS HEAD ARE `D-134`'S**, since this row changes no byte: `npm test` `2,991 passed / 2 skipped` of `2,993` over `137` files and all nine legs green over the `1.10.0` bump.

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-137`** as prose, unchanged from `D-137`'s and extended by that row only. **This row corrects a mis-reading, states the leaf-write result and the per-level ancestry it depends on, and keeps the two-phase gesture exactly where `D-132` put it.**

## D-139 — THE COMPOSITION INVENTORY: nine composers stand in `src/`, each judged by `D-138`'s one test — *is the write a LEAF of an enforced structure?* — and the answer splits them into three that are removable, three that must stay because they are DERIVATIONS, and three that need a decision this row does not take

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-06 · **Source (user, verbatim):** *"Proceed, and then list the other instances of composition to review for need"*

**THE TEST, AND WHY IT IS THE ONLY ONE THAT MATTERS.** `D-138` established that a composer exists for one reason: **to avoid a root write that replaces a value whose siblings must survive**. A write at a LEAF cannot destroy a sibling, so a leaf write needs no composer — **provided the ancestry is enforced to exist**, since the store creates no parent. Every composer below is judged by exactly those two questions, and no third.

**REMOVABLE ONCE THE ANCESTRY IS ENFORCED — the same argument as the panes, and nothing beyond it.**
**`composePanes`** (`src/main/view-record/index.ts:1210`, `commitPane`): the drag changes one member; `['panes', paneId, 'size']` cannot drop `bounds`. This is `D-138`'s own case.
**`composeZones`** (`:1313`, `commitZones`): its own docblock states the same reason — *"one act cannot erase a sibling's slot or its size"* — and a zone's four members (`slot`/`distance`/`size`/`display`) are leaves, so the argument transfers whole and the precondition is the same: `['zones', id]` must be guaranteed to stand.
**`composeMirror`'s ACTIVE RULE** (`:758`, `commitFocus`): only its `null → absence` clause is formatting, because `null` is refused at the boundary and `undefined` on a member is the store's declared absence. **THE MIRROR'S LIST IS NOT REMOVABLE** — see below — so this one splits.

**NOT REMOVABLE, BECAUSE THEY ARE DERIVATIONS AND NOT FORMATTING.**
**`themeMustStand`'s `whole`** (`:1081`): the declaration is RESOLVED (`resolveTheme(token, attributeName)`) — a member no caller sent, computed from the act. `D-136` moved formatting and kept enforcement; a resolution is neither, and it stays where the family's knowledge lives.
**Every repair's default** — `repairPanes`' `composePanes([], DEFAULT_PANE_ID, …)` (`:964`), `focusMustStand`'s `composeMirror({entries: [], activeId: null})` (`:998`), `zonesMustStand`'s `{}` (`:1040`): a repair's subject is data that is MISSING, so it composes the placeholder by definition.
**`writeWholeValue`** (`:385`): the repairs' one writer. A whole-value write is correct for a REPAIR and wrong for an ACT, and the distinction is the whole of `D-138`.

**NEED A DECISION THIS ROW DOES NOT TAKE — named so they are not silently left.**
**`composeMirror`'s ENTRIES** (`:1261`): the focus mirror carries a LIST. An act can open, close or reorder tabs, and a leaf write cannot express a list mutation — so either the crossing keeps a list composer or the app gains a per-entry address with its own ancestry. **This is `next-steps.md` row `2`'s open half**, and `D-136`'s "the renderer formats" does not answer it.
**`composeRecord` + `writeJoined` + `TabsCommitCarrier`** (`src/main/tabs-record/index.ts:540`, `:349`): the tab record is written WHOLE at `['tabs']` — a nested write creates no parent (the module's own note quotes the measured rule) — and its write is joined to a carrier so a repair can name its commit (`D-73`). **The largest instance in the tree and the one least like a leaf**: per-tab ancestry plus a per-tab address is a design change, not a simplification.
**`toStored(importOnce(userDataDir) ?? FIRST_RUN)`** (`src/main/operator-settings/index.ts:931`): the creation repair's row→stored mapping. It is formatting in the crossing, but its subject is a FILE the repair imported, so no renderer can supply it — **it stays unless the import itself moves**.

**AND THE ONE THAT IS ALREADY THE PATTERN.** `answerForCarrier` (`src/renderer/renderer.ts:793`) composes the focus answer in the RENDERER — `D-117` filed it as a finding, `D-136` made local formatting the pattern, and `D-138`'s leaf result makes it the *simplest* form of it. It is listed here because a reader comparing the two lists will look for it.

**NOT COMPOSERS, LISTED SO THE INVENTORY IS CLOSED.** `src/main/data/index.ts`'s five routes compose nothing — they validate a path and a tier and forward, which is why a declared `temp` root needs no crossing work. The renderer's `reading(...)` objects and the overlay's `renderTurn` declaration are ANSWERS, not stored values.

**THE THREE EDITS `D-138` NAMES ARE NOT LANDED IN THIS ROW, AND THE INVENTORY IS WHY THEY SHOULD COME IN THIS ORDER**: the panes edit's blast radius is `pane-drag-compliance`'s write-set rows and the view-record batteries, and knowing that `composeZones` moves with it — and that `composeMirror`'s list does NOT — is what keeps that pass to ONE change instead of two that disagree. `strata/next-steps.md` row `2` carries it.

**THE MEASUREMENTS AT THIS HEAD ARE `D-134`'S**, since this row changes no byte: `npm test` `2,991 passed / 2 skipped` of `2,993` over `137` files and all nine legs green over the `1.10.0` bump.

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-138`** as prose, unchanged from `D-138`'s and extended by that row only. **This row inventories every composer in the tree against one test, sorts them into removable / must-stay / undecided, and refuses to start the removal before the list is known.**

## D-140 — `composeMirror` FORMATS A STATE THE APP FORBIDS: there is required to be EXACTLY ONE active tab — enforced today by the tab record's own `exactly-one-active` constraint and DERIVED rather than stored by `landingOf` — so `activeId` cannot legitimately be absent, the composer's one spelling rule has no job, and the mirror's active member is a SECOND SPELLING of a fact `['tabs']` already enforces

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-06 · **Source (user, verbatim):** *"This refers to the active tab tracking. There is required to be exactly one active tab, thus there cannot be a null value that needs the mirror composition"*

**THE INVARIANT IS ALREADY IN THE TREE, AND IT IS THE TAB RECORD'S.** `src/main/tabs-record/index.ts:12` states *"THE INVARIANTS ARE THE ENGINE'S (design §4): `exactly-one-active`, the three field pairs, the EXISTENCE pair that mints …"*, and `TABS_CONSTRAINT_ID = 'exactly-one-active'` (`:192`) is that constraint's own name. `landingOf` (`:52`) derives the active row's id — *"DERIVED, NEVER STORED (D-76)"* — and the tabs client's witness is the same rule measured once: `activeIds.length === 1 && activeIds[0] === answer.landing`.

**SO `composeMirror`'S SPECIFIC CLAUSE HAS NO JOB.** Its docblock justifies the spelling by a MEASURED refusal — *"`null` is refused at the crossing's boundary and `undefined` with it"* — and that measurement is true. **BUT IT IS FORMATTING FOR A STATE THE APP'S OWN INVARIANT MAKES ILLEGAL**: if exactly one tab is active, `activeId` is always a value, so the absent-member arm expresses nothing the product can reach. **A COMPOSER WHOSE ONLY REASON IS A FORBIDDEN STATE IS ITSELF UNNECESSARY**, which is `D-139`'s "removable" class reached by a different route than the leaf argument.

**AND IT SETTLES THE REVIEW-FOR-NEED QUESTION I RAISED LAST TURN.** `['tabs']` enforces exactly-one-active at `file` and `landingOf` derives the active id from the rows; `['focus'].activeId` at `mem` is a SECOND SPELLING of that same fact, held as a working copy whose declared authority is the tab list. **TWO TRACKINGS OF ONE INVARIANT IS THE DEFINITION OF A SECOND AUTHORITY, HOWEVER CAREFULLY THE DOCBLOCK DISCLAIMS IT** — and the disclaimer is what makes it survivable today rather than what makes it right.

**WHAT FOLLOWS, IN THE ORDER IT SHOULD BE TAKEN.** (i) `composeMirror` reduces to a COPY — `{ entries: mirror.entries.map((e) => ({ ...e })) }` — with the absent-active clause deleted; `mirrorFrom`'s `active → null` arm STAYS as the defensive read it must be for a corrupt record, and stops describing a product state. (ii) **`focusMustStand`'s placeholder cannot be `composeMirror({entries: [], activeId: null})`** — a repair writing an absent active asserts the forbidden state, so the placeholder must seat an active id or the family's existence must be tied to the tab record's. (iii) The larger question stays with the user: whether the `focus` ROUTE is a product need or was the vehicle for `U-STORE-FOCUS`'s re-homing demonstration (`D-139`'s note) — and if it is the vehicle, `activeId` should be READ from `['tabs']` rather than stored beside it.

**WHAT THIS DOES TO ROW `2`.** The mirror's LIST was its non-leaf half and therefore its defence; the active member never had one. **THE LIST SURVIVES THIS ROW AND THE ACTIVE MEMBER DOES NOT**, so row `2` narrows again: the composition question there is the entries list alone, and the `activeId` half is a deletion with a repair to re-seat.

**THE MEASUREMENTS AT THIS HEAD ARE `D-134`'S**, since this row changes no byte: `npm test` `2,991 passed / 2 skipped` of `2,993` over `137` files and all nine legs green over the `1.10.0` bump.

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-139`** as prose, unchanged from `D-139`'s and extended by that row only. **This row removes a composer's justification by naming the invariant that contradicts it, identifies the mirror's active member as a second spelling, and leaves the route's own future to the user.**

## D-141 — THE `focus` TOOL IS A PRODUCT NEED AND IT MUST WRITE THE ACTIVE TAB DIRECTLY: an agent moves the UI and loads tab-dependent data through it, so the act belongs in the TAB RECORD's own terms at its own `file` tier — the `['focus']` mirror is a PROXY for an invariant that already has an owner, and it is `mem`, which cannot persist the thing the tool exists to change

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-06 · **Source (user, verbatim):** *"The focus tool is meant for MCP use so that the agent can move the UI and load tab-dependent data. The tool needs to persist, but it should be changing the active tab directly instead of a proxy, as again the active tab property should be required to exist"*

**THE TOOL STAYS, AND `D-139`/`D-140`'S OPEN QUESTION IS ANSWERED BY THIS ROW.** I had asked whether the `focus` route was a product need or the vehicle for `U-STORE-FOCUS`'s re-homing demonstration. **IT IS A PRODUCT NEED** — the agent's means of moving the UI and loading tab-dependent data — so nothing here deletes it. What changes is WHERE ITS ACT LANDS.

**THE THREE DEFECTS THE SENTENCE NAMES AT ONCE, AND THEY COMPOUND.** (i) **IT IS A PROXY**: `activeId` in `['focus']` is a second spelling of the fact `['tabs']` enforces as `exactly-one-active` and `landingOf` derives (`D-140`). (ii) **IT IS THE WRONG TIER**: the mirror is `mem`, so the state an agent moves the UI with *does not survive the process* — the tool cannot do what the user says it exists for, because the thing it persists is in the one tier that persists nothing. (iii) **IT CHANGES A DERIVED FACT RATHER THAN ITS INPUT**: the active tab is the id of the row reading active, so writing `activeId` records a conclusion and leaves the rows saying something else.

**WHAT THE ACT BECOMES.** The `focus` route seats the active tab **in the tab record's own terms** — the rows' own active flags and the order that holds them, written whole at `['tabs']` at `file`, with `exactly-one-active` doing the judging it already does. **THE INVARIANT IS THEN ENFORCED ON THE WRITE THE TOOL MAKES, NOT ON A COPY BESIDE IT**, the value is durable, and the renderer learns of it through the delivery the tab record already pushes — which is the sync arrangement `D-137` requires and the mirror could never supply across a restart.

**WHAT IS LEFT OF `['focus']`, STATED SO IT IS NOT ASSUMED AWAY.** If the mirror holds nothing but the entries and the active id, both of which the tab record owns (`order` and the rows), then the family has no member the act needs and `focus-must-stand` has nothing to guard. **THAT DELETION IS A DECISION AND NOT A CONSEQUENCE** — the focus MODEL (`src/shared/focus-model.ts`) may still be worth exercising over the tab record's state, and a model reading `['tabs']` needs no carrier of its own. **THE ROW THAT CARRIES THIS IS `next-steps.md` ROW `2`**, now narrowed to: seat the active tab in the tab record from the `focus` route, delete `composeMirror`'s active clause with its repair, and settle whether the focus family survives at all.

**AND IT RETIRES `D-140`'S STEP (iii) AND THE QUESTION I RAISED ABOUT ROW `2`'S SIZE**: the mirror's entries list stops being a composition problem the moment the entries ARE the tab record's `order`, so row `2` is a DELETION and a WRITE RETARGET rather than a formatting move across three families.

**THE MEASUREMENTS AT THIS HEAD ARE `D-134`'S**, since this row changes no byte: `npm test` `2,991 passed / 2 skipped` of `2,993` over `137` files and all nine legs green over the `1.10.0` bump.

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-140`** as prose, unchanged from `D-140`'s and extended by that row only. **This row keeps the tool, moves its act into the record that owns the invariant, names the tier contradiction that made the proxy unable to persist, and narrows row `2` to a deletion.**

## D-142 — DROP `focus`: the family goes whole — address, tier, declaration, declared-root entry, route and mirror — and the `focus` tool instead SETS THE NEW ACTIVE TAB and lets the tab record's repair drop the older one, which is a normalisation the record ALREADY performs

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-06 · **Source (user, verbatim):** *"Drop focus. The change should be able to operate by setting the new active tab and letting the repair drop the older one when it detects the exactly-one-active violation"*

**THE REPAIR THE RULING ASKS FOR ALREADY EXISTS, AND THAT IS WHAT MAKES THIS A DELETION RATHER THAN A BUILD.** `src/main/tabs-record/index.ts`'s healing body (`healRecord`, four call sites) carries an **ACTIVE NORMALISATION**: its own comment says the record *"converges on the next evaluation instead of re-violating"* and *"lets the active normalisation follow them"*, and it states that **no witness rides in the repair any more** — *"the landing member is [derived] … one active row among them IS the landing the next read answers"*. So a write that seats a second active row is normalised down to one by the record's own repair, at the address the invariant is registered at, judged by `exactly-one-active` (`TABS_CONSTRAINT_ID`). **THE ACT THE USER DESCRIBES IS AVAILABLE TODAY; what is missing is the ROUTE that makes it, and the family that must go.**

**WHAT IS DELETED, NAMED ONE BY ONE BECAUSE A FAMILY IS NOT ONE FILE.** `['focus']`: its address constant and `FOCUS_TIER`; the `focus-must-stand` declaration and its body; the `viewDeclaredRoots` entry (`D-101`'s declared-tier guard, which would otherwise refuse a legal write at a root nobody declares); the record's `commitFocus`, `composeMirror` and `mirrorFrom`; `view-surface`'s `commitFocus`; the channel constant, the `ipcMain.handle` site, the preload member and the `secure-panels` lockstep half; `view-client`'s route and its focus reads; and the renderer's focus role, `answerForCarrier`, `mirrorStateOf` and `persistTarget`. **THE VIEW RECORD'S FOUR ROOTS BECOME THREE**, so every document that counts them moves with the change — `docs/L2/view-record/interface.md` and every row that reads `viewConstraintDeclarations`' or `viewDeclaredRoots`' set (`declared-root.test.ts`'s equality row is the one that will say so).

**WHAT REPLACES IT, AND WHY IT IS SMALLER THAN WHAT IT REPLACES.** The `focus` route keeps its MCP name and its purpose — an agent moves the UI and loads tab-dependent data — and its act becomes **ONE WRITE AT `['tabs']`** that seats the new active row, with the record's own normalisation settling the pair. So the route goes from a proxy family (`mem`, unpersisted, a second spelling of a derived fact) to the record that owns the invariant (`file`, durable, enforced on the write itself) — and `D-137`'s sync requirement is met by the tab record's own push, which already exists.

**THE ORDER, BECAUSE ONE OF THESE MUST NOT LAND ALONE.** **RETARGET FIRST, THEN DELETE**: a focus tool pointed at the mirror while the mirror still stands is a working tool; a deleted family with a tool still pointed at it is a route that refuses. And the deletion must take the `viewDeclaredRoots` entry IN THE SAME EDIT as the declaration, or the declared-tier guard reds on a legal write (`D-101`'s own rule) — the same condition `D-132`/row `5` carries for the `temp` root.

**WHAT THIS DOES TO `D-140` AND `D-141`.** `D-140`'s three steps resolve BY DELETION rather than by editing: `composeMirror`'s absent-active clause disappears with `composeMirror`; `focusMustStand`'s placeholder becomes nothing because the declaration becomes nothing; and the "second spelling" stops being a spelling. `D-141`'s "what is left of `['focus']`" is answered: nothing, so `focus-must-stand` has nothing to guard and goes.

**AND RETARGETING FINDS A GAP THE DELETION WOULD OTHERWISE EXPOSE — THE RENDERER HAS NO TAB-RECORD WRITE ROUTE AT ALL.** `src/renderer/tabs-client.ts` declares the bridge member as `read` and `onChanged` and nothing else — *"TWO of L2 §2's four routes and not the other two"* — so `focus` cannot "set the new active tab" through the `tabs` member today. **TWO LAWFUL SHAPES, AND THE CHOICE IS THE NEXT PASS'S FIRST DECISION**: (a) a write route on the tabs surface, which is five lockstep sites and puts formatting at the crossing — the seat `D-136` moved AWAY from; or (b) the renderer composes the rows from its own committed reading and writes `{order, landing, rows}` through `data.write({path:['tabs'], tier:'file'})`, which is `D-136`'s seat and reuses a route that already exists — at the cost of one whole-record composition, which is exactly what `pending.md` row `2` books. **NEITHER IS TAKEN HERE**; the gap is recorded so the next pass chooses rather than discovering it mid-deletion.

**THE MEASUREMENTS AT THIS HEAD ARE `D-134`'S**, since this row changes no byte: `npm test` `2,991 passed / 2 skipped` of `2,993` over `137` files and all nine legs green over the `1.10.0` bump.

**AND THE USER SETTLED `(a)` VERSUS `(b)` — THE POINT OF THE COMPOSE CHANGES IS A TRANSPARENT IPC PASSTHROUGH** (`2026-11-06`, verbatim: *"The point of the compose changes is to allow the IPC to be a transparent passthrough"*). So **`(b)` IS THE SHAPE**: no write route is added to the tabs surface, because a route that composes is exactly the processing this ruling removes; the renderer composes the record from its own committed reading and hands it to `data.write({path:['tabs'], tier:'file'})`, whose handler forwards to the store's write verb. **"TRANSPARENT" MEANS NO TRANSFORMATION AND NOT NO REFUSAL**: `data.write` still answers `malformed-arguments`, `declared-tier`, `parent-absent` and the store's own refusals, because those are the STORE'S judgement passing THROUGH rather than the surface's decision — the distinction `D-136` drew between formatting, which moves, and enforcement, which does not. **THE COMPOSITION THAT REMAINS IS BOOKED AND NOT HIDDEN**: it is one whole-record composition in the renderer, which is `pending.md` row `2`'s `composeRecord` entry, and the pass that takes it either keeps it with that reason beside it or finds a per-tab address whose ancestry can be enforced.

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-141`** as prose, unchanged from `D-141`'s and extended by that row only. **This row deletes a family, names every site the deletion reaches, orders the two halves so neither lands alone, and records that the repair the user asked for was already there.**

## D-143 — THE FOCUS ACT LANDS ON THE TAB RECORD AND THE MIRROR FAMILY IS GONE: unit `focus-retirement` delivers `D-141`/`D-142` across five modules, the `refused` member's SHAPE is adjudicated to the WIDENED ledger because the ruling's "no transformation" governs members as well as values, and the blind exit walkthrough reads STALE on document currency and never on the surface

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-07 · **Source (measurement):** the four legs, the instruments and the walkthrough taken by this seat at this head, against the user's rulings `D-141`/`D-142` and the unit's L1 artifact.

**WHAT LANDED, ONE ROW PER MODULE** — `E-1`…`E-12` of `docs/L1/focus-retirement.md` §6, one writer each (`D-3`). **`renderer-wiring`**: the focus carrier, its four members, its two helpers and their headstones are deleted; the route is ONE body — read the tab record's committed reading, `composeRecord` it (`order` preserved, rows copied whole, the target row seated, the standing active row LEFT AS CARRIED), hand it to `data.write({path:['tabs'], tier:'file'})`, answer the record as re-read (`D-136`, `D-137`, `D-142`). **`store-view-record`**: the family is deleted whole — address, tier constant, declaration and body, `mirrorFrom`, `composeMirror`, the surface route, the channel constant, the client route and reads, both shared types — with the `viewDeclaredRoots` entry in the SAME hunk as the declaration (`D-101`), and four roots become three. **`app-shell` + `secure-panels`**: one edit across the twice-declared `view` member — the member, the `read` answer's `focus` and `source.focus`, and the `ViewFocusMirror` specifier (`D-112`). **`mcp-surface`**: name, title, `{target?, newTab?}`, gate group and the one invoke seam unmoved; the description and both schema sentences re-grained to the record's act (`D-18`). **`focus-model`**: retained as a design with no `src/` reader (`D-141` left it open), its carrier battery `tests/store-focus.test.ts` retired whole with its referent (`D-9`).

**THE ONE DESIGN QUESTION THE RULING DID NOT SPELL, AND THE READING THAT ANSWERS IT.** The tool's answer carries a refused write's outcome. Two shapes were lawful on paper: a three-member rebuild (`status`/`reason`/`message`) or the writer's own ledger. **THE SEAM RESOLVED TO THE WIDENED LEDGER** — `status`/`reason`/`message` always, plus `storeStatus`/`path`/`log` **where the writer carried them**, each spread in only where answered and never a wholesale spread — because `D-142` says the refusals are *"the STORE'S judgement passing THROUGH"* and *"no transformation"*, and a rebuild that keeps the sentence and drops the evidence has selected which of the store's members an agent may see, which is a transformation. The narrowing is recorded as the rejected arm with its cost, so the choice is reversible by one row.

**THE READINGS AT THIS HEAD.** `npm test` **`2,904 passed / 2 skipped` of `2,906`** over `137` files; `typecheck` **0**; `typecheck:tests` **0**; `build` **exit 0** — the four-leg floor. `crossings` **`28` rows, `0` failures** on a live `DISPLAY`, its `['focus']` subject retired with the family (`E-8`). `strata-marks.mjs check` **exit 0** over `295` gating sites in `40` files and `2,386` on notice. `strata-lint` exits `1` with its ONE accepted `register` FAIL and nothing else. The `panes`/`battery`/`divergence`/`ui` legs were NOT re-taken — this unit moves no pane, battery or overlay machinery — and a leg not measured is not claimed.

**AND THE EXIT WALKTHROUGH'S VERDICT IS `STALE`, WHICH IS A DOCUMENT DEFECT AND NOT A SURFACE ONE.** Twelve blind documentation steps were settled from the corpus; the graded defects are pages that still announce a licence as UNLANDED where `src/` already carries the landed text (seven documents across `mcp-surface`, `central-data`, `secure-panels`, `renderer-wiring` at L2 and `view-record`, `app-shell`, `secure-panels` at L3), plus one dead subject in `proposals.md` and one off-by-one in this brief's source count — the last two are L0's and are closed in this row's edits. **THE ROUTING IS THE OWNING LAYERS',** each over its own document (`walkthrough.md` §5, `D-3`), and the loop recommendation is that currency pass before any third run: the unit is NOT recommended as ready on a `STALE`, and this row presents it as landed-and-green with the walkthrough still owed.

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-142`** as prose, unchanged and extended by this row only. **This row records the landing of a user ruling, the one shape decision the ruling left open and the ground it was read on, the readings at this head, and a walkthrough verdict that routes to the documents rather than to the code.**

## D-144 — `data.read` ANSWERS EVERY NODE'S OWN `_tier` AND THE SEAM GAINS NO BYTE: unit `tier-readout` lands row `6` over `1.10.0`'s `readTiered` — one call, the release's own node shape, the arms that gain nothing — and the marked batteries re-grain because the leg's green is the whole tree's

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-07 · **Source:** the user's finding carried by `D-133` (*"The returned object should already include the tier data. If it doesn't that is a defect."*), the adoption `D-134` landed, and this seat's measurements at this head.

**WHAT LANDED, AND WHY IT IS A READOUT AND NOT AN ECHO.** `D-133`'s per-call echo was superseded by pointer before it was built, because one call can return nodes standing at **different** tiers. The answer's `value` is now the release's own `TieredNode` — reached as `ReturnType<ProfileStore['readTiered']>`, **one spelling** and no seam re-export (`D-53`) — so an object node answers `{_tier, …members}` inline, an array `{_tier, value:[…]}`, a leaf `{_tier, value:<literal>}`, and `_tier` is one token and never a set (`TierSet` is the argument's type). `read` makes **ONE** `readTiered` call, omitting the tier position when the caller named none (`D-101`: the tier is the caller's), and `kind` is **compiled from the node received**: `'row'` iff the node has two own keys with `value` among them and a primitive `value`, else `'branch'` — so an object whose own member is named `value` and every array node answer `branch`.

**THE ARMS THAT GAIN NOTHING, AND THE ONE THAT CHANGED ITS CLAIM.** The `absent` arm and every `DataLedger` arm carry no node and no tier — a refusal that reached no store has no tier to name, the same reading that keeps `log` off those arms (`D-107`). **The cold-profile arm moved its claim**: `read {path: []}` on a profile whose store was never constructed answers `absent` with no node, where it used to answer a branch the module had composed; on a live profile it answers the **store's own** empty register node, `{_tier}` alone, never `{}`.

**THE SEAM IS THE PIN, NOT A BYTE**: `src/main/storeroom.ts`, `mapper.ts`, `args.ts`, `store.ts` and `journal.ts` are unchanged (`H-4` verified by `git diff --name-only`), so `S-9`'s census stands at `15` members over `13` symbols with the verb third, and `list` is **unmoved** — its answer is addresses and a count, and no node carries a readout.

**THE RULING THIS UNIT NEEDED, AND ITS MEASUREMENT.** The four **marked** unit batteries and three marked integration files pin bare values, and `D-30` could be read as leaving them on notice. **THE LEG'S GREEN IS THE WHOLE TREE'S GREEN**: `npm test` is the stock runner over the whole tree with no mark-aware split (`brief.md` §6 says so in its own words), so a marked row that pins a superseded answer is a **red on the floor a unit closes on**. The fourteen unit sites and ten integration sites were therefore re-grained **in place**, their markers standing, no id retired — and one site's cast was fixed rather than the type (the probe moved onto the node's own `value`), because a cast through `unknown` would have preserved a shape the release no longer answers.

**THE READINGS AT THIS HEAD.** `npm test` **`2,915 passed / 2 skipped` of `2,917`** over `139` files, exit `0`; `typecheck` **0**; `typecheck:tests` **0**; `build` **0**; `npm run crossings` **`0` failures, `28` rows** live on `:0` (its `X-1`/`X-3`/`X-5`/`X-6`/`X-7`/`X-31` re-grained, `X-31`'s claim **strengthened** — a deleted address now answers `absent` with member set exactly `{status, path}`); `crossings` `28` rows / `78` driver calls in `brief.md` §6, re-taken; marks `306` gating sites in `42` files; `strata-lint` exits `1` with its ONE accepted `register` FAIL.

**AND THE EXIT WALKTHROUGH READS `STALE` ON CURRENCY, NOT ON THE CONTRACT** — ten blind steps all resolved and every central claim agrees byte for byte with `src/` and `dist/`, while `central-data`'s `behaviour.md` and `persistence.md`, `interface.md` §9's `H-4` sentence and `docs/L3/central-data/design.md`'s status still speak the pre-tiered state (`strata/records/tier-readout-walkthrough.md`). **The row LEFT `next-steps.md` with its work landed (`D-61`) and row `5` is the last actionable row**, and the unit is **not** presented as ready (`walkthrough.md` §5).

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-143`** as prose, unchanged and extended by this row only. **This row records a readout that costs the seam nothing, the one arm whose claim moved, the ruling that a marked battery cannot be left pinning a superseded answer, and a walkthrough verdict that routes to four documents rather than to the code.**

## D-145 — THE DRAG'S IN-PROCESS VALUE IS AN ADDRESS AND ITS RELEASE IS THE ONLY COMMIT: unit `pane-drag-temp` lands row `5` — `['drag']` declared and UNPAIRED at `temp`, seven bridge members at both halves, the ghost painted from the delivery — and the queue is EMPTY

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-07 · **Source:** the user's ruling `D-132`, measured against the tree at this head.

**WHAT LANDED.** The composition's in-process value stops being closure state and becomes an addressable fact: `['drag']` at **`temp`**, declared in `viewDeclaredRoots()` **and UNPAIRED** — no constraint, because no invariant holds a gesture transient and a repair whose subject is *"no drag has started"* would mint a placeholder for data whose absence is the product (`D-101`, `D-105`). The entry buys the family's one judgement, `declared-tier`, because the guard keys on the roots it is **handed** and not on the declared batch. `subscribe`'s array gained the address **in the constants' own hunk** (`D-137`; `P-3` is its instrument), so an outside write at `temp` delivers.

**THE ACT, IN `D-138`'S STYLE KEPT WHOLE**: both drag writes seat at the affordance's **one preview seam**, `state.valid` deciding the arm — `true` is the placement change (`commitDrag`, one write per placement change), `false` is the gesture's end (`clearDrag`). `release` keeps its sequence: sink → `commitPane` at `['panes']`/`file` → the ending, so **the file commit is the release's and the temp family never survives it**. Exactly one ending per gesture, gated by a closure flag that skips only the route.

**THE GHOST ASKS THE DELIVERY** (`D-144` is what made it answerable): one `data.read({path:['drag']})` per delivery, `data-ghost="temp"` painted iff the standing node's `_tier` is `temp` and the pane's member carries a usable size, **fails closed**, no poll, no timer, no retry — a lost delivery leaves a stale declaration, never a fabricated one. **The landed predicate reads the LEAF NODE** (`member.size.value`) because the readout answers `{_tier, value}`; the L4 measured that against a booted bundle and read the leaf rather than restating the design's bare-number form — a deviation recorded, not smoothed.

**THE PAIR AND THE CENSUSES MOVED TOGETHER**: the `view` member is **seven** at both halves (`read` · `commitPane` · `commitZones` · `commitTheme` · `commitDrag` · `clearDrag` · `onChanged`), one edit, and the crossing comparison is the only witness (`D-112`); `main()` registers **twenty** handlers; `viewDeclaredRoots()` publishes **four** roots and the union is **five**; `central-data`'s roots-equality row re-grained to the batch **plus the one unpaired entry**, so a second unpaired entry or a pair minted at `['drag']` reds it.

**THE READINGS AT THIS HEAD.** `npm test` **`2,928 passed / 2 skipped` of `2,930`** over `140` files, exit `0`; `typecheck` **0**; `typecheck:tests` **0**; `build` **0**; `crossings` **`0` failures over `28` rows**; the unit's own battery `P-1`…`P-6` **6/6 green** over seven Electron boots; marks `319` gating sites in `43` files; `strata-lint` exits `1` with its ONE accepted `register` FAIL. `P-4`'s end-of-reading arm names the **row** that stands, because the surface's delete refuses a branch address and answers `removed:false` — the row's acceptance was narrowed to say so rather than the contract bent to the row.

**AND THE EXIT WALKTHROUGH READS `STALE` FOR THE THIRD TIME, ON THE SAME CLASS.** Ten blind steps resolved and the substance agreed byte for byte with `src/` and `dist/`; what failed is **currency**: eight pages still assert the pair's absence, the five-member count, the eighteen-handler census and the four-root set in the present tense, and one page still states the ghost's predicate as a bare number (`strata/records/pane-drag-temp-walkthrough.md`). **THE PATTERN IS NOW NAMED AS THE LOOP'S OWN DEFECT, NOT A UNIT'S**: each layer measures at its own head, the landing falsifies those measurements, and no pass re-grains them because each pass is closed — so a unit's close must include the currency pass over its own design pages, taken **after** the landing, as a step of the close and not as a follow-up. `D-143` and `D-144` carry the two outstanding instances; this row carries the third and the recommendation.

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-144`** as prose, unchanged and extended by this row only. **This row records an unpaired root and why it is lawful, a ghost that asks the delivery and fails closed, the censuses that moved with the pair, and a repeated walkthrough verdict now diagnosed as the loop's own timing defect.**

## D-147 — THREE BLIND RUNS TAKE RUN `4` AND ALL THREE READ `STALE`, SO **NO UNIT CLOSES**: the two findings that matter are this loop's own — a pass that REPAIRS what a page recorded as owed must re-grain THAT page in the same pass, and a page re-grained WHOLE onto a new unit silently deletes its predecessor's subject

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-08 · **Source:** three fresh delegations (runners that had seen neither the code nor the design work) against three staged roots built at HEAD `ad25910` by `scripts/strata-walkthrough.mjs stage`, `169` files each.

**THE RUNS, AND WHAT THEY ARE WORTH.** Each root carries `strata/`, `docs/`, `src/`, `dist/`, the three root pointers, both path listings and an empty `work/`, and excludes `tests/`, `scripts/` and `node_modules/` — so no battery, leg or package claim could be EXECUTED in any root, and every such claim is recorded **unverifiable**, never falsified (`walkthrough.md` §3, `D-44`). The sets were carried forward with ONE re-authoring: `focus-retirement`'s step 5 said "four" declared roots and now says **five**, because `D-145` moved the set's own subject — an L0 instrument that is not re-grained becomes a finding against the documents it mis-states.

**THE VERDICTS, EACH `STALE` AND NONE `PASS`.** `focus-retirement` run `4`: **nine of twelve** steps settled from the sections they name, the surface agreeing with `src/` and `dist/` on the act, the repair, six zero-hit deleted symbols, five declared roots, one new `tab-<n>` row and the refusal answered before any route. `tier-readout` run `4`: **ten of ten** resolved with none guessed, agreeing on the per-node `_tier`, the leaf/container discriminator, one token, the register node, the cold arm, the tier-less refusal arms, ONE `readTiered` with the seam gaining no byte and `list` untouched. `pane-drag-temp` run `4`: **ten of ten** resolved with `docs-insufficient` and `undocumented` both EMPTY. **So the contracts are right in all three units and the verdicts turn on currency alone** — which is the third consecutive round of exactly that (`D-143`, `D-144`, `D-145`), and the first round in which the causes are named as two specific MECHANISMS rather than as a general pattern.

**FINDING (a) — THE PASS'S OWN LANDING, AND TWO RUNNERS FOUND IT INDEPENDENTLY.** `docs/L2/central-data/interface.md` §1a carried a bullet ending *"That comment is OWED and not taken here"*, saying `src/main/data/surface.ts`'s root header "still reads **four**". **`D-146` took exactly that repair and left the page that recorded it as owing untouched** — so the page now tells a reader to expect a state the surface has left. Both the `focus-retirement` and `tier-readout` runners reported it without knowing the other had. **THIS IS `D-145`'s LOOP DEFECT AT ITS NARROWEST AND MOST REPRODUCIBLE**: a repair has a READER as well as an owner, and the page that called it owed is that reader.

**FINDING (b) — A RE-GRAIN THAT DELETED ITS PREDECESSOR'S SUBJECT.** `focus-retirement`'s steps 6, 10 and 11 cite `docs/L2/renderer-wiring/interface.md` §2/§5 and `docs/L3/renderer-wiring/design.md` §2/§3/§6, and **none of those sections can answer them**: `D-145`'s currency pass re-grained both pages *"WHOLE"* onto `pane-drag-temp`, so the `focus-retirement` material now lives only in `docs/L1/focus-retirement.md` §3/§4, in `strata/records/focus-retirement-unit.md` and in the `src/` docblocks — and the unit record is explicitly not a design page (`D-52`). **A "whole" re-grain is the right instrument for a page whose SUBJECT moved and the wrong one for a page with TWO subjects**: the drag's turns and the tab-act's composition are different readers of the same wiring page, and replacing one with the other is a deletion wearing a revision number.

**FINDING (c) — THE PAGES WERE FIXED AND THE SOURCE PROSE WAS NOT.** `pane-drag-temp`'s single stale item is `src/renderer/renderer.ts:2542-2544`: the `renderGhost` docblock says a target that will not take an attribute "each REMOVE the declaration", where the code at `:2595` returns **ahead of the paint and removes nothing** — and `dist/renderer/renderer.js` carries the same gate, while `renderer-wiring` L2 §3, L3 §4 and `signatures.md` §1 all state the exception correctly. **`D-44` stages `src/` precisely so a claim about a comment is checkable, which makes a source docblock part of the documented surface and not packaging.** This is the arm `D-145` fixed on the pages and left in the prose.

**FINDING (d) — A SEAM FIGURE ONE LANDING BEHIND.** `docs/L1/central-data-management.md` §5's `S-9` row still measures the seam at *"14 members / 11 symbols"* imported by *"exactly two files"*, where `src/main/storeroom.ts` reads `15`/`13` and three files import the package. `undocumented` adds two smaller silences: the composition's `C1`–`C4` contract has no live design section (`renderer.ts` cites a `design.md` §3 that is now the release turn), and `src/main/data/types.ts`'s *"the view record's four"* does not say whether it counts the publication (four) or the declaration batch (three).

**TWO INSTRUMENT NOTES, STATED BESIDE THE VERDICTS AND NOT AMENDED INTO THE CORPUS** (§5). **(i)** `scripts/strata-walkthrough.mjs check` exits `1` on **three** cites in `strata/decisions.md` naming `docs/L1/panes-layout.md`, `docs/L2/panes-layout/interface.md` and `docs/L3/panes-layout/design.md` — documents the row that names them describes as a **future unit's OWED re-issue**, never as existing. Both runners that met them judged them correctly, so the corpus claim is true and the INSTRUMENT is what cannot distinguish a forward cite from a missing page; the corpus is not edited for it. **(ii)** `pane-drag-temp`'s run could not settle whether `tests/unit/renderer-wiring/preview-seat.test.ts` has landed, because `tests/` is out of every root — so `docs/L3/renderer-wiring/design.md` §8's "owed" sentence and `D-146`'s "4/4 green" now disagree in the staged universe, and the NEXT run must settle it from a root that carries `tests/`.

**THE FLOOR AT THE COMMITTED HEAD, AND ONE TRANSIENT NAMED RATHER THAN SWALLOWED.** `npm test` **`2,932 passed / 2 skipped` of `2,934`** over **`141`** files, exit `0`; `typecheck` **0**; `typecheck:tests` **0**; `build` **0**; `strata-marks check` **exit `0`** at **`323` gating sites in `44` files**; `refs` resolves **`146`** rows. **WHAT THIS ROW DOES NOT DO: IT CLOSES NOTHING.** All three units stay open with `"stage": "design"` in `strata/.strata/units/`, and each record now carries **run `4`'s `STALE`** in place of the run it replaced (§6). **A unit is recommended as ready only on `PASS`**, and the three closes that `D-146`'s brief presumed are exactly what these verdicts withhold. **THE LOOP RECOMMENDATION IS FOUR ITEMS, THREE OF THEM SMALL**: re-grain `central-data` L2 §1a and the `S-9` row (one pass clears `(a)` and `(d)` for two units), carry `focus-retirement`'s subject back onto the two `renderer-wiring` pages *beside* the drag's rather than instead of it (`(b)`), correct the one `renderGhost` docblock and its `DC-8(a)` description (`(c)`), and settle the seat row from a root that carries `tests/`. Then run `5`.

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-146`** as prose, unchanged and extended by this row only. **This row records three blind runs whose contracts all held and whose currency did not, a repair that left its own reader behind, a re-grain that deleted a predecessor's subject, and a source docblock that is documented surface because `D-44` made it checkable.**

## D-148 — THE FOUR RUN-4 FINDINGS CLOSE, AND THE GHOST CLAIM HAD **FIVE** CARRIERS AND NOT TWO: the page that recorded a repair as OWED is re-grained by the pass that takes the repair, `focus-retirement`'s subject is carried back BESIDE the drag's by compression because the ceiling's own escape hatch — a split by owner — is blocked by anchors this layer may not edit, and a source docblock's false clause was stated four times in one file

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-08 · **Source:** `D-147`'s loop recommendation, taken in five delegations against HEAD `1ca1de3`, each returning an envelope whose claims were then re-read against `src/` by L0.

**FINDING (a) CLOSED, AND THE RULE IT ESTABLISHES.** `docs/L2/central-data/interface.md` §1a recorded the `surface.ts` header repair as *"OWED and not taken here"* while `D-146` had taken it. The page now reports the bytes: **FIVE** declared roots, the fourth and fifth reached obliquely, the unpaired entry lawful and still buying its family's `declared-tier`. **THE RULE IS THE POINT**: when a pass takes a repair that another page recorded as owing, THAT PAGE IS PART OF THE PASS — the repair has a reader as well as an owner, and leaving the reader behind converts a closure into a fresh inconsistency in the same commit that earned it. The `types.ts` OWED clause the bullet also carried stays, because it still holds.

**FINDING (d) CLOSED BY MEASUREMENT.** `S-9` now reads **`15` members / `13` symbols** over **three** importing files, `src/main/storeroom.ts`'s own header no longer says fourteen, `src/main/data/types.ts` now says the tab record's six **DECLARATIONS** and the view record's four **PUBLISHED ROOTS** of which three carry a declaration — so the figure a reader meets can no longer be taken for the other noun — and `docs/L1/tier-readout.md` §1 now names WHICH `read` answers a caller's stored `_tier` (the store's own verb, which no `data.*` tool routes to a caller). ## D-150 — THE THREE CITATION DEFECTS CLOSE, AND `D-149`'s OWN ACCOUNT OF THE FIRST ONE WAS FALSE: `ASM-5`/`ASM-6` were never RENAMED — they never existed — the numbering hole was closed by SPLITTING a heading that stated two subjects rather than rewording six citers, and a repeated claim needs a CENSUS rather than a fix at the sites someone happened to remember

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-08 · **Source:** `strata/next-steps.md` row `1`, taken in four delegations against HEAD `62ce996`, with L0 re-reading each envelope's claims against the tree.

**DEFECT (i) CLOSED, AND THE PREMISE `D-149` RECORDED WAS WRONG.** `D-149` said `docs/L2/mcp-surface/interface.md`'s `ASM-5`/`ASM-6` cites named ids *"which that page renamed `ASM-RW-1`…`4`"*. **Measured, that is FALSE.** `renderer-wiring`'s register is `ASM-RW-1`…`ASM-RW-4` and those four claims are the ghost key, the drag's single seam, the ghost failing closed and `previewOf`'s deletion — **none of them carries either claim `ASM-5`/`ASM-6` was cited for**. There was no rename: the ids never existed under any name, and the claims they were attributed to live in `docs/L2/mcp-surface/interface.md` **§3's third arm** (a reading that is not a record, carried verbatim and unchanged) and **§4** (a `target` no standing row carries answering the record as read with the route's own `target-absent`). **So the fix was not a rename either**: every cite now names the SECTION plus the `D-n` that already backed the claim — `(D-127, L2 §3's third arm)` and `(D-18, L2 §4)` — in the page's own §3/§4 rows, in **five** `src/renderer/renderer.ts` docblocks and in **three** cites inside `tests/focus-tool-register.ts`. **`D-149`'s row is NOT reworded** (`D-46`): this paragraph is its correction, and a reader who takes `D-149`'s paraphrase of the defect takes the wrong one.

**DEFECT (ii) CLOSED WITHOUT REWORDING A SINGLE CITER, WHICH IS THE POINT.** `docs/L2/central-data/interface.md`'s numbering ran `§6` → `§8` with no `§7`, while six carriers cited a `§7` — `docs/L1/central-data-management.md` (twice, one of them its `ASM-12` row), `docs/L2/central-data/behaviour.md`'s *"§1-§7 fix the surface"* range, `src/renderer/renderer.ts` and `src/shared/types.ts`, `D-112`'s row, and `tests/integration/central-data/bridge-route.test.ts`. **NO §7 WAS INVENTED AND NO CITER WAS EDITED**: §6's own heading stated **two** subjects — *"The discipline: a refusal is a value, and no rendered surface"* — so the section was **SPLIT along the seam its heading already drew**, §6 keeping *"a refusal is a value"* and §7 becoming *"No rendered surface — a value is not a paint"* with the moved bullet carried BYTE-IDENTICAL. Every carrier now lands, `behaviour.md`'s range is finally true, and **`D-112`'s historical row became valid without being touched** — which is the whole reason a split beat a re-grain here. The split cost one line and the page sits at **`150`/`150`**, at its ceiling with no claim trimmed. **ONE REVERSE CASE WAS REAL and is re-pointed**: two cites in `tests/e2e/central-data/scenarios.test.ts` meant the half that MOVED and now say `§7`; its third cite (`§6`, *"refused as a value"*) correctly stays `§6`, which is how the split's two edges are both exercised.

**DEFECT (iii) CLOSED BY TWO POINTERS, NOT BY TWO COPIES.** `docs/L2/view-record/interface.md` §4 (*"The declared roots"*) and §5 (*"The refusal vocabulary"*) each gained ONE in-line clause naming where the neighbouring claim stands — §4 now says the roots and their tiers are its subject and that the **tier-blind subscription is §7's**; §5 says a delete's one-ROW scope is judged nowhere there but is `docs/L1/pane-drag-temp.md` §4 `P-4` → `docs/L3/central-data/design.md` §3. **A CENSUS IS THE INSTRUMENT FOR A REPEATED CLAIM, AND THIS CHAIN PROVES IT.** `D-148` found the ghost claim in **five** carriers where two had been routed; this pass found the `ASM-5`/`ASM-6` claim in **eight** (one page, five source docblocks, three register cites) where one had been routed — and each round of "fix the cite" found more, because a fix addressed the sites a reader remembered. **Only a tree-wide grep closed it**: the final census reads **`48`** surviving `ASM-5`/`ASM-6` mentions and **none of them is a `renderer-wiring` §7 pointer** — every survivor belongs to another page's OWN register (`settings-carrier-retirement`, `security-store-storeroom`, `central-data-management`) and is legitimate. **The rule this row adds to `D-148`'s**: when a claim is corrected, the instrument is a census over `src/`, `tests/` and `docs/` for every spelling of it, and a pass that fixes only the carriers it can name is a pass that will be re-opened.

**AND THE SAME CLASS WAS FOUND IN L0's OWN PROSE, WHICH IS RECORDED RATHER THAN QUIETLY PATCHED.** `D-149` and all three run-5 records said the three defects were *"carried in `strata/pending.md`"*; the row was `strata/next-steps.md` row `1`, and `pending.md`'s two rows carry neither. The **three records are corrected** — they are L0's living instruments, replaced on every run (§6) — while **`D-149`'s row stands and this paragraph is its correction** (`D-46`).
