## D-151 — `composeZones` GOES AND THE ZONE ID DOMAIN CLOSES WITH IT: per-member leaf writes need `['zones', id]` to stand, an OPEN id set cannot guarantee that, and a measurement on the live `1.10.0` shows even an EMPTY family is not enough — so the declared zone ids are created by the repair and an undeclared id is REFUSED

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-08 · **Source (user, verbatim):** *"Proceed with the composeZones removal"*, and on the shape put to the user, *"B — per-member leaf writes, closing the zone id domain"*.

**TWO PREMISES DID NOT SURVIVE MEASUREMENT, AND THE ROW OPENS WITH THEM BECAUSE BOTH WERE THE INSTRUCTION'S OWN GROUND.** **(1) `composePanes` WAS NEVER REMOVED.** `pending.md` row `2` states *"NOT ON THIS ROW: `composePanes` (landed with `D-143`'s unit)"*; measured, it is defined at `src/main/view-record/index.ts:705`, **still called on the write path at `:1099`**, and reused by `repairPanes` at `:870`. So `D-138`'s three edits — the per-pane ancestry, the composer's deletion from the write path, and `F3`'s delivery paint — **never landed**, and the precedent the zones removal was argued from does not exist. That sentence is FALSE and is corrected in `pending.md` by this row. **(2) `D-139`'s JUDGEMENT OF `composeZones` RESTED ON A PRECONDITION THE MODULE ITSELF FORBIDS.** `D-139` said the argument "transfers whole" and *"the precondition is the same: `['zones', id]` must be guaranteed to stand"* — but `src/main/view-record/index.ts:117-118` states the opposite in terms: *"ITS IDS ARE OPEN AND ITS PLACEHOLDER NAMES NO ZONE: a zone id is the caller's own … so this module cannot name a closed set"*, and `repairZones` writes `{}`, creating **no** zone record.

**THE MEASUREMENT, TAKEN ON THE LIVE `1.10.0` BECAUSE THE CORPUS'S OWN NOTE RECORDS THE RULE AT `1.9.1`.** One fresh instance per arm, `journalLength: 4096`, no `filePath`: `write(['zones','zone-2','slot'], 'mem', 'A')` over a store with **no** `['zones']` answers `AddressNotFound: 'zones' does not exist in '', it holds no keys`; **and over a store whose `['zones']` IS `{}` it answers `AddressNotFound: 'zone-2' does not exist in 'zones', it holds no keys`** — so the rule holds on the release this tree runs, and it is **sharper than the corpus recorded**: an empty family is not enough, the ZONE'S OWN NODE must stand. A write **at** that node, by contrast, works — `write(['zones','zone-2'], 'mem', {slot:'A'})` creates it and `write(['zones','zone-3'], …)` leaves `zone-2` untouched — but a second write at that node **replaces** the zone whole (`{slot:'A'}` then `{size:5}` reads back `{size:5}`, the `slot` gone), while a leaf write `['zones','zone-2','slot']` preserves the `size`. **That is D-135's member-merge finding, measured at the zone node rather than at the root.**

**THE RULING, IN ITS OWN TERMS.** The family composer is deleted and the write becomes **one leaf write per supplied member** — `['zones', id, 'slot']`, `['zones', id, 'distance']`, `['zones', id, 'size']`, `['zones', id, 'display']` — so a leaf write cannot drop a sibling member, cannot drop a sibling zone, and needs no copy of the family to be stale. **AND THE PRECONDITION IS MADE TRUE RATHER THAN ASSUMED**: the zone id domain **CLOSES** to the ids the app addresses, `zonesMustStand`'s repair **CREATES** those zone records (the `D-85` pattern — an absence-keyed repair that PERFORMS the thing it keys, not a marker that asserts it), and a caller naming an id outside that set is **REFUSED** rather than silently creating a zone. This is the one reading of *"B"* that can be built, and the user took it knowing it narrows `commitZones`' public contract.

**THREE CONSEQUENCES THAT MUST BE DESIGNED AND NOT DISCOVERED LATER.** **(a) THE PLACEHOLDER STOPS NAMING NO ZONE.** `D-91`'s placeholder is *"the family that names no zone"* — `{}` — and that value cannot satisfy the new precondition. The repair must write the declared zone ids each with an EMPTY record, and **`{}` IS ADMISSIBLE as a zone**: `zoneOf(id, {})` returns `{id}` rather than `null`, so `zoneNodeAdmits` admits it and a leaf write then lands. **`D-91`'s sentence is SUPERSEDED BY POINTER AND NOT REWORDED** (`D-46`). **(b) THE READ CHANGES FOR EVERY ZONE READER.** `renderZone` and `candidatesFor` fall back to the literal `'zone-1'`; after the repair, `zoneOf('zone-1')` answers a zone with no members instead of `null`, so the render turn's `zone?.size`/`zone?.display` become `undefined` where they were absent-of-zone. **That is a behaviour change and it belongs in the design, stated, not absorbed.** **(c) A REFUSAL TOKEN IS NEEDED FOR AN UNDECLARED ID** and the vocabulary is CLOSED (`docs/L2/view-record/interface.md` §5): either an existing token genuinely covers it or the set gains a member, and either way the page's §5 table gains or re-words its row.

**WHAT THIS ROW DOES NOT DO.** It takes no design and lands no byte: the zone id set, the repair's new placeholder, the refusal token and the hunks are `view-record`'s L3 to state and its L4 to land, and its battery is re-grained by the pass that owns it. **`composeZones` STILL STANDS at this head** — this row authorises its removal and records why the shape is what it is, and the row closes only when the composer, the repair and the battery move together.

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-150`** as prose, unchanged and extended by this row only. **This row records a false "landed" claim in the queue, a judgement whose precondition the module forbade, the nested-write rule re-measured on the release this tree runs and found sharper than recorded, and a user's ruling that closes a domain in order to make a leaf write lawful.**

## D-152 — `composeZones` IS DELETED AND THE ZONE ID DOMAIN IS CLOSED WITH IT: six hunks landed in two files, the repair now CREATES each declared zone record, an undeclared id is refused `undeclared-zone`, and the design's sharpest finding was that a LEAF write hands the family's constraint a SCALAR — which would have made the repair replace the family INSIDE a commit answering `committed`

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-08 · **Source:** `D-151`'s ruling, designed at `docs/L3/view-record/design.md` rev 11 and `docs/L2/view-record/interface.md` rev 11, landed by `view-record`'s L4, its batteries re-grained by the pass that owns them.

**WHAT LANDED, HUNK BY HUNK, AND EVERY HUNK IS ONE THE DESIGN NAMED.** **H1** `src/shared/view-shape.ts` gains `DEFAULT_ZONE_ID = 'zone-1'` on `DEFAULT_PANE_ID`'s pattern, `DECLARED_ZONE_IDS` derived from it, and `viewZonesPlaceholder()` answering every declared id with an EMPTY record. **H2** `commitZones` judges id-shape, then the id's DOMAIN (`undeclared-zone`, `address: ['zones', id]`), then the payload's shape, then writes **one leaf per supplied member** in `ViewZone`'s own order — `['zones',id,'slot']`, `'distance'`, `'size'`, `'display']`, each at `ZONES_TIER`, each handing that member's own value; the `zonesOf(standing)` read is gone, and an act supplying no member writes nothing and answers `committed`. **H3** `zonesMustStand` judges absence **only at the root**. **H4** `zoneNodeAdmits` refuses an id outside `DECLARED_ZONE_IDS` at every depth and `ZONE_UNUSABLE_MESSAGE` gains its clause — the closure lives at **two seats**, the route and the predicate, deliberately (`ASM-9`). **H5** `repairZones` writes `viewZonesPlaceholder()`. **H6** `composeZones` and `zoneValueOf` are **deleted whole** — `zoneValueOf` was grepped in `src/` and `tests/` and had no caller but the composer — with a tombstone in their place `D-142`'s style, and the docblocks whose claims the landing falsified re-grained by pointer. **No byte moved in `src/shared/view-channels.ts` or `src/renderer/view-client.ts`.**

**AND THE FINDING THAT WOULD HAVE BEEN A SILENT CORRUPTION IS STATED FIRST BECAUSE IT IS THE LANDING'S REAL RISK.** `context['value']` is the act's LANDED value and an ancestor's pair is asked, so **a leaf write hands `zonesMustStand` a SCALAR** — and the constraint's absence arm ran BEFORE it ever took `nodeOf`. Left as it stood, a leaf write would have been judged "the zone family does not stand", the repair would have fired **inside the caller's own commit**, and the whole family would have been replaced by the placeholder while the act answered `committed`. **`D-135`'s rule — a judgement must be taken at the node the write is at — was the difference between a refactor and a fault**, and the L3 pass found it by reading the pair rather than by running the battery.

**THE THREE CONSEQUENCES `D-151` SAID MUST BE DESIGNED AND NOT DISCOVERED.** **(a)** `D-91`'s placeholder stops naming no zone: it is now `{'zone-1': {}}`, lawful because `zoneOf(id, {})` answers `{id}` and `zoneNodeAdmits` admits it — **`D-91`'s sentence is SUPERSEDED BY POINTER and stands unrewritten** (`D-46`). **(b)** The read consequence is real and is recorded, not absorbed: `zoneOf('zone-1')` answers a memberless zone where it answered `null`, `read().zones` gains one member, and `zoneIn`'s `null` now means "undeclared"; **the two states stay distinguishable and the render turn's layout arguments are unmoved** (`undefined` and `[]` on both arms, read at their sites). **(c)** The vocabulary gains **`undeclared-zone`** — *"the zone id names no zone this record declares, and the zone family is addressed only by its declared ids"* — because neither `malformed-zone` (a payload) nor `malformed-zone-member` (a member) covers a well-formed id the record does not declare; §5's table carries it as the third token this module mints.

**THE BATTERY MOVED WITH THE COMPOSER, WHICH IS THE CONDITION `D-151` SET.** **Nineteen rows across four files** were re-grained in place — `zone-family.test.ts` (`Z-1`…`Z-6`, `Z-9`, `Z-10`), `crossing.test.ts`'s clause-2 delivery and bridge counts, clause-3 boot and landing-instant rows, and clause-4's censuses; `central-data`'s `declared-root.test.ts` (`D-2`, `D-4`, `D-9`) and `value-arms.test.ts` (`VA-5`, `VA-11`, `VA-12`) — **no row was deleted and none was weakened**, and the two directories read **`117/117` green over `12` files**. **ONE ROW'S SUBJECT GENUINELY LEFT AND IS TOMBSTONED RATHER THAN RE-POINTED**: `Z-9`'s *"a sibling zone survives"* half has **no subject at this head**, because the closed domain declares ONE id and with one declared id there is no sibling zone to preserve — so the row now asserts the **REFUSAL** (the new contract) and the sibling half retires in place with its dated reason. **A composer whose reason disappears takes its battery's subject with it, and a row re-pointed at a state that cannot be represented would be a lie wearing a green.**

**THE READINGS AT THIS HEAD.** `npm test` **`2,932 passed / 2 skipped` of `2,934`** over **`141`** files, exit `0` — the same figure as `D-149`'s, which is the check that the re-grain ADDED and LOST no row; `typecheck` **0**; `typecheck:tests` **0**; `build` **0**; `strata-marks check` **exit `0`**; the demo bundle rebuilt to **39 inputs, every digest agreeing**; `refs` resolves **`152`** rows.

**ONE CLAIM THE LANDING FALSIFIED ELSEWHERE IS RE-GRAINED IN THE SAME PASS, AND ONE IS ROUTED.** `docs/L1/focus-retirement.md` stated that *"`composeZones`, `composeRecord`'s review and `toStored` stay where that row puts them"* — the first third is now false and is re-grained by `D-148`'s rule (a landing re-grains the page that recorded its subject as standing). **ROUTED AND NOT TAKEN**: `src/renderer/renderer.ts` names the zone id as the literal `'zone-1'` twice (`zoneIdOf`'s fallback and `renderZone`) where the declared set now has a constant, so the renderer and the record can drift about which ids are declared — that is **`renderer-wiring`'s** file, a finding carried in `strata/next-steps.md`, not a hunk this module may take.

**WHAT THIS ROW DOES NOT DO, SAID PLAINLY BECAUSE THE QUEUE WOULD OTHERWISE LOOK SWEPT.** **`composePanes` STILL STANDS** — `D-138`'s three edits never landed, and `pending.md` row `2` now says so instead of claiming otherwise (`D-151`(1)). **`composeRecord` + `writeJoined` + `TabsCommitCarrier` and `toStored(importOnce(…))` still await their per-family decisions.** And **`D-139`'s judgement of `composeZones` is now SUPERSEDED IN FACT**: the row called it removable *"once the ancestry is enforced"* on the assumption the id set could stay open; it was removed by **closing** the set instead, which is a different act with a narrower contract, and a reader who takes `D-139`'s line as the record of this landing takes the wrong shape.

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-151`** as prose, unchanged and extended by this row only. **This row records a deletion that needed a domain closed to be lawful, a constraint whose absence arm had to be scoped to the root or the repair would have fired inside a passing commit, nineteen battery rows moved with their composer, and one row whose subject left and is tombstoned rather than dressed in a new expected value.**

## D-153 — THE LANDING'S LOOSE ENDS CLOSE, AND `D-152`'s OWN POINTERS WERE FALSE TWICE: a "ROUTED AND NOT TAKEN" that was taken, and a "carried in `next-steps.md`" with no row ever written — the SECOND consecutive row to promise a carrier it never added, which is now named as a defect of this SEAT rather than of a layer

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-08 · **Source:** the loose ends `D-152` left in other carriers, found by the passes that re-grained them, and by `D-152`'s own two claims being read back against the tree.

**THE SIX CARRIERS THAT STILL DESCRIBED THE PRE-LANDING STATE, ALL CLOSED.** **(i)** `docs/L2/view-record/interface.md` said *"it is NOT LANDED: `composeZones` still stands at this head"* and carried a pointer that existed only because the page wrote ahead of its landing — rev 12 states the closure as LANDED at `D-152` and re-grains the pointer rather than deleting a claim (its §8 now reads *"ONE COMPOSER STOOD OUTSIDE THIS TABLE AT REV 11 … AND IT IS NOW DELETED WHOLE"*). **(ii)** `docs/L3/view-record/design.md` claimed *"every hunk above is unlanded"* — rev 12 maps `D-152`'s H3–H6 onto that page's H4–H7, labels each hunk LANDED, and states the readings instead of claiming none. **(iii)** `src/main/view-surface/index.ts`'s `commitZones` comment was true of the SURFACE's two arms but read as the family's complete refusal set, its first clause being word-for-word the phrase §5 uses for `undeclared-zone` — it gains one clause naming that the id's DECLARED domain is the **record's** arm, in the register the same file uses one member over. **(iv)** `src/renderer/renderer.ts` spelled the zone id as a literal **three** times — `zoneIdOf`'s fallback, `renderZone`'s read, and **the layout call's id argument inside the same statement**, which the brief did not name and which would have let the read and the id handed on disagree inside one turn — all three now read `DEFAULT_ZONE_ID`; harmless while the domain was open, a drift risk the moment a declared set existed, which is what made closing it part of the landing. **(v)** Two `tests/pane-drag-compliance.test.ts` fixture comments called the crossing's family *"COMPOSED AT THE CROSSING (`composeZones`, D-91)"*. **(vi)** The file's last carrier, the comment calling the placeholder *"the one that names no zone"*, is re-grained — and its `let zones = {}` divergence from the repair's `{'zone-1': {}}` was **measured rather than repaired**: no row depends on it, because `candidatesFor` answers `[]` for a memberless zone too, so both arms answer alike.

**AND NOW THE PART THAT IS THIS SEAT'S OWN, BECAUSE THE ROW SHOULD NOT BURY IT.** `D-152` carries two claims about work outside its own files, and **both are false**. **(a) "ROUTED AND NOT TAKEN"** — the sentence says the renderer's `'zone-1'` literals are *"a finding carried in `strata/next-steps.md`, not a hunk this module may take"*. The finding was **TAKEN** in this same series: all three literals now read `DEFAULT_ZONE_ID`. **(b) "carried in `strata/next-steps.md`" — NO SUCH ROW WAS EVER WRITTEN.** `next-steps.md`'s table at `D-152`'s head carried exactly one row, the `composeZones` removal, and its departure note now records that the table is empty. **So `D-152` promised a carrier it never filled.** Rows are not reworded (`D-46`); this paragraph is their correction, and a reader who follows `D-152`'s pointer finds nothing at the other end.

**THIS IS THE SECOND CONSECUTIVE ROW TO MAKE EXACTLY THAT MISTAKE, AND THAT IS THE FINDING.** `D-149` said the three citation defects were *"carried in `strata/pending.md`"*; they were in `next-steps.md` row `1`, and `pending.md` carried neither — `D-150` corrected it. **One row later, the same seat wrote the mirror-image error**: the right FILE this time, and no entry in it. **Both were caught by a delegate reading the tree back, never by the author**, and both are the same class the whole session has been chasing — a claim about where a thing is, made from recollection instead of from a read. **THE RULE THIS ROW ADDS, AND IT IS A RULE OF THIS SEAT AND NOT OF A LAYER**: a pass that writes *"carried in `<file>`"* owes an edit to that file **in the same pass**, and the check is opening the file — not remembering having opened it. A pointer is only a pointer if its target exists; otherwise it is a sentence that reads like diligence.

**WHAT REMAINS, UNCHANGED BY THIS SERIES.** `pending.md`'s two rows: the audit's remaining seven of eleven, and the composed data writes — where **`composePanes` still stands** (`D-138`'s three edits never landed, `D-151`(1)) and `composeRecord`/`toStored` still await their per-family decisions. **`next-steps.md`'s table is EMPTY at this head.** No unit opened or closed on these rows; the three `D-149` closes stand.

## D-154 — `composePanes` IS DELETED AND `D-138`'s TWO EDITS FINALLY LAND: the panes case is the one `D-138` named first and it is the one that waited longest — the transposition of `D-152`'s zones shape closed the pane id domain, moved `bounds` as ONE member, refused an undeclared id, and turned a row GREEN-FOR-THE-WRONG-REASON that only a re-read caught

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-09 · **Source:** the user's instruction *"Get rid of composePanes like you were supposed to"*, which is `D-138`'s own ruling finally taken: *"(i) The pane record's own existence enforced per pane id …; (ii) the composition deleted from the write path — `commitPane` answers `store.write(['panes', paneId, 'size'], PANES_TIER, size)`."*

**THE ROW OPENS BY NAMING ITS OWN DELAY.** `D-138` is the ruling that established the leaf-write result, and **the panes were its own case** — the drag changes one member, so `['panes', paneId, 'size']` cannot drop `bounds`. Its three edits were named on 2026-11-06 and then waited behind three `STALE` walkthrough rounds, four residue-gap passes and the zones transposition, and `D-151`(1) had to record that `composePanes` was **still on the write path** the whole time. **This row is those edits landing.**

**THE DESIGN IS A TRANSPOSITION AND SAYS SO.** `docs/L3/view-record/design.md` rev 13 states H10–H17 as the panes form of `D-152`'s six hunks, and its differences from zones are all measured rather than stylistic. **`DECLARED_PANE_IDS` derives from `DEFAULT_PANE_ID` in `src/shared/view-shape.ts`** (one id, because the only producer of a pane act under `src/` is the release's `commitPane(DEFAULT_PANE_ID, { size: final })` and `paneIdOf`'s element-derived ids reach **reads only**). **THE PLACEHOLDER'S VALUE DOES NOT MOVE** — `repairPanes` already created `['panes','pane-a']` *with* its geometry, so what zones needed as a change (`{}` could not satisfy the precondition, because it created **no** record) is here only a **re-spelling** to `viewPanesPlaceholder()`; **that asymmetry is the one place the transposition had to reason instead of copy**, and the row states it because a reader comparing the two landings will otherwise read the pane placeholder as un-changed-by-accident. **`bounds` MOVES AS ONE MEMBER** — a leaf `['panes', id, 'bounds']` carrying the pair — because `panesAdmitAt` admits the pair's *field* leaves too, and splitting them could leave `{min: 300, max: 200}`, a record `paneOf` **drops** and no arm repairs. **`undeclared-pane` IS MINTED** as `docs/L2/view-record/interface.md` §5's **eighth** row, and that is a contract change stated as one.

**THE HAZARD `D-152` FOUND IN THE ZONES PAIR WAS STILL LIVE IN THE PANES PAIR, AND `H13` CLOSED IT.** `panesMustStand`'s existence arm ran **before** `nodeOf` and its own comment asserted *"THE EXISTENCE ARM ABOVE STAYS FIRST AND IS UNCHANGED"* — which a leaf write falsifies, because `context['value']` is the act's LANDED value and a leaf write hands the pair a **scalar**: the repair would have fired at the ROOT inside the caller's own commit and replaced the whole pane family with the placeholder while the act answered `committed`. **The arm now takes `nodeOf` first and answers absence only at the root, and the superseded sentence is replaced in place rather than annotated.** That the same fault sat in the sibling pair after being named in this one is the row's quiet lesson: **a fix in one family does not travel to the other, and the reader who assumes it does is the one who ships it.**

**AND ONE ROW WAS GREEN FOR THE WRONG REASON — CAUGHT ONLY BY RE-READING IT, NOT BY RUNNING IT.** `crossing.test.ts` clause 1 seeds a pane to test that the placeholder is in the record, and it seeded **`pane-b`** — an id the closed domain no longer declares. **The row kept passing**, not because its subject held, but because `paneNodeAdmits` answered `false` for the undeclared id and the pair then **abstained at a registration instant**, measuring that silence instead of a legal family. It is re-seeded at `DEFAULT_PANE_ID` with every placeholder assertion byte-for-byte unmoved. **A CLOSED DOMAIN CAN TURN A ROW GREEN-FOR-THE-WRONG-REASON**, and no census, count or red would have said so — the class is invisible to the instruments and visible only to a reader who asks *"is this id one the record declares?"*.

**THE BATTERY MOVED WITH THE COMPOSER.** Three reds, all predicted by the design: the two write censuses (`crossing.test.ts` clause 4's positive control **inverted** from a pane root write to **absent**, with a positive LEAF control added beside it; the `.write(VIEW_` count **`3 → 2`** there and in `zone-family.test.ts`'s `Z-6`, whose sentence *"exactly THREE routes write a whole family"* was false with the panes route gone), and **`ONE MEMBER IS FILLED, NOT ERASED`**, whose sibling-pane half **has no subject** — one declared pane means no sibling pane — so it is **tombstoned in place with its dated reason** and now asserts the **refusal** (`Z-9`'s rule, applied a second time); its size-alone half stays green **as the leaf write's own guarantee** rather than a composer's. **AND ONE SEAT IS MEASURED FOR THE FIRST TIME**: `paneNodeAdmits`' declared-id check is the panes twin of a zones seat that landed **unmeasured** at `D-152`; a `data.write` at `['panes','pane-z']` now drives it and reads `ConstraintPassRefused` with the family's own sentence. **`117/117` over `12` files, no row added or deleted.**

**TWO FURTHER CARRIERS CLOSED.** `src/main/view-record/index.ts`'s `defaultPaneGeometry` import became **dead** when `composePanes` and `repairPanes` stopped reading it — and **`tsc` is silent because `noUnusedLocals` is off**, so only a reader could catch it; it is removed, and the docblock that survives still describes something true (`viewPanesPlaceholder` derives through it in the shape file). And `paneIdOf` spelled **`'pane-a'`** as a literal while the record declares its ids — the same drift `D-153` closed for zones — so it now reads `DEFAULT_PANE_ID`, value unchanged.

**THE READINGS AT THIS HEAD.** `npm test` **`2,932 passed / 2 skipped` of `2,934`** over **`141`** files, exit `0` — the same figure as `D-149`'s and `D-152`'s, which is the third consecutive landing to add and lose no row; `typecheck` **0**; `typecheck:tests` **0**; `build` **0**; `strata-marks check` **exit `0`**; the demo bundle rebuilt to **39 inputs, every digest agreeing**; `refs` resolves **`154`** rows.

**ONE GAP IS OWED AND NAMED RATHER THAN PATCHED.** `crossing.test.ts` clause 2's delivery sentence fixes the pane figure as `1` per commit and gives the per-member rule only for zones, so it says nothing about a **two-member** pane act — `commitPane(id, { size, bounds })` is now **two** store verbs and two deliveries. **The row is SILENT, not false**, so it is not a contradiction to close here; `docs/L3/view-record/design.md` §7's `H12` names the re-grain and it is carried as owed.

**WHAT REMAINS.** `strata/pending.md` row `2` now carries **two** items and no more — **`composeRecord` + `writeJoined` + `TabsCommitCarrier`** and **`toStored(importOnce(…))`** — both still awaiting their per-family decisions; `next-steps.md`'s table is empty. **`D-139`'s "REMOVABLE ONCE THE ANCESTRY IS ENFORCED" set is now EMPTY**: both family composers it named are deleted, and **both were removed by closing their id domains rather than by leaving them open as `D-139` assumed** — so the row's judgement was right and its precondition wrong, twice, and a reader who takes it as the record of either landing takes the wrong shape.

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-153`** as prose, unchanged and extended by this row only. **This row records the ruling's own case landing last, a placeholder that was re-spelled rather than re-valued and why that asymmetry is real, a fault that had to be fixed twice because a fix in one family did not travel to the other, and a battery row that a closed domain had silently turned green for the wrong reason.**

## D-155 — `composeRecord` IS **KEPT**, AND THE REASON IS THAT A SPLIT WOULD NOT REMOVE IT: three of its five call sites are REPAIRS, the creation split is only lawful because its intermediate row is INVISIBLE, the removal needs the OPPOSITE order — and `D-139`'s test is thereby named NECESSARY BUT NOT SUFFICIENT and must be applied to the ACT and not to the function's name

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-09 · **Source:** `pending.md` row `2`'s last item — the one `D-139` filed under *"NEED A DECISION THIS ROW DOES NOT TAKE"* — settled by `store-tabs-record`'s L3 and re-read against the tree by L0.

**THE VERDICT IS KEPT, AND IT IS NOT A SHRUG.** `pending.md` row `2`'s own closure condition is *"each composer is either deleted with its ancestry enforced and its battery re-grained, or kept with the reason recorded beside it; **none is left unjudged**"*. This item is now **judged**, and the reason lives at `docs/L3/store-tabs-record/design.md` §10. **`composeRecord`, `writeJoined` and `TabsCommitCarrier` all stay; no hunk is owed to L4 and no battery row is re-grained.** The verdict is earned by three independent grounds, and the strongest of them is not about invariants at all.

**GROUND (1) — A SPLIT WOULD NOT REMOVE THE COMPOSER.** `composeRecord` has **five** call sites, and **three of them are REPAIRS** — `healRecord`'s (`:771`), `mintRecord`'s (`:935`) and `repairOrderSeat`'s (`:1045`) — against **two acts** (`commit` at `:1175`, `remove` at `:1220`). **`D-83` measured that a repair must write the rows it names**, and `D-139` itself classes the repairs' values as must-stay. So splitting the two act sites would leave `composeRecord` **standing for the class this corpus has already ruled must stay**: the "deletion" would delete nothing and cost two commits. **A composer whose call sites fall into two classes must be judged twice**, and `D-139` judged it once by name.

**GROUND (2) — THE CREATION SPLIT IS LAWFUL ONLY BECAUSE ITS INTERMEDIATE STATE IS INVISIBLE, AND IT WOULD MAKE EVERY CLEAN CREATION ANSWER `repaired: true`.** `orderNamesItsTabs` loops over `seat` alone, so a member the seat does not yet name passes its arm — and `viewFrom` builds `entries` **from `order`** (`for (const id of seated) … entries[id] = row`), so the row written first is **unreadable by every constraint and every view**. The intermediate is a **hole, not a violation**. But at the seat write two rows then read active, `exactly-one-active` refuses, `written` resolves to the new id, and `healRecord` keeps it — so **every clean creation would report a repair the split caused on purpose**, turning `D-70`'s `repaired` answer (the member that says whether the ENGINE repaired) into noise about the pass's own sequencing. **And a repair landing between the two writes files `composeRecord(order, rows)` without the unseated row — erasing the creation outright**, after which the seat write wears the seat-naming refusal.

**GROUND (3) — THE TWO ACTS NEED OPPOSITE SEQUENCES, AND THE REMOVAL'S REPAIR USES THE WRONG DISCIPLINE.** Creation can write member-then-seat; removal **cannot**, because `order` naming a member the record no longer holds is precisely the seat arm's sentence. So removal must write **seat-then-member** — and the body that answers a seat fault is `repairOrderSeat`, whose `NO_HINTS` resolves the survivor by the **seat's own order** (arm iii) where the removal's discipline is the **removed entry's pre-position** (arm ii), carried by `exactly-one-active`'s hints. **A split does not merely reorder writes; it changes which repair reasons about the act.** One write is what makes the act's order-dependence irrelevant — that is `composeRecord`'s reason, in one sentence.

**AND THE ANCESTRY WAS NEVER THE MISSING THING, WHICH IS THE CORRECTION THE CORPUS NEEDS.** Unlike the two composers already removed, `tabs-must-stand` **does** enforce the root and `viewOf`'s fallback **does** read `['tabs', id]`, so `D-139`'s stated precondition was **MET here** — and the composer was still required. **So the precondition is not what decided any of the three cases.** What decided them is one question `D-139` never asked: **is the ACT expressible as a leaf write?** For panes and zones it was, once their id domains closed; **for the tab record it is not, because a tab id is MINTED AT RUNTIME** (`newTab: true`, `mintTabId`) — and that is also why the two closed domains cannot be re-used as a device here: **closing the tab-id set would delete the feature.**

**`writeJoined` AND `TabsCommitCarrier` ARE NOT COMPOSERS AT ALL, AND `D-139`'s INVENTORY MIS-FILED THEM.** They appear at the same sites, but the carrier is `write`'s **fourth position at ANY address** — so a leaf write would name its commit just as well — and `D-73`'s ruling is that a repair write naming neither is a **compile error**: *"Repairs that do not pass the commit are unsupported."* **They are an ARITY DEVICE enforcing the user's own ruling, and they are separable from the composer in principle and kept independently in fact.** An inventory that classifies by site rather than by role put a type-level safeguard of `D-73` in a list of formatting devices; the row says so rather than letting the filing stand.

**THE METHOD CORRECTION, STATED SO THE NEXT INVENTORY DOES NOT REPEAT IT.** `D-138`'s test — *is the write a LEAF of an enforced structure?* — is **necessary and not sufficient**. Applied to the **function's name** it asked "can a leaf write destroy a sibling?" and answered for all nine composers alike; applied to the **ACT** it asks "is what this entry point does expressible as leaves?", which is what actually differs between the three families. **`D-139` was right about the test and wrong about two separate preconditions** — the ancestry (which was met for tabs and unsatisfied for panes and zones) and the act's expressibility (which was the real discriminator all along). Its *"removable once the ancestry is enforced"* set is now **EMPTY**, its *"need a decision"* set is **SETTLED**, and its *"not removable"* set stands unchanged.

**THE READINGS AT THIS HEAD.** `npm test` **`2,931 passed / 1 failed`** on the first reading and **`2,932 passed / 2 skipped` of `2,934`** over **`141`** files on the re-run, exit `0` — the failure being `tests/e2e/tier-readout/`'s `T-1` on `MCP error -32000: Connection closed`, the boot casualty class this corpus has carried since `D-147` and which reads **8/8 alone**; `tests/integration/store-tabs-record/` and `tests/unit/store-tabs-record/` **`52/52`** green, which is the re-grain's own check. Three pages moved and every one is at or under its ceiling: `docs/L3/store-tabs-record/design.md` **`121`/`150`** (rev 13, new §10), `docs/L2/store-tabs-record/interface.md` **`150`/`150`** (rev 6), `docs/L3/store-tabs-record/signatures.md` **`150`/`150`** (rev 5, transcribed — pinned by `signatures.test.ts` `C-7`).

**THE QUEUE AFTER THIS ROW.** **`pending.md` row `2` DEPARTS WITH EVERY ITEM JUDGED** — the row's closure condition is met item by item, and the file says so in its departure note. **ONE ROW STANDS AT THIS HEAD**: the audit's remaining seven of eleven (`D-126`). `next-steps.md`'s table is empty.

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-154`** as prose, unchanged and extended by this row only. **This row records the one composer that survives the test that removed its siblings, the three grounds that decided it — a repair class it would still serve, an invisibility that makes its split lawful and its answer dishonest, and two acts that need opposite sequences — and a method correction: the test is necessary, not sufficient, and it must be asked of the act and not of the name.**

## D-156 — THE TAB REMOVAL STOPS COMPOSING: `order` BECOMES A BIJECTION WITH THE ROWS, THE ACT BECOMES ONE `delete`, AND THE WORK SPLITS BETWEEN TWO REPAIRS THAT OWN DIFFERENT FAULTS — the user's ruling, and the three faults the measurement found behind it

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-09 · **Source (user, verbatim):** *"This doesn't need a compose to remove a record. It needs a repair that reassigns the active tab (if the active tab is deleted - this already exists) and a repair that drops that tab from the order array (There should be a constraint that forces the order array to exactly contain the available tab ids)"*

**THE RULING IS RIGHT, AND THE MECHANISM IS MEASURED ON THE LIVE `1.10.0`.** A constraint registered at `['tabs']`, one record standing: **`delete(['tabs','tab-1'], 'mem')` asks the pair at `['tabs']` (one ask, `ctx.address` `['tabs','tab-1']`, `ctx.value` `undefined`) and leaves `order: ['landing','tab-1']` naming a row that is GONE**; **`write(['tabs','tab-9'], row)` asks the same pair and leaves a row the `order` does not name.** So both directions are open, both reach the parent's pairs, and the two violations are mirror images — which is precisely the structure a bijection plus a repair closes.

**AND THE CONSTRAINT IS CURRENTLY HALF A BIJECTION, WHICH IS THE DEFECT THE RULING NAMES.** `orderNamesItsTabs` loops over `seat` only: every id IN `order` must have a plain member, and **nothing requires every member to be IN `order`**. So `order ⊆ rows` is enforced and `order ≡ rows` is not — and `rows ⊄ order` is not merely legal, it is **invisible**: `viewFrom` builds `entries` by walking `order`, so a row outside the seat is unreadable by every body and every view. **That hole is also what made `D-155`'s creation-split argument work at all**, so closing it changes the ground under that row: **`D-155`'s ground (1) — that a split would leave the composer serving the three repair sites — STANDS and is now the whole licence; its ground (3) is SUPERSEDED IN FACT** (the opposite-sequence asymmetry ceases to exist once the constraint settles membership in either direction); **its ground (2) stands**, which is why no creation-side hunk is taken.

**THE DESIGN, AS SETTLED, IN FOUR DECISIONS.** **(1) THE PAIR IS EXTENDED, NOT MULTIPLIED.** One fault — the seat disagrees with the rows — takes one id and one repair; a seventh pair would give one state two sentences and buy a second whole-record repair inside one attempt. Its sentence re-grains to *"…carries no sequence of exactly its own tabs, once each"* and it gains exactly one clause: **an own key other than `order` that the seat does not name**. **THE WIDENING IS A CONTRACT CHANGE AND IS STATED AS ONE** — no id, `reason`, address, tier or route moves, but a record that passes today refuses at the next write. **(2) THE TWO REPAIRS OWN DIFFERENT FAULTS**, which the ruling's own wording requires: `repairOrderSeat` owns **membership** and `healRecord` owns **activeness**. `healedFamily` gains a required `scope` — `'seat'` seats what the record holds, drops stray keys, fills the three defaults and **preserves every `active` flag**; `'family'` does all that *and* §7's precedence. **CONVERGENCE IS MEASURED, NOT HOPED**: the pass asks one refusal at a time and re-runs from the top, the batch order is contractual, so the seat repair lands first and `exactly-one-active` is asked on the record it left. **And the alternative fails in this tree**: a full-heal seat repair resolves arm (iii), its write leaves one active, and the re-run passes — so the activeness pair is never asked and **removing the active tab would silently re-seat the seat's first row instead of the pre-position**. **(3) THE ACT IS ONE `delete`.** Shape arms and `namesASeat` refuse before any store verb as they do now; the act takes the PRE view, refreshes `lastView`, answers already-absent without a store act, performs `delete(['tabs', id], TABS_RECORD_TIER)` — **no composition, no value** — refreshes `lastView` again, and answers from `standsAsIntended` against the **delete-only expectation** (`order` and rows minus the id, flags untouched). **(4) `commit` STAYS COMPOSED IN THIS PASS AND ITS ROW-WRITE IS OWED**, because a composed intent satisfies the bijection by construction — so the two acts cannot disagree about the record's shape while only one of them moves.

**`repaired` KEEPS ITS MEANING ON THE THREE CASES, AND THAT IS WHY THE EXPECTATION IS THE DELETE-ONLY ONE.** Removing a **non-active** tab → bare `committed` (the seat repair ran and left exactly what the removal meant). Removing the **active** tab with a survivor → `committed` **with `repaired: true`** (the successor is the engine's choosing, and predicting it would be a second copy of §7's precedence). Removing the **last** tab → `repaired: true` (the floor differs from the empty expectation). **A removal whose successor is the pre-position therefore cannot answer a bare `committed`** — which is exactly `D-70`'s signal working rather than being suppressed.

**THREE FAULTS THE MEASUREMENT FOUND BEHIND THE RULING, EACH OF WHICH WOULD HAVE SHIPPED AS SILENT DAMAGE.** **(a)** Under the six pairs a **descendant delete hands `tabsMustStand` `value: undefined`**, so it mints the floor and **erases the siblings** (measured) — the act cannot delete a row while that body reads the act's value. **(b)** A **leaf row write hands the seat pair the ROW**, so a repair taking its view from the act's value **replaces the family** (measured: two tab rows both erased). **(c)** The empty-view floor must survive **at the registration instant** or `storeCreate` throws. **So both bodies judge the record at the CONTEXT's address** — root or registration hands the value, anything deeper reads the store raw — **and the seat repair parses the record by its own keys, so it SEATS as well as drops.** None of the three is in the ruling; all three follow from taking it, and they are the reason a design pass exists between a right instruction and a landing.

**`composeRecord`'S FATE, RESTATED WITHOUT OVERCLAIMING.** It **survives**, for the repairs and for `commit`'s intent. **What changes is that the REMOVAL ACT no longer composes**: its site keeps the name and loses the write, and its product is the comparator's expectation rather than a written value. **No deletion of the composer happens here and this row does not claim one** (`D-155`'s ground 1 is the reason it could not have).

**THE GATE, AND IT IS THIS ROW'S FIRST DUTY.** The design pages record that **H1–H8 must not land on their word alone** — the ruling must be registered before a hunk lands. **This row is that registration**, so `store-tabs-record`'s L4 may now take H1–H8: the sentence, the address-discriminated body with its sixth clause, `repairOrderSeat`'s own-key parse with the `'seat'` scope, `healedFamily`'s required `scope`, `tabsMustStand`'s root-only absence, `remove`'s two `lastView` refreshes plus the delete plus the comparator, and eight docblocks replaced in place. **Its battery is the landing pass's** (`D-3`): five new `crossing.test.ts` rows and **two re-grains with their reasons** — the `repaired`-on-`remove` gloss, and the creation row seeding `{tab-1: row}` with no seat, which must now **seat** it rather than take the floor.

**THE READINGS AT THIS HEAD ARE `D-155`'S** — nothing in this row moves a byte: the design pages are its whole product and they sit at `docs/L3/store-tabs-record/design.md` **`126`/150** (rev 14), `docs/L2/store-tabs-record/interface.md` **`149`/150** (rev 7) and `docs/L3/store-tabs-record/signatures.md` **`150`/150** (rev 6, `C-7` pin satisfied). **The `1.10.0` probes were taken in `/tmp` and deleted; the package was never modified.**

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-155`** as prose, unchanged and extended by this row only. **This row records a ruling that turned a whole-record rewrite into one delete, the half-open constraint that made it necessary, a repair-ownership split whose alternative silently re-seats the wrong tab, three faults that only measurement surfaced, and the gate that says a hunk may not land before its ruling is filed.**

## D-157 — `D-156` LANDS: THE TAB REMOVAL IS **ONE DELETE**, `order` IS A BIJECTION WITH THE ROWS, AND THE TWO REPAIRS OWN DIFFERENT FAULTS — and the battery's own instrument had to move because **every clause of the seat pair has a REPAIR**, so a repaired fault could never measure a REFUSED write

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-09 · **Source:** `D-156`'s eight hunks, landed in `src/main/tabs-record/index.ts` alone, with its battery re-grained by the pass that owns it (`D-3`).

**WHAT LANDED.** `orderNamesItsTabs` gained its **sixth clause** — *an own key other than `order` the seat does not name* — so the pair now enforces `order ≡ rows` and not merely `order ⊆ rows`; **a row outside the seat is no longer legal, which matters because it was never merely legal but INVISIBLE** (`viewFrom` walks `order`). Its sentence re-grains to *"…carries no sequence of exactly its own tabs, once each"*. `healedFamily` takes a **required `scope`**: `'seat'` seats what the record holds, drops stray keys, fills the three field defaults and **preserves every `active` flag**; `'family'` does all that **and** §7's successor precedence — so `repairOrderSeat` owns **membership** and `healRecord` owns **activeness**, each by construction rather than by hope. `tabsMustStand`'s absence arm is **root-only**. **And `remove` performs ONE `delete(['tabs', id], TABS_RECORD_TIER)`**: shape arms and `namesASeat` refuse before any store verb, the act takes the PRE view and hands it to the observation, answers an already-absent tab **without a store act**, composes the **delete-only expectation and never writes it**, refreshes `lastView` on both arms, and answers from the comparator.

**THE ADDRESS RULE IS THE LANDING'S LOAD-BEARING HUNK, AND BOTH BODIES NOW OBEY IT.** `deeperThanRecord(address)` — an array strictly longer than `['tabs']` and prefixed by it — reads the record **raw through the handle**; the root (a registration ask, a root write, a hand-driven context) judges **the value the act hands**. Without it, `D-156`'s measured faults land as silent damage: **a leaf row write hands the seat pair the ROW** (two tab rows erased in the measurement) and **a descendant delete hands `tabsMustStand` `value: undefined`** (the floor minted over the siblings).

**THREE THINGS THE LANDING PASS MEASURED THAT THE DESIGN COULD NOT PREDICT.** **(i)** At `1.10.0` the **registration repair context carries BOTH `write` and `pending`**, so `isCommitCarrier` cannot discriminate the instant — `repairOrderSeat` branches on `typeof context.write === 'function'`, which is the `write`-member rule `D-73` states. **(ii)** **`delete(['tabs','never-was'])` throws `AddressNotFound`** rather than succeeding emptily, so the already-absent arm must precede every store verb — the ordering is not stylistic. **(iii)** The three `repaired` cases were each measured rather than reasoned: non-active → bare `committed`; active with a survivor → `committed, repaired: true` (the successor deliberately not predicted); last tab → `repaired: true` with the minted floor standing.

**THE BATTERY, AND THE INSTRUMENT DEFECT IT EXPOSED.** `crossing.test.ts` went **31 → 36 rows**: five new ones state what the change now **rests on** and nothing measured before — *a leaf row write SEATS* (`tab-9` lands in both the record and the seat), *a stray key is DROPPED*, *the LEAF-WRITE HAZARD CONTROL* (a row write must not wipe its siblings — the guard for the erasure measured above), *a descendant delete must NOT mint*, and *the delete direction* with its three `repaired` cases. One row was re-grained (the creation row seeding `{tab-1: row}` now **SEATS** it instead of taking the floor, per H3's own-key parse) and one gloss was re-grained (the `repaired`-on-`remove` description, from the composed intent to the delete-only expectation). **NO TOMBSTONE WAS NEEDED — no row's subject left**, which is the difference between this landing and the two composers before it.

**AND THE ROW THAT MEASURES ZERO DELIVERY HAD TO CHANGE ITS INSTRUMENT, WHICH IS A FINDING ABOUT THE CONSTRAINT SET.** That row asserts *a write a pair REFUSES delivers NOTHING*, and its refusing instrument was a **stray own key** on the record — which the new sixth clause now **repairs and drops**, so the pair passed on the re-run and no refusal occurred. **THE REASON IS THAT EVERY ONE OF THE SEAT PAIR'S SIX CLAUSES HAS A REPAIR**: a fault the module can name is repaired inside the caller's commit and the re-run passes, so **a repairable fault can never produce a refused write**. The instrument moved to a row's **`label`** — a member `healedFamily` copies through untouched, so the marker survives every repair and the write refuses again on the re-run — and the claim is now measured against an **unrepairable** fault, with a second half asserting the refused title landed nowhere. **A battery row whose instrument the module learns to repair is a row that silently stops measuring**, and only running it said so.

**THE READINGS AT THIS HEAD.** `npm test` **`2,937 passed / 2 skipped` of `2,939`** over **`141`** files, exit `0` — **up five rows and no file**, which is the whole battery move; `typecheck` **0**; `typecheck:tests` **0**; `build` **0**; `strata-marks check` **exit `0`** with the index REGENERATED at **`328` gating sites in `44` files** (`323` at `D-146`'s regen — the five new rows); `refs` resolves **`157`** rows; the demo bundle current at **39 inputs**. `tests/integration/store-tabs-record/` and `tests/integration/central-data/` **`111/111`** over `10` files.

**WHAT THIS ROW DOES NOT DO.** **`commit` still composes** — its intent satisfies the bijection by construction, so the two acts cannot disagree about the record's shape while only one moves — and **its row-write is OWED**, with the same delete-shaped argument already available to it. **`composeRecord` survives**, for the repairs and for `commit`'s intent and comparator; **nothing here claims its deletion**, and `D-155`'s ground (1) is why there was nothing to claim. **The audit's remaining seven of eleven still stand as `pending.md` row `1`.**

**ONE FRAGILITY WORTH NAMING FOR WHOEVER COMES NEXT.** With `D-155`'s judgement and this landing together, **`crossing.test.ts`'s pre-position row is now the ONLY place §7's successor precedence arm (ii) is measured** — so it must not be retired later without a replacement, and a future pass that finds it "duplicated" should read that sentence first.

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-156`** as prose, unchanged and extended by this row only. **This row records a whole-record rewrite becoming one delete, a constraint that became a bijection, two repairs that now own different faults by construction, three behaviours only a probe could establish, and a battery instrument that had to be made unrepairable before it could measure a refusal.**

## D-158 — CREATING A TAB IS ONE ROW WRITE AND THE SEAT REPAIR APPENDS IT: the mirror of `D-156`, and the two things creation does not share with removal — the APPEND POSITION stops being the act's to promise, and the PRE refresh becomes LOAD-BEARING because a stale observation would deactivate the tab the caller just opened

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-09 · **Source (user, verbatim):** *"Creating a new tab should be doable as a write, with the repair appending it to the order"* — `D-157`'s owed item, and the creation side of `D-156`'s ruling.

**THE MECHANISM WAS ALREADY PROVEN, SO NOTHING HERE IS SPECULATIVE.** `order` is a bijection with the rows (`D-157`), `repairOrderSeat` heals with `scope: 'seat'` — seating what the record holds by parsing it by its **own keys** and preserving every `active` flag — and a battery row landed at `D-157` already measures that `store.write(['tabs','tab-9'], 'file', row)` leaves the row **and** the seat naming it. **So the act is the only thing that changes**: `commit` stops composing and writing a whole record and writes **one row**.

**SETTLED QUESTION (1) — THE APPEND POSITION CONFIRMED FOR THE ACT'S OWN CASE, AND REFUTED AS A LAW.** A leaf write leaves exactly **one** stray key, and the seat parse's tail is `Object.keys` minus the seat key, so the new id lands **last** — the same position the composed act spelled with `[...order, row.id]`. Measured at the route shape: `['landing','tab-9']`, and the control `['landing','tab-1','tab-2','tab-9']`. **BUT WITH TWO OR MORE STRAYS THE TAIL IS THE STORE'S KEY-ORDERED IMAGE AND NOT INSERTION ORDER** — `profile-file.test.ts`'s boot row reads root keys `['panes','tabs','theme']`, `D-97` measured `{value, removal}` → `{removal, value}`, and JS own-key enumeration puts an index-like id first. **So the act's semantics do not change, and the position simply stops being the act's to promise**: a reader may rely on the **seat's array sequence**, never on having predicted where an append lands. That sentence belongs in the pages, because a caller who assumes insertion order will be right today and wrong the first time two strays appear.

**SETTLED QUESTION (2) — THE EVALUATION TRACE, VERIFIED, WITH TWO CORRECTIONS TO THE BRIEF'S READING.** For a new row written `active: true` beside a standing active row: **the seat pair refuses, the `'seat'` repair appends and preserves both flags, the re-run now has two actives, `exactly-one-active` refuses, `healRecord` keeps the written referent, and a third evaluation passes — one delivery, and the answer is `committed, repaired: true`.** The corrections: **(a)** `exactly-one-active` cannot speak first for a **stronger** reason than ask order — **an unseated row is INVISIBLE to `viewOf`**, whose entries are built from `order`, so to that pair the record reads exactly as it stood and would **pass**; the batch order and restart-from-top merely make its first ask land on the seated record. **(b)** `written` resolving to the new id does **not** depend on which refresh the act makes — every handle-side view lacks it, pre- or post-write — **but the PRE refresh is still OWED and load-bearing**: a **stale** `lastView` (a bypassing `data.write` never refreshes it) that carries that id's row makes `before !== undefined`, so `written` falls to `null` and `healedFamily`'s default `keep = standing[0]` **deactivates the tab the caller just created**. A refresh whose absence is invisible in the happy path is exactly the kind of line this corpus has learned to keep.

**THE COMPARATOR AND THE THREE CASES.** Expectation = `composeRecord(pre.order.includes(row.id) ? pre.order : [...pre.order, row.id], {...pre.entries, [row.id]: row})`, compared through `standsAsIntended` and **never written**. **(i) an UPDATE** — a row for an id the record already seats: no stray key, the bijection holds, no repair runs, **bare `committed`**. **(ii) a NEW row with `active: false`** — the seat repair runs and leaves exactly the expectation, so **bare `committed`**; the member changes only where a field default was filled, which is the row's own fault and is how the act answers today. **(iii) a NEW row with `active: true` beside an active row** — **`committed, repaired: true`**, the new tab the survivor. **A stray the act did not name is also `repaired: true`** — reported rather than hidden.

**THE GUARDS, AND ONE THAT IS NOW LOAD-BEARING ON ITS OWN.** `malformed-row` and `namesASeat` are unchanged and stay before any store verb — and **`namesASeat` is now the ONLY guard there, which makes it structural rather than tidy**: a leaf write at `['tabs','order']` would replace the seat, the pair would refuse (the seat is no longer an array), and `seatViewFrom` **skips the seat key** — so the repair would **drop the row the caller wrote** and keep everything else. The act must not refuse an otherwise-legal unseated row: **that write IS the creation**, the pair refuses inside the commit, and its repair seats it.

**WHAT THE ACT OWES AROUND THE WRITE.** `lastView` exactly as `remove` owes it — **pre** (one read, two uses: the comparator and the observation) and **post on both arms** — with the pre refresh load-bearing per the correction above. **FIELD DEFAULTS: nothing.** `healedFamily` fills `label`/`target`/`lastActiveAt` in **both** scopes, so the seat repair already lands them, and pre-filling them in the act would be a second authority — and would **suppress `repaired`**, which is the signal the renderer needs.

**`composeRecord`'S FATE, AGAIN WITHOUT OVERCLAIM.** It **stays**, for the three repairs **and for both comparators** — `remove`'s delete-only expectation and `commit`'s creation expectation — compared and never written (`D-155` ground 1). **`D-155`'s ground (2) is DISCHARGED and not waived**: a clean creation answers a bare `committed`, which is the honesty that ground was protecting.

**THE GATE, AND THIS ROW IS IT.** The design pages record that H9–H11 await a register row of their own, because `D-157` books this instruction as **OWED**. **This row is that registration**, so `store-tabs-record`'s L4 may now take: **H9** the act — pre `viewOf` read → `lastView` → **one leaf write** → post refresh on both arms → the creation expectation through `standsAsIntended`, with **five composition statements deleted**; **H10** five docblocks and headers replaced in place (the file header, `TabsRecord`'s `commit`, two in `composeRecord`, `createTabsRecord`'s); **H11** nothing else moves — no exported name, no surface, no channel, no type, and `signatures.md` byte-unmoved but for its rev token (`C-7`). **Its battery is the landing pass's** (`D-3`): **four new rows** — the creation through the route (seat appended, row admitted, one active, derived `landing`, **ONE delivery**); the `active: false` new row answered **bare `committed`** (a case nothing states today); **the stale-observation control**, which reds on H9 with its pre refresh removed — and the append position at the route (three creations in creation order; a re-`commit` does not move a seated id) — plus **three re-grains**: the two leaf-write rows' false gloss that *"`commit` is not changed in this pass"*, the section prose around them, and `repaired`-on-`commit`'s gloss to the creation expectation.

**THE READINGS AT THIS HEAD ARE `D-157`'S** — nothing in this row moves a byte; its product is the design pages, at `docs/L3/store-tabs-record/design.md` **`128`/150** (rev 15), `docs/L2/store-tabs-record/interface.md` **`149`/150** (rev 8) and `docs/L3/store-tabs-record/signatures.md` **`150`/150** (rev 7, `C-7` satisfied).

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-157`** as prose, unchanged and extended by this row only. **This row records the creation side of a ruling that already had its removal side, an append position that is right once and ungoverned after, a stale observation whose absence would deactivate the tab just created, and a guard that became structural the moment the seat could be written by address.**

## D-159 — `D-158` LANDS: CREATION IS **ONE ROW WRITE** AND THE SEAT REPAIR APPENDS — and the row must CORRECT ITS OWN PREDECESSOR, because the pre refresh it called LOAD-BEARING is **not observably so**: the activeness pair refreshes its own capture on every ask, so the design's owed control could not fail and the battery had to measure the invariant instead

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-09 · **Source:** `D-158`'s three hunks, landed in `src/main/tabs-record/index.ts` alone, with its battery re-grained and its residual docblocks closed by the passes that own them.

**WHAT LANDED.** `commit` performs **one leaf row write** — `store.write(['tabs', row.id], TABS_RECORD_TIER, row)` — and the seat repair appends the new id to `order`. **Six statements left the act** (the `seats()` read, the `next` append, the `entries` build, the member assignment, the `composeRecord` intent, and the whole-record `standing.write`) and **none survived**. Its flow is: four shape arms → **one `viewOf` read used twice** (observation and expectation) → the creation expectation `composeRecord(pre.order ∪ {id}, pre.entries ∪ {id: row})`, **composed and never written** → the leaf write → `lastView` refreshed on both arms → `standsAsIntended`. **The three `repaired` cases answer as designed**: an update of a seated id is a bare `committed` (no stray key, no repair); a new `active: false` row is a bare `committed` on purpose (the seat repair ran and left exactly the expectation); a new `active: true` row beside an active one is `committed, repaired: true` with the new tab the survivor. **And `namesASeat` is now the act's only pre-store guard, which makes it structural**: a write at `['tabs','order']` would be refused by the pair and then **dropped** by the seat parse's own-key skip — the caller's row would vanish.

**THE CORRECTION THIS ROW OWES ITS PREDECESSOR, AND IT IS THE SUBSTANTIVE ONE.** `D-158` stated that the act's **PRE** `lastView` refresh was **load-bearing**, on the reasoning that a stale observation carrying the new row's id would make `written` fall to `null` and let `healedFamily`'s default `keep = standing[0]` deactivate the tab just created. **MEASURED, IT IS NOT.** `exactlyOneActive` **assigns `lastView = post` at the top of its own body on every ask**, and the pairs standing at `['tabs']` are asked inside bypassing whole-record writes too — **so the observation is never stale at the ask.** L4 removed the refresh and everything stayed green; the battery pass then reproduced it and went further, building **the design's exact shape** (a bypassing write that drops an id, then a `commit` of the identical row) and finding that **green as well with the refresh removed**. **The line is kept — it makes the act's one read serve both the comparator and the observation — but it is kept for economy and not for the referent, and `D-158`'s claim is SUPERSEDED IN FACT** (rows are not reworded, `D-46`).

**AND THE DEAD CONTROL BECAME A REAL ROW, BECAUSE THE BATTERY REFUSED TO KEEP CEREMONY.** `D-158` owed a *stale-observation control* — a row that would red if the act's pre refresh were removed. **A row that cannot fail is a deleted row wearing a new name**, so the pass wrote the **actual invariant** instead: *the activeness pair keeps the row whose value moved **in the record it judges**, because its own ask refreshes the capture* — **and then PROVED it red** by deleting `exactlyOneActive`'s own `lastView = post`, which flips the survivor to the stale capture's answer (`tab-2`) where the record's answer is `tab-1`, and **proved it green** with H9's refresh removed. The source was restored byte-identical. **That is the difference between a measurement and a reassurance**, and it is now the corpus's second worked example of it after `D-157`'s unrepairable instrument.

**THE APPEND POSITION IS PROMISED AS A READING, NOT A LAW.** A creation appends (`['landing','tab-1']` from `['landing']`), and three creations produce creation order — but the battery's gloss pins **the seat's own array sequence**, not an insertion-order guarantee, because with two or more stray keys the parse's tail is the store's **key-ordered image** (`D-158`). A caller who assumes insertion order is right today and wrong the first time two strays appear, and the row says so.

**THE BATTERY.** `crossing.test.ts` **36 → 40 rows**: the creation through the route (seat appended, row admitted field-by-field, one active, derived `landing`, **ONE delivery**), the new-`active:false` bare answer (a case nothing stated), the append-position row, and the observation invariant above. **Three re-grains**: the two leaf-write rows' gloss that said *"`commit` is not changed in this pass"* (it is now that very leaf write — and the rows stay **store-driven** deliberately, because a `commit`-driven form would pass on a module composing beside it), the section prose around them, and `repaired`-on-`commit`'s gloss to the creation expectation. **No tombstone** — no subject left.

**THREE RESIDUAL DOCBLOCKS THE LANDING FALSIFIED, ALL CLOSED IN PLACE.** `rowOf`'s *"erased when `commit` rebuilds the record from `rowsNow`"*; `composeRecord`'s bullet still listing *"`commit`'s"* among its reasons; and `standsAsIntended`'s *"hold the record this act INTENDED, **write it**, and compare it"* — false for both acts since `D-157` and `D-158`. Each now states what is true: **the expectation is composed in memory and never written at either call site**, and the composer survives for **three repairs and two comparators**.

**THE READINGS AT THIS HEAD.** `npm test` **`2,941 passed / 2 skipped` of `2,943`** over **`141`** files — **up four rows and no file**; `typecheck` **0**; `typecheck:tests` **0**; `build` **0**; `strata-marks check` **exit `0`** with the index regenerated at **`332` gating sites in `44` files** (`328` at `D-157`'s regen); `refs` resolves **`159`** rows; the demo bundle current at **39 inputs**. ## D-160 — THE COMPOSER THAT RUNS IS THE RENDERER'S, AND THE RULING HAD LANDED ON A ROUTE NOTHING CALLS: two functions share the name, the live `focus` act still writes a whole record, and the decomposition keeps **ONE** element — the row — and deletes the rest

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-09 · **Source (user, verbatim):** *"Break out the elements of the composer that are needed for the repairs and make them separate functions"*, taken against a tree that turned out to have **two composers under one name**.

**THE FINDING, VERIFIED CLAUSE BY CLAUSE, AND NO PAGE HAD SAID IT.** **`src/main/tabs-record/index.ts`'s `composeRecord(order, entries)`** is private, nine lines, and serves **three repairs (which write it) and two comparators (which compare it)**. **`src/renderer/renderer.ts`'s `export function composeRecord(reading, seat)`** — *"THE RENDERER'S ONE WHOLE-RECORD COMPOSITION"* — is **the live tab-creation path**: `provident focus` → `mcp-server`'s `backend.invoke('focus')` → `renderer.ts`'s `case 'focus'` → `focusActRoute` → `dataRoute('write', { path: ['tabs'], tier: 'file', value: composeRecord(reading, seat) })` → `window.provident.data.write` → the data surface → the store. **`IPC_TABS_COMMIT` has NO caller in `src/renderer/`** — the preload exposes a `tabs.commit` member that the renderer's own `TabsClientBridge` does not declare (`read` and `onChanged` only), and the route's sole caller in the tree is `tests/e2e/store-crossings/crossings-live.mjs`. **So `D-158`'s leaf row write landed on a route the app never calls, while the act the app does call still wrote a whole record.** That is a coverage blind spot of the corpus's own making: two functions, one name, and no page distinguished them.

**THE ELEMENTS, EACH JUDGED.** The renderer composer's docblock names four: **C1** the seat (copied, extended by exactly one id on an open), **C2** every row copied whole, **S7** the unresolved arm, **C3/C4** the one row whose bytes move. **KEPT — and it is the whole of the act's product — `C3`/`C4` as `rowForSeat(seat)`**: an open writes `{id, target, active: true, lastActiveAt}` with the clock read once; a switch writes the reading's own row with `active: true` and a fresh stamp. **BECOME THE REPAIR'S — `C1`'s OPEN arm**, because the seat pair refuses the stray key and `repairOrderSeat`'s `'seat'` scope appends it; **and "the standing active row left exactly as carried"**, which is now enforced by the **ADDRESS** — the act does not name that row at all, and `healRecord` deactivates it rather than deleting it. **DELETED — `C2` whole** (a leaf write cannot drop a sibling), **`C1`'s SWITCH copy** (the seat already names that id), **`S7`'s internal guard** (it becomes the type `ActedSeat = Exclude<FocusSeat, {kind:'unresolved'}>`), **and `composeRecord` itself.**

**THE DECOMPOSITION IS ONE FUNCTION PLUS FOUR DELETIONS, AND A SECOND NAME WOULD BE A RENAME.** The instruction asked for the elements "as separate functions"; **measured, exactly one element survives as the act's own**, so the honest answer is `rowForSeat(seat)` beside `seatOf`, with `FocusSeat`'s seat arm carrying **the row `seatOf` resolved** — so the row is built once, no lookup is repeated, and no default is invented. **The address `['tabs', row.id]` at `file` stays where it is**: a function for it would be indirection.

**THE SWITCH ARM WRITES ITS ROW WHOLE, AND PER-MEMBER LEAVES ARE UNLAWFUL HERE.** Splitting `active` and `lastActiveAt` into `['tabs',id,'active']` and `['tabs',id,'lastActiveAt']` is **two commits, two passes, two deliveries and two log entries** — and `data.write` answers only the last, so **`repaired` would report half an act**. Worse, `active`'s repair **moves the row the second write would be about**, and a landed `active` with a refused stamp is a state **no pair refuses and no repair heals** — each verb restores its own state. That is `D-152`'s shape misapplied: sibling-safety is `D-138`/`D-154`'s reason, and these two members are not siblings but one act's two halves.

**THE TWO ROWS THAT PIN IT ARE RE-GRAINED, NOT RETIRED.** **`DC-6`** — *"`composeRecord`'s round trip, through the act"* — becomes the **leaf row write**: the value's key set is the row's own (no `order`, no `landing`), the standing active row is **absent from the write** and `active: false` in the **answer**, `order` is read off the answer with the opened id last, and exactly **two** `tabs.read()` calls stand. **Its realm double must be re-grained in the same pass** — it reads `value['order']`, so a bare row would **erase** the record there, and that hazard is now stated on the page rather than discovered by a red. **`S-9`**'s `/composeRecord\s*\(/` anchor becomes the **leaf address plus the composer's absence**, because retiring it would make the four absences beside it vacuous. **AND THE ROUND-TRIP PROPERTY GETS SHARPER RATHER THAN WEAKER**: it now spans **two objects** — the sent VALUE proves *no member invented and no whole record*, the ANSWER proves *`order` preserved, one active, the older row deactivated*.

**THE AFTER-READ STAYS, AND IS MORE OWED THAN BEFORE** (`D-70`). The answer is the record that **stands**, and the act can predict neither the append position (a reading, not a law — `D-159`) nor `exactly-one-active`'s survivor. One read before (the seat and the row), one after (the answer), and it feeds no delivery.

**THE SAME PASS MEASURED THE MODULE-SIDE COMPOSER AND DECLINED TO SPLIT IT.** For `store-tabs-record`'s `composeRecord`, **no element is used alone by any call site**, the seat copy is dead defence at all five (every caller hands a freshly built array), the seat-first placement is read by nobody, **and the drop — which this seat called load-bearing one turn ago — is NOT observable**: `canonicalRecord` re-projects the comparison by the seat, so a `{...pre.entries, order}` spelling answers the same `repaired`. **That refutes what this seat reported to the user and is recorded as a refutation**: the drop governs what may be **written** (a repair's value must be bijective or the pair refuses its own write), never what is **compared**. So the module keeps its nine lines and gains one named relation — `rowsSeatedUnder(order, entries)`, the seated pairs — and no second name beyond it.

**THE GATE, AND THIS ROW IS IT.** The pages record the hunks; nothing in `src/` has moved. **This row registers the ruling**, so `renderer-wiring`'s L4 may take **H1** the `TabsRecordRow` import, **H2** `FocusSeat`'s seat arm carrying its row, **H3** `seatOf` answering what it resolved, **H4** `ActedSeat` + `rowForSeat` with `composeRecord` deleted, **H5** S8's write at `['tabs', row.id]`/`file` through the existing passthrough, **H6** the three docblocks that name the composer. **Its battery is the landing pass's** (`D-3`): the `DC-6` and `S-9` re-grains above, `actRealm`'s `write` double, and four new rows — the leaf address, the one-row key set, the open row's minimality, and the two-read count.

**THE READINGS AT THIS HEAD ARE `D-159`'S** — nothing in this row moves a byte; its product is six design pages, every one at or under its ceiling: `docs/L3/renderer-wiring/design.md` **149**/150 (rev 11), `docs/L3/renderer-wiring/signatures.md` **149**/150 (rev 10), `docs/L2/renderer-wiring/interface.md` **149**/150 (rev 7), `docs/L3/store-tabs-record/design.md` **147**/150 (rev 16), `docs/L2/store-tabs-record/interface.md` **149**/150 (rev 9), `docs/L3/store-tabs-record/signatures.md` **150**/150 (rev 8).

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-159`** as prose, unchanged and extended by this row only. **This row records two composers under one name and a ruling that reached the one nothing calls, a decomposition that keeps a single element because a single element survives, a switch arm that cannot be split without letting `repaired` report half, and a refutation of this seat's own "load-bearing" claim about a drop that the comparison cannot see.**

## D-161 — `D-160` LANDS: THE LIVE `focus` ACT WRITES **ONE ROW**, `rowForSeat` IS ALL THAT SURVIVED THE COMPOSER, AND THE BATTERY'S REALM DOUBLE HAD TO BE REBUILT — over the old one a bare row would have PASSED FOR THE WRONG REASON

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-09 · **Source:** `D-160`'s six hunks, landed in `src/renderer/renderer.ts` alone, with its battery re-grained by the pass that owns it (`D-3`).

**WHAT LANDED.** `FocusSeat`'s acted arm carries **the row `seatOf` resolved** (`{ kind: 'seat'; row: TabsRecordRow }`), so no lookup is repeated and no default is invented downstream; `seatOf` answers what it resolved; **`composeRecord` is deleted** from this file with its docblock; and in its place stands the one element that turned out to be the act's whole product:

```ts
export function rowForSeat(seat: ActedSeat): TabsRecordRow {
  const lastActiveAt = new Date().toISOString()          // the clock, read ONCE, at the call
  return seat.kind === 'open'
    ? { id: seat.id, target: seat.target, active: true, lastActiveAt }
    : { ...seat.row, active: true, lastActiveAt }
}
```

**And S8 is one leaf address**: `dataRoute('write', { path: ['tabs', row.id], tier: 'file', value: row })` — the same passthrough, one row, **no composed record**. The act writes **no `order`, no `landing`, no `active: false`**, and does not name the row that stood active at all: the seat repair appends the id, and `healRecord` deactivates the standing row by the address rather than by the act's hand. **The after-read stays and is not redundant** (`D-70`): the answer is the record that stands, and the act can predict neither the append position (`D-159`) nor the survivor.

**THE BATTERY'S REALM DOUBLE WAS THE TRAP, AND REBUILDING IT WAS THE LOAD-BEARING HALF OF THIS PASS.** `actRealm`'s `write` read `value['order']` and rebuilt the record from it — so handed a **bare row** it would have **erased** the record (`order: []`, `rows: []`). Over that double a leaf row write could have **passed for the wrong reason**: the assertions about the written value would hold while the answer was a fiction. It now **applies the record's own two repairs** to the record it holds — `'seat'` appends the written row's id to `order`, `exactly-one-active` keeps the **written referent** and deactivates the rest — **and refuses a write it cannot place** (a root `['tabs']` path, or a value that is not a row) instead of silently rebuilding. It also logs every read as an ordered snapshot, so *"one before, one after"* is **recorded rather than asserted**.

**THE ROWS MOVED, NONE WEAKENED, AND EACH NEW ONE WAS PROVED ABLE TO FAIL.** **`DC-6`** is re-grained, not retired: one write at the leaf, the value's key set the row's own with **no `order` and no `landing`**, the standing row **absent from the write** and `active: false` in the **answer**, `order` read off the answer with the opened id **last**, exactly **two** reads, no app-graph push. **Its round-trip property survives and sharpens** — it now spans the sent value and the answer, which is two objects where it was one. **`S-9`**'s anchor becomes the **leaf address present plus the composer's absence**, with an in-row control that fires on the deleted call form and one that distinguishes `['tabs']` from `['tabs', row.id]` — because the four absences the row already asserts would be vacuous without a live subject. **And four rows are new**: `DC-9` the leaf address on both arms, `DC-10` the one-row key set (open = exactly four members; switch = the reading's row whole, as its control), `DC-11` the **open row's minimality** (no `label`, no `error`, with a switching control carrying both), and `DC-12` the **two-read count** with pre/post snapshots and `D-70`'s after-read as the answer. **They went into the compliance file rather than `focus-tool.test.ts` for a stated reason**: that file carries a `rework` mark and reports **on notice** (`D-30`), and a row pinning a landed shape must live where it **gates**.

**AND EVERY ONE OF THEM WAS CHECKED AGAINST THE PRE-LANDING SOURCE.** On a throwaway copy of `HEAD`'s `renderer.ts` in `/tmp`, `DC-6` and `DC-9`…`DC-12` all **failed**; reverting the address red the `S-9` leaf half; removing the after-read red three of them. The copy was deleted and `src/` was never touched. **That is the check this corpus keeps having to relearn: a row that cannot fail is a deleted row wearing a new name**, and it is now the third worked example after `D-157`'s unrepairable instrument and `D-159`'s observation invariant.

**THE READINGS AT THIS HEAD.** `npm test` **`2,945 passed / 2 skipped` of `2,947`** over **`141`** files, exit `0` — **up four rows and no file**; `typecheck` **0**; `typecheck:tests` **0**; `build` **0**; `strata-marks check` **exit `0`** with the index regenerated at **`336` gating sites in `44` files** (`332` at `D-159`'s regen); the demo bundle rebuilt to **39 inputs, every digest agreeing**.

**WHAT THIS COMPLETES.** **The ruling is now on the path the app uses.** `provident focus` no longer writes a whole record: it writes one row, the seat repair appends the id, and the activeness repair settles the pair — so **both** tab-record acts and **the live renderer act** now write single operations, and every family invariant is settled by a repair that owns exactly one fault. **Two composers still stand and neither is an act's writer**: `renderer-wiring`'s is deleted outright, and `store-tabs-record`'s survives for its three repairs and two comparators with `rowsSeatedUnder` named beside it (`D-160`) — **that one-line extraction is designed and NOT yet landed**, and it is the only work this series leaves designed-but-unlanded.

**ITS REGISTER IS `D-71` TO `D-160`** as prose, unchanged and extended by this row only. **This row records a composer reduced to the single element that was its product, a realm double that had to learn the repairs before it could be trusted, two anchors re-grained rather than retired, four rows proved able to fail before they were called measurements, and a ruling that finally reached the path the app actually uses.**

## D-162 — `rowsSeatedUnder` LANDS AND THE MODULE-SIDE COMPOSER STOPS AT ONE NAMED RELATION: the element with a subtlety is extracted, the assembly keeps its nine lines, and the extraction is a LEGIBILITY act whose failure mode is measured rather than imagined

**Status:** ACTIVE · **Date:** 2026-11-09 · **Source:** `D-160`'s registration — the one item `D-161` recorded as designed and not landed.

**WHAT LANDED, AND NOTHING BEYOND IT.** `rowsSeatedUnder(order, entries)` is extracted: **the seated pairs, in seat order — for each id the SEAT names, that id's row iff the entries map holds one** — so the value can never carry an id the seat does not name. `composeRecord` keeps its **name, signature and output shape** (the seat member first, the seat array copied) and is re-spelled over those pairs, so the relation and the assembly read as two expressions instead of one indivisible loop. Its docblock names the relation. **`remove`'s expectation comment gained exactly one clause** — that `pre.entries` is handed over **WHOLE**, the removed row still in it, and the **filtered seat** is what excludes it; the old sentence said "the pre reading's `order` and its rows minus the id", which described the outcome without saying who performs it. **`rowOf`'s docblock was left untouched** — an earlier pass had already re-grained it, and the module's rule is that a docblock is corrected by the pass whose subject it is.

**ONE RELATION AND NO SECOND NAME, ON A MEASUREMENT RATHER THAN A TASTE.** `D-160` measured the five call sites and found **no element used alone by any of them**: the seat copy is dead defence everywhere (every caller hands a freshly built array), the seat-first placement is read by nobody, and the drop — which this seat had called load-bearing one turn before — is **invisible to the comparison**, because `canonicalRecord` re-projects by the seat. **So the honest cut is the one element with a real subtlety named, and the nine lines kept.** A three-function decomposition was available and was declined in writing, which is the difference between a legibility act and a rename dressed as a design.

**THE FAILURE MODE IS MEASURED, AND IT IS THE ONE THAT WOULD HAVE BEEN SILENT.** Both tab batteries read **`61/61` identical before and after**, the full suite is unchanged at **`2,945 passed / 2 skipped` of `2,947`** over `141` files, and `signatures.test.ts` `C-7` stands — because a private function is not in the signature surface. **A refactor whose whole risk is a changed emitted order or a dropped member is proved harmless only by a suite that would notice either**, and this one is the same suite that caught the erasures at `D-157` and `D-159`.

**WHAT THIS COMPLETES.** `D-161`'s "designed and not landed" item is now landed, so **this series leaves nothing designed-but-unlanded**. **Both tab-record composers are accounted for and neither is an act's writer**: `renderer-wiring`'s is deleted (`D-161`) and `store-tabs-record`'s serves **three repairs and two comparators**, with `rowsSeatedUnder` naming the relation it rests on. **`pending.md` row `1` — the audit's remaining seven of eleven — is the only queued work left.**
